## XXII A Dress Rehearsal / 第二十二章 彩排

===Original===
Some people in Trevor’s place might have taken the earliest opportunity of confronting Rand-Brown, so as to settle the matter in hand without delay. Trevor thought of doing this, but finally decided to let the matter rest for a day, until he should have found out with some accuracy what chance he stood.

After four o’clock, therefore, on the next day, having had tea in his study, he went across to the baths, in search of O’Hara. He intended that before the evening was over the Irishman should have imparted to him some of his skill with the hands. He did not know that for a man absolutely unscientific with his fists there is nothing so fatal as to take a boxing lesson on the eve of battle. A little knowledge is a dangerous thing. He is apt to lose his recklessness﻿—which might have stood by him well﻿—in exchange for a little quite useless science. He is neither one thing nor the other, neither a natural fighter nor a skilful boxer.

This point O’Hara endeavoured to press upon him as soon as he had explained why it was that he wanted coaching on this particular afternoon.

The Irishman was in the gymnasium, punching the ball, when Trevor found him. He generally put in a quarter of an hour with the punching-ball every evening, before Moriarty turned up for the customary six rounds.

===Chinese===
换了别人处在特雷弗（Trevor）的位置，多半会立刻抓住机会去找兰德-布朗（Rand-Brown）当面对质，好把手里这桩事尽快了结。特雷弗也想过这么做，但最后还是决定先搁上一天，等摸清了自己的胜算再作道理。

于是第二天下午四点以后，他在书房里喝过了茶，便往浴室那边去找奥哈拉（O’Hara）。他打算在天黑之前，让这位爱尔兰人把自己手上的一些本事传授给他。他并不知道，对一个拳头全无章法的人来说，临战前夜学拳击是最致命的事。一知半解是件危险的东西。他很可能会丢掉自己那股不管不顾的猛劲——那原本也许能帮上大忙——换来一点全无用处的花架子。到头来他两头不靠：既算不上天生的斗士，也当不成有章法的拳手。

特雷弗刚说明了自己为什么偏在这个下午求人指点，奥哈拉就急着把这一点讲给他听。

特雷弗找到他时，这位爱尔兰人正在体育馆里打吊球。他每天傍晚照例要练上一刻钟吊球，然后莫里亚蒂（Moriarty）才来跟他进行雷打不动的六个回合。

===Original===
“Want me to teach ye a few tricks?” he said. “What’s that for?”

“I’ve got a mill coming on soon,” explained Trevor, trying to make the statement as if it were the most ordinary thing in the world for a school prefect, who was also captain of football, head of a house, and in the cricket eleven, to be engaged for a fight in the near future.

“Mill!” exclaimed O’Hara. “You! An’ why?”

“Never mind why,” said Trevor. “I’ll tell you afterwards, perhaps. Shall I put on the gloves now?”

“Wait,” said O’Hara, “I must do my quarter of an hour with the ball before I begin teaching other people how to box. Have ye a watch?”

“Yes.”

“Then time me. I’ll do four rounds of three minutes each, with a minute’s rest in between. That’s more than I’ll do at Aldershot, but it’ll get me fit. Ready?”

“Time,” said Trevor.

===Chinese===
「要俺教你几手？」他说，「这是图什么？」

「我不久要打一场架，」特雷弗解释道，尽力把这话说得稀松平常，仿佛一个身为级长、橄榄球队长、宿舍舍长又入了板球校队的人，近期约了一场打斗，是天底下再寻常不过的事。

「打架！」奥哈拉叫道，「你！为什么？」

「为什么先别管，」特雷弗说，「回头我兴许会告诉你。现在就戴上拳套吧？」

「等等，」奥哈拉说，「先教别人打拳之前，俺那刻钟吊球还欠着呢。你带着表吗？」

「带着。」

「那你给俺计时。俺打四轮，每轮三分钟，中间歇一分钟。到了奥尔德肖特（Aldershot），俺还打不了这么多呢，可这能让俺练出状态。预备？」

「开始。」特雷弗喊道。

===Original===
He watched O’Hara assailing the swinging ball with considerable envy. Why, he wondered, had he not gone in for boxing? Everybody ought to learn to box. It was bound to come in useful some time or other. Take his own case. He was very much afraid﻿—no, afraid was not the right word, for he was not that. He was very much of opinion that Rand-Brown was going to have a most enjoyable time when they met. And the final house-match was to be played next Monday. If events turned out as he could not help feeling they were likely to turn out, he would be too battered to play in that match. Donaldson’s would probably win whether he played or not, but it would be bitter to be laid up on such an occasion. On the other hand, he must go through with it. He did not believe in letting other people take a hand in settling his private quarrels.

But he wished he had learned to box. If only he could hit that dancing, jumping ball with a fifth of the skill that O’Hara was displaying, his wiriness and pluck might see him through. O’Hara finished his fourth round with his leathern opponent, and sat down, panting.

===Chinese===
他望着奥哈拉向那只来回晃荡的吊球发起猛攻，心里好生羡慕。他想，自己当初怎么就没去练拳击呢？人人都该学学拳击，早晚总有派上用场的时候。就拿他自己眼下的事来说吧。他很怕——不，「怕」字用得不对，他并不是怕。他心里非常肯定，等到两人碰面的时候，兰德-布朗准会痛痛快快地享受一番。而宿舍决赛下周一就要开打。倘若事情真朝他不由自主预感的那个方向演变，到时候他会被打得爬不起来，上不了场。不管他上不上场，唐纳森宿舍（Donaldson’s）多半都会赢，可偏偏在这种节骨眼上躺着养伤，滋味实在难受。话又说回来，这一架他非打不可。他素来不赞成让别人插手替自己了结私怨。

但他还是恨不得自己学过拳击。哪怕他打那只蹦蹦跳跳的球时，能有奥哈拉所显露功夫的两成，凭他一身韧劲和胆气，说不定也能撑过去。奥哈拉跟那个皮革对手打完第四轮，坐下来直喘气。

===Original===
“Pretty useful, that,” commented Trevor, admiringly.

“Ye should see Moriarty,” gasped O’Hara.

“Now, will ye tell me why it is you’re going to fight, and with whom you’re going to fight?”

“Very well. It’s with Rand-Brown.”

“Rand-Brown!” exclaimed O’Hara. “But, me dearr man, he’ll ate you.”

===Chinese===
「相当有两下子。」特雷弗赞叹道。

「你该瞧瞧莫里亚蒂。」奥哈拉喘着气说。

「好了，现在你总可以告诉俺，你为什么要打架，要跟谁打架了吧？」

「好吧。是跟兰德-布朗。」

「兰德-布朗！」奥哈拉叫了起来，「可是，俺亲爱的伙计，他会把你生吞活剥了的。」

===Original===
Trevor gave a rather annoyed laugh. “I must say I’ve got a nice, cheery, comforting lot of friends,” he said. “That’s just what Clowes has been trying to explain to me.”

“Clowes is quite right,” said O’Hara, seriously. “Has the thing gone too far for ye to back out? Without climbing down, of course,” he added.

“Yes,” said Trevor, “there’s no question of my getting out of it. I daresay I could. In fact, I know I could. But I’m not going to.”

“But, me dearr man, ye haven’t an earthly chance. I assure ye ye haven’t. I’ve seen Rand-Brown with the gloves on. That was last term. He’s not put them on since Moriarty bate him in the middles, so he may be out of practice. But even then he’d be a bad man to tackle. He’s big an’ he’s strong, an’ if he’d only had the heart in him he’d have been going up to Aldershot instead of Moriarty. That’s what he’d be doing. An’ you can’t box at all. Never even had the gloves on.”

“Never. I used to scrap when I was a kid, though.”

===Chinese===
特雷弗笑得有几分恼火。「不得不说，我交的这一伙朋友，可真会宽心鼓劲。」他说，「克劳斯（Clowes）跟我掰扯的也是这么一套。」

「克劳斯说得一点不错，」奥哈拉正色道，「事情已经闹到没法抽身的地步了吗？当然，俺指的是不低头服软的那种抽身，」他补了一句。

「是啊，」特雷弗说，「脱身是谈不上的。我敢说我办得到。其实，我知道我办得到。可我不打算那么干。」

「可是，俺亲爱的伙计，你压根儿没有半点胜算。俺向你保证，你一点胜算都没有。俺见过兰德-布朗戴上拳套的样子。那是上学期的事。自打莫里亚蒂在中量级里把他打败，他就再没戴过拳套，所以手也许生了。可就算这样，他也是个难对付的角色。他又高又壮，当年只要有点心气，上奥尔德肖特的就是他，轮不到莫里亚蒂。他本来该是去那儿的人。而你压根儿不会打拳，连拳套都没戴过。」

「从没戴过。不过小时候倒常跟人扭打。」

===Original===
“That’s no use,” said O’Hara, decidedly. “But you haven’t said what it is that ye’ve got against Rand-Brown. What is it?”

“I don’t see why I shouldn’t tell you. You’re in it as well. In fact, if it hadn’t been for the bat turning up, you’d have been considerably more in it than I am.”

“What!” cried O’Hara. “Where did you find it? Was it in the grounds? When was it you found it?”

Whereupon Trevor gave him a very full and exact account of what had happened. He showed him the two letters from the League, touched on Milton’s connection with the affair, traced the gradual development of his suspicions, and described with some approach to excitement the scene in Ruthven’s study, and the explanations that had followed it.

===Chinese===
「那顶不了用，」奥哈拉断然说道，「可你还没说，你跟兰德-布朗之间到底有什么过节。是什么？」

「我看没什么不能对你说的。这件事你也有份。说实在的，要不是那根球棒冒了出来，你卷进去的分量比我深得多。」

「什么！」奥哈拉叫道，「你们在哪儿找到它的？是在操场上吗？是什么时候找到的？」

于是特雷弗把前后经过原原本本、一五一十地讲给他听。他给他看了联盟（the League）的两封来信，提到弥尔顿（Milton）与此事的牵连，细数了自己的怀疑如何一步步加深，还带着几分激动，描绘了拉思文（Ruthven）书房里那一幕，以及随之而来的种种解释。

===Original===
“Now do you wonder,” he concluded, “that I feel as if a few rounds with Rand-Brown would do me good.”

O’Hara breathed hard.

“My word!” he said. “I’d like to see ye kill him.”

“But,” said Trevor, “as you and Clowes have been pointing out to me, if there’s going to be a corpse, it’ll be me. However, I mean to try. Now perhaps you wouldn’t mind showing me a few tricks.”

===Chinese===
「现在你该明白了吧，」他末了说道，「为什么我觉得跟兰德-布朗打上几个回合，对我大有好处。」

奥哈拉重重地喘了口气。

「好家伙！」他说，「俺真想看你把他揍个半死。」

「不过，」特雷弗说，「正像你和克劳斯一直跟我指出的，真要出人命的话，躺下的准是我。话虽如此，我还是要试一试。现在，也许你不介意指点我几手了吧？」

===Original===
“Take my advice,” said O’Hara, “and don’t try any of that foolery.”

“Why, I thought you were such a believer in science,” said Trevor in surprise.

“So I am, if you’ve enough of it. But it’s the worst thing ye can do to learn a trick or two just before a fight, if you don’t know anything about the game already. A tough, rushing fighter is ten times as good as a man who’s just begun to learn what he oughtn’t to do.”

“Well, what do you advise me to do, then?” asked Trevor, impressed by the unwonted earnestness with which the Irishman delivered this pugilistic homily, which was a paraphrase of the views dinned into the ears of every novice by the school instructor.

“I must do something.”

“The best thing ye can do,” said O’Hara, thinking for a moment, “is to put on the gloves and have a round or two with me. Here’s Moriarty at last. We’ll get him to time us.”

===Chinese===
「听俺一句劝，」奥哈拉说，「那种胡闹的花招，一个也别学。」

「咦，我还以为你最讲究拳法章法呢。」特雷弗讶然道。

「讲究是讲究，前提是你学得够多。可要是你对这门道本来就一窍不通，临打架前才去学个一招半式，那是天下最糟的事。一个横冲直撞的猛汉，比一个刚开始学着弄明白哪些事不该做的人，强上十倍。」

「那么依你看，我该怎么办呢？」特雷弗问道。爱尔兰人说这番拳经训诫时那股少见的认真劲儿打动了他——这套话不过是把校里拳击教官灌进每个新手耳朵里的老生常谈换了个说法而已。

「我总得做点什么。」

「你最好的办法，」奥哈拉想了想，说，「就是戴上拳套，跟俺练上一两个回合。莫里亚蒂总算来了。咱们让他计时。」

===Original===
As much explanation as was thought good for him having been given to the newcomer, to account for Trevor’s newly-acquired taste for things pugilistic, Moriarty took the watch, with instructions to give them two minutes for the first round.

“Go as hard as you can,” said O’Hara to Trevor, as they faced one another, “and hit as hard as you like. It won’t be any practice if you don’t. I shan’t mind being hit. It’ll do me good for Aldershot. See?”

Trevor said he saw.

“Time,” said Moriarty.

===Chinese===
新来的这位得到了一番在他听来恰到好处的解释，说明特雷弗何以忽然对拳击发生了兴趣。随后莫里亚蒂接过表，领到的吩咐是第一回合给他们两分钟。

「拿出全力来打，」两人面对面站定时，奥哈拉对特雷弗说，「下手多重都随你。不使劲就起不到练习的作用。挨打俺不在乎，正好为奥尔德肖特练练抗击。明白吗？」

特雷弗说明白。

「开始。」莫里亚蒂喊道。

===Original===
Trevor went in with a will. He was a little shy at first of putting all his weight into his blows. It was hard to forget that he felt friendly towards O’Hara. But he speedily awoke to the fact that the Irishman took his boxing very seriously, and was quite a different person when he had the gloves on. When he was so equipped, the man opposite him ceased to be either friend or foe in a private way. He was simply an opponent, and every time he hit him was one point. And, when he entered the ring, his only object in life for the next three minutes was to score points. Consequently Trevor, sparring lightly and in rather a futile manner at first, was woken up by a stinging flush hit between the eyes. After that he, too, forgot that he liked the man before him, and rushed him in all directions. There was no doubt as to who would have won if it had been a competition. Trevor’s guard was of the most rudimentary order, and O’Hara got through when and how he liked. But though he took a good deal, he also gave a good deal, and O’Hara confessed himself not altogether sorry when Moriarty called “Time.”

===Chinese===
特雷弗鼓足了劲头冲上去。起初他有点不敢把全身的力气都压进拳头里——心里对奥哈拉的交情一时放不下。但他很快便醒悟到，这位爱尔兰人对待拳击是极其认真的，一戴上拳套，整个人就换了个样。全套武装之后，对面那个人对他来说就不再是私交意义上的朋友或对头，而纯粹是一名对手，每击中一记就是一分。而一旦进了场，接下来三分钟里他人生唯一的目标就是得分。于是，起先只是轻轻比划、打得不着边际的特雷弗，被一记结结实实打在两眼之间、火辣辣的正拳打醒了。从那一刻起，他也忘了自己喜欢对面这个人，追着他四面八方地猛攻。倘若这是一场正式比赛，谁输谁赢毫无悬念。特雷弗的防守纯属入门水平，奥哈拉想什么时候突破、怎么突破都随心所欲。不过他挨了许多拳，也还出去许多拳，等到莫里亚蒂喊「到时」的时候，奥哈拉自己也承认，他并不觉得全然轻松。

===Original===
“Man,” he said regretfully, “why ever did ye not take up boxing before? Ye’d have made a splendid middleweight.”

“Well, have I a chance, do you think?” inquired Trevor.

“Ye might do it with luck,” said O’Hara, very doubtfully. “But,” he added, “I’m afraid ye’ve not much chance.”

And with this poor encouragement from his trainer and sparring-partner, Trevor was forced to be content.

===Chinese===
「伙计，」他惋惜地说，「你以前到底为什么不练拳击呢？你本可以成为一名出色的中量级拳手。」

「那么依你看，俺还有没有胜算？」特雷弗问。

「运气好的话，说不定能成。」奥哈拉说，语气大可存疑，「不过，」他又补上一句，「恐怕你的胜算不大。」

从教练兼陪练那里领了这么一句可怜巴巴的鼓励，特雷弗也只好知足了。

## XV A Sprain and a Vacant Place / 第十五章 扭伤与空缺

===Original===
“I say,” said Clowes, helping him up, “I’m awfully sorry. Did I do it? How did it happen?”

Barry was engaged in making various attempts at standing on the injured leg. The process seemed to be painful.

“Shall I get a stretcher or anything? Can you walk?”

“If you’d help me over to the house, I could manage all right. What a beastly nuisance! It wasn’t your fault a bit. Only you tackled me when I was just trying to swerve, and my ankle was all twisted.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，」克劳斯（Clowes）扶他起来，说，「真是对不住。是我弄的吗？怎么弄的？」

巴里（Barry）正一次次试着用那条伤腿站立。这过程看上去颇为痛苦。

「要不要我去弄副担架什么的？你还能走吗？」

「你扶我回宿舍去，我还能对付。真是倒霉透顶！一点儿也不怪你。只是我正要变向，你偏在那时候扑我，脚踝整个拧了。」

===Original===
Drummond came up, carrying Barry’s blazer and sweater.

“Hullo, Barry,” he said, “what’s up? You aren’t crocked?”

“Something gone wrong with my ankle. That my blazer? Thanks. Coming over to the house? Clowes was just going to help me over.”

Clowes asked a Donaldson’s junior, who was lurking near at hand, to fetch his blazer and carry it over to the house, and then made his way with Drummond and the disabled Barry to Seymour’s. Having arrived at the senior day-room, they deposited the injured three-quarter in a chair, and sent McTodd, who came in at the moment, to fetch the doctor.

===Chinese===
德拉蒙德（Drummond）抱着巴里的运动夹克和毛衣走了过来。

「喂，巴里，」他说，「怎么了？没摔坏吧？」

「脚踝出了点毛病。那是我的夹克吗？谢谢。要回宿舍去吗？克劳斯正要扶我过去呢。」

克劳斯叫一个正好在附近晃悠的唐纳森宿舍（Donaldson’s）低年级生去把他的夹克取来送到宿舍，然后同德拉蒙德一起，扶着伤残的巴里往西摩宿舍（Seymour’s）去。到了高年级活动室，他们把这位受伤的三分卫安置在椅子上，又打发恰好进来的麦克托德（McTodd）去请医生。

===Original===
Dr. Oakes was a big man with a breezy manner, the sort of doctor who hits you with the force of a sledgehammer in the small ribs, and asks you if you felt anything *then*. It was on this principle that he acted with regard to Barry’s ankle. He seized it in both hands and gave it a wrench.

“Did that hurt?” he inquired anxiously.

Barry turned white, and replied that it did.

Dr. Oakes nodded wisely.

“Ah! H’m! Just so. ’Myes. Ah.”

===Chinese===
奥克斯医生（Dr. Oakes）是个大块头，一副大大咧咧的做派，属于那类医生：抡起大锤般的力道朝你肋下狠狠一击，然后问你*这下*有没有感觉。他诊治巴里的脚踝，奉行的正是这条原则。他双手抓住脚踝，猛地一拧。

「疼不疼？」他关切地问道。

巴里脸都白了，回答说疼。

奥克斯医生胸有成竹地点点头。

「啊！嗯！正是。嗯—是的。啊。」

===Original===
“Is it bad?” asked Drummond, awed by these mystic utterances.

“My dear boy,” replied the doctor, breezily, “it is always bad when one twists one’s ankle.”

“How long will it do me out of footer?” asked Barry.

“How long? How long? How long? Why, fortnight. Fortnight,” said the doctor.

“Then I shan’t be able to play next Saturday?”

“Next Saturday? Next Saturday? My dear boy, if you can put your foot to the ground by next Saturday, you may take it as evidence that the age of miracles is not past. Next Saturday, indeed! Ha, ha.”

===Chinese===
「伤得重吗？」德拉蒙德问，被这些神谕般的言辞镇住了。

「我亲爱的孩子，」医生轻快地答道，「脚脖子扭了，从来都是重的事。」

「这得让我多久打不了橄榄球？」巴里问。

「多久？多久？多久？嗐，两个星期。两个星期。」医生说。

「那我下星期六是上不了场了？」

「下星期六？下星期六？我亲爱的孩子，要是你下星期六就能脚沾地，那就可以证明奇迹的时代还没有过去。还下星期六呢！哈哈。」

===Original===
It was not altogether his fault that he treated the matter with such brutal levity. It was a long time since he had been at school, and he could not quite realise what it meant to Barry not to be able to play against Ripton. As for Barry, he felt that he had never loathed and detested anyone so thoroughly as he loathed and detested Dr. Oakes at that moment.

“I don’t see where the joke comes in,” said Clowes, when he had gone. “I bar that man.”

“He’s a beast,” said Drummond. “I can’t understand why they let a tout like that be the school doctor.”

Barry said nothing. He was too sore for words.

===Chinese===
他把这事说得这么没心没肺，倒也不能全怪他。他离校多年，实在体会不到打不了里普顿（Ripton）那场比赛对巴里意味着什么。至于巴里，他觉得自己这辈子从没有像此刻这样，把一个人恨得如此彻骨——这个人就是奥克斯医生。

「我看不出这有什么好笑的，」医生走后，克劳斯说，「这人我看不顺眼。」

「他就是个畜生，」德拉蒙德说，「我真不明白，怎么会叫这么个江湖郎中当校医。」

巴里一言不发。他又疼又气，说不出话来。

===Original===
What Dr. Oakes said to his wife that evening was: “Over at the school, my dear, this afternoon. This afternoon. Boy with a twisted ankle. Nice young fellow. Very much put out when I told him he could not play football for a fortnight. But I chaffed him, and cheered him up in no time. I cheered him up in no time, my dear.”

“I’m sure you did, dear,” said Mrs. Oakes. Which shows how differently the same thing may strike different people. Barry certainly did not look as if he had been cheered up when Clowes left the study and went over to tell Trevor that he would have to find a substitute for his right wing three-quarter against Ripton.

===Chinese===
那天晚上，奥克斯医生对太太是这么说的：「今天下午在学校，亲爱的。今天下午。一个脚踝扭了的男孩。不错的年轻人。我告诉他两个星期不能打橄榄球，他懊丧得很。可我跟他打了打趣，一转眼就把他逗高兴了。一转眼就把他逗高兴了，亲爱的。」

「你准是的，亲爱的。」奥克斯太太说。这说明同一件事落在不同人眼里，观感可以相差多远。克劳斯离开书房，过去告诉特雷弗（Trevor），对里普顿一战得另找一名右翼三分卫的时候，巴里可一点儿也不像被人逗高兴了的样子。

===Original===
Trevor had left the field without noticing Barry’s accident, and he was tremendously pleased at the result of the game.

“Good man,” he said, when Clowes came in, “you saved the match.”

“And lost the Ripton match probably,” said Clowes, gloomily.

“What do you mean?”

“That last time I brought down Barry I crocked him. He’s in his study now with a sprained ankle. I’ve just come from there. Oakes has seen him, and says he mustn’t play for a fortnight.”

“Great Scott!” said Trevor, blankly. “What on earth shall we do?”

“Why not move Strachan up to the wing, and put somebody else back instead of him? Strachan is a good wing.”

===Chinese===
特雷弗离场时没注意到巴里受伤，对比赛结果则是心花怒放。

「好样的，」克劳斯进来时他说，「这场比赛是你保住的。」

「多半也把里普顿那场输掉了。」克劳斯阴郁地说。

「你这话什么意思？」

「我最后一次把巴里放倒的时候，把他摔伤了。他现在正待在书房里，脚踝扭了。我刚从那儿过来。奥克斯看过了，说两个星期不许打球。」

「天哪！」特雷弗茫然道，「这可怎么办？」

「何不把斯特拉坎（Strachan）顶到翼锋，再找别人补他的最后卫？斯特拉坎是个好翼锋。」

===Original===
Trevor shook his head.

“No. There’s nobody good enough to play back for the first. We mustn’t risk it.”

“Then I suppose it must be Rand-Brown?”

“I suppose so.”

“He may do better than we think. He played quite a decent game today. That try he got wasn’t half a bad one.”

“He’d be all right if he didn’t funk. But perhaps he wouldn’t funk against Ripton. In a match like that anybody would play up. I’ll ask Milton and Allardyce about it.”

“I shouldn’t go to Milton today,” said Clowes. “I fancy he’ll want a night’s rest before he’s fit to talk to. He must be a bit sick about this match. I know he expected Seymour’s to win.”

===Chinese===
特雷弗摇了摇头。

「不行。能为一队打最后卫的人一个都没有。咱们冒不起这个险。」

「那就只好用兰德-布朗（Rand-Brown）了？」

「恐怕是。」

「他也许会比咱们想的打得好。他今天那场就打得不赖。他达阵的那一下相当不坏。」

「他要是不怯场就没事。不过对里普顿他兴许不会怯。那样的比赛，谁都会拼的。我去问问弥尔顿（Milton）和阿拉代斯（Allardyce）。」

「今天可别去找弥尔顿，」克劳斯说，「我看他得歇上一晚，才有精神跟人说话。这场比赛他准有点窝火。我知道他原本指望西摩宿舍赢的。」

===Original===
He went out, but came back almost immediately.

“I say,” he said, “there’s one thing that’s just occurred to me. This’ll please the League. I mean, this ankle business of Barry’s.”

The same idea had struck Trevor. It was certainly a respite. But he regretted it for all that. What he wanted was to beat Ripton, and Barry’s absence would weaken the team. However, it was good in its way, and cleared the atmosphere for the time. The League would hardly do anything with regard to the carrying out of their threat while Barry was on the sick-list.

Next day, having given him time to get over the bitterness of defeat in accordance with Clowes’ thoughtful suggestion, Trevor called on Milton, and asked him what his opinion was on the subject of the inclusion of Rand-Brown in the first fifteen in place of Barry.

“He’s the next best man,” he added, in defence of the proposal.

“I suppose so,” said Milton. “He’d better play, I suppose. There’s no one else.”

===Chinese===
他出了门，可几乎立刻又折了回来。

「我说，」他说，「我刚想起一件事。这下联盟（the League）该高兴了。我是说，巴里这脚踝的事。」

特雷弗也想到了这一层。这确实是个喘息之机。但他心里还是惋惜。他要的是打败里普顿，而巴里缺阵会削弱全队。不过就其本身而言也算好事，一时之间让空气清朗了些。巴里还挂在病号名单上的时候，联盟多半不会动手兑现他们的威胁。

第二天，特雷弗依照克劳斯考虑周到的建议，给弥尔顿留出了消化败绩的时间，然后登门拜访，问他怎么看让兰德-布朗顶替巴里进一队十五人名单这件事。

「他是下一个最好的人选了。」他为这项提议辩护似的补了一句。

「恐怕是，」弥尔顿说，「还是让他上吧，我看。没有别人了。」

===Original===
“Clowes thought it wouldn’t be a bad idea to shove Strachan on the wing, and put somebody else back.”

“Who is there to put?”

“Jervis?”

“Not good enough. No, it’s better to be weakish on the wing than at back. Besides, Rand-Brown may do all right. He played well against you.”

“Yes,” said Trevor. “Study looks a bit better now,” he added, as he was going, having looked round the room. “Still a bit bare, though.”

Milton sighed. “It will never be what it was.”

“Forty-three theatrical photographs want some replacing, of course,” said Trevor. “But it isn’t bad, considering.”

===Chinese===
「克劳斯觉得，把斯特拉坎推上翼锋、再找别人打最后卫，倒也不是坏主意。」

「能找谁去打？」

「杰维斯（Jervis）怎么样？」

「不够格。不行，翼锋弱一点总比最后卫弱一点强。再说，兰德-布朗也许打得不错。他跟你们那场就打得挺好。」

「是啊，」特雷弗说。他临走时环视了一圈房间，又补上一句：「书房现在看着像样点了。不过还是有点空。」

弥尔顿叹了口气。「它再也不是从前的样子了。」

「四十三张剧照当然得补上一些，」特雷弗说，「不过就现状而言，还不算糟。」

===Original===
“How’s yours?”

“Oh, mine’s all right, except for the absence of photographs.”

“I say, Trevor.”

“Yes?” said Trevor, stopping at the door. Milton’s voice had taken on the tone of one who is about to disclose dreadful secrets.

“Would you like to know what I think?”

“What?”

“Why, I’m pretty nearly sure who it was that ragged my study?”

===Chinese===
「你那间怎么样？」

「哦，我那间没事，就是照片没了。」

「我说，特雷弗。」

「什么事？」特雷弗在门口停下脚步。弥尔顿的嗓音里带上了那种即将吐露可怕秘密的调子。

「你想不想知道我是怎么想的？」

「怎么想的？」

「嘿，捣了我书房的人是谁，我差不多已经有数了。」

===Original===
“By Jove! What have you done to him?”

“Nothing as yet. I’m not quite sure of my man.”

“Who is the man?”

“Rand-Brown.”

“By Jove! Clowes once said he thought Rand-Brown must be the President of the League. But then, I don’t see how you can account for *my* study being wrecked. He was out on the field when it was done.”

“Why, the League, of course. You don’t suppose he’s the only man in it? There must be a lot of them.”

“But what makes you think it was Rand-Brown?”

===Chinese===
「好家伙！你把他怎么样了？」

「暂时还没怎么。这人我还没有十足把握。」

「那人是谁？」

「兰德-布朗。」

「好家伙！克劳斯有一回就说过，他猜想兰德-布朗准是联盟的会长。可是我不明白，*我*书房被捣的事又怎么算到他头上。事发的时候他可正在球场上。」

「嘿，当然是联盟干的。你总不会以为里面就他一个人吧？他们肯定有一大批。」

「可你凭什么认定是兰德-布朗？」

===Original===
Milton told him the story of Shoeblossom, as Barry had told it to him. The only difference was that Trevor listened without any of the scepticism which Milton had displayed on hearing it. He was getting excited. It all fitted in so neatly. If ever there was circumstantial evidence against a man, here it was against Rand-Brown. Take the two cases. Milton had quarrelled with him. Milton’s study was wrecked “with the compliments of the League.” Trevor had turned him out of the first fifteen. Trevor’s study was wrecked “with the compliments of the League.” As Clowes had pointed out, the man with the most obvious motive for not wishing Barry to play for the school was Rand-Brown. It seemed a true bill.

“I shouldn’t wonder if you’re right,” he said, “but of course one can’t do anything yet. You want a lot more evidence. Anyhow, we must play him against Ripton, I suppose. Which is his study? I’ll go and tell him now.”

“Ten.”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿把休布洛瑟姆（Shoeblossom）的事讲给他听，正像巴里当初讲给自己听的那样。唯一的不同是，特雷弗听着，全无弥尔顿当初听说时的那种怀疑。他渐渐兴奋起来。这一切扣得太严丝合缝了。要是世上真有什么旁证能落到一个人头上，如今就落在兰德-布朗头上了。看这两桩案子吧。弥尔顿跟他吵过架。弥尔顿的书房被捣了，「联盟敬赠」。特雷弗把他从一队十五人里刷了下去。特雷弗的书房被捣了，「联盟敬赠」。正如克劳斯指出的，最不愿让巴里代表学校出赛的人当中，动机最明显的就是兰德-布朗。看来罪名成立。

「你要是说对了，我一点不奇怪，」他说，「不过眼下当然还不能动手。还需要多得多的证据。不管怎样，对里普顿还得让他上场，我看。他的书房是哪间？我现在就去告诉他。」

「十号。」

===Original===
Trevor knocked at the door of study Ten. Rand-Brown was sitting over the fire, reading. He jumped up when he saw that it was Trevor who had come in, and to his visitor it seemed that his face wore a guilty look.

“What do you want?” said Rand-Brown.

It was not the politest way of welcoming a visitor. It increased Trevor’s suspicions. The man was afraid. A great idea darted into his mind. Why not go straight to the point and have it out with him here and now? He had the League’s letter about the bat in his pocket. He would confront him with it and insist on searching the study there and then. If Rand-Brown were really, as he suspected, the writer of the letter, the bat must be in this room somewhere. Search it now, and he would have no time to hide it. He pulled out the letter.

“I believe you wrote that,” he said.

Trevor was always direct.

Rand-Brown seemed to turn a little pale, but his voice when he replied was quite steady.

“That’s a lie,” he said.

===Chinese===
特雷弗敲响了十号书房的门。兰德-布朗正坐在炉边读书。看见进来的是特雷弗，他霍地跳了起来，在这位来客看来，他脸上隐隐带着做贼心虚的神色。

「你来干什么？」兰德-布朗说。

这可算不上待客的礼貌用语。特雷弗的疑心更重了。此人心虚了。一个大胆的念头闪进他脑海：何不单刀直入，就在此时此地跟他摊牌？他口袋里正装着联盟关于球棒的那封信。他要用它当面对质，并且坚持当场搜查这间书房。倘若兰德-布朗真如他所疑，就是那封信的作者，球棒必定藏在这间屋子的什么地方。现在就搜，让他来不及转移。他掏出了那封信。

「我相信这就是你写的。」他说。

特雷弗向来直来直去。

兰德-布朗似乎白了一下脸，但回答时嗓音相当平稳。

「那是扯谎。」他说。

===Original===
“Then, perhaps,” said Trevor, “you wouldn’t object to proving it.”

“How?”

“By letting me search your study?”

“You don’t believe my word?”

“Why should I? You don’t believe mine.”

Rand-Brown made no comment on this remark.

“Was that what you came here for?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「那么，也许，」特雷弗说，「你不介意证明一下。」

「怎么证明？」

「让我搜搜你的书房，如何？」

「你不信我的话？」

「我凭什么信？你也不信我的。」

对这句话，兰德-布朗未作评论。

「你就是为了这个来的？」他问。

===Original===
“No,” said Trevor; “as a matter of fact, I came to tell you to turn out for running and passing with the first tomorrow afternoon. You’re playing against Ripton on Saturday.”

Rand-Brown’s attitude underwent a complete transformation at the news. He became friendliness itself.

“All right,” he said. “I say, I’m sorry I said what I did about lying. I was rather sick that you should think I wrote that rot you showed me. I hope you don’t mind.”

“Not a bit. Do you mind my searching your study?”

For a moment Rand-Brown looked vicious. Then he sat down with a laugh.

“Go on,” he said; “I see you don’t believe me. Here are the keys if you want them.”

===Chinese===
「不是，」特雷弗说，「实话说，我是来通知你，明天下午跟一队练跑动和传接。星期六对里普顿，你上场。」

一听这话，兰德-布朗的态度来了个彻底的大转变。他整个人成了友好二字的化身。

「好啊，」他说，「我说，刚才那些扯谎不扯谎的话，我很抱歉。你居然认为你给我看的那通鬼话是我写的，我心里挺不痛快。希望你别介意。」

「一点也不。你介意我搜搜你的书房吗？」

有一瞬间，兰德-布朗露出凶相。随即他笑着坐下了。

「搜吧，」他说，「我明白你不信我。要用钥匙的话，这儿给你。」

===Original===
Trevor thanked him, and took the keys. He opened every drawer and examined the writing-desk. The bat was in none of these places. He looked in the cupboards. No bat there.

“Like to take up the carpet?” inquired Rand-Brown.

“No, thanks.”

“Search me if you like. Shall I turn out my pockets?”

“Yes, please,” said Trevor, to his surprise. He had not expected to be taken literally.

Rand-Brown emptied them, but the bat was not there. Trevor turned to go.

===Chinese===
特雷弗道了谢，接过钥匙。他打开每一个抽屉，检查了写字台。这些地方都没有球棒。他又查了橱柜。也不见球棒。

「要不要把地毯掀起来？」兰德-布朗问道。

「不必了，谢谢。」

「愿意的话连我一起搜。要我把口袋翻出来吗？」

「好，请吧。」特雷弗说，这倒出于他的意料。他没料到对方会把话当真。

兰德-布朗把口袋掏了个空，球棒不在里面。特雷弗转身要走。

===Original===
“You’ve not looked inside the legs of the chairs yet,” said Rand-Brown. “They may be hollow. There’s no knowing.”

“It doesn’t matter, thanks,” said Trevor. “Sorry for troubling you. Don’t forget tomorrow afternoon.”

And he went, with the very unpleasant feeling that he had been badly scored off.

===Chinese===
「你还没看椅子腿里面呢，」兰德-布朗说，「说不定是空心的。谁知道呢。」

「不碍事了，谢谢。」特雷弗说，「打扰你了。别忘明天下午。」

他走了，心里怀着一种极不痛快的感觉：这回可是被对方占尽了上风。

## X Being a Chapter of Accidents / 第十章 事故迭起的一章

===Original===
On the evening following O’Hara’s adventure in the vaults, Barry and McTodd were in their study, getting out the tea-things. Most Wrykinians brewed in the winter and Easter terms, when the days were short and lockup early. In the summer term there were other things to do﻿—nets, which lasted till a quarter to seven (when lockup was), and the baths﻿—and brewing practically ceased. But just now it was at its height, and every evening, at a quarter past five, there might be heard in the houses the sizzling of the succulent sausage and other rare delicacies. As a rule, one or two studies would club together to brew, instead of preparing solitary banquets. This was found both more convivial and more economical. At Seymour’s, studies numbers five, six, and seven had always combined from time immemorial, and Barry, on obtaining study six, had carried on the tradition. In study five were Drummond and his friend De Bertini. In study seven, which was a smaller room and only capable of holding one person with any comfort, one James Rupert Leather-Twigg (that was his singular name, as Mr. Gilbert has it) had taken up his abode. The name of Leather-Twigg having proved, at an early date in his career, too great a mouthful for Wrykyn, he was known to his friends and acquaintances by the euphonious title of Shoeblossom. The charm about the genial Shoeblossom was that you could never tell what he was going to do next. All that you could rely on with any certainty was that it would be something which would have been better left undone.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉在地窖里历险的第二天晚上，巴里（Barry）和麦克托德（McTodd）正在书房里往外拿茶具。冬天学期和复活节学期白昼短、锁门早，多数里金人（Wrykinians）都要烧茶。到了夏天学期，另有别的事可忙——网练要练到六点三刻（正是锁门时间），还有浴室——烧茶便几乎绝迹。可眼下正是烧茶的最旺季，每天傍晚五点一刻，各宿舍里都能听见滋滋作响的香肠和别的好些珍馐美馔。按惯例，烧茶总是三两个书房拼伙，不兴各自单开筵席。这样既热闹，又省钱，两全其美。在西摩宿舍（Seymour’s），五号、六号、七号书房自古以来就联合烧茶，巴里一住进六号书房，便把传统延续了下来。五号书房里住着德拉蒙德（Drummond）和他的朋友德·贝尔蒂尼（De Bertini）。七号书房是间小屋，舒舒服服只容得下一个人，被一位詹姆斯·鲁珀特·莱瑟-特威格（James Rupert Leather-Twigg）占了（用吉尔伯特先生（Mr. Gilbert）的话说，这便是他那独一无二的大名）。莱瑟-特威格这个姓氏，在他学生生涯早期就被证明对里金来说过于拗口，于是亲朋故旧都管他叫那个响亮动听的称号——休布洛瑟姆（Shoeblossom）。这位好性儿的休布洛瑟姆身上的妙处在于，你永远说不准他下一步要干什么。你唯一能打包票的是，那准是件不做为妙的事。

===Original===
It was just five o’clock when Barry and McTodd started to get things ready. They were not high enough up in the school to have fags, so that they had to do this for themselves.

Barry was still in football clothes. He had been out running and passing with the first fifteen. McTodd, whose idea of exercise was winding up a watch, had been spending his time since school ceased in the study with a book. He was in his ordinary clothes. It was therefore fortunate that, when he upset the kettle (he nearly always did at some period of the evening’s business), the contents spread themselves over Barry, and not over himself. Football clothes will stand any amount of water, whereas McTodd’s “Youth’s winter suiting at forty-two shillings and sixpence” might have been injured. Barry, however, did not look upon the episode in this philosophical light. He spoke to him eloquently for a while, and then sent him downstairs to fetch more water. While he was away, Drummond and De Bertini came in.

===Chinese===
五点整，巴里和麦克托德着手张罗起来。两人在学校里年级不够高，还使唤不上跑腿的低年级生，只好事事亲力亲为。

巴里还穿着橄榄球衣，他刚跟一队十五人练完跑动传接回来。麦克托德呢，他对于运动的全部见解就是给表上弦，放学后就一直待在书房里看书，身上还是便装。因此，幸而当他打翻水壶的时候（每晚这档子事里他几乎总要打翻一回），壶里的东西都泼在了巴里身上，而不是他自己身上。橄榄球衣泼多少水都扛得住，麦克托德那套「四十二先令六便士的青年冬季呢料西装」可就兴许要遭殃了。不过巴里可不这么达观地看待这次事故。他对着他慷慨陈词了一阵，然后打发他下楼再去打水。他不在的当口，德拉蒙德和德·贝尔蒂尼进来了。

===Original===
“Hullo,” said Drummond, “tea ready?”

“Not much,” replied Barry, bitterly, “not likely to be, either, at this rate. We’d just got the kettle going when that ass McTodd plunged against the table and upset the lot over my bags. Lucky the beastly stuff wasn’t boiling. I’m soaked.”

“While we wait﻿—the sausages﻿—Yes?﻿—a good idea﻿—McTodd, he is downstairs﻿—but to wait? No, no. Let us. Shall we? Is it not so? Yes?” observed Bertie, lucidly.

“Now construe,” said Barry, looking at the linguist with a bewildered expression. It was a source of no little inconvenience to his friends that De Bertini was so very fixed in his determination to speak English. He was a trier all the way, was De Bertini. You rarely caught him helping out his remarks with the language of his native land. It was English or nothing with him. To most of his circle it might as well have been Zulu.

===Chinese===
「喂，」德拉蒙德说，「茶好了吗？」

「好什么好，」巴里苦涩地答道，「照这个速度，也别指望能好了。水壶刚烧上，麦克托德那头蠢驴就一头撞上桌子，把一壶全泼在我裤子上。幸好那该死的东西还没开。我浑身都湿透了。」

「趁我们等——香肠——对吧？——好主意——麦克托德，他在楼下——可是要等？不，不。让我们来。好不好？是不是这样？对吧？」伯蒂（Bertie）发表了一通明白晓畅的见解。

「来来，翻译翻译。」巴里说，一脸茫然地望着这位语言大师。德·贝尔蒂尼铁了心非说英语不可，这给他的朋友们添了数不清的麻烦。他可是一路死磕到底的人，这位德·贝尔蒂尼。你难得逮住他用祖国的语言给自己的话搭个台阶。在他这儿，不是英语就干脆不言语。对交游圈子里的多数人来说，那英语跟祖鲁语也差不了多少。

===Original===
Drummond, either through natural genius or because he spent more time with him, was generally able to act as interpreter. Occasionally there would come a linguistic effort by which even he freely confessed himself baffled, and then they would pass on unsatisfied. But, as a rule, he was equal to the emergency. He was so now.

“What Bertie means,” he explained, “is that it’s no good us waiting for McTodd to come back. He never could fill a kettle in less than ten minutes, and even then he’s certain to spill it coming upstairs and have to go back again. Let’s get on with the sausages.”

The pan had just been placed on the fire when McTodd returned with the water. He tripped over the mat as he entered, and spilt about half a pint into one of his football boots, which stood inside the door, but the accident was comparatively trivial, and excited no remark.

===Chinese===
德拉蒙德或是天赋过人，或是跟他相处的时间多，一般总能充当翻译。偶尔也会冒出一段连他自己都爽快承认无能为力的语言杰作，那时大家便只好带着遗憾揭过不提。但通常他都应付得来。眼下也应付来了。

「伯蒂的意思，」他解释道，「是说咱们干等麦克托德回来没有用。他灌一把水壶从来得十分钟起步，就算灌好了，上楼时准还得洒掉半路，再跑一趟。咱们还是先煎香肠吧。」

平底锅刚架上火，麦克托德就端着水回来了。进门时他让门垫绊了一下，约有半品脱水泼进了立在门边的一只橄榄球靴里，不过这事故相形之下无足轻重，没引起任何议论。

===Original===
“I wonder where that slacker Shoeblossom has got to,” said Barry. “He never turns up in time to do any work. He seems to regard himself as a beastly guest. I wish we could finish the sausages before he comes. It would be a sell for him.”

“Not much chance of that,” said Drummond, who was kneeling before the fire and keeping an excited eye on the spluttering pan, “*you* see. He’ll come just as we’ve finished cooking them. I believe the man waits outside with his ear to the keyhole. Hullo! Stand by with the plate. They’ll be done in half a jiffy.”

Just as the last sausage was deposited in safety on the plate, the door opened, and Shoeblossom, looking as if he had not brushed his hair since early childhood, sidled in with an attempt at an easy nonchalance which was rendered quite impossible by the hopeless state of his conscience.

===Chinese===
「真不知道休布洛瑟姆那个懒骨头晃到哪儿去了，」巴里说，「干活他从来赶不上趟。他倒把自己当成个什么贵客。真盼着咱们能在他来之前把香肠吃完，好让他扑个空。」

「门儿都没有，」德拉蒙德说。他跪在炉前，兴奋地盯着噼啪作响的平底锅，「你瞧着吧。他专等咱们刚煎好那工夫来。我看这家伙就在外头贴着钥匙孔听动静呢。喂！拿好盘子。转眼就得。」

最后一条香肠刚安然落进盘子，门开了，休布洛瑟姆侧身溜了进来。他那副样子，仿佛从记事起就没梳过头，又竭力装出一副闲适洒脱的做派——可惜他良心的账烂得一塌糊涂，这做派怎么也装不像。

===Original===
“Ah,” he said, “brewing, I see. Can I be of any use?”

“We’ve finished years ago,” said Barry.

“Ages ago,” said McTodd.

A look of intense alarm appeared on Shoeblossom’s classical features.

“You’ve not finished, really?”

“We’ve finished cooking everything,” said Drummond. “We haven’t begun tea yet. Now, are you happy?”

===Chinese===
「啊，」他说，「烧茶呢，我看见了。有什么要我效劳的吗？」

「八百年前就完事了。」巴里说。

「老早老早就完了。」麦克托德说。

休布洛瑟姆那副古典式的面容上顿时浮现出深重的惊惶。

「你们不是真完了吧？」

「能做的全做完了，」德拉蒙德说，「茶倒还没开喝。这下你高兴了吧？」

===Original===
Shoeblossom was. So happy that he felt he must do something to celebrate the occasion. He felt like a successful general. There must be *something* he could do to show that he regarded the situation with approval. He looked round the study. Ha! Happy thought﻿—the frying-pan. That useful culinary instrument was lying in the fender, still bearing its cargo of fat, and beside it﻿—a sight to stir the blood and make the heart beat faster﻿—were the sausages, piled up on their plate.

Shoeblossom stooped. He seized the frying-pan. He gave it one twirl in the air. Then, before anyone could stop him, he had turned it upside down over the fire. As has been already remarked, you could never predict exactly what James Rupert Leather-Twigg would be up to next.

When anything goes out of the frying-pan into the fire, it is usually productive of interesting byproducts. The maxim applies to fat. The fat was in the fire with a vengeance. A great sheet of flame rushed out and up. Shoeblossom leaped back with a readiness highly creditable in one who was not a professional acrobat. The covering of the mantelpiece caught fire. The flames went roaring up the chimney.

===Chinese===
休布洛瑟姆确实高兴。高兴得觉得必须干点什么来庆祝这大好时刻。他觉得自己像位凯旋的将军。总得*有点什么*是他能做的，好表明他对眼前局面的赞许。他环视书房。哈！妙计来了——平底锅。那件有用的炊具正躺在炉挡里，锅里还载着一汪油，旁边——这景象足以令人热血沸腾、心跳加速——是那一盘堆叠整齐的香肠。

休布洛瑟姆弯下腰。他抄起平底锅。他在空中把它抡了一圈。随后，不等任何人来得及阻拦，他已把锅口朝下扣向了火。前面已经说过，詹姆斯·鲁珀特·莱瑟-特威格下一步要干什么，谁也料不准。

但凡有什么东西跳出煎锅又落进火里，通常都会产生些有趣的副产品。这条格言对油同样适用。油落进火里，落得轰轰烈烈。一大片火苗腾地窜起。休布洛瑟姆闪身跃开，那机敏劲儿，对一位并非职业杂技演员的人来说实在难能可贵。壁炉台的罩布烧着了。火焰呼啸着冲上烟囱。

===Original===
Drummond, cool while everything else was so hot, without a word moved to the mantelpiece to beat out the fire with a football shirt. Bertie was talking rapidly to himself in French. Nobody could understand what he was saying, which was possibly fortunate.

By the time Drummond had extinguished the mantelpiece, Barry had also done good work by knocking the fire into the grate with the poker. McTodd, who had been standing up till now in the far corner of the room, gaping vaguely at things in general, now came into action. Probably it was force of habit that suggested to him that the time had come to upset the kettle. At any rate, upset it he did﻿—most of it over the glowing, blazing mass in the grate, the rest over Barry. One of the largest and most detestable smells the study had ever had to endure instantly assailed their nostrils. The fire in the study was out now, but in the chimney it still blazed merrily.

“Go up on to the roof and heave water down,” said Drummond, the strategist. “You can get out from Milton’s dormitory window. And take care not to chuck it down the wrong chimney.”

===Chinese===
万物皆热而他独冷，德拉蒙德一言不发，走到壁炉台边，用一件橄榄球衫把火扑打熄灭。伯蒂正用法语飞快地自言自语。没人听得懂他在说什么，这兴许是件幸事。

德拉蒙德把壁炉台的火扑灭时，巴里也战果颇丰，用火钩把火捣进了炉膛。麦克托德此前一直站在屋子最远的角落里，茫然地对着万物张嘴呆看，这时也行动起来了。多半是习惯的力量提醒他，打翻水壶的时候到了。反正他是打翻了——大半泼进炉膛里那团烧得正旺的火堆，剩下的泼在巴里身上。书房有史以来不得不忍受的最大、最可憎的一股气味，登时直冲众人的鼻孔。书房里的火这下灭了，烟囱里的火却还在欢快地烧着。

「上屋顶，往烟囱里灌水，」战略家德拉蒙德说，「可以从弥尔顿（Milton）宿舍的寝室窗户爬出去。留神别浇错了烟囱。」

===Original===
Barry was starting for the door to carry out these excellent instructions, when it flew open.

“Pah! What have you boys been doing? What an abominable smell. Pah!” said a muffled voice. It was Mr. Seymour. Most of his face was concealed in a large handkerchief, but by the look of his eyes, which appeared above, he did not seem pleased. He took in the situation at a glance. Fires in the house were not rarities. One facetious sportsman had once made a rule of setting the senior day-room chimney on fire every term. He had since left (by request), but fires still occurred.

“Is the chimney on fire?”

“Yes, sir,” said Drummond.

“Go and find Herbert, and tell him to take some water on to the roof and throw it down.” Herbert was the boot and knife cleaner at Seymour’s.

===Chinese===
巴里正要出门去执行这道英明的指令，门却猛地开了。

「呸！你们这帮小子干什么来着？好一股可憎的臭味。呸！」一个捂得严严实实的声音说道。是西摩先生（Mr. Seymour）。他的大半张脸都埋在一块大手帕里，但从手帕上方露出的那双眼睛看，他似乎不太高兴。他一眼便看清了局势。宿舍里失火并非稀罕事。曾有一位爱说笑的运动健将立下规矩，每学期必把高年级活动室的烟囱点着一回。此人后来离校了（应校方之请），火灾却依然时有发生。

「烟囱着火了吗？」

「是的，先生。」德拉蒙德说。

「去找赫伯特（Herbert），叫他提些水上屋顶浇下去。」赫伯特是西摩宿舍擦靴擦刀的校工。

===Original===
Barry went. Soon afterwards a splash of water in the grate announced that the intrepid Herbert was hard at it. Another followed, and another. Then there was a pause. Mr. Seymour thought he would look up to see if the fire was out. He stooped and peered into the darkness, and, even as he gazed, splash came the contents of the fourth pail, together with some soot with which they had formed a travelling acquaintance on the way down. Mr. Seymour staggered back, grimy and dripping. There was dead silence in the study. Shoeblossom’s face might have been seen working convulsively.

The silence was broken by a hollow, sepulchral voice with a strong Cockney accent.

“Did yer see any water come down then, sir?” said the voice.

Shoeblossom collapsed into a chair, and began to sob feebly.

===Chinese===
巴里去了。不一会儿，炉膛里哗啦一响，宣告无畏的赫伯特正干得热火朝天。接着又是一下，又一下。随后停顿了。西摩先生想抬头看看火灭了没有。他俯身朝黑暗里张望，就在他凝望的当口，第四桶水哗啦浇了下来，还带着一路搭伴而下的旅伴——煤烟。西摩先生踉跄后退，浑身乌黑，滴滴答答淌着水。书房里死一般沉寂。休布洛瑟姆的脸抽搐个不停，有目共睹。

打破沉寂的是一个空洞洞、坟腔儿一般的声音，带着浓重的伦敦土腔。

「您瞧见有没有水下来吗，先生？」那声音说。

休布洛瑟姆瘫进椅子里，开始有气无力地抽噎。

---

===Original===
“﻿—disgraceful﻿ ﻿… scandalous﻿ ﻿… get *up*, Leather-Twigg﻿ ﻿… not to be trusted﻿ ﻿… *babies﻿*… three hundred lines, Leather-Twigg﻿ ﻿… abominable﻿ ﻿… surprised﻿ ﻿… ought to be ashamed of yourselves﻿ ﻿… *double*, Leather-Twigg﻿ ﻿… not fit to have studies﻿ ﻿… atrocious﻿ ﻿… —”

Such were the main heads of Mr. Seymour’s speech on the situation as he dabbed desperately at the soot on his face with his handkerchief. Shoeblossom stood and gurgled throughout. Not even the thought of six hundred lines could quench that dauntless spirit.

===Chinese===
「——不像话……岂有此理……给我站*直*，莱瑟-特威格……靠不住……*娃娃*……罚抄三百行，莱瑟-特威格……恶劣透顶……令人震惊……你们该害臊……*翻倍*，莱瑟-特威格……不配拥有书房……无法无天……——」

西摩先生一边用手帕拼命按脸上的煤灰，一边对局势发表演说，以上便是几大要点。休布洛瑟姆自始至终站着咕噜作响。哪怕是六百行罚抄的念头，也浇不灭他那颗无畏的心。

===Original===
“Finally,” perorated Mr. Seymour, as he was leaving the room, “as you are evidently not to be trusted with rooms of your own, I forbid you to enter them till further notice. It is disgraceful that such a thing should happen. Do you hear, Barry? And you, Drummond? You are not to enter your studies again till I give you leave. Move your books down to the senior day-room tonight.”

And Mr. Seymour stalked off to clean himself.

“Anyhow,” said Shoeblossom, as his footsteps died away, “we saved the sausages.”

It is this indomitable gift of looking on the bright side that makes us Englishmen what we are.

===Chinese===
「最后，」西摩先生临出屋时作结道，「既然你们显然不配让人放心拥有自己的房间，我禁止你们再进那几间屋，另行通知为止。发生这种事，实在丢脸。听见没有，巴里？还有你，德拉蒙德？我不准你们再进书房，直到我点头。今晚就把你们的书搬到高年级活动室去。」

说完，西摩先生昂首阔步地洗脸去也。

「不管怎么说，」脚步声远去后，休布洛瑟姆说，「香肠是保住了。」

正是这种专往好处想的不屈天赋，造就了我们英国人的今天。

## XXIV Conclusion / 第二十四章 结局

===Original===
Into the story at this point comes the narrative of Charles Mereweather Cook, aged fourteen, a day-boy.

Cook arrived at the school on the tenth of March, at precisely nine o’clock, in a state of excitement.

He said there was a row on in the town.

Cross-examined, he said there was no end of a row on in the town.

During morning school he explained further, whispering his tale into the attentive ear of Knight of the School House, who sat next to him.

What sort of a row, Knight wanted to know.

===Chinese===
此时进入故事的，是一份来自查尔斯·梅里威瑟·库克（Charles Mereweather Cook）的叙述，此人十四岁，走读生。

三月十日早晨九点整，库克来到学校，一副亢奋不已的状态。

他说镇上出乱子了。

经盘问，他说镇上出的乱子大了去了。

上午上课时他作了进一步说明，把他的见闻悄悄灌进邻座学校宿舍（School House）的奈特（Knight）那只专注的耳朵里。

奈特想知道是哪门子乱子。

===Original===
Cook deposed that he had been riding on his bicycle past the entrance to the Recreation Grounds on his way to school, when his eye was attracted by the movements of a mass of men just inside the gate. They appeared to be fighting. Witness did not stop to watch, much as he would have liked to do so. Why not? Why, because he was late already, and would have had to scorch anyhow, in order to get to school in time. And he had been late the day before, and was afraid that old Appleby (the master of the form) would give him beans if he were late again. Wherefore he had no notion of what the men were fighting about, but he betted that more would be heard about it. Why? Because, from what he saw of it, it seemed a jolly big thing. There must have been quite three hundred men fighting. (Knight, satirically, “*Pile* it on!”) Well, quite a hundred, anyhow. Fifty a side. And fighting like anything. He betted there would be something about it in the *Wrykyn Patriot* tomorrow. He shouldn’t wonder if somebody had been killed. What were they scrapping about? How should *he* know!

Here Mr. Appleby, who had been trying for the last five minutes to find out where the whispering noise came from, at length traced it to its source, and forthwith requested Messrs. Cook and Knight to do him two hundred lines, adding that, if he heard them talking again, he would put them into the extra lesson. Silence reigned from that moment.

===Chinese===
库克作证如下：他骑车上学，路过休闲广场（Recreation Grounds）大门口时，门里一大群人的动静吸引了他的目光。那些人像是在打架。证人没有停车观望，尽管他非常乐意看上一看。为什么不停？嗐，因为他已经迟到了，要想按时到校，横竖都得玩命蹬车。再说他前一天就迟到过，生怕老阿普尔比（Appleby）（该班的任课教师）要是见他再迟到，会叫他吃不了兜着走。因此那伙人为什么打架，他毫无所知，但他敢打赌，此事必有下文。为什么？因为依他看的那一眼判断，这事儿气派得很。打架的少说也有三百人。（奈特挖苦道：「接着吹！」）好吧，好歹足足有一百人。一边五十个。而且打得天昏地暗。他敢打赌，明天的《里金爱国者报》（*Wrykyn Patriot*）上准有动静。闹不好还死了人呢。他们为什么打？他哪儿知道！

这时，阿普尔比先生（Mr. Appleby）已经花了五分钟工夫想弄清窃窃私语声出自何处，终于追到了源头，当即要求库克与奈特二位先生替他抄两百行，并补充说，倘若再听见他们讲话，就把他们塞进留校补习。从那一刻起，天下太平。

===Original===
Next day, while the form was wrestling with the moderately exciting account of Caesar’s doings in Gaul, Master Cook produced from his pocket a newspaper cutting. This, having previously planted a forcible blow in his friend’s ribs with an elbow to attract the latter’s attention, he handed to Knight, and in dumb show requested him to peruse the same. Which Knight, feeling no interest whatever in Caesar’s doings in Gaul, and having, in consequence, a good deal of time on his hands, proceeded to do. The cutting was headed “Disgraceful Fracas,” and was written in the elegant style that was always so marked a feature of the *Wrykyn Patriot*.

“We are sorry to have to report,” it ran, “another of those deplorable ebullitions of local Hooliganism, to which it has before now been our painful duty to refer. Yesterday the Recreation Grounds were made the scene of as brutal an exhibition of savagery as has ever marred the fair fame of this town. Our readers will remember how on a previous occasion, when the fine statue of Sir Eustace Briggs was found covered with tar, we attributed the act to the malevolence of the Radical section of the community. Events have proved that we were right. Yesterday a body of youths, belonging to the rival party, was discovered in the very act of repeating the offence. A thick coating of tar had already been administered, when several members of the rival faction appeared. A free fight of a peculiarly violent nature immediately ensued, with the result that, before the police could interfere, several of the combatants had received severe bruises. Fortunately the police then arrived on the scene, and with great difficulty succeeded in putting a stop to the fracas. Several arrests were made.

“We have no desire to discourage legitimate party rivalry, but we feel justified in strongly protesting against such dastardly tricks as those to which we have referred. We can assure our opponents that they can gain nothing by such conduct.”

There was a good deal more to the effect that now was the time for all good men to come to the aid of the party, and that the constituents of Sir Eustace Briggs must look to it that they failed not in the hour of need, and so on. That was what the *Wrykyn Patriot* had to say on the subject.

===Chinese===
第二天，全班正跟凯撒在高卢的事迹这篇还算有点意思的课文搏斗时，库克少爷从口袋里掏出一角剪报。他先用胳膊肘在朋友肋上狠命一顶，唤起对方的注意，随即将剪报递给奈特，并以哑剧手势请他一览。凯撒在高卢的事迹本就叫奈特兴味索然，手上因此闲出大把工夫，便照办了。剪报的标题是「可耻斗殴」，用的是《里金爱国者报》一贯以来那标志性的典雅文笔。

报纸写道：「我们沉痛地报道，本地流氓行径又一次恶性发作，此类事件此前已多次令我们痛心提及。昨日，休闲广场沦为一场残暴兽性的展演之地，其恶劣程度，为本镇清誉所仅见。读者诸君当还记得，先前那尊精美的尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士（Sir Eustace Briggs）雕像被发现涂满柏油之时，我们即断定此乃本地激进派一党的恶意所为。事态的发展证明我们无误。昨日，一伙属于对立党派的青年被人当场发现，正欲重演故技。厚厚一层柏油刚刚涂就，对立派数名成员即行现身。一场火药味格外浓烈的大混战当即爆发，结果在警察赶到干涉之前，多名斗殴者已受重创。所幸警察随即抵达现场，费尽九牛二虎之力方才平息这场斗殴。已有数人被捕。

我们无意打击正当的党派竞逐，但对此类前述之卑劣伎俩，我们深感有理由严正抗议。我们可以向对手保证，此等行径于彼毫无益处。」

后面还有长篇大论，大意是说值此之际凡我良善之士皆当效力本党，尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士的选民诸君务须留意，危急时刻切莫掉队，云云。这便是《里金爱国者报》就此话题的高论。

===Original===
O’Hara managed to get hold of a copy of the paper, and showed it to Clowes and Trevor.

“So now,” he said, “it’s all right, ye see. They’ll never suspect it wasn’t the same people that tarred the statue both times. An’ ye’ve got the bat back, so it’s all right, ye see.”

“The only thing that’ll trouble you now,” said Clowes, “will be your conscience.”

O’Hara intimated that he would try and put up with that.

“But isn’t it a stroke of luck,” he said, “that they should have gone and tarred Sir Eustace again so soon after Moriarty and I did it?”

Clowes said gravely that it only showed the force of good example.

“Yes. They wouldn’t have thought of it, if it hadn’t been for us,” chortled O’Hara. “I wonder, now, if there’s anything else we could do to that statue!” he added, meditatively.

“My good lunatic,” said Clowes, “don’t you think you’ve done almost enough for one term?”

“Well, ’myes,” replied O’Hara thoughtfully, “perhaps we have, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
奥哈拉（O’Hara）设法弄到了一份报纸，拿给克劳斯（Clowes）和特雷弗（Trevor）看。

「这下好了，」他说，「没事了，你瞧。谁也不会怀疑两回给雕像涂柏油的不是同一伙人。球棒也回到你们手里了，所以没事了，你瞧。」

「如今唯一能折磨你的，」克劳斯说，「只剩你的良心了。」

奥哈拉表示，他可以试着忍一忍。

「不过这运气不是好得很吗，」他说，「俺和莫里亚蒂（Moriarty）刚给尤斯塔斯爵士涂完，他们这么快就又去涂了一回？」

克劳斯正色道，这无非显示了榜样的力量。

「可不是。要不是有咱们带头，他们压根想不到这一出，」奥哈拉咯咯直乐，「俺这就想啊，那尊雕像还有什么是咱们不能干的呢！」他若有所思地补了一句。

「我亲爱的疯子，」克劳斯说，「你就不觉得这一个学期你干得已经够多了吗？」

「呃—是啊，」奥哈拉若有所思地答道，「兴许是够多了，俺想。」

---

===Original===
The term wore on. Donaldson’s won the final house-match by a matter of twenty-six points. It was, as they had expected, one of the easiest games they had had to play in the competition. Bryant’s, who were their opponents, were not strong, and had only managed to get into the final owing to their luck in drawing weak opponents for the trial heats. The real final, that had decided the ownership of the cup, had been Donaldson’s v. Seymour’s.

Aldershot arrived, and the sports. Drummond and O’Hara covered themselves with glory, and brought home silver medals. But Moriarty, to the disappointment of the school, which had counted on his pulling off the middles, met a strenuous gentleman from St. Paul’s in the final, and was prematurely outed in the first minute of the third round. To him, therefore, there fell but a medal of bronze.

===Chinese===
学期一天天过去。宿舍决赛，唐纳森宿舍（Donaldson’s）以二十六分之差夺魁。正如他们所预料的，这是整个锦标赛里他们打过的最轻松的比赛之一。对手布赖恩特宿舍（Bryant’s）实力不济，之所以能挤进决赛，全靠预赛抽签时运气好，净抽到弱旅。真正决定奖杯归属的决赛，是唐纳森宿舍对西摩宿舍（Seymour’s）那一战。

奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）之行连同运动会接踵而至。德拉蒙德（Drummond）和奥哈拉双双载誉而归，捧回了银质奖章。而莫里亚蒂，全校原指望他拿下中量级，却令大家失望了：决赛中他撞上一位来自圣保罗中学（St. Paul’s）的剽悍君子，第三回合头一分钟便过早出局。于是落到他手里的，只有一枚铜章。

===Original===
It was on the Sunday after the sports that Trevor’s connection with the bat ceased﻿—as far, that is to say, as concerned its unpleasant character (as a piece of evidence that might be used to his disadvantage). He had gone to supper with the headmaster, accompanied by Clowes and Milton. The headmaster nearly always invited a few of the house prefects to Sunday supper during the term. Sir Eustace Briggs happened to be there. He had withdrawn his insinuations concerning the part supposedly played by a member of the school in the matter of the tarred statue, and the headmaster had sealed the entente cordiale by asking him to supper.

An ordinary man might have considered it best to keep off the delicate subject. Not so Sir Eustace Briggs. He was on to it like glue. He talked of little else throughout the whole course of the meal.

===Chinese===
运动会后的那个星期日，特雷弗与球棒的瓜葛才算终结——也就是说，就其不愉快的性质而言（作为一件可能被用来不利于他的证物）。那天他跟克劳斯和弥尔顿（Milton）一起去校长家吃晚饭。学期里，校长几乎总要请几位宿舍级长去吃星期日晚餐。尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士恰好在座。他已撤回了先前那些含沙射影的话——影射涂柏油事件中有学校学生参与——校长便以请他共进晚餐的方式，缔结了诚挚友好之谊。

寻常人也许会觉得，这等微妙话题还是不碰为妙。尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士却不。他粘上这话题就跟抹了胶似的。整顿饭从头到尾，他谈的几乎别无他事。

===Original===
“My suspicions,” he boomed, towards the conclusion of the feast, “which have, I am rejoiced to say, proved so entirely void of foundation and significance, were aroused in the first instance, as I mentioned before, by the narrative of the man Samuel Wapshott.”

Nobody present showed the slightest desire to learn what the man Samuel Wapshott had had to say for himself, but Sir Eustace, undismayed, continued as if the whole table were hanging on his words.

“The man Samuel Wapshott,” he said, “distinctly asserted that a small gold ornament, shaped like a bat, was handed by him to a lad of age coeval with these lads here.”

===Chinese===
「本人的疑心，」宴会接近尾声时，他嗡声嗡气地说道，「诚可欣幸，业已证实全然无凭无据、无关宏旨。如前所述，此疑心最初系由塞缪尔·沃普肖特（Samuel Wapshott）其人的叙述所引发。」

在座没有一个人对流露出丝毫想知道塞缪尔·沃普肖特其人说过什么的意思，但尤斯塔斯爵士毫不气馁，继续讲下去，仿佛满桌人都屏息等着他的下文。

「塞缪尔·沃普肖特其人，」他说，「言之凿凿地声称，一件形似球棒的小小金饰，曾由他亲手交付给一个年纪与在座诸位少年相仿的少年。」

===Original===
The headmaster interposed. He had evidently heard more than enough of the man Samuel Wapshott.

“He must have been mistaken,” he said briefly. “The bat which Trevor is wearing on his watch-chain at this moment is the only one of its kind that I know of. You have never lost it, Trevor?”

Trevor thought for a moment. *He* had never lost it. He replied diplomatically, “It has been in a drawer nearly all the term, sir,” he said.

“A drawer, hey?” remarked Sir Eustace Briggs. “Ah! A very sensible place to keep it in, my boy. You could have no better place, in my opinion.”

And Trevor agreed with him, with the mental reservation that it rather depended on whom the drawer belonged to.

===Chinese===
校长插话了。关于塞缪尔·沃普肖特其人，他显然已经听得够多，多得不能再多了。

「他一定是弄错了。」他简短地说，「特雷弗此刻表链上挂着的那根球棒，据我所知是世间仅有的一根。你从来没弄丢过它吧，特雷弗？」

特雷弗想了一想。*他*确实从来没弄丢过。他外交辞令地答道：「这个学期它差不多一直待在抽屉里，先生。」

「抽屉里，嘿？」尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士评点道，「啊！那可是个存放它的聪明地方，我的孩子。依我看，再没有更好的去处了。」

特雷弗对此表示赞同，心里却留了一手：那得看抽屉是谁的。

## IX Mainly About Ferrets / 第九章 雪貂专章

===Original===
“Ow!” exclaimed the captive, with no uncertain voice. “Let go, you ass, you’re hurting.”

The voice was a treble voice. This surprised O’Hara. It looked very much as if he had put up the wrong bird. From the dimensions of the arm which he was holding, his prisoner seemed to be of tender years.

“Let go, Harvey, you idiot. I shall kick.”

Before the threat could be put into execution, O’Hara, who had been fumbling all this while in his pocket for a match, found one loose, and struck a light. The features of the owner of the arm﻿—he was still holding it﻿—were lit up for a moment.

===Chinese===
「嗷！」被擒者嗓音绝不含糊地叫道，「撒手，你这蠢驴，弄疼我了。」

那是一副童声高音。这让奥哈拉（O’Hara）吃了一惊。看样子他八成是捉错了鸟儿。从手里攥着的那条胳膊的尺寸判断，他的俘虏年纪尚幼。

「撒手啊，哈维（Harvey），你这个呆子。我要踢人了。」

威胁还没来得及付诸实行，奥哈拉——他这一路一直在口袋里摸索火柴——摸到了一根散装的，划亮了火。那条胳膊的主人的五官——他还攥着那条胳膊呢——被照亮了一瞬。

===Original===
“Why, it’s young Renford!” he exclaimed. “What are you doing down here?”

Renford, however, continued to pursue the topic of his arm, and the effect that the vice-like grip of the Irishman had had upon it.

“You’ve nearly broken it,” he said, complainingly.

“I’m sorry. I mistook you for somebody else. Who’s that with you?”

“It’s me,” said an ungrammatical voice.

“Who’s me?”

“Harvey.”

At this point a soft yellow light lit up the more immediate neighbourhood. Harvey had brought a bicycle lamp into action.

===Chinese===
「咦，这不是小伦福德（Renford）吗！」他叫道，「你们到这儿底下干什么来了？」

伦福德却继续揪住他胳膊这个话题不放，细究那位爱尔兰人老虎钳般的手劲在上面造成的效果。

「你差点把它弄断了。」他控诉道。

「对不住。俺把你错当成别人了。跟你一道的是谁？」

「是我。」一个不合文法的嗓音说道。

「『我』是谁？」

「哈维。」

这时，一团柔和的黄光照亮了眼前一带。哈维把一盏自行车灯点亮了。

===Original===
“That’s more like it,” said Renford. “Look here, O’Hara, you won’t split, will you?”

“I’m not an informer by profession, thanks,” said O’Hara.

“Oh, I know it’s all right, really, but you can’t be too careful, because one isn’t allowed down here, and there’d be a beastly row if it got out about our being down here.”

“And *they* would be cobbed,” put in Harvey.

“Who are they?” asked O’Hara.

“Ferrets. Like to have a look at them?”

“*Ferrets!*”

===Chinese===
「这还差不多，」伦福德说，「喂，奥哈拉，你不会去告密吧？」

「俺又不以告密为业，多谢关心。」奥哈拉说。

「哦，我知道其实没事，可小心无大错嘛，因为这儿底下是不许人来的，我们下来的事要是传出去，非闹翻天不可。」

「而且*它们*还得挨收拾。」哈维插嘴道。

「它们是谁？」奥哈拉问。

「雪貂。想看看它们吗？」

「*雪貂*！」

===Original===
“Yes. Harvey brought back a couple at the beginning of term. Ripping little beasts. We couldn’t keep them in the house, as they’d have got dropped on in a second, so we had to think of somewhere else, and thought why not keep them down here?”

“Why, indeed?” said O’Hara. “Do ye find they like it?”

“Oh, *they* don’t mind,” said Harvey. “We feed ’em twice a day. Once before breakfast﻿—we take it in turns to get up early﻿—and once directly after school. And on half-holidays and Sundays we take them out on to the downs.”

“What for?”

“Why, rabbits, of course. Renford brought back a saloon-pistol with him. We keep it locked up in a box﻿—don’t tell anyone.”

===Chinese===
「是啊。开学的时候哈维带回了一对。顶有趣的小家伙。宿舍里养不得，一秒钟就会被查抄，所以我们只好另想办法，一合计，干吗不养在这底下？」

「可不是，干吗不呢？」奥哈拉说，「你们发现它们喜欢这儿吗？」

「哦，*它们*不介意，」哈维说，「我们一天喂它们两顿。早饭前一顿——我们轮着早起——放学后立刻一顿。逢半天假和星期天，我们就带它们上丘原去。」

「去干什么？」

「咳，当然是打兔子。伦福德带回了一支打靶用的汽手枪。我们把它锁在一只箱子里——可别告诉别人。」

===Original===
“And what do ye do with the rabbits?”

“We pot at them as they come out of the holes.”

“Yes, but when ye hit ’em?”

“Oh,” said Renford, with some reluctance, “we haven’t exactly hit any yet.”

“We’ve got jolly near, though, lots of times,” said Harvey. “Last Saturday I swear I wasn’t more than a quarter of an inch off one of them. If it had been a decent-sized rabbit, I should have plugged it middle stump; only it was a small one, so I missed. But come and see them. We keep ’em right at the other end of the place, in case anybody comes in.”

===Chinese===
「那你们拿兔子怎么办？」

「它们从洞里钻出来的时候，我们就打。」

「行，可你们打中了又怎么办？」

「哦，」伦福德颇不情愿地说，「严格讲，我们还没有打中过一只。」

「不过好多次都差一点点就中了，」哈维说，「上礼拜六我发誓，有一只离准头顶多差四分之一英寸。要是只够个头的兔子，我就正打它中柱了；偏偏是只小的，就打偏了。来见识见识吧。我们把它们养在这地方顶里头那一头，万一有人进来也瞧不见。」

===Original===
“Have you ever seen anybody down here?” asked O’Hara.

“Once,” said Renford. “Half-a-dozen chaps came down here once while we were feeding the ferrets. We waited till they’d got well in, then we nipped out quietly. They didn’t see us.”

“Did you see who they were?”

“No. It was too dark. Here they are. Rummy old crib this, isn’t it? Look out for your shins on the chairs. Switch on the light, Harvey. There, aren’t they *rippers*? Quite tame, too. They know us quite well. They know they’re going to be fed, too. Hullo, Sir Nigel! This is Sir Nigel. Out of the ‘White Company,’ you know. Don’t let him nip your fingers. This other one’s Sherlock Holmes.”

“Cats-s-s﻿—s!!” said O’Hara. He had a sort of idea that that was the right thing to say to any animal that could chase and bite.

===Chinese===
「你们在这儿底下见过人吗？」奥哈拉问。

「见过一回，」伦福德说，「有半打家伙下来过一回，赶上我们正喂雪貂。我们等他们进得深了，就悄悄溜了出去。他们没瞧见我们。」

「看清他们是谁了吗？」

「没有。太黑了。它们在这儿呢。这破地方够古怪的吧？当心椅子磕了胫骨。开灯，哈维。瞧，它们是不是*宝贝蛋*？还特别温驯。它们跟我们熟得很。也知道要开饭了。喂，奈杰尔爵士（Sir Nigel）！这位就是奈杰尔爵士。《白衣伙伴》（‘White Company’）里出来的，你知道的。别让它咬了手指头。这另一只是夏洛克·福尔摩斯（Sherlock Holmes）。」

「猫咪——咪！！」奥哈拉说。他模模糊糊地觉得，对任何能追能咬的动物，说这句准没错。

===Original===
Renford was delighted to be able to show his ferrets off to so distinguished a visitor.

“What were you down here about?” inquired Harvey, when the little animals had had their meal, and had retired once more into private life.

O’Hara had expected this question, but he did not quite know what answer to give. Perhaps, on the whole, he thought, it would be best to tell them the real reason. If he refused to explain, their curiosity would be roused, which would be fatal. And to give any reason except the true one called for a display of impromptu invention of which he was not capable. Besides, they would not be likely to give away his secret while he held this one of theirs connected with the ferrets. He explained the situation briefly, and swore them to silence on the subject.

Renford’s comment was brief.

“By Jove!” he observed.

Harvey went more deeply into the question.

===Chinese===
伦福德能为这么一位显赫的来客展示自己的雪貂而心花怒放。

「你到这儿底下来干什么？」等小动物们用过餐、重新退回私人生活之后，哈维问道。

奥哈拉料到有此一问，却不太知道该怎么回答。他盘算着，总的说来，也许还是把真实缘由告诉他们为好。要是他拒不解释，反而勾起他们的好奇心，那就糟了。而编一套不真实的理由，又需要即兴创作的本事，他没这个能耐。再说，他捏着他们养雪貂这个把柄，他们多半也不会泄露他的秘密。他简明扼要地讲了情况，并要他们就此事发誓守口如瓶。

伦福德的评语很简短。

「好家伙！」他发表感想道。

哈维则把问题钻研得更深一层。

===Original===
“What makes you think they meet down here?” he asked.

“I saw some fellows cutting out of here last night. And you say ye’ve seen them here, too. I don’t see what object they could have down here if they weren’t the League holding a meeting. I don’t see what else a chap would be after.”

“He might be keeping ferrets,” hazarded Renford.

“The whole school doesn’t keep ferrets,” said O’Hara. “You’re unique in that way. No, it must be the League, an’ I mean to wait here till they come.”

“Not all night?” asked Harvey. He had a great respect for O’Hara, whose reputation in the school for out-of-the-way doings was considerable. In the bright lexicon of O’Hara he believed there to be no such word as “impossible.”

===Chinese===
「你凭什么认为他们在这底下碰头？」他问。

「俺昨儿晚上看见几个家伙从这儿溜出去。你们也说在这儿见过他们。他们要不是联盟（the League）开会，跑这底下来能有什么图头。俺想不出一个小子还能在这儿干什么别的。」

「兴许是养雪貂呢。」伦福德试探着说。

「全校可不都养雪貂，」奥哈拉说，「这方面你们是独一份。不，准是联盟，俺打算在这儿守株待兔，等他们来。」

「不会要守一整夜吧？」哈维问。他对奥哈拉敬重有加，此君在学校里以离奇行事闻名。在他心目中那部光明的奥哈拉大词典里，他相信压根没有「不可能」这个词。

===Original===
“No,” said O’Hara, “but till lockup. You two had better cut now.”

“Yes, I think we’d better,” said Harvey.

“And don’t ye breathe a word about this to a soul”﻿—a warning which extracted fervent promises of silence from both youths.

“This,” said Harvey, as they emerged on to the gravel, “is something like. I’m jolly glad we’re in it.”

“Rather. Do you think O’Hara will catch them?”

“He must if he waits down there long enough. They’re certain to come again. Don’t you wish you’d been here when the League was on before?”

“I should think I did. Race you over to the shop. I want to get something before it shuts.”

“Right ho!” And they disappeared.

===Chinese===
「不，」奥哈拉说，「守到锁门。你们俩最好这就开溜。」

「嗯，我看也是。」哈维说。

「还有，这事对谁也不许走漏半个字」——这一警告从两位少年口中榨出了热烈的信守沉默的保证。

「这才叫事儿，」两人走上砾石道时，哈维说，「咱们也掺和进去了，真叫人高兴。」

「可不是。你觉得奥哈拉能逮着他们吗？」

「只要他在底下守得够久，准能逮着。他们肯定还会来。联盟上回活动的时候你要是在场，那该多好？」

「可不是嘛。跟你赛跑去小卖部。我得赶在关门前买点东西。」

「好嘞！」说罢两人便没影了。

===Original===
O’Hara waited where he was till six struck from the clock-tower, followed by the sound of the bell as it rang for lockup. Then he picked his way carefully through the groves of chairs, barking his shins now and then on their out-turned legs, and, pushing open the door, went out into the open air. It felt very fresh and pleasant after the brand of atmosphere supplied in the vault. He then ran over to the gymnasium to meet Moriarty, feeling a little disgusted at the lack of success that had attended his detective efforts up to the present. So far he had nothing to show for his trouble except a good deal of dust on his clothes, and a dirty collar, but he was full of determination. He could play a waiting game.

It was a pity, as it happened, that O’Hara left the vault when he did. Five minutes after he had gone, six shadowy forms made their way silently and in single file through the doorway of the vault, which they closed carefully behind them. The fact that it was after lockup was of small consequence. A good deal of latitude in that way was allowed at Wrykyn. It was the custom to go out, after the bell had sounded, to visit the gymnasium. In the winter and Easter terms, the gymnasium became a sort of social club. People went there with a very small intention of doing gymnastics. They went to lounge about, talking to cronies, in front of the two huge stoves which warmed the place. Occasionally, as a concession to the look of the thing, they would do an easy exercise or two on the horse or parallels, but, for the most part, they preferred the role of spectator. There was plenty to see. In one corner O’Hara and Moriarty would be sparring their nightly six rounds (in two batches of three rounds each). In another, Drummond, who was going up to Aldershot as a featherweight, would be putting in a little practice with the instructor. On the apparatus, the members of the gymnastic six, including the two experts who were to carry the school colours to Aldershot in the spring, would be performing their usual marvels. It was worth dropping into the gymnasium of an evening. In no other place in the school were so many sights to be seen.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉在原地守到钟楼敲响六点，接着锁门的钟声响起。然后他小心翼翼地穿过椅子的丛林，小腿时不时磕在椅腿外翻处，推开那扇门，走进露天里。比起地窖供应的那款空气，外头的新鲜空气闻来分外清爽宜人。随后他跑往体育馆去会莫里亚蒂（Moriarty），心下对自己迄今侦探努力的一无所获颇感丧气。到目前为止单说辛苦，他拿得出手的成果只有衣裳上一层厚灰和一条脏领子，但他斗志满满。持久战他打得起。

可惜的是，奥哈拉偏偏挑那个时候离开了地窖。他走后五分钟，六条黑影排成一路纵队，悄无声息地穿过地窖的门洞，又小心地随手把门关上。此时已过锁门时刻，这一点无关紧要。在里金（Wrykyn），这方面得到的宽纵着实不小。钟声敲过之后再出门去体育馆，是这里的惯例。冬天学期和复活节学期，体育馆便俨然成了一个社交俱乐部。人们去那儿，练体操的心思极小。他们是去闲逛的，在给这地方供暖的两只大炉子跟前跟好友闲聊。偶尔，为了照顾一下场面，他们也会在木马或双杠上做一两个轻松的动作，但多数时候，他们更乐意当看客。可看的东西有的是。一角，是奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂每晚照例的六回合对练（分两场，每场三回合）。另一角，是以羽量级身份即将赴奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）的德拉蒙德（Drummond），正跟教练加练一阵。器械上，体操六人队的队员们——包括来年春天要为学校把校队荣誉扛到奥尔德肖特去的那两位高手——正照常施展他们的绝活。傍晚时分到体育馆串个门，值。全校再没有哪个地方，能看到这么多名堂。

===Original===
When you were surfeited with sightseeing, you went off to your house. And this was where the peculiar beauty of the gymnasium system came in. You went up to any master who happened to be there﻿—there was always one at least﻿—and observed in suave accents, “Please, sir, can I have a paper?” Whereupon, he, taking a scrap of paper, would write upon it, “J. O. Jones (or A. B. Smith or C. D. Robinson) left gymnasium at such-and-such a time.” And, by presenting this to the menial who opened the door to you at your house, you went in rejoicing, and all was peace.

Now, there was no mention on the paper of the hour at which you came to the gymnasium﻿—only of the hour at which you left. Consequently, certain lawless spirits would range the neighbourhood after lockup, and, by putting in a quarter of an hour at the gymnasium before returning to their houses, escape comment. To this class belonged the shadowy forms previously mentioned.

===Chinese===
看饱了热闹，你便回自己的宿舍去。体育馆制度的妙处正在于此。你走向随便哪位恰好在场的教师——总归至少有一位——用温文尔雅的声调说：「求您了，先生，能赏张条子吗？」于是他取一张纸条，在上面写：「J. O. 琼斯（或 A. B. 史密斯，或 C. D. 鲁滨逊）于某时某刻离馆。」把这张条子呈给你宿舍门口给你开门的校工，你便欢天喜地地进去了，万事大吉。

可你看，条子上并不写你几点来体育馆——只写你几点走。于是乎，若干不守规矩的家伙便在锁门之后到四下里游荡，只要回宿舍前先去体育馆消磨一刻钟，便可免遭盘问。前文提到的那几条黑影，正属此族。

===Original===
O’Hara had forgotten this custom, with the result that he was not at the vault when they arrived. Moriarty, to whom he confided between the rounds the substance of his evening’s discoveries, reminded him of it. “It’s no good watching before lockup,” he said. “After six is the time they’ll come, if they come at all.”

“Bedad, ye’re right,” said O’Hara. “One of these nights we’ll take a night off from boxing, and go and watch.”

“Right,” said Moriarty. “Are ye ready to go on?”

“Yes. I’m going to practise that left swing at the body this round. The one Fitzsimmons does.” And they “put ’em up” once more.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉把这条惯例忘了，结果他们到的时候他不在地窖。莫里亚蒂——奥哈拉趁回合间隙把当晚所见所获的要点密告给了他——提醒了他。「锁门前守是白守，」他说，「他们要来，也是六点以后来。」

「嗐呀，你说得对，」奥哈拉说，「哪天晚上咱们歇一晚不打拳，去守着。」

「成，」莫里亚蒂说，「接着来？」

「来。这一回合俺要练那记左摆拳打躯干。菲茨西蒙斯（Fitzsimmons）使的那手。」说罢两人又「举起了拳头」。

## V Mill Receives Visitors / 第五章 米尔接待来客

===Original===
Trevor’s first idea was that somebody had sent the letter for a joke﻿—Clowes for choice.

He sounded him on the subject after breakfast.

“Did you send me that letter?” he inquired, when Clowes came into his study to borrow a *Sportsman*.

“What letter? Did you send the team for tomorrow up to the sporter? I wonder what sort of a lot the Town are bringing.”

“About not giving Barry his footer colours?”

Clowes was reading the paper.

“Giving whom?” he asked.

“Barry. Can’t you listen?”

“Giving him what?”

“Footer colours.”

“What about them?”

Trevor sprang at the paper, and tore it away from him. After which he sat on the fragments.

===Chinese===
特雷弗（Trevor）的第一个念头是，这封信是有人开玩笑寄来的——首选嫌疑人是克劳斯（Clowes）。

早饭后他就此试探了他一番。

「那封信是你寄给我的吗？」克劳斯到他书房来借一份《体育人报》（*Sportsman*）时，他问道。

「什么信？明天的出场名单你送到体育用品店去了吗？不知道镇队这次会拉来一支什么货色。」

「就是那封说别给巴里（Barry）发橄榄球队色的？」

克劳斯正在看报。

「给谁发？」他问。

「巴里。你就不能好好听人说话？」

「给他发什么？」

「橄榄球队色。」

「队色怎么了？」

特雷弗扑向报纸，从他手里夺下来撕了。然后他坐在了碎片上。

===Original===
“Did you send me a letter about not giving Barry his footer colours?”

Clowes surveyed him with the air of a nurse to whom the family baby has just said some more than usually good thing.

“Don’t stop,” he said, “I could listen all day.”

Trevor felt in his pocket for the note, and flung it at him. Clowes picked it up, and read it gravely.

“What *are* footer colours?” he asked.

“Well,” said Trevor, “it’s a pretty rotten sort of joke, whoever sent it. You haven’t said yet whether you did or not.”

===Chinese===
「你到底寄没寄那封不让给巴里发橄榄球队色的信？」

克劳斯端详着他，那神情活像一位保姆，眼看着家里的宝宝又说出了一句格外妙的话。

「别停，」他说，「我能听一整天。」

特雷弗从口袋里摸出那张字条，朝他摔了过去。克劳斯拾起来，郑重其事地读了。

「橄榄球队色*究竟*是什么东西？」他问。

「哼，」特雷弗说，「不管是谁寄的，这玩笑都够蹩脚的。你还没说你到底寄没寄呢。」

===Original===
“What earthly reason should I have for sending it? And I think you’re making a mistake if you think this is meant as a joke.”

“You don’t really believe this League rot?”

“You didn’t see Mill’s study ‘after treatment.’ I did. Anyhow, how do you account for the card I showed you?”

“But that sort of thing doesn’t happen at school.”

“Well, it *has* happened, you see.”

“Who do you think did send the letter, then?”

“The President of the League.”

“And who the dickens is the President of the League when he’s at home?”

===Chinese===
「我寄它图什么？再说，你要是以为这是玩笑，那就想岔了。」

「你不会真信联盟（the League）这套鬼话吧？」

「米尔（Mill）的书房『处理之后』的模样你是没见着。我可见了。再说，我给你看的那张卡片，你又怎么解释？」

「可这种事在学校里是不会发生的。」

「嗐，它*偏偏*发生了，你瞧。」

「那么你认为信是谁寄的？」

「联盟的会长。」

「那位联盟会长究竟是何方神圣？」

===Original===
“If I knew that, I should tell Mill, and earn his blessing. Not that I want it.”

“Then, I suppose,” snorted Trevor, “you’d suggest that on the strength of this letter I’d better leave Barry out of the team?”

“Satirically in brackets,” commented Clowes.

“It’s no good your jumping on *me*,” he added. “I’ve done nothing. All I suggest is that you’d better keep more or less of a lookout. If this League’s anything like the old one, you’ll find they’ve all sorts of ways of getting at people they don’t love. I shouldn’t like to come down for a bath some morning, and find you already in possession, tied up like Robinson. When they found Robinson, he was quite blue both as to the face and speech. He didn’t speak very clearly, but what one could catch was well worth hearing. I should advise you to sleep with a loaded revolver under your pillow.”

“The first thing I shall do is find out who wrote this letter.”

“I should,” said Clowes, encouragingly. “Keep moving.”

===Chinese===
「我要是知道，早就告诉米尔，赚他一声祝福了。倒也不是稀罕那个。」

「这么说，」特雷弗嗤了一声，「你的高见是，凭着这封信，我最好把巴里从名单里划掉？」

「括号里注明：此话系讽刺。」克劳斯评点道。

「你也犯不着冲*我*来，」他补充道，「我什么也没干。我的全部建议不过是，你最好多多少少留点神。这个联盟要是跟从前那个有几分相像，你就会发现他们整治不喜欢的人，花样多得很。我可不想哪天早上下楼去洗澡，发现你已经捷足先登，被人捆成罗宾逊（Robinson）那样。当年人们找到罗宾逊的时候，他的脸色和话语都发青了。他说话已经不利索，可勉强听得清的那些，字字值钱。我奉劝你睡觉时枕头底下压一把上了膛的左轮枪。」

「我要办的头一件事，就是查出这封信是谁写的。」

「这就对了，」克劳斯鼓励道，「继续推进。」

===Original===
In Seymour’s house the Mill’s study incident formed the only theme of conversation that morning. Previously the sudden elevation to the first fifteen of Barry, who was popular in the house, at the expense of Rand-Brown, who was unpopular, had given Seymour’s something to talk about. But the ragging of the study put this topic entirely in the shade. The study was still on view in almost its original condition of disorder, and all day comparative strangers flocked to see Mill in his den, in order to inspect things. Mill was a youth with few friends, and it is probable that more of his fellow-Seymourites crossed the threshold of his study on the day after the occurrence than had visited him in the entire course of his school career. Brown would come in to borrow a knife, would sweep the room with one comprehensive glance, and depart, to be followed at brief intervals by Smith, Robinson, and Jones, who came respectively to learn the right time, to borrow a book, and to ask him if he had seen a pencil anywhere. Towards the end of the day, Mill would seem to have wearied somewhat of the proceedings, as was proved when Master Thomas Renford, aged fourteen (who fagged for Milton, the head of the house), burst in on the thin pretence that he had mistaken the study for that of his rightful master, and gave vent to a prolonged whistle of surprise and satisfaction at the sight of the ruins. On that occasion, the incensed owner of the dismantled study, taking a mean advantage of the fact that he was a prefect, and so entitled to wield the rod, produced a handy swagger-stick from an adjacent corner, and, inviting Master Renford to bend over, gave him six of the best to remember him by. Which ceremony being concluded, he kicked him out into the passage, and Renford went down to the junior day-room to tell his friend Harvey about it.

===Chinese===
在西摩宿舍（Seymour’s），米尔书房事件成了那天早上唯一的话题。此前，人缘好的巴里顶替了不得人心的兰德-布朗（Rand-Brown）骤然升入一队十五人，已经够西摩宿舍议论一阵的了。可书房被捣一事一出，这话题顿时黯然失色。书房还几乎保持着最初那副狼藉原状对外开放，一整天里，八竿子打不着的陌路人也蜂拥着到米尔的巢穴里来参观。米尔是个朋友寥寥的少年，事发第二天跨进他书房门槛的西摩同窗，多半比他整个学生生涯里来访过的加起来还多。布朗进来借把小刀，目光把屋子一扫无余，转身就走；随后不多时，史密斯、罗宾逊和琼斯接踵而至，分别是来对表、借书、问他有没有在哪儿见过一支铅笔的。临近傍晚，米尔对这套把戏似乎有些腻了，这一点得到证明：十四岁的托马斯·伦福德少爷（他给舍长弥尔顿（Milton）跑腿）闯了进来，借口稀薄得很——说是走错了书房，认错了正主儿——一见废墟便吹起一声悠长的口哨，又惊又喜。这一次，这位被捣了书房的愤懑主人，卑劣地利用了自己身为级长、有权动用戒尺的便宜，从近旁墙角抄起一根顺手的短杖，请伦福德少爷弯下腰去，结结实实赏了他六下好叫他长记性。这套仪式完结，他又把他一脚踢出门外，伦福德便下楼到低年级活动室，把这事讲给他的朋友哈维（Harvey）听。

===Original===
“Gave me six, the cad,” said he, “just because I had a look at his beastly study. Why shouldn’t I look at his study if I like? I’ve a jolly good mind to go up and have another squint.”

Harvey warmly approved the scheme.

“No, I don’t think I will,” said Renford with a yawn. “It’s such a fag going upstairs.”

“Yes, isn’t it?” said Harvey.

“And he’s such a beast, too.”

“Yes, isn’t he?” said Harvey.

“I’m jolly glad his study *has* been ragged,” continued the vindictive Renford.

“It’s jolly exciting, isn’t it?” added Harvey. “And I thought this term was going to be slow. The Easter term generally is.”

===Chinese===
「赏了我六下，那个下流胚，」他说，「就因为我看了看他那破书房。我乐意看他书房，怎么就不行？我真想这就再上去瞄一眼。」

哈维热烈赞成这个计划。

「算了，还是不了。」伦福德打个哈欠说，「上楼太费劲。」

「可不是嘛。」哈维说。

「而且他还是个畜生。」

「可不是嘛。」哈维说。

「他的书房*就是*被捣了，我高兴得很。」睚眦必报的伦福德继续道。

「带劲儿得很，是不是？」哈维补充道，「我原以为这个学期会很无聊呢。复活节学期一般都这样。」

===Original===
This remark seemed to suggest a train of thought to Renford, who made the following cryptic observation. “Have you seen them today?”

To the ordinary person the words would have conveyed little meaning. To Harvey they appeared to teem with import.

“Yes,” he said, “I saw them early this morning.”

“Were they all right?”

“Yes. Splendid.”

“Good,” said Renford.

===Chinese===
这句话似乎给伦福德牵出了一串思路，他发表了如下隐语：「你今天看过它们了吗？」

在寻常人听来，这话几乎不着边际。在哈维听来，却字字千钧。

「看了，」他说，「今天一大早我就去看过。」

「它们都还好？」

「好得很。精神极了。」

「好。」伦福德说。

===Original===
Barry’s friend Drummond was one of those who had visited the scene of the disaster early, before Mill’s energetic hand had repaired the damage done, and his narrative was consequently in some demand.

“The place was in a frightful muck,” he said. “Everything smashed except the table; and ink all over the place. Whoever did it must have been fairly sick with him, or he’d never have taken the trouble to do it so thoroughly. Made a fair old hash of things, didn’t he, Bertie?”

“Bertie” was the form in which the school elected to serve up the name of De Bertini. Raoul de Bertini was a French boy who had come to Wrykyn in the previous term. Drummond’s father had met his father in Paris, and Drummond was supposed to be looking after Bertie. They shared a study together. Bertie could not speak much English, and what he did speak was, like Mill’s furniture, badly broken.

“Pardon?” he said.

“Doesn’t matter,” said Drummond, “it wasn’t anything important. I was only appealing to you for corroborative detail to give artistic verisimilitude to a bald and unconvincing narrative.”

Bertie grinned politely. He always grinned when he was not quite equal to the intellectual pressure of the conversation. As a consequence of which, he was generally, like Mrs. Fezziwig, one vast, substantial smile.

===Chinese===
巴里的朋友德拉蒙德（Drummond）属于赶早探访过灾难现场的一批人——那时米尔那只勤快的手尚未修复损失——因此他的叙述颇有些供不应求。

「那地方真是乱得天翻地覆，」他说，「除了桌子，样样都砸了；墨水泼得满处都是。干这事的不管是哪位，准是恨他恨得不轻，不然才犯不着下这么细的功夫。把东西搅了个稀巴烂，是不是，伯蒂（Bertie）？」

「伯蒂」是全校给德·贝尔蒂尼这个名字指定的上桌形式。拉乌尔·德·贝尔蒂尼（Raoul de Bertini）是个法国男孩，上学期来到里金（Wrykyn）。德拉蒙德的父亲在巴黎结识了他父亲，照顾伯蒂的差事便算在了德拉蒙德头上。两人合用一间书房。伯蒂说不了几句英语，而他说出口的那几句，也跟米尔的家具一样，破损得厉害。

「请再说一遍？」他说。

「不要紧，」德拉蒙德说，「不是什么要紧事。我不过是想请你也补充点佐证细节，好给这段光秃秃、难以服人的叙述添几分艺术上的逼真。」

伯蒂客气地咧嘴一笑。每逢谈话的智力压力让他招架不住时，他一律咧嘴一笑。其结果便是，他整个人通常就像费兹威格太太（Mrs. Fezziwig）一样，是一副硕大而敦实的笑容。

===Original===
“I never liked Mill much,” said Barry, “but I think it’s rather bad luck on the man.”

“Once,” announced McTodd, solemnly, “he kicked me﻿—for making a row in the passage.” It was plain that the recollection rankled.

Barry would probably have pointed out what an excellent and praiseworthy act on Mill’s part that had been, when Rand-Brown came in.

“Prefects’ meeting?” he inquired. “Or haven’t they made you a prefect yet, McTodd?”

McTodd said they had not.

Nobody present liked Rand-Brown, and they looked at him rather inquiringly, as if to ask what he had come for. A friend may drop in for a chat. An acquaintance must justify his intrusion.

===Chinese===
「我一向不大喜欢米尔，」巴里说，「可这事实在是够他倒霉的。」

「有一回，」麦克托德（McTodd）郑重宣布，「他踢过我——就因为我在走廊里吵闹。」显然这段旧恨至今刺痛着他。

巴里多半正要指出，米尔那一脚踢得何等英明、何等值得称颂，这时兰德-布朗进来了。

「级长开会呢？」他问，「还是说他们至今没封你当级长，麦克托德？」

麦克托德说没有。

在座没人喜欢兰德-布朗，大家都相当探究地望着他，仿佛在问他来干什么。朋友可以顺路进来闲聊。泛泛之交则必须为自己的闯入给出交代。

===Original===
Rand-Brown ignored the silent inquiry. He seated himself on the table, and dragged up a chair to rest his legs on.

“Talking about Mill, of course?” he said.

“Yes,” said Drummond. “Have you seen his study since it happened?”

“Yes.”

Rand-Brown smiled, as if the recollection amused him. He was one of those people who do not look their best when they smile.

“Playing for the first tomorrow, Barry?”

“I don’t know,” said Barry, shortly. “I haven’t seen the list.”

He objected to the introduction of the topic. It is never pleasant to have to discuss games with the very man one has ousted from the team.

Drummond, too, seemed to feel that the situation was an embarrassing one, for a few minutes later he got up to go over to the gymnasium.

“Any of you chaps coming?” he asked.

Barry and McTodd thought they would, and the three left the room.

“Nothing like showing a man you don’t want him, eh, Bertie? What do you think?” said Rand-Brown.

Bertie grinned politely.

===Chinese===
兰德-布朗不理会这无声的质询。他在桌上坐下，又拖过一把椅子来搁腿。

「不用说，正聊米尔呢？」他说。

「是啊，」德拉蒙德说，「事发之后你看过他的书房吗？」

「看过。」

兰德-布朗微微一笑，仿佛回想起来觉得有趣。他属于那种一笑起来就折损颜值的人。

「明天为一队上场吗，巴里？」

「不知道，」巴里简短地说，「名单我还没见着。」

他不喜欢提起这个话头。跟自己亲手从队里挤掉的那个人讨论比赛，从来不是件愉快的事。

德拉蒙德似乎也觉得这局面尴尬，几分钟后便起身要去体育馆。

「有哪位一块儿去吗？」他问。

巴里和麦克托德觉得可以去，三人便出了屋。

「让一个人明白你不待见他，这招最灵了，呃，伯蒂？你说呢？」兰德-布朗说。

伯蒂客气地咧嘴一笑。

## XII News of the Gold Bat / 第十二章 金色球棒的消息

===Original===
Shoeblossom sat disconsolately on the table in the senior day-room. He was not happy in exile. Brewing in the senior day-room was a mere vulgar brawl, lacking all the refining influences of the study. You had to fight for a place at the fire, and when you had got it ’twas not always easy to keep it, and there was no privacy, and the fellows were always bear-fighting, so that it was impossible to read a book quietly for ten consecutive minutes without some ass heaving a cushion at you or turning out the gas. Altogether Shoeblossom yearned for the peace of his study, and wished earnestly that Mr. Seymour would withdraw the order of banishment. It was the not being able to read that he objected to chiefly. In place of brewing, the ex-proprietors of studies five, six, and seven now made a practice of going to the school shop. It was more expensive and not nearly so comfortable﻿—there is a romance about a study brew which you can never get anywhere else﻿—but it served, and it was not on this score that he grumbled most. What he hated was having to live in a bear-garden. For Shoeblossom was a man of moods. Give him two or three congenial spirits to back him up, and he would lead the revels with the *abandon* of a Mr. Bultitude (after his return to his original form). But he liked to choose his accomplices, and the gay sparks of the senior day-room did not appeal to him. They were not intellectual enough. In his lucid intervals, he was accustomed to be almost abnormally solemn and respectable. When not promoting some unholy rag, Shoeblossom resembled an elderly gentleman of studious habits. He liked to sit in a comfortable chair and read a book. It was the impossibility of doing this in the senior day-room that led him to try and think of some other haven where he might rest. Had it been summer, he would have taken some literature out on to the cricket-field or the downs, and put in a little steady reading there, with the aid of a bag of cherries. But with the thermometer low, that was impossible.

He felt very lonely and dismal. He was not a man with many friends. In fact, Barry and the other three were almost the only members of the house with whom he was on speaking-terms. And of these four he saw very little. Drummond and Barry were always out of doors or over at the gymnasium, and as for McTodd and De Bertini, it was not worth while talking to the one, and impossible to talk to the other. No wonder Shoeblossom felt dull. Once Barry and Drummond had taken him over to the gymnasium with them, but this had bored him worse than ever. They had been hard at it all the time﻿—for, unlike a good many of the school, they went to the gymnasium for business, not to lounge﻿—and he had had to sit about watching them. And watching gymnastics was one of the things he most loathed. Since then he had refused to go.

===Chinese===
