休布洛瑟姆（Shoeblossom）郁郁寡欢地坐在高年级活动室的桌子上。流放的日子不好过。在高年级活动室里烧茶，纯属一场庸俗的混战，全无书房里那种种教化人心的妙处。你得为一个炉边位置搏杀，抢到手了还未必守得住；没有半点清静可言；那帮家伙永远在扭打混战，你想安安静静连读十分钟书都不可能，保准有哪个蠢驴朝你扔坐垫，或者把煤气灯拧灭。总之，休布洛瑟姆渴念着自己书房里的安宁，衷心盼着西摩先生（Mr. Seymour）收回那道放逐令。他最反对的首先是没法读书。五号、六号、七号书房的原主人们如今改换了门庭，烧茶改成去学校小卖部。这更费钱，也远不如从前舒坦——书房茶会自有一股别处再也寻不着的诗意——但也凑合用了，而他抱怨最多的倒还不在这上头。他恨的是不得不活在一座斗熊场里。因为休布洛瑟姆是个情绪起落的人。给他两三个投缘的伙伴撑腰，他也能以布尔蒂丘德先生（Mr. Bultitude）（变回原形之后）那般的*放纵*统领狂欢。可他喜欢自己挑选同伙，而高年级活动室里那些活宝对他没有吸引力。他们的智力不够格。在他神志清明的间歇里，他习惯摆出近乎过分的庄重体面。不策划什么伤天害理的胡闹时，休布洛瑟姆俨然一位好学的老绅士。他喜欢窝在一张舒服的椅子里读书。正是在高年级活动室里做不成这件事，才引得他动脑筋另觅一处可以安身的港湾。若是夏天，他会带上一本书上板球场或丘原，就着一袋樱桃，稳稳当当读上一阵。可寒暑表正低，那可行不通。

他感到说不出的孤单凄凉。他这人朋友不多。事实上，巴里（Barry）和另外三个几乎是他在这宿舍里仅有的说得上话的人。而这四位他又难得见到。德拉蒙德（Drummond）和巴里总在户外，或者泡在体育馆那边；至于麦克托德（McTodd）和德·贝尔蒂尼，一个不值得跟他说话，一个没法跟他说话。也难怪休布洛瑟姆觉得无聊。巴里和德拉蒙德有一回带他去过体育馆，可那次把他闷得更凶。两人从头到尾练得热火朝天——他们跟学校里不少人不一样，去体育馆是办公事，不是去闲逛的——他只得干坐在一旁看。而观看体操恰恰是他最痛恨的事情之一。打那以后他就不肯去了。

===Original===
That night matters came to a head. Just as he had settled down to read, somebody, in flinging a cushion across the room, brought down the gas apparatus with a run, and before light was once more restored it was teatime. After that there was preparation, which lasted for two hours, and by the time he had to go to bed he had not been able to read a single page of the enthralling work with which he was at present occupied.

He had just got into bed when he was struck with a brilliant idea. Why waste the precious hours in sleep? What was that saying of somebody’s, “Five hours for a wise man, six for somebody else﻿—he forgot whom﻿—eight for a fool, nine for an idiot,” or words to that effect? Five hours sleep would mean that he need not go to bed till half past two. In the meanwhile he could be finding out exactly what the hero *did* do when he found out (to his horror) that it was his cousin Jasper who had really killed the old gentleman in the wood. The only question was﻿—how was he to do his reading? Prefects were allowed to work on after lights out in their dormitories by the aid of a candle, but to the ordinary mortal this was forbidden.

===Chinese===
那天晚上，事态到了爆发点。他刚坐下要读书，不知谁把一只坐垫满屋子扔，一下把煤气灯装置打落在地，等灯光重新亮起来，已到吃茶的钟点。之后是晚自习，一上两个钟头，等到该上床睡觉，他眼下读得入迷的那部大作，连一页都没能读上。

他刚钻进被窝，一个妙主意当头砸下。干吗把大好光阴浪费在睡觉上？哪位名人不是说过：「智者五小时，某某六小时——忘了是谁——蠢人八小时，白痴九小时」，或者诸如此类的话？睡五个小时，就意味着他可以熬到两点半再睡。这段时间里，他正好可以弄个明白：主人公发现（惊骇欲绝地发现）林子里真正干掉老绅士的原来是表亲贾斯珀（Jasper）之后，*究竟*干了些什么。唯一的问题是——书怎么读？级长获准熄灯后在寝室里点蜡烛用功，寻常凡人却无此特权。

===Original===
Then he was struck with another brilliant idea. It is a curious thing about ideas. You do not get one for over a month, and then there comes a rush of them, all brilliant. Why, he thought, should he not go and read in his study with a dark lantern? He had a dark lantern. It was one of the things he had found lying about at home on the last day of the holidays, and had brought with him to school. It was his custom to go about the house just before the holidays ended, snapping up unconsidered trifles, which might or might not come in useful. This term he had brought back a curious metal vase (which looked Indian, but which had probably been made in Birmingham the year before last), two old coins (of no mortal use to anybody in the world, including himself), and the dark lantern. It was reposing now in the cupboard in his study nearest the window.

He had brought his book up with him on coming to bed, on the chance that he might have time to read a page or two if he woke up early. (He had always been doubtful about that man Jasper. For one thing, he had been seen pawning the old gentleman’s watch on the afternoon of the murder, which was a suspicious circumstance, and then he was not a nice character at all, and just the sort of man who would be likely to murder old gentlemen in woods.) He waited till Mr. Seymour had paid his nightly visit﻿—he went the round of the dormitories at about eleven﻿—and then he chuckled gently. If Mill, the dormitory prefect, was awake, the chuckle would make him speak, for Mill was of a suspicious nature, and believed that it was only his unintermitted vigilance which prevented the dormitory ragging all night.

===Chinese===
紧接着他又被第二个妙主意砸中了。主意这东西真是奇妙。一个多月也逮不着一个，然后忽啦一大群涌来，个个精彩。他想，干吗不打上一盏遮光提灯，到自己书房里去读？他有遮光提灯。那是假期最后一天在家里四处乱放着的东西里捡的，顺手带来了学校。每逢假期将尽，他照例要在家里转上一圈，顺手收些无主的小物件，日后未必用得着，也未必用不着。这学期他带回来一只古怪的金属花瓶（看着像印度货，多半是前年在伯明翰造的）、两枚旧钱币（对世上任何人——包括他自己——都毫无用处），还有这盏遮光提灯。它此刻正安卧在他书房里最靠窗的那口柜子里。

上床时他把书随身带了上来，碰运气想着万一醒得早，兴许能读上一两页。（他对贾斯珀这个人一直疑心重重。一来，案发当天下午有人瞧见他典当了老绅士的表，这情形可疑得很；二来，这人也压根不是个好角色，正是那种会在林子里谋害老绅士的料。）他等西摩先生查完夜——他十一点光景挨个儿巡视寝室——然后轻轻咯咯笑了一声。要是寝室级长米尔（Mill）醒着，这一声笑准会叫他出声，因为米尔生性多疑，坚信全靠自己时刻不懈的警惕，才镇得这间寝室没闹个通宵。

===Original===
Mill *was* awake.

“Be quiet, there,” he growled. “Shut up that noise.”

Shoeblossom felt that the time was not yet ripe for his departure. Half an hour later he tried again. There was no rebuke. To make certain he emitted a second chuckle, replete with sinister meaning. A slight snore came from the direction of Mill’s bed. Shoeblossom crept out of the room, and hurried to his study. The door was not locked, for Mr. Seymour had relied on his commands being sufficient to keep the owner out of it. He slipped in, found and lit the dark lantern, and settled down to read. He read with feverish excitement. The thing was, you see, that though Claud Trevelyan (that was the hero) knew jolly well that it was Jasper who had done the murder, the police didn’t, and, as he (Claud) was too noble to tell them, he had himself been arrested on suspicion. Shoeblossom was skimming through the pages with starting eyes, when suddenly his attention was taken from his book by a sound. It was a footstep. Somebody was coming down the passage, and under the door filtered a thin stream of light. To snap the dark slide over the lantern and dart to the door, so that if it opened he would be behind it, was with him, as Mr. Claud Trevelyan might have remarked, the work of a moment. He heard the door of study number five flung open, and then the footsteps passed on, and stopped opposite his own den. The handle turned, and the light of a candle flashed into the room, to be extinguished instantly as the draught of the moving door caught it.

===Chinese===
米尔*偏偏*醒着。

「安静，那边的，」他低吼道，「把那声响给我停了。」

休布洛瑟姆觉得出走的时机尚未成熟。半小时后他又试了一次。没有斥责。为保险起见他又发出一声咯咯轻笑，含义险恶。米尔床的方向传来一声轻微的鼾息。休布洛瑟姆蹑手蹑脚溜出寝室，直奔书房。门没锁——西摩先生倚仗的是自己的禁令足以把主人挡在门外。他闪身进去，摸出遮光提灯点上，埋头读了起来。他读得心潮滚烫。原来是这样：克劳德·特雷维莲（Claud Trevelyan）（就是主人公）虽然心知肚明凶手就是贾斯珀，警方却蒙在鼓里，而他（克劳德）太高尚，不肯吐露，自己倒因嫌疑被抓了起来。休布洛瑟姆双眼圆睁，一页页飞掠而过，忽然，一阵声响把他的注意力从书上拽开。是脚步声。有人正顺着走廊过来，门缝底下渗进来一线细细的光。啪地合上提灯的遮光板、箭步蹿到门后——门若打开，他正好藏在门背——这一套动作，用克劳德·特雷维莲先生的话说，不过片刻之功。他听见五号书房的门被甩开，随后脚步声继续前行，在他那间巢穴对面停住。门把手转动，烛光一闪照进屋来，随即被门扇带起的风扑灭。

===Original===
Shoeblossom heard his visitor utter an exclamation of annoyance, and fumble in his pocket for matches. He recognised the voice. It was Mr. Seymour’s. The fact was that Mr. Seymour had had the same experience as General Stanley in *The Pirates of Penzance*:

> The man who finds his conscience ache,
 

 No peace at all enjoys;
 

 And, as I lay in bed awake,
 

 I thought I heard a noise.

Whether Mr. Seymour’s conscience ached or not, cannot, of course, be discovered. But he had certainly thought he heard a noise, and he had come to investigate.
The search for matches had so far proved fruitless. Shoeblossom stood and quaked behind the door. The reek of hot tin from the dark lantern grew worse momentarily. Mr. Seymour sniffed several times, until Shoeblossom thought that he must be discovered. Then, to his immense relief, the master walked away. Shoeblossom’s chance had come. Mr. Seymour had probably gone to get some matches to relight his candle. It was far from likely that the episode was closed. He would be back again presently. If Shoeblossom was going to escape, he must do it now, so he waited till the footsteps had passed away, and then darted out in the direction of his dormitory.

===Chinese===
休布洛瑟姆听见来客懊恼地叫了一声，在口袋里摸索火柴。他听出了那嗓音。是西摩先生的。事实是，西摩先生的遭遇与《彭赞斯的海盗》（*The Pirates of Penzance*）里的斯坦利将军（General Stanley）如出一辙：

> 谁的良心隐隐作痛，
 

 谁就全无安宁可言；
 

 而我躺在床上清醒，
 

 恍惚听见一声响动。

西摩先生的良心到底痛没痛，自然无从查考。但他确乎觉得自己听见了响动，于是前来查个究竟。
火柴至今没摸着。休布洛瑟姆立在门后发抖。遮光提灯里透出的热铁皮味一阵浓过一阵。西摩先生抽了好几回鼻子，抽得休布洛瑟姆以为自己必被发现无疑。随后，令他大松一口气，那位舍监走开了。休布洛瑟姆的机会来了。西摩先生多半是找火柴重新点蜡烛去了。这桩公案远不可能就此了结。他转眼就会回来。休布洛瑟姆要逃，就趁现在。于是他等脚步声远去，便朝着自己寝室的方向箭步飞奔。

===Original===
As he was passing Milton’s study, a white figure glided out of it. All that he had ever read or heard of spectres rushed into Shoeblossom’s petrified brain. He wished he was safely in bed. He wished he had never come out of it. He wished he had led a better and nobler life. He wished he had never been born.

The figure passed quite close to him as he stood glued against the wall, and he saw it disappear into the dormitory opposite his own, of which Rigby was prefect. He blushed hotly at the thought of the fright he had been in. It was only somebody playing the same game as himself.

He jumped into bed and lay down, having first plunged the lantern bodily into his jug to extinguish it. Its indignant hiss had scarcely died away when Mr. Seymour appeared at the door. It had occurred to Mr. Seymour that he had smelt something very much out of the ordinary in Shoeblossom’s study, a smell uncommonly like that of hot tin. And a suspicion dawned on him that Shoeblossom had been in there with a dark lantern. He had come to the dormitory to confirm his suspicions. But a glance showed him how unjust they had been. There was Shoeblossom fast asleep. Mr. Seymour therefore followed the excellent example of my Lord Tomnoddy on a celebrated occasion, and went off to bed.

===Chinese===
路过弥尔顿（Milton）的书房时，一条白影从里面滑了出来。休布洛瑟姆那吓僵的脑子里，霎时涌进了平生读过听过的一切鬼怪传说。他巴望自己安稳躺在床上。他巴望自己从没下过床。他巴望自己这一辈子活得更端正更高尚。他巴望自己压根没生下来过。

他贴墙钉在那儿，那白影擦身而过，他眼看着它隐没进对面那间寝室——里格比（Rigby）是那间的级长。想到自己方才那副惊惶，他脸上发烧。不过是有人跟他玩着同一套把戏罢了。

他先把提灯整个按进水罐里灭掉，然后跳上床躺下。铁皮发出的愤怒嘶声还没完全消停，西摩先生已出现在门口。西摩先生忽然想起，休布洛瑟姆书房里有股很不寻常的气味，一股酷似烧热的铁皮的味儿。他心头升起一丝怀疑：休布洛瑟姆一直待在里头，就着遮光提灯。他是来寝室验证疑心的。可一眼望去，疑心显得多么不公道。休布洛瑟姆正酣睡着呢。西摩先生于是效法汤诺迪勋爵（my Lord Tomnoddy）在著名一幕中的光辉榜样，径自睡觉去了。

---

===Original===
It was the custom for the captain of football at Wrykyn to select and publish the team for the Ripton match a week before the day on which it was to be played. On the evening after the Nomads’ match, Trevor was sitting in his study writing out the names, when there came a knock at the door, and his fag entered with a letter.

“This has just come, Trevor,” he said.

“All right. Put it down.”

The fag left the room. Trevor picked up the letter. The handwriting was strange to him. The words had been printed. Then it flashed upon him that he had received a letter once before addressed in the same way﻿—the letter from the League about Barry. Was this, too, from that address? He opened it.

It was.

He read it, and gasped. The worst had happened. The gold bat was in the hands of the enemy.

===Chinese===
里金（Wrykyn）的惯例是：橄榄球队长要在里普顿（Ripton）比赛开打前一个星期选定并公布出场名单。游牧人队（the Nomads）一战的那个晚上，特雷弗（Trevor）正坐在书房里誊写名单，忽听有人敲门，他的跑腿生拿着一封信进来了。

「刚到的，特雷弗。」他说。

「好。搁下吧。」

跑腿生出了屋。特雷弗拾起信。这手迹他眼生。字是印出来的。他猛然想起，从前他也收到过一封这样写的信——联盟（the League）寄来的关于巴里的那封。这封，莫非也出自同一处？他拆开了。

果然。

他读完，倒抽一口冷气。最坏的情形发生了。金色球棒落入了敌手。

## XVIII O’Hara Excels Himself / 第十八章 奥哈拉超水平发挥

===Original===
It was Renford’s turn next morning to get up and feed the ferrets. Harvey had done it the day before.

Renford was not a youth who enjoyed early rising, but in the cause of the ferrets he would have endured anything, so at six punctually he slid out of bed, dressed quietly, so as not to disturb the rest of the dormitory, and ran over to the vault. To his utter amazement he found it locked. Such a thing had never been done before in the whole course of his experience. He tugged at the handle, but not an inch or a fraction of an inch would the door yield. The policy of the Open Door had ceased to find favour in the eyes of the authorities.

A feeling of blank despair seized upon him. He thought of the dismay of the ferrets when they woke up and realised that there was no chance of breakfast for them. And then they would gradually waste away, and some day somebody would go down to the vault to fetch chairs, and would come upon two mouldering skeletons, and wonder what they had once been. He almost wept at the vision so conjured up.

There was nobody about. Perhaps he might break in somehow. But then there was nothing to get to work with. He could not kick the door down. No, he must give it up, and the ferrets’ breakfast-hour must be postponed. Possibly Harvey might be able to think of something.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨轮到伦福德（Renford）早起喂雪貂。前一天是哈维（Harvey）喂的。

伦福德不是个爱早起的少年，但为了雪貂的事业，什么他都肯忍受。于是六点整，他从床上溜下来，轻手轻脚穿好衣服，免得搅扰寝室里其他人，一路跑向地窖。他大吃一惊：地窖竟上了锁。以他全部阅历而论，这种事还从未有过。他拽住门把手死命拉，门却一分一毫也不肯让步。「门户开放」政策已经失宠，不再得当权者的青睐了。

一阵茫然的绝望攫住了他。他想到雪貂们醒来时发觉早餐无望的沮丧。然后它们会日渐消瘦，终有一天，有人下地窖搬椅子，会撞见两具腐朽的骸骨，纳闷它们生前是何物。这番景象几乎令他落泪。

四下无人。兴许他能想个法子撬进去。可手头又没有家伙什。门总不能踹开。不，只得作罢，雪貂的早餐时间必须推迟了。兴许哈维能想出办法。

===Original===
“Fed ’em?” inquired Harvey, when they met at breakfast.

“No, I couldn’t.”

“Why on earth not? You didn’t oversleep yourself?”

Renford poured his tale into his friend’s shocked ears.

“My hat!” said Harvey, when he had finished, “what on earth are we to do? They’ll starve.”

Renford nodded mournfully.

“Whatever made them go and lock the door?” he said.

He seemed to think the authorities should have given him due notice of such an action.

===Chinese===
「喂过了？」早餐碰面时哈维问。

「没有，喂不成。」

「到底为什么？你不是睡过头了吧？」

伦福德把事情原委灌进了朋友那对震惊的耳朵。

「我的老天！」他讲完后哈维说，「这可怎么办？它们会饿死的。」

伦福德悲苦地点点头。

「他们到底为什么要去把门锁上？」他说。

他似乎认为，当局采取这种行动之前，理应向他发布正式通知。

===Original===
“You’re sure they have locked it? It isn’t only stuck or something?”

“I lugged at the handle for hours. But you can go and see for yourself if you like.”

Harvey went, and, waiting till the coast was clear, attached himself to the handle with a prehensile grasp, and put his back into one strenuous tug. It was even as Renford had said. The door was locked beyond possibility of doubt.

Renford and he went over to school that morning with long faces and a general air of acute depression. It was perhaps fortunate for their purpose that they did, for had their appearance been normal it might not have attracted O’Hara’s attention. As it was, the Irishman, meeting them on the junior gravel, stopped and asked them what was wrong. Since the adventure in the vault, he had felt an interest in Renford and Harvey.

===Chinese===
「你肯定他们是上了锁？不会只是卡住了什么的？」

「那把手我拽了老半天。不信你自己去看看。」

哈维去了，等到四下无人，用一只灵长类才有的手劲攀住把手，铆足全身力气猛拉了一把。果如伦福德所言。门上了锁，毫无疑问。

那天早晨，两人拉长着脸去上学，周身笼着一股深重的愁云惨雾。于他们的大事而言，这倒是件幸事，因为倘若神色如常，也许引不起奥哈拉（O’Hara）的注意。而眼下，这位爱尔兰人在低年级砾石道上迎面碰上他们，便停下问出了什么事。自地窖一役之后，他对伦福德和哈维就一直怀有一份关注。

===Original===
The two told their story in alternate sentences like the Strophe and Antistrophe of a Greek chorus. (“Steichomuthics,” your Greek scholar calls it, I fancy. Ha, yes! Just so.)

“So ye can’t get in because they’ve locked the door, an’ ye don’t know what to do about it?” said O’Hara, at the conclusion of the narrative.

Renford and Harvey informed him in chorus that that *was* the state of the game up to present date.

“An’ ye want me to get them out for you?”

Neither had dared to hope that he would go so far as this. What they had looked for had been at the most a few thoughtful words of advice. That such a master-strategist as O’Hara should take up their cause was an unexampled piece of good luck.

“If you only would,” said Harvey.

“We should be most awfully obliged,” said Renford.

“Very well,” said O’Hara.

They thanked him profusely.

O’Hara replied that it would be a privilege.

He should be sorry, he said, to have anything happen to the ferrets.

Renford and Harvey went on into school feeling more cheerful. If the ferrets could be extracted from their present tight corner, O’Hara was the man to do it.

===Chinese===
两人你一句我一句地把事情讲了，活像希腊合唱队的正曲与反曲。（我猜，贵希腊学者管这叫「轮词对白」。哈，正是！正是。）

「这么说，门叫他们锁了，你们进不去，也不知道该怎么办？」故事讲完，奥哈拉说。

伦福德和哈维齐声告知他，截至当日，*确实*是这么个战局。

「你们是想让俺把它们弄出来？」

两人谁都没敢指望他肯出这么大的力。他们指望的至多不过几句周到的指教。像奥哈拉这样一位战略大师肯接下他们的事业，真是破天荒的好运。

「你要是肯的话。」哈维说。

「我们就感激不尽了。」伦福德说。

「好说。」奥哈拉说。

两人千恩万谢。

奥哈拉答道，这是他的荣幸。

他说，雪貂们要是有个三长两短，他也于心不忍。

伦福德和哈维继续往学校走去，心情开朗了些。雪貂们若能从眼下的绝境里捞出来，干这事的非奥哈拉莫属。

===Original===
O’Hara had not made his offer of assistance in any spirit of doubt. He was certain that he could do what he had promised. For it had not escaped his memory that this was a Tuesday﻿—in other words, a mathematics morning up to the quarter to eleven interval. That meant, as has been explained previously, that, while the rest of the school were in the form-rooms, he would be out in the passage, if he cared to be. There would be no witnesses to what he was going to do.

But, by that curious perversity of fate which is so often noticeable, Mr. Banks was in a peculiarly lamblike and long-suffering mood this morning. Actions for which O’Hara would on other days have been expelled from the room without hope of return, today were greeted with a mild “Don’t do that, please, O’Hara,” or even the ridiculously inadequate “O’Hara!” It was perfectly disheartening. O’Hara began to ask himself bitterly what was the use of ragging at all if this was how it was received. And the moments were flying, and his promise to Renford and Harvey still remained unfulfilled.

He prepared for fresh efforts.

So desperate was he, that he even resorted to crude methods like the throwing of paper balls and the dropping of books. And when your really scientific ragger sinks to this, he is nearing the end of his tether. O’Hara hated to be rude, but there seemed no help for it.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉主动请缨，心里没有半点迟疑。他确信自己能兑现承诺。因为他没有忘记：今天是星期二——换言之，到十点三刻课间为止是个数学早晨。如前文所释，这意味着全校都坐在教室里的时候，只要他乐意，他可以待在外面走廊上。他要干的事，不会有目击者。

可是，命运那常有的乖张脾气又发作了：班克斯先生（Mr. Banks）今天早晨的心绪温顺得出奇，隐忍得出奇。换作别的日子，奥哈拉干下这些勾当早被逐出教室、永不准回返，今天却只换来一句温和的「别那样，奥哈拉，劳驾」，甚至只是那句可笑地不相称的「奥哈拉！」这实在叫人灰心。奥哈拉开始愤愤自问：捣乱要是落得这般待遇，捣乱还有什么意思。而时间正在飞逝，他对伦福德和哈维的承诺仍未兑现。

他准备发起新一轮攻势。

他急得没辙，甚至使出了扔纸团、摔书本这类粗鄙手段。而一旦一位真正讲科学的捣乱分子沦落到这一步，说明他已智穷力竭。奥哈拉不愿失礼，可看来别无他法。

===Original===
The striking of a quarter past ten improved his chances. It had been privily agreed upon beforehand amongst the members of the class that at a quarter past ten everyone was to sneeze simultaneously. The noise startled Mr. Banks considerably. The angelic mood began to wear off. A man may be long-suffering, but he likes to draw the line somewhere.

“Another exhibition like that,” he said, sharply, “and the class stays in after school, O’Hara!”

“Sir?”

“Silence.”

“I said nothing, sir, really.”

“Boy, you made a catlike noise with your mouth.”

“What *sort* of noise, sir?”

===Chinese===
十点一刻的钟声改善了形势。班里诸位事先已秘密议定：十点一刻整，全体同时打喷嚏。那声响把班克斯先生吓了一大跳。天使般的心绪开始消退。人再能忍，也总得有个界限。

「再这样来一回，」他厉声道，「全班放学后留堂，奥哈拉！」

「先生？」

「肃静。」

「我什么也没说，先生，真的。」

「小子，你用嘴发出了一声猫一样的响动。」

「*哪一种*响动，先生？」

===Original===
The form waited breathlessly. This peculiarly insidious question had been invented for mathematical use by one Sandys, who had left at the end of the previous summer. It was but rarely that the master increased the gaiety of nations by answering the question in the manner desired.

Mr. Banks, off his guard, fell into the trap.

“A noise like this,” he said curtly, and to the delighted audience came the melodious sound of a “Mi-aou,” which put O’Hara’s effort completely in the shade, and would have challenged comparison with the war-cry of the stoutest mouser that ever trod a tile.

A storm of imitations arose from all parts of the room. Mr. Banks turned pink, and, going straight to the root of the disturbance, forthwith evicted O’Hara.

O’Hara left with the satisfying feeling that his duty had been done.

===Chinese===
全班屏息以待。这道格外阴险的问题，是上一届夏天结束时离校的一位桑迪斯（Sandys）为数学课发明的。教师能按众人期望的方式回答这个问题、为万国平添欢乐的情形，殊为罕见。

班克斯先生一时不察，落入了圈套。

「这样的响动。」他简短地说。随即便向欣喜若狂的听众送出一声婉转的「喵——嗷」，把奥哈拉的表演彻底比了下去，足可与历来踏过瓦片的最壮实的捕鼠勇士的战吼一较高下。

满屋四面八方顿时掀起模仿的风暴。班克斯先生涨红了脸，直捣骚乱之根源，当即把奥哈拉逐出了教室。

奥哈拉离开时心满意足，只觉责任已了。

===Original===
Mr. Banks’ room was at the top of the middle block. He ran softly down the stairs at his best pace. It was not likely that the master would come out into the passage to see if he was still there, but it might happen, and it would be best to run as few risks as possible.

He sprinted over to the junior block, raised the trapdoor, and jumped down. He knew where the ferrets had been placed, and had no difficulty in finding them. In another minute he was in the passage again, with the trapdoor bolted behind him.

He now asked himself﻿—what should he do with them? He must find a safe place, or his labours would have been in vain.

Behind the fives-court, he thought, would be the spot. Nobody ever went there. It meant a run of three hundred yards there and the same distance back, and there was more than a chance that he might be seen by one of the Powers. In which case he might find it rather hard to explain what he was doing in the middle of the grounds with a couple of ferrets in his possession when the hands of the clock pointed to twenty minutes to eleven.

But the odds were against his being seen. He risked it.

When the bell rang for the quarter to eleven interval the ferrets were in their new home, happily discussing a piece of meat﻿—Renford’s contribution, held over from the morning’s meal﻿—and O’Hara, looking as if he had never left the passage for an instant, was making his way through the departing mathematical class to apologise handsomely to Mr. Banks﻿—as was his invariable custom﻿—for his disgraceful behaviour during the morning’s lesson.

===Chinese===
班克斯先生的教室在中座顶层。他迈开全速，放轻脚步奔下楼梯。教师不大可能追到走廊上看他还在不在，但这种事难说，还是越少冒险越好。

他一口气冲刺到低年级楼，掀起活板门，跳了下去。他知道雪貂安放在哪儿，毫不费力就找到了它们。一分钟后他已回到走廊上，身后活板门闩得好好的。

他这时问自己——拿它们怎么办？必须找个稳妥的去处，否则辛劳全要白费。

墙手球场（fives-court）后面，他想，是个好去处。那地方从来没人去。这意味着来回各三百码的奔跑，而且他相当有可能撞见哪位权威人士。真撞见了，时钟指着十点四十分，他怀里揣着两只雪貂站在操场中央，这局面他可不容易解释。

但被撞见的概率终究不大。他赌了这一把。

十点三刻课间的铃声响起时，雪貂们已在新居里美滋滋地分食一块肉——伦福德的贡献，从早晨那顿里省下的——而奥哈拉呢，一副从未离开过走廊半步的模样，正穿过散场的数学班，去大大方方地向班克斯先生赔礼——这是他雷打不动的惯例——为的是他上午课堂上的可耻行径。

## VIII O’Hara on the Track / 八、奥哈拉循迹追查

===Original===
Tuesday mornings at Wrykyn were devoted—up to the quarter to eleven interval—to the study of mathematics. That is to say, instead of going to their form-rooms, the various forms visited the out-of-the-way nooks and dens at the top of the buildings where the mathematical masters were wont to lurk, and spent a pleasant two hours there playing round games or reading fiction under the desk. Mathematics being one of the few branches of school learning which are of any use in after life, nobody ever dreamed of doing any work in that direction, least of all O’Hara. It was a theory of O’Hara’s that he came to school to enjoy himself. To have done any work during a mathematics lesson would have struck him as a positive waste of time, especially as he was in Mr. Banks’ class. Mr. Banks was a master who simply cried out to be ragged. Everything he did and said seemed to invite the members of his class to amuse themselves, and they amused themselves accordingly. One of the advantages of being under him was that it was possible to predict to a nicety the moment when one would be sent out of the room. This was found very convenient.

O’Hara’s ally, Moriarty, was accustomed to take his mathematics with Mr. Morgan, whose room was directly opposite Mr. Banks’. With Mr. Morgan it was not quite so easy to date one’s expulsion from the room under ordinary circumstances, and in the normal wear and tear of the morning’s work, but there was one particular action which could always be relied upon to produce the desired result.

In one corner of the room stood a gigantic globe. The problem—how did it get into the room?—was one that had exercised the minds of many generations of Wrykinians. It was much too big to have come through the door. Some thought that the block had been built round it, others that it had been placed in the room in infancy, and had since grown. To refer the question to Mr. Morgan would, in six cases out of ten, mean instant departure from the room. But to make the event certain, it was necessary to grasp the globe firmly and spin it round on its axis. That always proved successful. Mr. Morgan would dash down from his dais, address the offender in spirited terms, and give him his marching orders at once and without further trouble.

Moriarty had arranged with O’Hara to set the globe rolling at ten sharp on this particular morning. O’Hara would then so arrange matters with Mr. Banks that they could meet in the passage at that hour, when O’Hara wished to impart to his friend his information concerning the League.

===Chinese===
在里金（Wrykyn），星期二上午——直到十点三刻课间休息为止——都奉献给了数学。也就是说，各个班级不去本班教室，而是去登门拜访教学楼顶上那些犄角旮旯——数学教员们惯常潜伏的巢穴——在那儿愉快地消磨两个钟头，或者玩团体游戏，或者在课桌底下看小说。数学既是学校功课中少数几门日后人生还用得着的学问，自然谁也做梦想不到要往那个方向用功，奥哈拉（O’Hara）更是想都不想。奥哈拉有一个理论：他上学校是来享乐的。要在数学课上做功课，在他看来简直是明摆着糟蹋光阴，何况他还在班克斯先生（Mr. Banks）的班上。班克斯先生是那种天生招人捉弄的教员。他的一举一动、一言一语，仿佛都在邀请班上同学自寻其乐，同学们也就当仁不让。在他门下读书的好处之一，是可以分毫不差地预料自己什么时候会被轰出教室。这一点大家觉得非常方便。

奥哈拉的盟友莫里亚蒂（Moriarty），上数学课照例归摩根先生（Mr. Morgan）管教，他的教室正对着班克斯先生的。在摩根先生手下，寻常情形下，单凭一上午功课的正常损耗，还不容易掐算出自己哪一刻被逐出教室，但有一个特定动作永远可靠，必定能收到预期的效果。

教室一角立着一个硕大无朋的地球仪。它是怎么进到屋里来的——这个难题耗费了一代又一代里金人的心血。它太大了，绝不可能从门里进来。有人认为整座教学楼是绕着它盖起来的，也有人认为它尚在幼年就被安置在屋里，后来才长大的。把这个问题拿去请教摩根先生，十次有六次意味着立刻离开教室。但若要此事万无一失，还须双手抓紧地球仪，推着它绕轴旋转。这一招向来灵验。摩根先生会从讲台上直冲下来，慷慨激昂地把肇事者训斥一番，随即毫不拖泥带水地勒令他滚蛋。

莫里亚蒂已经和奥哈拉约好，就在这天上午十点整把地球仪转起来。奥哈拉届时再和班克斯先生把事情安排妥当，好让两人能在那个钟点到走廊上碰头——奥哈拉有关于联盟（the League）的消息要透露给朋友。

===Original===
O’Hara promised to be at the trysting-place at the hour mentioned.

He did not think there would be any difficulty about it. The news that the League had been revived meant that there would be trouble in the very near future, and the prospect of trouble was meat and drink to the Irishman in O’Hara. Consequently he felt in particularly good form for mathematics (as he interpreted the word). He thought that he would have no difficulty whatever in keeping Mr. Banks bright and amused. The first step had to be to arouse in him an interest in life, to bring him into a frame of mind which would induce him to look severely rather than leniently on the next offender. This was effected as follows:—

It was Mr. Banks’ practice to set his class sums to work out, and, after some three-quarters of an hour had elapsed, to pass round the form what he called “solutions.” These were large sheets of paper, on which he had worked out each sum in his neat handwriting to a happy ending. When the head of the form, to whom they were passed first, had finished with them, he would make a slight tear in one corner, and, having done so, hand them on to his neighbour. The neighbour, before giving them to *his* neighbour, would also tear them slightly. In time they would return to their patentee and proprietor, and it was then that things became exciting.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉答应届时准时候在那个约会地点。

他觉得这事不会有什么难处。联盟死灰复燃的消息意味着不久的将来必出乱子，而乱子的前景，对奥哈拉骨子里那个爱尔兰人来说，便是家常便饭、玉液琼浆。因此他觉得自己这天上午的数学状态（按他对这个词的理解）特别好。他认为自己毫不费力便能让班克斯先生精神焕发、兴致盎然。第一步必须先唤起他对人生的兴致，让他进入一种心境，使他看待下一名肇事者时宁严勿宽。这一步是照下述方法完成的：——

班克斯先生的习惯是给全班布置算题，等大约三刻钟过去，再把他所谓的「解答」发给全班传阅。那是一张大张的纸，上面用他工整的字迹把每道题都推演到一个圆满的结局。班级头名——解答先传到他手里——看完之后，会在一角轻轻撕个小口，然后传给邻座。邻座在传给*他自己的*邻座之前，也要稍微撕上一撕。到头来解答物归原主，回到那位发明人兼所有人手里，这时候好戏就开场了。

===Original===
“Who tore these solutions like this?” asked Mr. Banks, in the repressed voice of one who is determined that he *will* be calm.

No answer. The tattered solutions waved in the air.

He turned to Harringay, the head of the form.

“Harringay, did you tear these solutions like this?”

Indignant negative from Harringay. What he had done had been to make the small tear in the top left-hand corner. If Mr. Banks had asked, “Did you make this small tear in the top left-hand corner of these solutions?” Harringay would have scorned to deny the impeachment. But to claim the credit for the whole work would, he felt, be an act of flat dishonesty, and an injustice to his gifted collaborateurs.

“No, sir,” said Harringay.

“Browne!”

“Yes, sir?”

“Did you tear these solutions in this manner?”

“No, sir.”

And so on through the form.

===Chinese===
「是谁把解答撕成这个样子的？」班克斯先生问道，嗓音压抑，十足是一个打定主意*非要*保持冷静的人。

无人应答。破破烂烂的解答在空中晃了晃。

他转向班级头名哈林盖（Harringay）。

「哈林盖，解答是你撕成这样的吗？」

哈林盖愤然否认。他做的只是左上角那个小口子。倘若班克斯先生问的是「解答左上角这个小口是你撕的吗」，哈林盖会不屑于矢口否认这项指控。可若把整件作品的功劳都据为己有，他觉得未免是彻头彻尾的不老实，也是对他那些才华横溢的合作者们不公。

「不是，先生。」哈林盖说。

「布朗（Browne）！」

「到，先生？」

「解答是你这样撕的吗？」

「不是，先生。」

如此这般，问遍全班。

===Original===
Then Harringay rose after the manner of the debater who is conscious that he is going to say the popular thing.

“Sir—” he began.

“Sit down, Harringay.”

Harringay gracefully waved aside the absurd command.

“Sir,” he said, “I think I am expressing the general consensus of opinion among my—ahem—fellow-students, when I say that this class sincerely regrets the unfortunate state the solutions have managed to get themselves into.”

“Hear, hear!” from a back bench.

“It is with—”

“Sit *down*, Harringay.”

“It is with heartfelt—”

“Harringay, if you do not sit down—”

“As your ludship pleases.” This sotto voce.

And Harringay resumed his seat amidst applause. O’Hara got up.

===Chinese===
接着哈林盖站了起来，那派头十足像一位深知自己即将说出深得人心之语的辩论家。

「先生——」他开口道。

「坐下，哈林盖。」

哈林盖优雅地一挥手，把这道荒谬的命令撇到一边。

「先生，」他说，「我想，当我说本班对解答们竟沦落到这般不幸的境地深表遗憾时，我表达的正是我——咳——各位同窗的普遍共识。」

「好哇，好哇！」后排长凳上有人喝彩。

「本着——」

「*坐下*，哈林盖。」

「本着由衷的——」

「哈林盖，你要是不坐下——」

「悉听堂上老爷尊便。」这句是压着嗓子说的。

哈林盖在掌声中落座。奥哈拉站了起来。

===Original===
“As me frind who has just sat down was about to observe—”

“Sit down, O’Hara. The whole form will remain after the class.”

“—the unfortunate state the solutions have managed to get thimsilves into is sincerely regretted by this class. Sir, I think I am ixprissing the general consensus of opinion among my fellow-students whin I say that it is with heartfelt sorrow—”

“O’Hara!”

“Yes, sir?”

“Leave the room instantly.”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
「正如俺这位刚坐下的朋友正要指出的——」

「坐下，奥哈拉。全班课后留堂。」

「——解答们竟沦落到这般不幸的境地，本班深表遗憾。先生，俺想，当俺说俺们怀着由衷的悲痛——时，俺表达的正是俺各位同学的一致意见——」

「奥哈拉！」

「怎么，先生？」

「立刻出去。」

「是，先生。」

===Original===
From the tower across the gravel came the melodious sound of chimes. The college clock was beginning to strike ten. He had scarcely got into the passage, and closed the door after him, when a roar as of a bereaved spirit rang through the room opposite, followed by a string of words, the only intelligible one being the noun-substantive “globe,” and the next moment the door opened and Moriarty came out. The last stroke of ten was just booming from the clock.

There was a large cupboard in the passage, the top of which made a very comfortable seat. They climbed on to this, and began to talk business.

“An’ what was it ye wanted to tell me?” inquired Moriarty.

O’Hara related what he had learned from Trevor that morning.

===Chinese===
楼塔那边越过砾石场地传来悦耳的钟乐。学院大钟开始敲十点。他刚进走廊、带上门，对面教室里便响起一声仿佛丧亲孤魂的咆哮，紧跟着一串字句，其中唯一听得清的是一个名词——「地球仪」，下一刻房门开处，莫里亚蒂走了出来。十点的最后一响正从大钟里嗡嗡传出。

走廊里有一口大橱柜，柜顶是个很舒服的座位。两人爬上去，开始谈正事。

「你到底要告诉俺什么呀？」莫里亚蒂问。

奥哈拉把当天早晨从特雷弗（Trevor）那里听来的消息讲了一遍。

===Original===
“An’ do ye know,” said Moriarty, when he had finished, “I half suspected, when I heard that Mill’s study had been ragged, that it might be the League that had done it. If ye remember, it was what they enjoyed doing, breaking up a man’s happy home. They did it frequently.”

“But I can’t understand them doing it to Trevor at all.”

“They’ll do it to anybody they choose till they’re caught at it.”

“If they are caught, there’ll be a row.”

“We must catch ’em,” said Moriarty. Like O’Hara, he revelled in the prospect of a disturbance. O’Hara and he were going up to Aldershot at the end of the term, to try and bring back the light and middleweight medals respectively. Moriarty had won the lightweight in the previous year, but, by reason of putting on a stone since the competition, was now no longer eligible for that class. O’Hara had not been up before, but the Wrykyn instructor, a good judge of pugilistic form, was of opinion that he ought to stand an excellent chance. As the prizefighter in *Rodney Stone* says, “When you get a good Irishman, you can’t better ’em, but they’re dreadful ’asty.” O’Hara was attending the gymnasium every night, in order to learn to curb his “dreadful ’astiness,” and acquire skill in its place.

===Chinese===
「你晓得不，」他讲完后莫里亚蒂说，「俺一听说米尔（Mill）的书房被人抄了，就猜到八成是联盟干的。你要是还记得，拆散人家的安乐窝正是他们最爱干的赏心乐事。他们常干这个。」

「可俺实在想不通他们干吗对特雷弗下手。」

「他们想对谁下手就对谁下手，除非被人当场逮住。」

「要是逮住了，可有一场好吵。」

「咱们非逮住他们不可。」莫里亚蒂说。他和奥哈拉一样，一想到要出乱子便心花怒放。学期结束时，他和奥哈拉要上奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）去，分别争夺轻量级和中量级的奖牌。莫里亚蒂去年拿过轻量级，但自从那次比赛以后体重添了一石（约六公斤），如今没资格再打那个级别了。奥哈拉以前没去过，但里金的拳击教官——一位品评拳艺的好手——认为他大有希望。正如《罗德尼·斯通》（*Rodney Stone*）里那位职业拳手所说：「碰上个好爱尔兰人，你再找不出更棒的了，可他们那火性子实在吓人。」奥哈拉如今夜夜去体育馆，为的是学会克制他那份「吓人的火性子」，代之以技巧。

===Original===
“I wonder if Trevor would be any good in a row,” said Moriarty.

“He can’t box,” said O’Hara, “but he’d go on till he was killed entirely. I say, I’m getting rather tired of sitting here, aren’t you? Let’s go to the other end of the passage and have some cricket.”

So, having unearthed a piece of wood from the debris at the top of the cupboard, and rolled a handkerchief into a ball, they adjourned.

===Chinese===
「不知道特雷弗打起架来顶不顶用。」莫里亚蒂说。

「他不会拳击，」奥哈拉说，「可他会一直打到死透为止。我说，坐在这儿我都坐腻了，你呢？咱们去走廊那头打板球吧。」

于是他们从柜顶的杂物里刨出一块木片，又把手绢卷成球，转移阵地去了。

===Original===
Recalling the stirring events of six years back, when the League had first been started, O’Hara remembered that the members of that enterprising society had been wont to hold meetings in a secluded spot, where it was unlikely that they would be disturbed. It seemed to him that the first thing he ought to do, if he wanted to make their nearer acquaintance now, was to find their present rendezvous. They must have one. They would never run the risk involved in holding mass-meetings in one another’s studies. On the last occasion, it had been an old quarry away out on the downs. This had been proved by the not-to-be-shaken testimony of three schoolhouse fags, who had wandered out one half-holiday with the unconcealed intention of finding the League’s place of meeting. Unfortunately for them, they *had* found it. They were going down the path that led to the quarry before-mentioned, when they were unexpectedly seized, blindfolded, and carried off. An impromptu court-martial was held—in whispers—and the three explorers forthwith received the most spirited “touching-up” they had ever experienced. Afterwards they were released, and returned to their house with their zeal for detection quite quenched. The episode had created a good deal of excitement in the school at the time.

===Chinese===
回想起六年前联盟初创时那段轰轰烈烈的往事，奥哈拉记起那个敢作敢为的社团的成员们惯于在一处幽僻地点集会，在那里不大可能被人搅扰。他觉得，自己眼下若想同他们近距离结识，头一件事就是找到他们如今的巢穴。他们必定有一个。他们绝不会冒险在彼此的书房里开大会。上一回，那地点是丘原深处的一座旧采石场。这是三名学校宿舍跑腿生证实的——他们的证词坚不可摧：某个半天假，他们抱着不加掩饰的意图出门蹓跶，就是要找到联盟的集会地点。不幸的是，他们还真找到了。他们正沿着通往前述采石场的小路往下走，冷不丁被人一把抓住、蒙上眼睛、架走了。一场临时军事法庭——压着嗓子开庭——随即开审，三位探险者立时挨了平生最带劲的一顿「修理」。随后他们被释放，回到宿舍，侦查的热情早已熄灭得干干净净。这件事当时在学校里引起过好一阵轰动。

===Original===
On three successive afternoons, O’Hara and Moriarty scoured the downs, and on each occasion they drew blank. On the fourth day, just before lockup, O’Hara, who had been to tea with Gregson, of Day’s, was going over to the gymnasium to keep a pugilistic appointment with Moriarty, when somebody ran swiftly past him in the direction of the boardinghouses. It was almost dark, for the days were still short, and he did not recognise the runner. But it puzzled him a little to think where he had sprung from. O’Hara was walking quite close to the wall of the College buildings, and the runner had passed between it and him. And he had not heard his footsteps. Then he understood, and his pulse quickened as he felt that he was on the track. Beneath the block was a large sort of cellar-basement. It was used as a storeroom for chairs, and was never opened except when prize-day or some similar event occurred, when the chairs were needed. It was supposed to be locked at other times, but never was. The door was just by the spot where he was standing. As he stood there, half-a-dozen other vague forms dashed past him in a knot. One of them almost brushed against him. For a moment he thought of stopping him, but decided not to. He could wait.

On the following afternoon he slipped down into the basement soon after school. It was as black as pitch in the cellar. He took up a position near the door.

It seemed hours before anything happened. He was, indeed, almost giving up the thing as a bad job, when a ray of light cut through the blackness in front of him, and somebody slipped through the door. The next moment, a second form appeared dimly, and then the light was shut off again.

O’Hara could hear them groping their way past him. He waited no longer. It is difficult to tell where sound comes from in the dark. He plunged forward at a venture. His hand, swinging round in a semicircle, met something which felt like a shoulder. He slipped his grasp down to the arm, and clutched it with all the force at his disposal.

===Chinese===
一连三个下午，奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂把丘原梳了个遍，回回空手而归。第四天，快到锁门时分，奥哈拉在戴氏宿舍（Day’s）的格雷格森（Gregson）那儿喝过茶，正要去体育馆赴与莫里亚蒂的拳击之约，忽然有人飞快地从他身边跑过，朝寄宿宿舍的方向去了。天几乎黑透了——白昼还短着呢——他没认出那个奔跑的人。可那人究竟是从哪儿冒出来的，倒叫他有点纳闷。奥哈拉走得离学院大楼的墙根很近，那人正是从墙和他之间穿过去的。而他竟没听见脚步声。随即他明白了，只觉脉搏一紧：他踩上线索了。楼底下有一个大地窖般的地下室，用作储藏椅子的库房，除了颁奖日或类似场合要用椅子，从不开启。照理其余时候都该上着锁，实际从来没锁过。那扇门就在他站的地方旁边。他正站在那儿，又有六条影影绰绰的人影结成一簇，从他身边飞掠而过，其中一个几乎擦着他的身子。有一瞬他想拦住那人，但还是决定不拦。他等得起。

第二天下午，一下课他就溜进了地下室。地窖里漆黑如沥青。他在门边占了个位置。

仿佛过了几个钟头也没动静。他确实都快要认栽放弃这桩买卖了，忽有一线亮光刺破他面前的黑暗，一个人影从门里闪了进来。紧接着第二条人影影影绰绰地出现，随后光又被关上了。

奥哈拉听得见他们摸索着从他身边走过。他不再等了。黑暗中很难辨清声音的来处。他瞅准一个方向冒险猛扑过去。他的手抡了半圈，碰上一个摸着像肩膀的东西。他顺势把手滑到那条臂膀上，使出全身的力气死死攥住。

## I The Fifteenth Place / 一、第十五个名额

===Original===
“Outside!”

“Don’t be an idiot, man. I bagged it first.”

“My dear chap, I’ve been waiting here a month.”

“When you fellows have *quite* finished rotting about in front of that bath don’t let *me* detain you.”

“Anybody seen that sponge?”

“Well, look here”—this in a tone of compromise—“let’s toss for it.”

“All right. Odd man out.”

All of which, being interpreted, meant that the first match of the Easter term had just come to an end, and that those of the team who, being day boys, changed over at the pavilion, instead of performing the operation at leisure and in comfort, as did the members of houses, were discussing the vital question—who was to have first bath?

===Chinese===
「有本事出去说！」

「别犯傻了，伙计。是我先占下的。」

「亲爱的老兄，我在这儿都等了一个月了。」

「等你们几位在那浴缸跟前*彻底*折腾够了，可千万别让*我*耽误了你们。」

「有人瞧见那块海绵了吗？」

「呃，这么着吧」——这口气透着妥协——「咱们掷硬币决定。」

「行。掷出不一样的出局。」

把这些话翻译过来，意思是：复活节学期的第一场比赛刚刚落幕，队里那几位走读生正在更衣亭换衣服——不像各宿舍的寄宿生那样可以从从容容、舒舒服服地在自家屋里办这件事——顺便讨论那个重大问题：谁先洗澡？

===Original===
The Field Sports Committee at Wrykyn—that is, at the school which stood some half-mile outside that town and took its name from it—were not lavish in their expenditure as regarded the changing accommodation in the pavilion. Letters appeared in every second number of the *Wrykinian*, some short, others long, some from members of the school, others from Old Boys, all protesting against the condition of the first, second, and third fifteen dressing-rooms. “Indignant” would inquire acidly, in half a page of small type, if the editor happened to be aware that there was no hairbrush in the second room, and only half a comb. “Disgusted O. W.” would remark that when he came down with the Wandering Zephyrs to play against the third fifteen, the water supply had suddenly and mysteriously failed, and the W.Z.’s had been obliged to go home as they were, in a state of primeval grime, and he thought that this was “a very bad thing in a school of over six hundred boys,” though what the number of boys had to do with the fact that there was no water he omitted to explain. The editor would express his regret in brackets, and things would go on as before.

===Chinese===
里金（Wrykyn）的运动委员会——也就是坐落在里金镇外约半英里、并从镇上得名的那所学校——在更衣亭更衣设施上的开销并不阔绰。《里金人》（*Wrykinian*）每隔一期便登一封来信，有长有短，有在校生写的，也有老校友（Old Boys）写的，全都在抗议一队、二队、三队更衣室的状况。「愤慨者」会用半页小号铅字尖刻地质问，编辑大人是否碰巧知道二队更衣室里没有发刷、梳子也只剩半把。「恶心的老里金人」则会写道：他随流浪和风队（Wandering Zephyrs）来和三队比赛时，供水突然莫名其妙地断了，和风队诸位只好带着太古洪荒般的积垢原样回家，他认为「对一所六百多名学生的学校来说，这可是件非常糟糕的事」——至于学生人数和没水这件事有什么相干，他略去不谈。编辑会在括号里表示遗憾，然后一切照旧。

===Original===
There was only one bath in the first fifteen room, and there were on the present occasion six claimants to it. And each claimant was of the fixed opinion that, whatever happened subsequently, he was going to have it first. Finally, on the suggestion of Otway, who had reduced tossing to a fine art, a mystic game of Tommy Dodd was played. Otway having triumphantly obtained first innings, the conversation reverted to the subject of the match.

The Easter term always opened with a scratch game against a mixed team of masters and old boys, and the school usually won without any great exertion. On this occasion the match had been rather more even than the average, and the team had only just pulled the thing off by a couple of tries to a goal. Otway expressed an opinion that the school had played badly.

===Chinese===
一队更衣室里只有一个浴缸，眼下对它主张权利的有六位。每位主张者都抱着一个坚定不移的看法：无论随后发生什么，自己都要头一个洗。最后，依照把掷硬币琢磨成了一门精妙艺术的奥特韦（Otway）的提议，大家玩了一局神秘的汤米·多德（Tommy Dodd）。奥特韦凯旋般夺得了头一个下水的资格，谈话便回到比赛这个题目上来了。

复活节学期总以一场混编练习赛开场——对手是教员与老校友拼凑的混合队，学校一方通常不费什么力气便告捷。这一场却比寻常势均力敌得多，校队只以两次达阵对一次破门险险拿下。奥特韦发表意见说，学校队打得很糟。

===Original===
“Why on earth don’t you forwards let the ball out occasionally?” he asked. Otway was one of the first fifteen halves.

“They were so jolly heavy in the scrum,” said Maurice, one of the forwards. “And when we did let it out, the outsides nearly always mucked it.”

“Well, it wasn’t the halves’ fault. We always got it out to the centres.”

“It wasn’t the centres,” put in Robinson. “They played awfully well. Trevor was ripping.”

“Trevor always is,” said Otway; “I should think he’s about the best captain we’ve had here for a long time. He’s certainly one of the best centres.”

“Best there’s been since Rivers-Jones,” said Clephane.

Rivers-Jones was one of those players who mark an epoch. He had been in the team fifteen years ago, and had left Wrykyn to captain Cambridge and play three years in succession for Wales. The school regarded the standard set by him as one that did not admit of comparison. However good a Wrykyn centre three-quarter might be, the most he could hope to be considered was “the best *since* Rivers-Jones.” “Since” Rivers-Jones, however, covered fifteen years, and to be looked on as the best centre the school could boast of during that time, meant something. For Wrykyn knew how to play football.

===Chinese===
「你们这帮前锋到底为什么不偶尔把球传出来？」他问道。奥特韦是一队的前卫之一。

「并列争球里他们壮得要命，」前锋之一的莫里斯（Maurice）说，「再说我们好不容易传出来，外围的人又十有八九接砸了。」

「反正不是前卫的错。我们回回都把球送到了中锋手里。」

「也不是中锋，」罗宾逊（Robinson）插嘴道，「他们打得棒极了。特雷弗（Trevor）简直神了。」

「特雷弗一向神，」奥特韦说，「我看他大概是很久以来我们这儿最好的队长了。他无疑是数一数二的中锋。」

「自里弗斯-琼斯（Rivers-Jones）以来最棒的。」克莱芬（Clephane）说。

里弗斯-琼斯是那种开宗立纪的球员。十五年前他在校队效力，离开里金后又执掌剑桥校队，连续三年代表威尔士出战。学校把他立下的标准视为不容比较之物。一个里金的中路三分卫无论多么出色，所能指望的最高评价也不过是「里弗斯-琼斯*以来*最好的」。「以来」固然涵盖了十五年，可被看作这十五年里学校拿得出手的最佳中锋，那也是有分量的。因为里金人是懂橄榄球的。

===Original===
Since it had been decided thus that the faults in the school attack did not lie with the halves, forwards, or centres, it was more or less evident that they must be attributable to the wings. And the search for the weak spot was even further narrowed down by the general verdict that Clowes, on the left wing, had played well. With a beautiful unanimity the six occupants of the first fifteen room came to the conclusion that the man who had let the team down that day had been the man on the right—Rand-Brown, to wit, of Seymour’s.

“I’ll bet he doesn’t stay in the first long,” said Clephane, who was now in the bath, *vice* Otway, retired. “I suppose they had to try him, as he was the senior wing three-quarter of the second, but he’s no earthly good.”

“He only got into the second because he’s big,” was Robinson’s opinion. “A man who’s big and strong can always get his second colours.”

“Even if he’s a funk, like Rand-Brown,” said Clephane. “Did any of you chaps notice the way he let Paget through that time he scored for them? He simply didn’t attempt to tackle him. He could have brought him down like a shot if he’d only gone for him. Paget was running straight along the touchline, and hadn’t any room to dodge. I know Trevor was jolly sick about it. And then he let him through once before in just the same way in the first half, only Trevor got round and stopped him. He was rank.”

“Missed every other pass, too,” said Otway.

Clephane summed up.

“He was rank,” he said again. “Trevor won’t keep him in the team long.”

“I wish Paget hadn’t left,” said Otway, referring to the wing three-quarter who, by leaving unexpectedly at the end of the Christmas term, had let Rand-Brown into the team. His loss was likely to be felt. Up till Christmas Wrykyn had done well, and Paget had been their scoring man. Rand-Brown had occupied a similar position in the second fifteen. He was big and speedy, and in second fifteen matches these qualities make up for a great deal. If a man scores one or two tries in nearly every match, people are inclined to overlook in him such failings as timidity and clumsiness. It is only when he comes to be tried in football of a higher class that he is seen through. In the second fifteen the fact that Rand-Brown was afraid to tackle his man had almost escaped notice. But the habit would not do in first fifteen circles.

“All the same,” said Clephane, pursuing his subject, “if they don’t play him, I don’t see who they’re going to get. He’s the best of the second three-quarters, as far as I can see.”

===Chinese===
既然学校进攻的毛病既不在前卫、前锋，也不在中锋身上，那么多半只能归咎于翼锋了。又由于大家一致判定左路的克劳斯（Clowes）打得不错，薄弱点的搜寻范围进一步收窄。一队更衣室里的六个人以漂亮的全体一致得出结论：那天拖累全队的，是右路那位——也就是西摩宿舍（Seymour’s）的兰德-布朗（Rand-Brown）。

「我打赌他在一队待不长，」克莱芬说——此刻他已接替卸任的奥特韦，进了浴缸。「我猜他们不得不试用他一下，谁让他是二队资历最老的翼锋呢，可他实在一无是处。」

「他不过是靠块头大才进的二队，」这是罗宾逊的高见，「又高又壮的人总能混上二队队色。」

「哪怕他是个怂包，比如兰德-布朗，」克莱芬说，「你们哪位注意到佩吉特（Paget）替他们达阵那次他是怎么放人过去的没有？他压根儿就没打算擒抱。他只要扑上去，一眨眼就能把人放倒。佩吉特当时贴着边线直线跑，根本没有闪躲的余地。我知道特雷弗为这事气得够呛。上半场他还用一模一样的方式放过去一回，幸亏特雷弗绕过去把人拦下了。他差劲透了。」

「接球还隔一个漏一个。」奥特韦说。

克莱芬作了总结。

「他就是差劲透了，」他又说一遍，「特雷弗不会让他久留队中的。」

「真希望佩吉特没走，」奥特韦说——他指的是那位在圣诞学期末不辞而别、从而把兰德-布朗放进校队的翼锋。他的流失怕是要让人尝到苦头。到圣诞为止里金战绩不俗，佩吉特正是他们的得分主力。兰德-布朗在二队里占着一个相仿的位置。他人高马大、跑得又快，而在二队的比赛里，这两样品质足以抵消许多短处。一个人要是几乎场场拿一两次达阵，大家便倾向于不去计较他胆小、笨拙之类的毛病。只有等他接受更高层次的橄榄球检验时，才会被一眼看穿。兰德-布朗不敢擒抱对手这个事实，在二队几乎无人留意。但这套做派在一队的圈子里可行不通。

「话虽如此，」克莱芬不肯放下他的题目，「要是不用他，我倒看不出他们还能用谁。依我看，他是二队三分卫里最好的。」

===Original===
It was this very problem that was puzzling Trevor, as he walked off the field with Paget and Clowes, when they had got into their blazers after the match. Clowes was in the same house as Trevor—Donaldson’s—and Paget was staying there, too. He had been head of Donaldson’s up to Christmas.

“It strikes me,” said Paget, “the school haven’t got over the holidays yet. I never saw such a lot of slackers. You ought to have taken thirty points off the sort of team you had against you today.”

“Have you ever known the school play well on the second day of term?” asked Clowes. “The forwards always play as if the whole thing bored them to death.”

“It wasn’t the forwards that mattered so much,” said Trevor. “They’ll shake down all right after a few matches. A little running and passing will put them right.”

“Let’s hope so,” Paget observed, “or we might as well scratch to Ripton at once. There’s a jolly sight too much of the mince-pie and Christmas pudding about their play at present.” There was a pause. Then Paget brought out the question towards which he had been moving all the time.

===Chinese===
而特雷弗此刻为之伤脑筋的，正是这个难题。赛后换上运动夹克，他和佩吉特、克劳斯一起走出球场。克劳斯和特雷弗同宿舍——唐纳森宿舍（Donaldson’s）——佩吉特眼下也寄住那儿。到圣诞为止，他一直是唐纳森宿舍的舍长。

「要我说，」佩吉特道，「全校还没从假期里缓过劲儿来呢。我从没见过这么一帮懒骨头。今天对面那种水平的队，你们本该拿下三十分的。」

「你可曾见过全校在开学第二天打得好？」克劳斯问，「前锋们打起来永远一副无聊到死的样子。」

「倒不全是前锋的问题，」特雷弗说，「再打几场他们就磨合顺了。多跑动多传球，自会调整过来。」

「但愿如此，」佩吉特说，「不然咱们干脆立刻向里普顿（Ripton）认输算了。眼下这球风里，肉馅饼和圣诞布丁的气味实在太重了。」一阵沉默。随后佩吉特抛出了他一直绕着圈子逼近的那个问题。

===Original===
“What do you think of Rand-Brown?” he asked.

It was pretty clear by the way he spoke what he thought of that player himself, but in discussing with a football captain the capabilities of the various members of his team, it is best to avoid a too positive statement one way or the other before one has heard his views on the subject. And Paget was one of those people who like to know the opinions of others before committing themselves.

Clowes, on the other hand, was in the habit of forming his views on his own account, and expressing them. If people agreed with them, well and good: it afforded strong presumptive evidence of their sanity. If they disagreed, it was unfortunate, but he was not going to alter his opinions for that, unless convinced at great length that they were unsound. He summed things up, and gave you the result. You could take it or leave it, as you preferred.

“I thought he was bad,” said Clowes.

“Bad!” exclaimed Trevor, “he was a disgrace. One can understand a chap having his off-days at any game, but one doesn’t expect a man in the Wrykyn first to funk. He mucked five out of every six passes I gave him, too, and the ball wasn’t a bit slippery. Still, I shouldn’t mind that so much if he had only gone for his man properly. It isn’t being out of practice that makes you funk. And even when he did have a try at you, Paget, he always went high.”

“That,” said Clowes thoughtfully, “would seem to show that he was game.”

===Chinese===
「你觉得兰德-布朗怎么样？」他问。

从他的口气听来，他本人对那位球员的看法已相当了然。不过，同一位橄榄球队长讨论他队中诸位的能耐时，最好先听听队长本人的意见，再亮出或褒或贬的论断。佩吉特正是那种喜欢先摸清别人意见、再摊牌自己立场的人。

克劳斯则相反，他的习惯是自个儿拿主意，并且说出来。要是别人同意，再好不过：这为对方神志清明提供了有力的推定证据。要是不同意，那很不幸，但他绝不会因此更改观点，除非被人花上九牛二虎之力说服那观点站不住脚。他把事情归纳一番，把结论端给你。听不听随你。

「我认为他打得很糟。」克劳斯说。

「糟！」特雷弗叫道，「简直是耻辱。谁玩什么游戏都有状态不佳的日子，这可以理解，可谁也别指望里金一队的人怯阵。我传给他的球，他六球里接砸五球，那球又一点儿不滑。不过，要是他扑人的时候够干脆，接砸球我倒也不那么计较。手生可不会叫人怯阵。而且就算他真朝你扑过去，佩吉特，他回回都扑得老高。」

「那个，」克劳斯若有所思地说，「倒似乎表明他有股子胆气。」

===Original===
Nobody so much as smiled. Nobody ever did smile at Clowes’ essays in wit, perhaps because of the solemn, almost sad, tone of voice in which he delivered them. He was tall and dark and thin, and had a pensive eye, which encouraged the more soulful of his female relatives to entertain hopes that he would some day take orders.

“Well,” said Paget, relieved at finding that he did not stand alone in his views on Rand-Brown’s performance, “I must say I thought he was awfully bad myself.”

“I shall try somebody else next match,” said Trevor. “It’ll be rather hard, though. The man one would naturally put in, Bryce, left at Christmas, worse luck.”

Bryce was the other wing three-quarter of the second fifteen.

“Isn’t there anybody in the third?” asked Paget.

“Barry,” said Clowes briefly.

“Clowes thinks Barry’s good,” explained Trevor.

“He *is* good,” said Clowes. “I admit he’s small, but he can tackle.”

===Chinese===
谁都没有笑。谁也从不在克劳斯施展机智时发笑，这或许是因为他发表高见时那副郑重其事、近乎哀伤的腔调。他个子高、皮肤黑、身板瘦，一双眼睛多思善感，惹得他那些女眷里比较多情善感的几位满怀希望，指望他有朝一日接受圣职。

「唉，」佩吉特说——发现自己对兰德-布朗表现的评价并非孤家寡人，他松了口气——「不瞒你说，我自己也觉得他糟透了。」

「下一场我换个人试试，」特雷弗说，「不过挺难办。顺理成章该顶上的人——布莱斯（Bryce）——圣诞走了，真倒霉。」

布莱斯是二队的另一名翼锋。

「三队就没有人了吗？」佩吉特问。

「巴里（Barry）。」克劳斯言简意赅。

「克劳斯觉得巴里不错。」特雷弗解释道。

「他*就是*不错，」克劳斯说，「我承认他个头小，可他能擒抱。」

===Original===
“The question is, would he be any good in the first? A chap might do jolly well for the third, and still not be worth trying for the first.”

“I don’t remember much about Barry,” said Paget, “except being collared by him when we played Seymour’s last year in the final. I certainly came away with a sort of impression that he could tackle. I thought he marked me jolly well.”

“There you are, then,” said Clowes. “A year ago Barry could tackle Paget. There’s no reason for supposing that he’s fallen off since then. We’ve seen that Rand-Brown *can’t* tackle Paget. Ergo, Barry is better worth playing for the team than Rand-Brown. Q.E.D.”

“All right, then,” replied Trevor. “There can’t be any harm in trying him. We’ll have another scratch game on Thursday. Will you be here then, Paget?”

“Oh, yes. I’m stopping till Saturday.”

“Good man. Then we shall be able to see how he does against you. I wish you hadn’t left, though, by Jove. We should have had Ripton on toast, the same as last term.”

===Chinese===
「问题是，他上了一队还行不行？一个人在三队可能打得很欢，到一队还是不值一试。」

「关于巴里我记得的不多，」佩吉特说，「只记得去年决赛我们对西摩宿舍时被他擒抱过一回。我下场时确实带着一种印象：这人能擒抱。我觉得他盯我盯得够严实。」

「这就成了，」克劳斯说，「一年前巴里能擒抱佩吉特。没有理由认为他此后退步了。我们已经看到兰德-布朗*不能*擒抱佩吉特。所以，巴里比兰德-布朗更值得代表球队出场。证毕。」

「那好，」特雷弗答道，「试试他总没坏处。星期四我们再打一场练习赛。到时候你还在吗，佩吉特？」

「哦，在。我待到星期六。」

「好样的。那我们正好看看他对付你表现如何。不过，天哪，真希望你没走。不然咱们本可以像上学期那样，把里普顿当盘中餐。」

===Original===
Wrykyn played five schools, but six school matches. The school that they played twice in a season was Ripton. To win one Ripton match meant that, however many losses it might have sustained in the other matches, the school had had, at any rate, a passable season. To win two Ripton matches in the same year was almost unheard of. This year there had seemed every likelihood of it. The match before Christmas on the Ripton ground had resulted in a win for Wrykyn by two goals and a try to a try. But the calculations of the school had been upset by the sudden departure of Paget at the end of term, and also of Bryce, who had hitherto been regarded as his understudy. And in the first Ripton match the two goals had both been scored by Paget, and both had been brilliant bits of individual play, which a lesser man could not have carried through.

The conclusion, therefore, at which the school reluctantly arrived, was that their chances of winning the second match could not be judged by their previous success. They would have to approach the Easter term fixture from another—a non-Paget—standpoint. In these circumstances it became a serious problem: who was to get the fifteenth place? Whoever played in Paget’s stead against Ripton would be certain, if the match were won, to receive his colours. Who, then, would fill the vacancy?

“Rand-Brown, of course,” said the crowd.

But the experts, as we have shown, were of a different opinion.

===Chinese===
里金与五所学校交手，校际比赛却打六场。一个赛季打两场的那所学校便是里普顿。赢下一场里普顿，意味着不管其余比赛输了多少，这个赛季好歹算过得去。同年赢下两场里普顿，则几乎是闻所未闻。今年偏偏样样迹象都显示有此可能。圣诞前在里普顿球场的那场比赛，里金以两记破门加一次达阵对一次达阵告捷。可学校的如意算盘被学期末佩吉特的突然离校搅乱了，一同离校的还有布莱斯——他此前一直被视为佩吉特的替补。而第一场里普顿赛中的两记破门都是佩吉特拿下的，且两次都是个人表演的华彩片段，换个逊一筹的人是打不出来的。

因此，全校勉强得出的结论是：第二场取胜的把握，不能拿先前的成功来衡量。他们必须换一个角度——一个没有佩吉特的角度——来看待复活节学期的这场赛事。于是乎，一个严肃的问题浮出水面：第十五个名额归谁？谁要是顶替佩吉特出战里普顿，一旦获胜，必定能拿到校队队色。那么，谁来填补这个空缺呢？

「当然是兰德-布朗。」众人说。

但正如前文所示，行家们的看法另是一样。

## XX The Finding of the Bat / 二十、球棒重见天日

===Original===
Trevor waited till the headmaster had gone back to his library, gave him five minutes to settle down, and then went in.

The headmaster looked up inquiringly.

“My essay, sir,” said Trevor.

“Ah, yes. I had forgotten.”

Trevor opened the notebook and began to read what he had written. He finished the paragraph which owed its insertion to Clowes, and raced hurriedly on to the next. To his surprise the flippancy passed unnoticed, at any rate, verbally. As a rule the headmaster preferred that quotations from back numbers of *Punch* should be kept out of the prefects’ English Essays. And he generally said as much. But today he seemed strangely preoccupied. A split infinitive in paragraph five, which at other times would have made him sit up in his chair stiff with horror, elicited no remark. The same immunity was accorded to the insertion (inspired by Clowes, as usual) of a popular catch phrase in the last few lines. Trevor finished with the feeling that luck had favoured him nobly.

===Chinese===
特雷弗等校长回了书房，又给他五分钟安顿，然后走了进去。

校长抬起头来，眼中带着询问。

「我的作文，先生。」特雷弗说。

「啊，对了。我都忘了。」

特雷弗打开笔记本，开始念他写的东西。他念完那段托克劳斯之福才得以入选的文字，急急赶往下一段。出乎意料，那些轻浮话竟无人置喙——至少口头上如此。按惯例，校长是不主张级长的英语作文里出现《笨拙》（*Punch*）过刊引文的，而且一般都会照此明说。可今天他显得莫名地心事重重。第五段里有个分裂不定式，若在平时，准会吓得他直挺挺地从椅子上弹起来，此刻却没招来一句评语。结尾几行里（照例又是克劳斯的主意）塞进的一句流行口头禅，也同样得到了赦免。特雷弗念完，只觉得运气帮了大忙。

===Original===
“Yes,” said the headmaster, seemingly roused by the silence following on the conclusion of the essay. “Yes.” Then, after a long pause, “Yes,” again.

Trevor said nothing, but waited for further comment.

“Yes,” said the headmaster once more, “I think that is a very fair essay. Very fair. It wants a little more—er—not quite so much—*um*—yes.”

Trevor made a note in his mind to effect these improvements in future essays, and was getting up, when the headmaster stopped him.

“Don’t go, Trevor. I wish to speak to you.”

===Chinese===
「唔。」校长说，似乎是作文念完后的沉默把他惊醒的。「唔。」接着，停了好一阵，又是一声「唔」。

特雷弗不作声，等着下文。

「唔，」校长又说了一遍，「我想这是一篇很不错的作文。很不错。就是还需要稍微多一点点——呃——又不要那么多——*唔*——就这样。」

特雷弗在心里记下，往后的作文要照此改进，正要起身，校长叫住了他。

「别走，特雷弗。我有话跟你说。」

===Original===
Trevor’s first thought was, perhaps naturally, that the bat was going to be brought into discussion. He was wondering helplessly how he was going to keep O’Hara and his midnight exploit out of the conversation, when the headmaster resumed. “An unpleasant thing has happened, Trevor—”

“Now we’re coming to it,” thought Trevor.

“It appears, Trevor, that a considerable amount of smoking has been going on in the school.”

Trevor breathed freely once more. It was only going to be a mere conventional smoking row after all. He listened with more enjoyment as the headmaster, having stopped to turn down the wick of the reading-lamp which stood on the table at his side, and which had begun, appropriately enough, to smoke, resumed his discourse.

“Mr. Dexter—”

Of course, thought Trevor. If there ever was a row in the school, Dexter was bound to be at the bottom of it.

“Mr. Dexter has just been in to see me. He reported six boys. He discovered them in the vault beneath the junior block. Two of them were boys in your house.”

Trevor murmured something wordless, to show that the story interested him.

===Chinese===
特雷弗头一个念头——也许很自然——是那根球棒要被搬到台面上来了。他正一筹莫展地琢磨着，怎样才能把奥哈拉和他那桩午夜壮举从谈话里撇开，校长又开口了。「出了一件不愉快的事，特雷弗——」

「这就来了。」特雷弗想。

「看来，特雷弗，学校里抽烟的勾当委实不少。」

特雷弗又畅快地呼吸起来。到头来不过是一场例行公事的吸烟风波罢了。他听得更有兴致了。校长停下话头，把他手边桌上的读书灯捻暗了些——那盏灯不早不晚，恰好也开始冒烟了——随后接着讲下去。

「德克斯特先生（Mr. Dexter）——」

果然，特雷弗想。学校里但凡出点乱子，德克斯特必定脱不了干系。

「德克斯特先生刚才来过我这儿。他上报了六个学生，是在低年级教学楼底下的地窖里逮住的。其中两个是你们宿舍的。」

特雷弗咕哝了一句不成话的什么，表示这个故事让他很感兴趣。

===Original===
“You knew nothing of this, of course—”

“No, sir.”

“No. Of course not. It is difficult for the head of a house to know all that goes on in that house.”

Was this his beastly sarcasm? Trevor asked himself. But he came to the conclusion that it was not. After all, the head of a house is only human. He cannot be expected to keep an eye on the private life of every member of his house.

“This must be stopped, Trevor. There is no saying how widespread the practice has become or may become. What I want you to do is to go straight back to your house and begin a complete search of the studies.”

“Tonight, sir?” It seemed too late for such amusement.

“Tonight. But before you go to your house, call at Mr. Seymour’s, and tell Milton I should like to see him. And, Trevor.”

“Yes, sir?”

“You will understand that I am leaving this matter to you to be dealt with by you. I shall not require you to make any report to me. But if you should find tobacco in any boy’s room, you must punish him well, Trevor. Punish him well.”

===Chinese===
「这事你自然毫不知情——」

「不知道，先生。」

「嗯，当然。一个宿舍的舍长，要想知道宿舍里发生的一切，也实在为难。」

这是不是他那套可恶的挖苦？特雷弗自问。但他断定不是。舍长到底也是凡人，总不能指望他盯住宿舍里每一个人的私生活。

「此事必须刹住，特雷弗。这风气已经蔓延到什么地步、还会蔓延到什么地步，谁也说不好。我要你做的，是径直回宿舍去，把各间书房彻底搜一遍。」

「今晚吗，先生？」干这种乐子事，这钟点似乎太晚了。

「今晚。不过回宿舍之前，先到西摩宿舍跑一趟，告诉弥尔顿（Milton）我要见他。还有，特雷弗。」

「什么事，先生？」

「你要明白，这件事我交给你，就由你全权处理。我不要求你向我汇报。不过，你要是在哪个学生房里搜出烟草，务必好好惩治他，特雷弗。好好惩治。」

===Original===
This meant that the culprit must be “touched up” before the house assembled in the dining-room. Such an event did not often occur. The last occasion had been in Paget’s first term as head of Donaldson’s, when two of the senior day-room had been discovered attempting to revive the ancient and dishonourable custom of bullying. This time, Trevor foresaw, would set up a record in all probability. There might be any number of devotees of the weed, and he meant to carry out his instructions to the full, and make the criminals more unhappy than they had been since the day of their first cigar. Trevor hated the habit of smoking at school. He was so intensely keen on the success of the house and the school at games, that anything which tended to damage the wind and eye filled him with loathing. That anybody should dare to smoke in a house which was going to play in the final for the House Football Cup made him rage internally, and he proposed to make things bad and unrestful for such.

To smoke at school is to insult the divine weed. When you are obliged to smoke in odd corners, fearing every moment that you will be discovered, the whole meaning, poetry, romance of a pipe vanishes, and you become like those lost beings who smoke when they are running to catch trains. The boy who smokes at school is bound to come to a bad end. He will degenerate gradually into a person that plays dominoes in the smoking-rooms of A.B.C. shops with friends who wear bowler hats and frock coats.

Much of this philosophy Trevor expounded to Clowes in energetic language when he returned to Donaldson’s after calling at Seymour’s to deliver the message for Milton.

Clowes became quite animated at the prospect of a real row.

===Chinese===
这就是说，肇事者得赶在全宿舍到饭厅集合之前先挨一顿「修理」。这种事不常有。上一回还是佩吉特头一个学期当唐纳森舍长的时候，高年级活动室的两个学生被发现在企图复活那古老而不光彩的欺凌旧例。这一回，特雷弗预见，十有八九要创下纪录。抽这号的没准儿大有人在，而他打算把命令执行到十分，让这些罪人过得比他们抽头一支雪茄那天以来的任何时候都不痛快。特雷弗深恶学生在校抽烟。他一心盼着宿舍和学校在各项比赛里夺魁，凡是有损肺活量和眼力的勾当，都叫他由衷憎恶。宿舍眼看就要打进宿舍橄榄球杯的决赛，竟有人胆敢抽烟，这让他怒火中烧，他决意叫这号人的日子过得又糟又不得安生。

在学校里抽烟，是对神圣烟草原的亵渎。当你不得不躲在犄角旮旯里抽，时时刻刻提心吊胆怕被发现，一斗烟的全部意义、诗意和罗曼史便荡然无存，你就沦落成那种赶火车还叼着烟的迷失灵魂了。在学校抽烟的少年注定没有好下场。他会渐渐堕落成那样一种人：在 A.B.C. 茶馆的吸烟室里，跟一帮戴圆顶礼帽、穿长礼服的朋友打多米诺骨牌。

在去过西摩宿舍、给弥尔顿捎了口信、回到唐纳森宿舍之后，特雷弗用铿锵有力的语言向克劳斯宣讲了这套高论的大半。

一想到真要出一场乱子，克劳斯顿时来了精神。

===Original===
“We shall be able to see the skeletons in their cupboards,” he observed. “Every man has a skeleton in his cupboard, which follows him about wherever he goes. Which study shall we go to first?”

“We?” said Trevor.

“We,” repeated Clowes firmly. “I am not going to be left out of this jaunt. I need bracing up—I’m not strong, you know—and this is just the thing to do it. Besides, you’ll want a bodyguard of some sort, in case the infuriated occupant turns and rends you.”

“I don’t see what there is to enjoy in the business,” said Trevor, gloomily. “Personally, I bar this kind of thing. By the time we’ve finished, there won’t be a chap in the house I’m on speaking terms with.”

“Except me, dearest,” said Clowes. “I will never desert you. It’s of no use asking me, for I will never do it. Mr. Micawber has his faults, but I will *never* desert Mr. Micawber.”

“You can come if you like,” said Trevor; “we’ll take the studies in order. I suppose we needn’t look up the prefects?”

“A prefect is above suspicion. Scratch the prefects.”

“That brings us to Dixon.”

===Chinese===
「这下咱们可以看看他们柜子里的骷髅了，」他发表见解，「人人柜里都有一具骷髅，走到哪儿跟到哪儿。先上哪间书房？」

「咱们？」特雷弗说。

「咱们，」克劳斯斩钉截铁地重复道，「这趟热闹我可不能落下。我需要提提神——你知道，我身子骨不强——这事儿正合适。再说，你总得有个贴身保镖，免得被惹急了的主人回身把你撕了。」

「我看不出这差事有什么可乐的，」特雷弗阴郁地说，「我个人最烦这种事。等咱们办完，宿舍里就没有一个我还搭腔的人了。」

「除了我，亲爱的，」克劳斯说，「我永远不会抛弃你。求也没用，我绝不干这事。米考伯先生（Mr. Micawber）纵有千般不是，我*永远*不会抛弃米考伯先生。」

「你愿意来就来，」特雷弗说，「咱们按顺序查书房。级长们的就不用查了吧？」

「级长超然于嫌疑之外。级长，划掉。」

「那就轮到狄克逊（Dixon）了。」

===Original===
Dixon was a stout youth with spectacles, who was popularly supposed to do twenty-two hours’ work a day. It was believed that he put in two hours sleep from eleven to one, and then got up and worked in his study till breakfast.

He was working when Clowes and Trevor came in. He dived head foremost into a huge Liddell and Scott as the door opened. On hearing Trevor’s voice he slowly emerged, and a pair of round and spectacled eyes gazed blankly at the visitors. Trevor briefly explained his errand, but the interview lost in solemnity owing to the fact that the bare notion of Dixon storing tobacco in his room made Clowes roar with laughter. Also, Dixon stolidly refused to understand what Trevor was talking about, and at the end of ten minutes, finding it hopeless to try and explain, the two went. Dixon, with a hazy impression that he had been asked to join in some sort of round game, and had refused the offer, returned again to his Liddell and Scott, and continued to wrestle with the somewhat obscure utterances of the chorus in Aeschylus’ *Agamemnon*. The results of this fiasco on Trevor and Clowes were widely different. Trevor it depressed horribly. It made him feel savage. Clowes, on the other hand, regarded the whole affair in a spirit of rollicking farce, and refused to see that this was a serious matter, in which the honour of the house was involved.

===Chinese===
狄克逊是个戴眼镜的胖小伙，公认一天做二十二小时功课。据信他从十一点睡到一点，补足两小时觉，随后起床，在书房里一直用功到早饭。

克劳斯和特雷弗进门时，他正在用功。门一开，他一头扎进一部厚厚的利德尔-斯科特词典（Liddell and Scott）。听见特雷弗的声音，他才慢慢探出头来，一双圆瞪瞪的镜片眼睛茫然望着来客。特雷弗三言两语说明了来意，可惜这场会见的庄严气氛全毁了——单是「狄克逊在房里囤烟」这个念头，就笑得克劳斯前仰后合。再加上狄克逊又木然死活听不懂特雷弗在说什么，十分钟后，两人见解释无望，只好走人。狄克逊脑子里模模糊糊留着个印象，仿佛有人邀他参加某种团体游戏，被他谢绝了，于是重新扎回他的利德尔-斯科特，继续跟埃斯库罗斯《阿伽门农》（*Agamemnon*）里合唱队那些颇为晦涩的台词较劲。这场惨败对特雷弗和克劳斯的效果大不相同。特雷弗被弄得心情恶劣，火冒三丈；克劳斯却把整件事看作一出欢天喜地的闹剧，拒不承认这是件牵涉宿舍荣誉的正经事。

===Original===
The next study was Ruthven’s. This fact somewhat toned down the exuberances of Clowes’s demeanour. When one particularly dislikes a person, one has a curious objection to seeming in good spirits in his presence. One feels that he may take it as a sort of compliment to himself, or, at any rate, contribute grins of his own, which would be hateful. Clowes was as grave as Trevor when they entered the study.

Ruthven’s study was like himself, overdressed and rather futile. It ran to little china ornaments in a good deal of profusion. It was more like a drawing-room than a school study.

“Sorry to disturb you, Ruthven,” said Trevor.

“Oh, come in,” said Ruthven, in a tired voice. “Please shut the door; there is a draught. Do you want anything?”

“We’ve got to have a look round,” said Clowes.

“Can’t you see everything there is?”

Ruthven hated Clowes as much as Clowes hated him.

Trevor cut into the conversation again.

“It’s like this, Ruthven,” he said. “I’m awfully sorry, but the Old Man’s just told me to search the studies in case any of the fellows have got baccy.”

===Chinese===
下一间书房是拉思文（Ruthven）的。这一事实多少收敛了克劳斯举止中的兴高采烈。一个人特别讨厌谁的时候，就会生出一种古怪的忌讳：不愿在那人面前显得兴头头的。总觉得对方也许会引为某种恭维，至少也会自己凑上来咧嘴而笑，那就可恨了。走进书房时，克劳斯和特雷弗一样面色沉重。

拉思文的书房跟他本人一个样：打扮过头，华而不实。屋里泛滥着大批小瓷摆设，与其说是学生书房，不如说是一间客厅。

「抱歉打扰了，拉思文。」特雷弗说。

「哦，进来吧。」拉思文的声音有气无力，「请把门关上，有过堂风。有事吗？」

「我们得四处看看。」克劳斯说。

「有什么好看的，不就是眼前这些吗？」

拉思文恨克劳斯，恰如克劳斯恨拉思文。

特雷弗又把话头接了过去。

「是这么回事，拉思文，」他说，「实在抱歉，老头子刚才吩咐我搜查各间书房，看有没有人存了烟草原。」

===Original===
Ruthven jumped up, pale with consternation.

“You can’t. I won’t have you disturbing my study.”

“This is rot,” said Trevor, shortly, “I’ve got to. It’s no good making it more unpleasant for me than it is.”

“But I’ve no tobacco. I swear I haven’t.”

“Then why mind us searching?” said Clowes affably.

“Come on, Ruthven,” said Trevor, “chuck us over the keys. You might as well.”

“I won’t.”

===Chinese===
拉思文霍地跳起来，惊得脸色发白。

「你们不能搜。我不许你们搅扰我的书房。」

「别犯傻了，」特雷弗没好气地说，「我必须搜。别把事情弄得更叫我不痛快了。」

「可我没有烟草。我发誓没有。」

「那何必怕我们搜呢？」克劳斯和颜悦色地说。

「算了吧，拉思文，」特雷弗说，「把钥匙扔过来。何必呢。」

「偏不。」

===Original===
“Don’t be an ass, man.”

“We have here,” observed Clowes, in his sad, solemn way, “a stout and serviceable poker.” He stooped, as he spoke, to pick it up.

“Leave that poker alone,” cried Ruthven.

Clowes straightened himself.

“I’ll swap it for your keys,” he said.

“Don’t be a fool.”

“Very well, then. We will now crack our first crib.”

Ruthven sprang forward, but Clowes, handing him off in football fashion with his left hand, with his right dashed the poker against the lock of the drawer of the table by which he stood.

===Chinese===
「别犯驴脾气了，伙计。」

「我们这里，」克劳斯用他哀伤而郑重的腔调评点道，「有一根粗壮耐用的拨火棍。」说话间他弯腰把它拾了起来。

「把那拨火棍放下。」拉思文叫道。

克劳斯直起身子。

「我拿它换你的钥匙。」他说。

「别犯傻。」

「那好吧。咱们这就来撬第一口小保险柜。」

拉思文扑上前去，克劳斯却用左手照橄榄球里的推挡把他格开，右手抡起拨火棍，砸在他身旁那张桌子的抽屉锁上。

===Original===
The lock broke with a sharp crack. It was not built with an eye to such onslaught.

“Neat for a first shot,” said Clowes, complacently. “Now for the Umustaphas and shag.”

But as he looked into the drawer he uttered a sudden cry of excitement. He drew something out, and tossed it over to Trevor.

“Catch, Trevor,” he said quietly. “Something that’ll interest you.”

Trevor caught it neatly in one hand, and stood staring at it as if he had never seen anything like it before. And yet he had—often. For what he had caught was a little golden bat, about an inch long by an eighth of an inch wide.

===Chinese===
锁「咔」的一声脆响断了。造它的人可没打算让它经受这等猛攻。

「头一炮就这么利索，」克劳斯心满意足，「接着找优穆斯塔法（Umustaphas）烟丝和板烟丝吧。」

可他往抽屉里一看，突然发出一声激动的喊叫。他掏出一样东西，抛给特雷弗。

「接住，特雷弗，」他平静地说，「一样会让你感兴趣的东西。」

特雷弗单手利落地接住，站在那儿直勾勾盯着它，仿佛生平从未见过这样的东西。可他见过——还不止一次。因为他接住的，是一只小小的金色球棒，约一英寸长、八分之一英寸宽。

## II The Gold Bat / 二、金色球棒

===Original===
Trevor did not take long to resume a garb of civilisation. He never wasted much time over anything. He was gifted with a boundless energy, which might possibly have made him unpopular had he not justified it by results. The football of the school had never been in such a flourishing condition as it had attained to on his succeeding to the captaincy. It was not only that the first fifteen was good. The excellence of a first fifteen does not always depend on the captain. But the games, even down to the very humblest junior game, had woken up one morning—at the beginning of the previous term—to find themselves, much to their surprise, organised going concerns. Like the immortal Captain Pott, Trevor was “a terror to the shirker and the lubber.” And the resemblance was further increased by the fact that he was “a toughish lot,” who was “little, but steel and india-rubber.” At first sight his appearance was not imposing. Paterfamilias, who had heard his son’s eulogies on Trevor’s performances during the holidays, and came down to watch the school play a match, was generally rather disappointed on seeing five feet six where he had looked for at least six foot one, and ten stone where he had expected thirteen. But then, what there was of Trevor was, as previously remarked, steel and india-rubber, and he certainly played football like a miniature Stoddart. It was characteristic of him that, though this was the first match of the term, his condition seemed to be as good as possible. He had done all his own work on the field and most of Rand-Brown’s, and apparently had not turned a hair. He was one of those conscientious people who train in the holidays.

===Chinese===
特雷弗没费多少工夫便重新披上了文明人的装束。他做什么都不多耗时间。他天生气力无穷，若不是总有实绩作证，这份精力很可能招人反感。学校的橄榄球从没有像他接任队长以来这样兴旺过。这还不单说一队出色——一队出色未必总归功于队长。而是全校各项比赛，哪怕是最不起眼的低年级赛事，都在上学期开头的某天早晨一觉醒来，惊奇地发现自己成了井井有条的营利企业。特雷弗与不朽的波特船长（Captain Pott）一样，是「偷懒者和笨汉的灾星」。而两者的相似还不止于此：他也是「顶不好惹的一号」，「个子小，却是钢和橡皮做的」。乍一看，他的仪表并不起眼。做父亲的假期里听儿子把特雷弗的功绩夸上了天，专程跑来看学校比赛，一见真人只有五英尺六（约一米六八），不是他想当然的至少六英尺一；只有十石（约六十三公斤），不是他预期的十三石——多半要大失所望。然而，特雷弗其人既然如前所述是钢和橡皮做的，他打起橄榄球来确实像一具袖珍版的斯托达特（Stoddart）。他的特点还在于：虽是学期头一场比赛，他的竞技状态却好得不能再好。他在场上干完了自己那份活儿，还捎带干了兰德-布朗的大半份，居然面不改色。他是那种假期里也照章训练的良心之士。

===Original===
When he had changed, he went down the passage to Clowes’ study. Clowes was in the position he frequently took up when the weather was good—wedged into his window in a sitting position, one leg in the study, the other hanging outside over space. The indoor leg lacked a boot, so that it was evident that its owner had at least had the energy to begin to change. That he had given the thing up after that, exhausted with the effort, was what one naturally expected from Clowes. He would have made a splendid actor: he was so good at resting.

“Hurry up and dress,” said Trevor; “I want you to come over to the baths.”

“What on earth do you want over at the baths?”

“I want to see O’Hara.”

“Oh, yes, I remember. Dexter’s are camping out there, aren’t they? I heard they were. Why is it?”

“One of the Dexter kids got measles in the last week of the holidays, so they shunted all the beds and things across, and the chaps went back there instead of to the house.”

In the winter term the baths were always boarded over and converted into a sort of extra gymnasium where you could go and box or fence when there was no room to do it in the real gymnasium. Socker and stump-cricket were also largely played there, the floor being admirably suited to such games, though the light was always rather tricky, and prevented heavy scoring.

===Chinese===
换好衣服，他顺着走廊去克劳斯的书房。克劳斯正摆着天气好时惯摆的姿势——以坐姿嵌在窗框里，一条腿在书房内，另一条悬在窗外的半空。室内那条腿没穿靴子，可见其主人至少还有力气开始换衣服。至于换了这一半便筋疲力尽、就此作罢，那正是克劳斯意料中事。他本可以成为一名出色的演员：他太擅长休息了。

「快点穿上衣服，」特雷弗说，「我要你陪我去趟浴场。」

「你去浴场干什么？」

「我要见奥哈拉（O’Hara）。」

「哦，对，我想起来了。德克斯特宿舍的人正扎营住那儿呢，是不是？我听说是。怎么回事？」

「假期最后一星期，德克斯特宿舍有个小家伙出了麻疹，于是他们把床铺什物统统挪了过去，那帮人就住到那边去，不回宿舍了。」

冬季学期里，浴场总要铺上木板，改成一间附加体育馆，正牌体育馆腾不开地方时，尽可以去那儿练拳击或击剑。足球和残桩板球也在那儿大行其道，地面打这类游戏再合适不过，只是光线总有些刁钻，叫人打不出高分。

===Original===
“I should think,” said Clowes, “from what I’ve seen of Dexter’s beauties, that Dexter would like them to camp out at the bottom of the baths all the year round. It would be a happy release for him if they were all drowned. And I suppose if he had to choose any one of them for a violent death, he’d pick O’Hara. O’Hara must be a boon to a housemaster. I’ve known chaps break rules when the spirit moved them, but he’s the only one I’ve met who breaks them all day long and well into the night simply for amusement. I’ve often thought of writing to the S.P.C.A. about it. I suppose you could call Dexter an animal all right?”

“O’Hara’s right enough, really. A man like Dexter would make any fellow run amuck. And then O’Hara’s an Irishman to start with, which makes a difference.”

There is usually one house in every school of the black sheep sort, and, if you go to the root of the matter, you will generally find that the fault is with the master of that house. A housemaster who enters into the life of his house, coaches them in games—if an athlete—or, if not an athlete, watches the games, umpiring at cricket and refereeing at football, never finds much difficulty in keeping order. It may be accepted as fact that the juniors of a house will never be orderly of their own free will, but disturbances in the junior day-room do not make the house undisciplined. The prefects are the criterion. If you find them joining in the general “rags,” and even starting private ones on their own account, then you may safely say that it is time the master of that house retired from the business, and took to chicken-farming. And that was the state of things in Dexter’s. It was the most lawless of the houses. Mr. Dexter belonged to a type of master almost unknown at a public school—the usher type. In a private school he might have passed. At Wrykyn he was out of place. To him the whole duty of a housemaster appeared to be to wage war against his house.

When Dexter’s won the final for the cricket cup in the summer term of two years back, the match lasted four afternoons—four solid afternoons of glorious, up-and-down cricket. Mr. Dexter did not see a single ball of that match bowled. He was prowling in sequestered lanes and broken-down barns out of bounds on the off-chance that he might catch some member of his house smoking there. As if the whole of the house, from the head to the smallest fag, were not on the field watching Day’s best bats collapse before Henderson’s bowling, and Moriarty hit up that marvellous and unexpected fifty-three at the end of the second innings!

That sort of thing definitely stamps a master.

===Chinese===
「照我对德克斯特那帮宝贝的观察，」克劳斯说，「德克斯特巴不得他们一年到头扎营在浴场池底。他们要是全淹死了，对他倒是愉快的解脱。而倘若非挑一个横死不可，我猜他会挑奥哈拉。奥哈拉对舍监来说真是份厚礼。我见过有人一时兴起犯规的，可全天候犯规、一直犯到深夜、纯粹当消遣的，我只见过他一个。我常想为这事给防止虐待动物协会（S.P.C.A.）写信。德克斯特总归可以算一头动物吧？」

「奥哈拉其实不坏。德克斯特那种人换谁来都得逼疯。再说奥哈拉先就是个爱尔兰人，这就不一样了。」

每所学校通常总有一个害群之马式的宿舍，而追根究底，你多半会发现毛病出在那个宿舍的舍监身上。一位舍监若能融入宿舍的生活，是运动员便指导各项比赛，不是运动员便到场观赛，板球赛当裁判、橄榄球赛做执法，他从不会觉得维持秩序有多难。可以当作定论的是：一个宿舍的低年级生绝不会自愿守规矩，可低年级活动室的骚乱并不等于宿舍无纪律。标准在级长。倘若你发现他们也跟着起哄捣乱，甚至自作主张另开新局，那你尽可断言：这位舍监该从这行退休，改行养鸡去了。德克斯特宿舍正是这副光景。它是各宿舍中最无法无天的一个。德克斯特先生属于公学里几乎绝迹的一类教员——私塾助教型。在私立预备学校他也许还过得去，在里金他纯属错位。在他看来，舍监的全部天职，就是同自己的宿舍开战。

两年前的夏季学期，德克斯特宿舍打进板球杯决赛并最终捧杯，那场球打了四个下午——整整四个下午辉煌拉锯、跌宕起伏的板球。德克斯特先生却连一个投出的球都没看着。他正在界外的幽僻小巷和破败谷仓间巡行，指望侥幸逮住他宿舍的某个人在那儿抽烟。仿佛当时全宿舍从舍长到最小的跑腿生不是都在球场上，看戴氏宿舍（Day’s）的最佳击球手在亨德森（Henderson）的投球面前土崩瓦解，看莫里亚蒂（Moriarty）在第二局末尾敲出那记惊天动地、出人意料的五十三分似的！

这号做派，足以给一位教员定性。

===Original===
“What do you want to see O’Hara about?” asked Clowes.

“He’s got my little gold bat. I lent it him in the holidays.”

===Chinese===
「你要见奥哈拉什么事？」克劳斯问。

「他拿着我那只小金球棒。假期里我借给他了。」

===Original===
A remark which needs a footnote. The bat referred to was made of gold, and was about an inch long by an eighth broad. It had come into existence some ten years previously, in the following manner. The inter-house cricket cup at Wrykyn had originally been a rather tarnished and unimpressive vessel, whose only merit consisted in the fact that it was of silver. Ten years ago an Old Wrykinian, suddenly reflecting that it would not be a bad idea to do something for the school in a small way, hied him to the nearest jeweller’s and purchased another silver cup, vast withal and cunningly decorated with filigree work, and standing on a massive ebony plinth, round which were little silver lozenges just big enough to hold the name of the winning house and the year of grace. This he presented with his blessing to be competed for by the dozen houses that made up the school of Wrykyn, and it was formally established as the house cricket cup. The question now arose: what was to be done with the other cup? The School House, who happened to be the holders at the time, suggested disinterestedly that it should become the property of the house which had won it last. “Not so,” replied the Field Sports Committee, “but far otherwise. We will have it melted down in a fiery furnace, and thereafter fashioned into eleven little silver bats. And these little silver bats shall be the guerdon of the eleven members of the winning team, to have and to hold for the space of one year, unless, by winning the cup twice in succession, they gain the right of keeping the bat for yet another year. How is that, umpire?” And the authorities replied, “O men of infinite resource and sagacity, verily is it a cold day when *you* get left behind. Forge ahead.” But, when they had forged ahead, behold! it would not run to eleven little silver bats, but only to ten little silver bats. Thereupon the headmaster, a man liberal with his cash, caused an eleventh little bat to be fashioned—for the captain of the winning team to have and to hold in the manner aforesaid. And, to single it out from the others, it was wrought, not of silver, but of gold. And so it came to pass that at the time of our story Trevor was in possession of the little gold bat, because Donaldson’s had won the cup in the previous summer, and he had captained them—and, incidentally, had scored seventy-five without a mistake.

===Chinese===
这句话需要加个注。所说的球棒是纯金打的，约一英寸长、八分之一英寸宽。它的问世大约在十年前，经过如下。里金的宿舍板球杯原本是一只色泽暗淡、毫不起眼的器皿，唯一的优点在于它是银子做的。十年前，一位老里金人忽然想到，不妨为学校略尽绵薄，便奔赴最近的珠宝店，又购得一只银杯——体量宏大，通身饰以精巧的金银丝工艺，立在一方厚重的乌木底座上，底座四周镶着一圈小银菱牌，尺寸恰好够刻上夺杯宿舍的名字和纪年。他连祝福一起捐出，供组成里金学校的十二个宿舍竞逐，此杯遂正式立为宿舍板球杯。于是问题来了：原来那只杯子怎么处置？学校宿舍——当时恰好是它持有原杯——大公无私地建议，此杯应归最后赢过它的宿舍所有。「不然，」运动委员会答道，「恰恰相反。我们要把它扔进烈火熔炉化掉，再铸成十一只小银球棒。这十一只小银球棒将是获胜队十一名成员的奖赏，持有并保管一年；除非他们连续两年夺杯，赢得再保管一年的权利。如何，裁判？」校方答复道：「足智多谋的诸位，诚哉，你们落后之日，必是天寒地冻之时。奋勇前进吧。」可是等他们奋勇前进之后，看哪！银子只够铸十只小银球棒，铸不成十一只。于是校长——一位花钱大方的人——出资让人补铸了第十一只小球棒，给获胜队队长照上述方式持有并保管。而为了把它与其余十只区分开，这一只的用材不是银子，而是黄金。于是就有了本书故事开始的时节特雷弗握着小金球棒这回事——因为上个夏天唐纳森宿舍夺了杯，而他正是队长——顺带一提，他还未失一手地砍下了七十五分。

===Original===
“Well, I’m hanged if I would trust O’Hara with my bat,” said Clowes, referring to the silver ornament on his own watch-chain; “he’s probably pawned yours in the holidays. Why did you lend it to him?”

“His people wanted to see it. I know him at home, you know. They asked me to lunch the last day but one of the holidays, and we got talking about the bat, because, of course, if we hadn’t beaten Dexter’s in the final, O’Hara would have had it himself. So I sent it over next day with a note asking O’Hara to bring it back with him here.”

“Oh, well, there’s a chance, then, seeing he’s only had it so little time, that he hasn’t pawned it yet. You’d better rush off and get it back as soon as possible. It’s no good waiting for me. I shan’t be ready for weeks.”

“Where’s Paget?”

“Teaing with Donaldson. At least, he said he was going to.”

“Then I suppose I shall have to go alone. I hate walking alone.”

“If you hurry,” said Clowes, scanning the road from his post of vantage, “you’ll be able to go with your fascinating pal Ruthven. He’s just gone out.”

===Chinese===
「啧，要是我，才不肯把球棒托给奥哈拉，」克劳斯说，摸了摸自己表链上那只银坠子，「他多半早把你那只在假期里当掉了。你干吗借给他？」
