「他家里的人想见识见识。你知道，我在家乡跟他熟。假期倒数第二天他们请我吃午饭，我们聊起了球棒——因为，当然啦，要不是我们在决赛里赢了德克斯特宿舍，这只球棒本来该归奥哈拉的。于是第二天我把球棒送了过去，附了张条子，请奥哈拉回校时捎回来。」

「哦，那么，看在他到手没几天的份上，兴许还没来得及当掉。你最好赶紧跑去，越快收回越好。等我没用，我几个星期也准备不好。」

「佩吉特呢？」

「在唐纳森那儿喝茶。至少他说是要去。」

「那我只好一个人去了。我讨厌一个人走路。」

「你要是抓紧，」克劳斯从他居高临下的观察哨扫视着路面说，「还赶得上跟你那位魅力四射的伙伴拉思文（Ruthven）同行。他刚出门。」

===Original===
Trevor dashed downstairs in his energetic way, and overtook the youth referred to.

Clowes brooded over them from above like a sorrowful and rather disgusted Providence. Trevor’s liking for Ruthven, who was a Donaldsonite like himself, was one of the few points on which the two had any real disagreement. Clowes could not understand how any person in his senses could of his own free will make an intimate friend of Ruthven.

“Hullo, Trevor,” said Ruthven.

“Come over to the baths,” said Trevor, “I want to see O’Hara about something. Or were you going somewhere else.”

“I wasn’t going anywhere in particular. I never know what to do in term-time. It’s deadly dull.”

Trevor could never understand how anyone could find term-time dull. For his own part, there always seemed too much to do in the time.

“You aren’t allowed to play games?” he said, remembering something about a doctor’s certificate in the past.

“No,” said Ruthven. “Thank goodness,” he added.

Which remark silenced Trevor. To a person who thanked goodness that he was not allowed to play games he could find nothing to say. But he ceased to wonder how it was that Ruthven was dull.

===Chinese===
特雷弗以他风风火火的劲头冲下楼去，赶上了所说的那位少年。

克劳斯在楼上俯视着他们，像一位哀伤而相当倒胃口的天意。特雷弗喜欢拉思文——后者和他一样是唐纳森宿舍的人——这是两人之间真正谈不拢的少数几点之一。克劳斯实在不明白，任何一个神志清明的人，怎么会出于自愿跟拉思文结成密友。

「你好啊，特雷弗。」拉思文说。

「去浴场吧，」特雷弗说，「我有点事要见奥哈拉。还是你另有去处？」

「我也没什么特别要去的地方。学期里我从来不知道该干什么。闷得要死。」

特雷弗永远无法理解，怎么会有人觉得学期无聊。就他自己而言，学期里的时间总是不够用。

「你不许参加比赛？」他问，想起从前有关医生证明的什么事。

「不许。」拉思文说。「谢天谢地。」他补了一句。

这句话让特雷弗哑口无言。对一个庆幸自己不许参加比赛的人，他实在无话可说。不过他也不再奇怪拉思文怎么会无聊了。

===Original===
They proceeded to the baths together in silence. O’Hara, they were informed by a Dexter’s fag who met them outside the door, was not about.

“When he comes back,” said Trevor, “tell him I want him to come to tea tomorrow directly after school, and bring my bat. Don’t forget.”

The fag promised to make a point of it.

===Chinese===
两人默默无言，一道往浴场去。门口碰上一个德克斯特宿舍的跑腿生，告诉他们奥哈拉不在。

「他回来的时候，」特雷弗说，「告诉他我要他明天一下课就来喝茶，把我的球棒带来。别忘了。」

那跑腿生保证一定牢记在心。

## XI The House-Matches / 十一、宿舍联赛

===Original===
It was something of a consolation to Barry and his friends—at any rate, to Barry and Drummond—that directly after they had been evicted from their study, the house-matches began. Except for the Ripton match, the house-matches were the most important event of the Easter term. Even the sports at the beginning of April were productive of less excitement. There were twelve houses at Wrykyn, and they played on the “knocking-out” system. To be beaten once meant that a house was no longer eligible for the competition. It could play “friendlies” as much as it liked, but, play it never so wisely, it could not lift the cup. Thus it often happened that a weak house, by fluking a victory over a strong rival, found itself, much to its surprise, in the semifinal, or sometimes even in the final. This was rarer at football than at cricket, for at football the better team generally wins.

===Chinese===
对巴里和他的朋友们——至少对巴里和德拉蒙德（Drummond）来说——多少是种安慰：他们刚被逐出书房，宿舍联赛就开始了。除里普顿一战外，宿舍联赛是复活节学期最要紧的大事，连四月初的运动会都不及它热闹。里金有十二个宿舍，打的是淘汰制。输掉一场，那个宿舍便失去了竞逐资格。往后尽可以爱打多少「友谊赛」打多少，可任凭打得再精明，也举不起奖杯了。于是常有这样的事：一个弱队侥幸赢了个强敌，惊愕地发现自己已站进半决赛，有时甚至进了决赛。这种事橄榄球比板球少见，因为橄榄球总是较强的队赢。

===Original===
The favourites this year were Donaldson’s, though some fancied Seymour’s. Donaldson’s had Trevor, whose leadership was worth almost more than his play. In no other house was training so rigid. You could tell a Donaldson’s man, if he was in his house-team, at a glance. If you saw a man eating oatmeal biscuits in the shop, and eyeing wistfully the while the stacks of buns and pastry, you could put him down as a Donaldsonite without further evidence. The captains of the other houses used to prescribe a certain amount of self-abnegation in the matter of food, but Trevor left his men barely enough to support life—enough, that is, of the things that are really worth eating. The consequence was that Donaldson’s would turn out for an important match all muscle and bone, and on such occasions it was bad for those of their opponents who had been taking life more easily. Besides Trevor they had Clowes, and had had bad luck in not having Paget. Had Paget stopped, no other house could have looked at them. But by his departure, the strength of the team had become more nearly on a level with that of Seymour’s.

Some even thought that Seymour’s were the stronger. Milton was as good a forward as the school possessed. Besides him there were Barry and Rand-Brown on the wings. Drummond was a useful half, and five of the pack had either first or second fifteen colours. It was a team that would take some beating.

===Chinese===
今年的夺标热门是唐纳森宿舍，尽管也有人看好西摩宿舍。唐纳森有特雷弗，他的统帅之才几乎比他的球技更值钱。别家宿舍的训练都没有这么严。一个唐纳森的人，只要是宿舍队队员，你一眼便认得出。要是你看见谁在小卖部里嚼燕麦饼干，一边眼巴巴望着成堆的甜面包和糕点，不必更多证据，就可断定他是唐纳森的人。别家队长在吃的方面也讲究几分清规戒律，特雷弗却只给手下留了勉强维持生命的份——也就是说，只留那些真正值得吃的东西的份。结果是，一到重大比赛，唐纳森出场便个个筋骨如铁，这种时候，那些日子过得松快的对手可要倒霉了。除了特雷弗，他们还有克劳斯，只是运气不佳，失去了佩吉特。佩吉特若留下来，其他宿舍连望他们一眼的资格都没有。可他一走，球队的实力便大体落到与西摩宿舍平起平坐了。

有人甚至认为西摩更强。弥尔顿是全校数得着的好前锋。他之外，两边路上还有巴里和兰德-布朗。德拉蒙德是名实用的前卫，前锋群里五人有校一队或二队队色。这是一支不那么容易打赢的球队。

===Original===
Trevor came to that conclusion early. “If we can beat Seymour’s, we’ll lift the cup,” he said to Clowes.

“We’ll have to do all we know,” was Clowes’ reply.

They were watching Seymour’s pile up an immense score against a scratch team got up by one of the masters. The first round of the competition was over. Donaldson’s had beaten Templar’s, Seymour’s the School House. Templar’s were rather stronger than the School House, and Donaldson’s had beaten them by a rather larger score than that which Seymour’s had run up in their match. But neither Trevor nor Clowes was inclined to draw any augury from this. Seymour’s had taken things easily after halftime; Donaldson’s had kept going hard all through.

“That makes Rand-Brown’s fourth try,” said Clowes, as the wing three-quarter of the second fifteen raced round and scored in the corner.

“Yes. This is the sort of game he’s all right in. The man who’s marking him is no good. Barry’s scored twice, and both good tries, too.”

“Oh, there’s no doubt which is the best man,” said Clowes. “I only mentioned that it was Rand-Brown’s fourth as an item of interest.”

The game continued. Barry scored a third try.

===Chinese===
特雷弗早早就得出了这个结论。「只要能赢西摩，我们就能捧杯。」他对克劳斯说。

「咱们得把看家本领全使出来。」克劳斯答道。

两人正看西摩宿舍对一支由某位教员拼凑的临时队大把捞分。第一轮已经打完。唐纳森赢了坦普勒宿舍（Templar’s），西摩赢了学校宿舍。坦普勒比校舍略强，唐纳森赢他们的比分也比西摩那场刷出来的更大。但特雷弗和克劳斯都不愿由此占卜什么。西摩下半场踢得松；唐纳森则从头到尾咬牙硬打。

「这已是兰德-布朗的第四次达阵了。」克劳斯说。这时二队的那位翼锋绕过人群，在角旗处得分。

「是啊。这种比赛他还行。盯他的人不顶用。巴里进了两个，还都是漂亮的达阵。」

「哦，两人谁强没有疑问，」克劳斯说，「我提兰德-布朗的第四次达阵，不过是当作一条趣闻。」

比赛继续。巴里拿下第三次达阵。

===Original===
“We’re drawn against Appleby’s next round,” said Trevor. “We can manage them all right.”

“When is it?”

“Next Thursday. Nomads’ match on Saturday. Then Ripton, Saturday week.”

“Who’ve Seymour’s drawn?”

“Day’s. It’ll be a good game, too. Seymour’s ought to win, but they’ll have to play their best. Day’s have got some good men.”

“Fine scrum,” said Clowes. “Yes. Quick in the open, too, which is always good business. I wish they’d beat Seymour’s.”

“Oh, we ought to be all right, whichever wins.”

===Chinese===
「下一轮我们抽到对阿普尔比宿舍（Appleby’s），」特雷弗说，「对付他们不在话下。」

「什么时候？」

「下星期四。星期六打游牧人队。再下个星期六打里普顿。」

「西摩抽到谁了？」

「戴氏。那也会是场好球。西摩该赢，可他们得拿出最高水平。戴氏有几个好手。」

「好一群并列争球，」克劳斯说，「嗯，拉开之后也快，这永远是桩好买卖。真希望他们把西摩赢了。」

「哦，不管谁赢，我们都不怕。」

===Original===
Appleby’s did not offer any very serious resistance to the Donaldson attack. They were outplayed at every point of the game, and, before halftime, Donaldson’s had scored their thirty points. It was a rule in all in-school matches—and a good rule, too—that, when one side led by thirty points, the match stopped. This prevented those massacres which do so much towards crushing all the football out of the members of the beaten team; and it kept the winning team from getting slack, by urging them on to score their thirty points before halftime. There were some houses—notoriously slack—which would go for a couple of seasons without ever playing the second half of a match.

Having polished off the men of Appleby, the Donaldson team trooped off to the other game to see how Seymour’s were getting on with Day’s. It was evidently an exciting match. The first half had been played to the accompaniment of much shouting from the ropes. Though coming so early in the competition, it was really the semifinal, for whichever team won would be almost certain to get into the final. The school had turned up in large numbers to watch.

“Seymour’s looking tired of life,” said Clowes. “That would seem as if his fellows weren’t doing well.”

===Chinese===
阿普尔比宿舍没对唐纳森的攻势构成什么像样的抵抗。他们在比赛的每个环节都被压着打，中场休息还没到，唐纳森已拿满三十分。校内比赛有条规矩——也是条好规矩——一方领先三十分，比赛即告终止。这避免了那些大屠杀式的惨案，免得把输方队员身上的橄榄球细胞赶尽杀绝；同时也督促胜方赶在中场前拿满三十分，免得他们松懈。有几个宿舍——松懈得出名——能连着两个赛季一场球的下半场都打不上。

收拾完阿普尔比的众人，唐纳森队成群结队转赴另一块场地，看西摩对戴氏战况如何。那显然是场扣人心弦的比赛。上半场是在围绳边震天的呐喊伴奏下打完的。虽然在赛程里出现得早，这实际就是半决赛，因为谁赢了谁几乎稳进决赛。全校倾巢出动，前来观战。

「西摩瞧着都活腻了，」克劳斯说，「看来他的手下打得不太妙。」

===Original===
“What’s been happening here?” asked Trevor of an enthusiast in a Seymour’s house cap whose face was crimson with yelling.

“One goal all,” replied the enthusiast huskily. “Did you beat Appleby’s?”

“Yes. Thirty points before halftime. Who’s been doing the scoring here?”

“Milton got in for us. He barged through out of touch. We’ve been pressing the whole time. Barry got over once, but he was held up. Hullo, they’re beginning again. Buck up, Seymour’s.”

His voice cracking on the high note, he took an immense slab of vanilla chocolate as a remedy for hoarseness.

“Who scored for Day’s?” asked Clowes.

“Strachan. Rand-Brown let him through from their twenty-five. You never saw anything so rotten as Rand-Brown. He doesn’t take his passes, and Strachan gets past him every time.”

===Chinese===
「这儿战况如何？」特雷弗问一个戴西摩宿舍帽子的热心人，那人喊得满脸通红。

「一球对一球。」热心人声音沙哑地答道，「你们赢了阿普尔比吗？」

「赢了。中场前就拿满三十分。这边的分都是谁拿的？」

「弥尔顿替我们进了一个。他从边线外硬撞进来的。我们一直压着打。巴里过去过一回，被架住了。哎，又开球了。加把劲啊，西摩！」

高音劈了嗓，他掏出一大板香草巧克力，权作治哑的药。

「戴氏的分是谁进的？」克劳斯问。

「斯特拉坎（Strachan）。兰德-布朗在他们二十五码线上放他过去的。你从没见过兰德-布朗那么烂的。他接不住球，斯特拉坎回回从他身边过去。」

===Original===
“Is Strachan playing on the wing?”

Strachan was the first fifteen fullback.

“Yes. They’ve put young Bassett back instead of him. Seymour’s. Buck up, Seymour’s. We-ell played! There, did you ever see anything like it?” he broke off disgustedly.

The Seymourite playing centre next to Rand-Brown had run through to the back and passed out to his wing, as a good centre should. It was a perfect pass, except that it came at his head instead of his chest. Nobody with any pretensions to decent play should have missed it. Rand-Brown, however, achieved that feat. The ball struck his hands and bounded forward. The referee blew his whistle for a scrum, and a certain try was lost.

From the scrum the Seymour’s forwards broke away to the goal-line, where they were pulled up by Bassett. The next minute the defence had been pierced, and Drummond was lying on the ball a yard across the line. The enthusiast standing by Clowes expended the last relics of his voice in commemorating the fact that his side had the lead.

“Drummond’ll be good next year,” said Trevor. And he made a mental note to tell Allardyce, who would succeed him in the command of the school football, to keep an eye on the player in question.

The triumph of the Seymourites was not long lived. Milton failed to convert Drummond’s try. From the dropout from the twenty-five line Barry got the ball, and punted into touch. The throw-out was not straight, and a scrum was formed. The ball came out to the Day’s halves, and went across to Strachan. Rand-Brown hesitated, and then made a futile spring at the first fifteen man’s neck. Strachan handed him off easily, and ran. The Seymour’s fullback, who was a poor player, failed to get across in time. Strachan ran round behind the posts, the kick succeeded, and Day’s now led by two points.

After this the game continued in Day’s half. Five minutes before time was up, Drummond got the ball from a scrum nearly on the line, passed it to Barry on the wing instead of opening up the game by passing to his centres, and Barry slipped through in the corner. This put Seymour’s just one point ahead, and there they stayed till the whistle blew for no-side.

===Chinese===
「斯特拉坎打的翼锋吗？」

斯特拉坎是校一队的最后卫。

「是。他们把小巴塞特（Bassett）撤回去顶了他的位置。西摩，加把劲！西摩！打——得好！瞧，你见过这种球吗？」他厌恶地说不下去了。

与兰德-布朗相邻打中锋的那个西摩队员一路突到最后卫跟前，把球分向边路，好中锋正该如此。这一传堪称完美，只是球到的是他脑袋的高度，而非胸口。但凡自称会打球的人都不该接丢。然而兰德-布朗完成了这桩壮举。球撞上他的双手，向前弹了出去。裁判鸣哨判并列争球，一次到手的达阵就此泡汤。

并列争球后，西摩前锋一拥而上直扑球门线，被巴塞特拦停。下一分钟防线已被凿穿，德拉蒙德正压着球躺在球门线内一码处。站在克劳斯身边的热心人掏出嗓子里残存的最后一点声音，庆祝本队领先这一事实。

「德拉蒙德明年会很出色。」特雷弗说。他心里记下一笔：要告诉将来接掌全校橄榄球的阿拉代斯（Allardyce），留意这员球员。

西摩众人的凯旋没能持续多久。弥尔顿附加攻门失手。二十五码线重启开球，巴里得球，一脚踢出边线。掷界外球不直，又成并列争球。球出到戴氏前卫脚下，又横传给斯特拉坎。兰德-布朗迟疑了一下，朝那位校一队球员的脖子虚扑了一把。斯特拉坎轻轻一推便摆脱了他，撒腿就跑。西摩的最后卫本就水平不济，没来得及封住去路。斯特拉坎绕到门柱后面，攻门命中，戴氏此刻领先两分。

此后比赛一直胶着在戴氏半场。终场前五分钟，德拉蒙德在几乎贴线处从争球中得球，没有分给中锋拉开局面，而是传给边路的巴里，巴里从角上溜了过去。西摩由此恰好领先一分，并保持到终场哨响。

===Original===
Milton walked over to the boardinghouses with Clowes and Trevor. He was full of the match, particularly of the iniquity of Rand-Brown. “I slanged him on the field,” he said. “It’s a thing I don’t often do, but what else *can* you do when a man plays like that? He lost us three certain tries.”

“When did you administer your rebuke?” inquired Clowes.

“When he had let Strachan through that second time, in the second half. I asked him why on earth he tried to play footer at all. I told him a good kiss-in-the-ring club was about his form. It was rather cheap, but I felt so frightfully sick about it. It’s sickening to be let down like that when you’ve been pressing the whole time, and ought to be scoring every other minute.”

“What had he to say on the subject?” asked Clowes.

“Oh, he gassed a bit until I told him I’d kick him if he said another word. That shut him up.”

“You ought to have kicked him. You want all the kicking practice you can get. I never saw anything feebler than that shot of yours after Drummond’s try.”

“I’d like to see *you* take a kick like that. It was nearly on the touchline. Still, when we play you, we shan’t need to convert any of our tries. We’ll get our thirty points without that. Perhaps you’d like to scratch?”

“As a matter of fact,” said Clowes confidentially, “I am going to score seven tries against you off my own bat. You’ll be sorry you ever turned out when we’ve finished with you.”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿同克劳斯和特雷弗一道往寄宿宿舍走去。他满脑子都是这场球，尤其是兰德-布朗的滔天大罪。「我在场上骂了他，」他说，「这种事我不常干，可一个人打成那样，你*还能*怎么样？他丢了我们三次必进的达阵。」

「你的训斥是何时执行的呢？」克劳斯探问。

「下半场他第二次放斯特拉坎过去之后。我问他到底为什么要打橄榄球。我告诉他，「环中接吻」（kiss-in-the-ring）俱乐部什么的倒是适合他的水平。这话是刻薄了点，可我实在气坏了。全场一直压着打，本该每隔一分钟进一球，却这样被拆台，谁受得了。」

「对此他有什么高见？」克劳斯问。

「哦，他叽里呱啦了一阵，直到我说他再吐一个字我就踹他。这才闭嘴。」

「你当时就该踹。你正缺踢功练习呢。德拉蒙德达阵后你那一脚，我从没见过比那更软弱的。」

「我倒想看看*你*踢那种球。那几乎贴着边线了。不过等我们对你们的时候，我们的达阵一律不必加踢。不靠那个我们也能拿满三十分。要不你们干脆弃权得了？」

「实不相瞒，」克劳斯推心置腹地说，「我打算凭*我自己这根球棒*，独进你们七个达阵。等我们收拾完你们，你们要后悔今天出过场。」

## XXI The League Revealed / 二十一、联盟现形

===Original===
“What do you think of that?” said Clowes.

Trevor said nothing. He could not quite grasp the situation. It was not only that he had got the idea so firmly into his head that it was Rand-Brown who had sent the letters and appropriated the bat. Even supposing he had not suspected Rand-Brown, he would never have dreamed of suspecting Ruthven. They had been friends. Not very close friends—Trevor’s keenness for games and Ruthven’s dislike of them prevented that—but a good deal more than acquaintances. He was so constituted that he could not grasp the frame of mind required for such an action as Ruthven’s. It was something absolutely abnormal.

Clowes was equally surprised, but for a different reason. It was not so much the enormity of Ruthven’s proceedings that took him aback. He believed him, with that cheerful intolerance which a certain type of mind affects, capable of anything. What surprised him was the fact that Ruthven had had the ingenuity and even the daring to conduct a campaign of this description. Cribbing in examinations he would have thought the limit of his crimes. Something backboneless and underhand of that kind would not have surprised him in the least. He would have said that it was just about what he had expected all along. But that Ruthven should blossom out suddenly as quite an ingenious and capable criminal in this way, was a complete surprise.

===Chinese===
「这你怎么看？」克劳斯说。

特雷弗一言不发。他一时把握不住局面。这还不光是因为他先前铁了心认定写信、昧下球棒的是兰德-布朗。就算他从没怀疑过兰德-布朗，他也做梦想不到去怀疑拉思文。他们曾是朋友。算不上密友——特雷弗酷爱运动、拉思文厌憎运动，这拦着他们更亲近——可也远不止泛泛之交。他的天性使他无法理解干出拉思文这种事所需的心态。那完全是反常的。

克劳斯同样吃惊，缘由却是另一码事。让他愕然的倒不是拉思文此举的骇人程度。以某种头脑惯有的那种兴冲冲的不宽容，他本来就认定拉思文什么事都干得出来。让他吃惊的是，拉思文竟有这份巧思、甚至这份胆量来指挥一场这样规格的战役。考试作弊原本已被他认作此君罪行的上限。那类没脊梁的阴损勾当，一点儿也不会叫他意外，他还会说这恰好是一直料想之中的。可拉思文竟一下子绽放成相当灵巧能干的罪犯，这是彻头彻尾的意外。

===Original===
“Well, perhaps *you*’ll make a remark?” he said, turning to Ruthven.

Ruthven, looking very much like a passenger on a Channel steamer who has just discovered that the motion of the vessel is affecting him unpleasantly, had fallen into a chair when Clowes handed him off. He sat there with a look on his pasty face which was not good to see, as silent as Trevor. It seemed that whatever conversation there was going to be would have to take the form of a soliloquy from Clowes.

Clowes took a seat on the corner of the table.

“It seems to me, Ruthven,” he said, “that you’d better say *something*. At present there’s a lot that wants explaining. As this bat has been found lying in your drawer, I suppose we may take it that you’re the impolite letter-writer?”

===Chinese===
「那么，*你*是不是打算说点什么？」他转向拉思文说。

拉思文活像一位刚发现船身晃得自己难受的英吉利海峡渡轮乘客，被克劳斯一格便跌坐进椅子里。他坐在那儿，浆糊似的脸上一副不宜入目的神情，跟特雷弗一样沉默。看来不管要谈什么，都只能由克劳斯以独白的形式进行了。

克劳斯在桌角上坐了下来。

「依我看，拉思文，」他说，「你最好还是说*点什么*。眼下有一大堆事等着解释。既然这根球棒是在你抽屉里找到的，我想我们可以认定，那几封无礼的信函出自你的手笔了吧？」

===Original===
Ruthven found his voice at last.

“I’m not,” he cried; “I never wrote a line.”

“Now we’re getting at it,” said Clowes. “I thought you couldn’t have had it in you to carry this business through on your own. Apparently you’ve only been the sleeping partner in this show, though I suppose it was you who ragged Trevor’s study? Not much sleeping about that. You took over the acting branch of the concern for that day only, I expect. Was it you who ragged the study?”

Ruthven stared into the fire, but said nothing.

“Must be polite, you know, Ruthven, and answer when you’re spoken to. Was it you who ragged Trevor’s study?”

“Yes,” said Ruthven.

“Thought so.”

“Why, of course, I met you just outside,” said Trevor, speaking for the first time. “You were the chap who told me what had happened.”

===Chinese===
拉思文终于找回了声音。

「不是我，」他嚷道，「我一个字都没写过。」

「这就对上了，」克劳斯说，「我本就觉得你没本事独自把这买卖办到底。看来你在这台戏里只挂了个隐名合伙人，不过我猜，抄特雷弗书房的是你吧？那一手可不大像在睡觉。我料你只在那一天暂代了本企业执行部门的职务。书房是你抄的吗？」

拉思文盯着炉火，一言不发。

「要有礼貌，知道吗，拉思文，别人跟你说话要应声。书房是你抄的吗？」

「是。」拉思文说。

「料到是。」

「可不是嘛，我就在外头碰见你的，」特雷弗头一回开口，「跟我报告出事的正是老兄你。」

===Original===
Ruthven said nothing.

“The ragging of the study seems to have been all the active work he did,” remarked Clowes.

“No,” said Trevor, “he posted the letters, whether he wrote them or not. Milton was telling me—you remember? I told you. No, I didn’t. Milton found out that the letters were posted by a small, light-haired fellow.”

“That’s him,” said Clowes, as regardless of grammar as the monks of Rheims, pointing with the poker at Ruthven’s immaculate locks. “Well, you ragged the study and posted the letters. That was all your share. Am I right in thinking Rand-Brown was the other partner?”

Silence from Ruthven.

“Am I?” persisted Clowes.

“You may think what you like. I don’t care.”

“Now we’re getting rude again,” complained Clowes. “*Was* Rand-Brown in this?”

===Chinese===
拉思文没作声。

「抄书房看来就是他全部的实际工作了。」克劳斯评点道。

「不，」特雷弗说，「信是他去寄的，不管写没写。弥尔顿跟我讲过——你记得吗？我跟你说过的。不，我没说过。弥尔顿查明，那些信是个个子小小、头发浅黄的家伙寄的。」

「正是他老兄，」克劳斯说，全然不顾语法，跟兰斯的僧侣们一个样，一面用拨火棍指着拉思文一丝不苟的头发，「好，你抄了书房，寄了信。你的份额就这些。我没想错吧，另一位合伙人是兰德-布朗？」

拉思文沉默。

「想错没有？」克劳斯追问。

「你爱怎么想怎么想。我不在乎。」

「这就又没礼貌了，」克劳斯抱怨道，「兰德-布朗*到底*掺没掺和？」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Ruthven.

“Thought so. And who else?”

“No one.”

“Try again.”

“I tell you there was no one else. Can’t you believe a word a chap says?”

“A word here and there, perhaps,” said Clowes, as one making a concession, “but not many, and this isn’t one of them. Have another shot.”

Ruthven relapsed into silence.

“All right, then,” said Clowes, “we’ll accept that statement. There’s just a chance that it may be true. And that’s about all, I think. This isn’t my affair at all, really. It’s yours, Trevor. I’m only a spectator and camp-follower. It’s your business. You’ll find me in my study.” And putting the poker carefully in its place, Clowes left the room. He went into his study, and tried to begin some work. But the beauties of the second book of Thucydides failed to appeal to him. His mind was elsewhere. He felt too excited with what had just happened to translate Greek. He pulled up a chair in front of the fire, and gave himself up to speculating how Trevor was getting on in the neighbouring study. He was glad he had left him to finish the business. If he had been in Trevor’s place, there was nothing he would so greatly have disliked as to have someone—however familiar a friend—interfering in his wars and settling them for him. Left to himself, Clowes would probably have ended the interview by kicking Ruthven into the nearest approach to pulp compatible with the laws relating to manslaughter. He had an uneasy suspicion that Trevor would let him down far too easily.

===Chinese===
「掺和了。」拉思文说。

「料到了。还有谁？」

「没谁了。」

「再想想。」

「我跟你说没有别人了。人说的话你一个字都不能信吗？」

「偶尔一个字兴许能，」克劳斯说，一副让步的架势，「但不多，这一个不在其列。再猜一回。」

拉思文重新陷入沉默。

「那好，」克劳斯说，「这句话我们收下了。它没准儿还真有几分真。我想也就到此为止了吧。这事本来压根儿与我无关，是你的事，特雷弗。我不过是个看客兼随军小贩。这是你的买卖。到书房找我。」说罢他把拨火棍仔细放回原位，出了屋。他回到自己书房，试图开始用功，可修昔底德（Thucydides）第二卷的妙处引不起他的兴致。他的心在别处。刚发生的事让他兴奋得译不进希腊文。他拉了把椅子到炉火跟前，一门心思揣测隔壁书房里特雷弗进展如何。把收尾留给特雷弗一个人，他为此暗自高兴。倘若换了是他，天下最叫他厌恶的事，莫过于有人——哪怕是再熟的朋友——插手他的战事、替他收官。若是任着克劳斯来，这场谈话多半会以把拉思文踢成最接近肉泥、而又不逾越过失杀人法条的形态告终。他隐隐有种不安的预感：特雷弗会把那人放过得太便宜。

===Original===
The handle turned. Trevor came in, and pulled up another chair in silence. His face wore a look of disgust. But there were no signs of combat upon him. The toe of his boot was not worn and battered, as Clowes would have liked to have seen it. Evidently he had not chosen to adopt active and physical measures for the improvement of Ruthven’s moral well-being.

“Well?” said Clowes.

“My word, what a hound!” breathed Trevor, half to himself.

“My sentiments to a hair,” said Clowes, approvingly. “But what have you done?”

“I didn’t do anything.”

“I was afraid you wouldn’t. Did he give any explanation? What made him go in for the thing at all? What earthly motive could he have for not wanting Barry to get his colours, bar the fact that Rand-Brown didn’t want him to? And why should he do what Rand-Brown told him? I never even knew they were pals, before today.”

“He told me a good deal,” said Trevor. “It’s one of the beastliest things I ever heard. They neither of them come particularly well out of the business, but Rand-Brown comes worse out of it even than Ruthven. My word, that man wants killing.”

“That’ll keep,” said Clowes, nodding. “What’s the yarn?”

===Chinese===
门把手一转，特雷弗进来，默默拖过另一把椅子。他脸上一副厌恶的神情，身上却不见交战的痕迹。他的靴尖没有克劳斯乐见的那种磨损和凹陷。显然，他没有选择采取积极而物理的措施来促进拉思文的道德健康。

「怎么样？」克劳斯说。

「好家伙，好一条恶狗！」特雷弗半是自言自语地吐出一句。

「跟我心思分毫不差，」克劳斯嘉许道，「可你是怎么处置的？」

「我什么也没做。」

「我就怕你什么都不做。他给解释了吗？他到底为什么要掺和这事？不想让巴里拿队色，他还能有什么屁动机——除了兰德-布朗不想让他拿？再说他凭什么对兰德-布朗言听计从？今天以前，我压根不知道他们俩是朋友。」

「他倒是说了不少，」特雷弗说，「那是我平生听过的最下作的事情之一。两个人在这事里都不见得体面，可兰德-布朗比拉思文还要难看。好家伙，那人真该杀。」

「那事不急，」克劳斯点头道，「到底怎么回事，说来听听？」

===Original===
“Do you remember about a year ago a chap named Patterson getting sacked?”

Clowes nodded again. He remembered the case well. Patterson had had gambling transactions with a Wrykyn tradesman, had been found out, and had gone.

“You remember what a surprise it was to everybody. It wasn’t one of those cases where half the school suspects what’s going on. Those cases always come out sooner or later. But Patterson nobody knew about.”

“Yes. Well?”

“Nobody,” said Trevor, “except Ruthven, that is. Ruthven got to know somehow. I believe he was a bit of a pal of Patterson’s at the time. Anyhow—they had a row, and Ruthven went to Dexter—Patterson was in Dexter’s—and sneaked. Dexter promised to keep his name out of the business, and went straight to the Old Man, and Patterson got turfed out on the spot. Then somehow or other Rand-Brown got to know about it—I believe Ruthven must have told him by accident some time or other. After that he simply had to do everything Rand-Brown wanted him to. Otherwise he said that he would tell the chaps about the Patterson affair. That put Ruthven in a dead funk.”

“Of course,” said Clowes; “I should imagine friend Ruthven would have got rather a bad time of it. But what made them think of starting the League? It was a jolly smart idea. Rand-Brown’s, of course?”

“Yes. I suppose he’d heard about it, and thought something might be made out of it if it were revived.”

===Chinese===
「你还记得大约一年前，有个叫帕特森（Patterson）的被开除吗？」

克劳斯又点点头。那案子他记得清楚。帕特森同一个里金商人有过赌博交易，被查了出来，走人了。

「你记得当时大家多么意外。那不属于半所学校都有所风闻的那类案子。那类案子迟早东窗事发。可帕特森这事，谁都不知道。」

「记得。后来呢？」

「谁都不知道，」特雷弗说，「——也就是说，除了拉思文。拉思文不知怎么知道了。我相信他当时跟帕特森还有点交情。反正——两人吵了一架，拉思文就去找德克斯特——帕特森是德克斯特宿舍的——告了密。德克斯特答应不把他的名字牵进此事，转身就去找老头子，帕特森当场被扫地出门。后来不知怎么的，兰德-布朗又知道了这事——我看是拉思文哪回不留神说漏了嘴。从那以后，兰德-布朗要他干什么，他就只能干什么。不然他扬言要把帕特森那桩事捅给大伙儿。这可把拉思文吓了个半死。」

「这就对了，」克劳斯说，「可以想见，咱们的拉思文朋友日子不会好过。可他们怎么会想到把联盟办起来？这主意妙得很。自然是兰德-布朗出的？」

「是。我猜他听人讲过旧事，觉得让它复活一遭还能榨出点名堂。」

===Original===
“And were Ruthven and he the only two in it?”

“Ruthven swears they were, and I shouldn’t wonder if he wasn’t telling the truth, for once in his life. You see, everything the League’s done so far could have been done by him and Rand-Brown, without anybody else’s help. The only other studies that were ragged were Mill’s and Milton’s—both in Seymour’s.

“Yes,” said Clowes.

There was a pause. Clowes put another shovelful of coal on the fire.

“What are you going to do to Ruthven?”

“Nothing.”

“Nothing? Hang it, he doesn’t deserve to get off like that. He isn’t as bad as Rand-Brown—quite—but he’s pretty nearly as finished a little beast as you could find.”

“Finished is just the word,” said Trevor. “He’s going at the end of the week.”

“Going? What! sacked?”

“Yes. The Old Man’s been finding out things about him, apparently, and this smoking row has just added the finishing-touch to his discoveries. He’s particularly keen against smoking just now for some reason.”

===Chinese===
「联盟里就他们两个？」

「拉思文发誓只有他们俩，而这回他倒没准儿平生头一次说了实话。你想，联盟到目前为止干的每一件事，都他跟兰德-布朗两个人就干得来，用不着别人搭手。此外被抄的书房就米尔的和弥尔顿的——都在西摩宿舍。」

「嗯。」克劳斯说。

一阵沉默。克劳斯往炉子里又添了一铲煤。

「你打算怎么处置拉思文？」

「不处置。」

「不处置？见鬼，他可不该就这么脱身。他是没有兰德-布朗那么坏——差一点——可他也是打着灯笼难找的、彻头彻尾的小畜生。」

「彻头彻尾这词用得正好，」特雷弗说，「他周末就走。」

「走？什么！被开除了？」

「是。看样子老头子一直在查他的事，这场吸烟风波恰好给他查到的东西补上了点睛一笔。不知为什么，他眼下对吸烟尤其深恶痛绝。」

===Original===
“But was Ruthven in it?”

“Yes. Didn’t I tell you? He was one of the fellows Dexter caught in the vault. There were two in this house, you remember?”

“Who was the other?”

“That man Dashwood. Has the study next to Paget’s old one. He’s going, too.”

“Scarcely knew him. What sort of a chap was he?”

“Outsider. No good to the house in any way. He won’t be missed.”

“And what are you going to do about Rand-Brown?”

“Fight him, of course. What else could I do?”

===Chinese===
「可拉思文也在其中？」

「在。我没跟你说过吗？他就是德克斯特在地窖里逮住的其中一个。咱们宿舍占了两个，记得吗？」

「另一个是谁？」

「那个达什伍德（Dashwood）。书房在佩吉特旧书房隔壁。他也要走。」

「几乎不认识。他是个什么样的人？」

「局外人。对宿舍毫无用处。走了也没人会想他。」

「那兰德-布朗你打算怎么办？」

「跟他打一架，当然。不然还能怎么办？」

===Original===
“But you’re no match for him.”

“We’ll see.”

“But you *aren’t*,” persisted Clowes. “He can give you a stone easily, and he’s not a bad boxer either. Moriarty didn’t beat him so very cheaply in the middleweight this year. You wouldn’t have a chance.”

Trevor flared up.

“Heavens, man,” he cried, “do you think I don’t know all that myself? But what on earth would you have me do? Besides, he may be a good boxer, but he’s got no pluck at all. I might outstay him.”

“Hope so,” said Clowes.

But his tone was not hopeful.

===Chinese===
「可你不是他的对手。」

「走着瞧。」

「可你*就是*不是，」克劳斯不改口，「他轻轻松松就能让你一石体重，拳击也不含糊。今年中量级上莫里亚蒂赢他都赢得不轻松。你没有胜算。」

特雷弗火了。

「天哪，伙计，」他嚷道，「你以为这些我自己不知道？可你到底要我怎么办？再说，他拳或许打得好，可他毫无胆气。我兴许能耗得过他。」

「但愿如此。」克劳斯说。

可他的口气毫无指望。

## IV The League’s Warning / 四、联盟的警告

===Original===
The team to play in any match was always put upon the notice-board at the foot of the stairs in the senior block a day before the date of the fixture. Both first and second fifteens had matches on the Thursday of this week. The second were playing a team brought down by an old Wrykinian. The first had a scratch game.

When Barry, accompanied by McTodd, who shared his study at Seymour’s and rarely left him for two minutes on end, passed by the notice-board at the quarter to eleven interval, it was to the second fifteen list that he turned his attention. Now that Bryce had left, he thought he might have a chance of getting into the second. His only real rival, he considered, was Crawford, of the School House, who was the other wing three-quarter of the third fifteen. The first name he saw on the list was Crawford’s. It seemed to be written twice as large as any of the others, and his own was nowhere to be seen. The fact that he had half expected the calamity made things no better. He had set his heart on playing for the second this term.

Then suddenly he noticed a remarkable phenomenon. The other wing three-quarter was Rand-Brown. If Rand-Brown was playing for the second, who was playing for the first?

===Chinese===
凡有比赛，出场队伍总在赛前一天张贴在高年级教学楼楼梯脚的布告栏上。这个星期四，一队和二队都有比赛。二队的对手是一位老里金人带来的一支队伍。一队打一场练习赛。

十点三刻课间，巴里路过布告栏，身边跟着麦克托德（McTodd）——此君与他合用西摩宿舍的书房，几乎从没连续离开他两分钟——他的注意力首先落在二队名单上。如今布莱斯走了，他觉得自己或许有机会进二队。他认定自己唯一真正的对手是学校宿舍的克劳福德（Crawford），即三队的另一名翼锋。他在名单上看到的头一个名字就是克劳福德的，那名字仿佛写得比谁都大上一倍，而他自己则遍寻不见。这场灾祸他本是半预料到的，可这丝毫没让他好受些。这学期他一心巴望着进二队。

突然，他注意到了一个异乎寻常的现象。另一名翼锋竟是兰德-布朗。兰德-布朗打二队了，那一队上的是谁？

===Original===
He looked at the list.

“*Come* on,” he said hastily to McTodd. He wanted to get away somewhere where his agitated condition would not be noticed. He felt quite faint at the shock of seeing his name on the list of the first fifteen. There it was, however, as large as life. “M. Barry.” Separated from the rest by a thin red line, but still there. In his most optimistic moments he had never dreamed of this. McTodd was reading slowly through the list of the second. He did everything slowly, except eating.

“Come on,” said Barry again.

McTodd had, after much deliberation, arrived at a profound truth. He turned to Barry, and imparted his discovery to him in the weighty manner of one who realises the importance of his words.

“Look here,” he said, “your name’s not down here.”

“I know. *Come* on.”

“But that means you’re not playing for the second.”

===Chinese===
他看那张一队名单。

「*快*走。」他匆匆对麦克托德说。他想找个不惹人注意的地方去平复自己的失态。看见自己的名字赫然列在一队名单上，那一惊让他几乎晕眩。可它确确实实在那儿，如假包换。「M. 巴里」。跟其余人名隔着一道细细的红线，可到底在那儿。他最乐观的时刻也做梦没梦见过这个。麦克托德还在慢慢吞吞地读二队名单。他干什么都慢，唯独吃除外。

「快走。」巴里又说了一遍。

麦克托德反复斟酌之后，抵达了一条深刻的真理。他转向巴里，以一个深知自己字字千钧的人的郑重态度，把这一发现传授给他。

「你瞧，」他说，「这儿没有你的名字。」

「我知道。*快*走。」

「可那就说明你不打二队了。」

===Original===
“Of course it does. Well, if you aren’t coming, I’m off.”

“But, look here—”

Barry disappeared through the door. After a moment’s pause, McTodd followed him. He came up with him on the senior gravel.

“What’s up?” he inquired.

“Nothing,” said Barry.

“Are you sick about not playing for the second?”

“No.”

“You are, really. Come and have a bun.”

In the philosophy of McTodd it was indeed a deep-rooted sorrow that could not be cured by the internal application of a new, hot bun. It had never failed in his own case.

===Chinese===
「当然说明。你要是不来，我自己走了。」

「可是，你瞧——」

巴里一阵风似的出了门。麦克托德顿了片刻，跟了上去，在高年级砾石场上追上了他。

「怎么啦？」他探问。

「没事。」巴里说。

「是进不了二队，心里难受？」

「不是。」

「你就是。来，吃个甜面包去。」

按麦克托德的哲学，凡是内服一个滚热的新鲜甜面包治不好的悲伤，都算不得根深蒂固。至少在他自己身上，此方从无失手。

===Original===
“Bun!” Barry was quite shocked at the suggestion. “I can’t afford to get myself out of condition with beastly buns.”

“But if you aren’t playing—”

“You ass. I’m playing for the first. Now, do you see?”

McTodd gaped. His mind never worked very rapidly. “What about Rand-Brown, then?” he said.

“Rand-Brown’s been chucked out. Can’t you understand? You *are* an idiot. Rand-Brown’s playing for the second, and I’m playing for the first.”

“But you’re—”

He stopped. He had been going to point out that Barry’s tender years—he was only sixteen—and smallness would make it impossible for him to play with success for the first fifteen. He refrained owing to a conviction that the remark would not be wholly judicious. Barry was touchy on the subject of his size, and McTodd had suffered before now for commenting on it in a disparaging spirit.

===Chinese===
「甜面包！」巴里对这个提议大感震惊，「我可不能让该死的甜面包毁了状态。」

「可你要是不打二队——」

「你这个笨蛋。我打的是一队。这下明白了吗？」

麦克托德目瞪口呆。他的脑子向来转得不快。「那兰德-布朗怎么办？」他说。

「兰德-布朗被踢出去啦。你到底明不明白？你*真是*个白痴。兰德-布朗打二队，我打一队。」

「可你——」

他收住了。他本想指出，巴里年纪稚嫩——他只有十六岁——个头又小，想在一队打出名堂是不可能的。但他忍住了，因为他确信这话未免欠考虑。巴里对自己的块头讳莫如深，麦克托德从前就为出言贬损这一点付出过代价。

===Original===
“I tell you what we’ll do after school,” said Barry, “we’ll have some running and passing. It’ll do you a lot of good, and I want to practise taking passes at full speed. You can trot along at your ordinary pace, and I’ll sprint up from behind.”

McTodd saw no objection to that. Trotting along at his ordinary pace—five miles an hour—would just suit him.

“Then after that,” continued Barry, with a look of enthusiasm, “I want to practise passing back to my centre. Paget used to do it awfully well last term, and I know Trevor expects his wing to. So I’ll buck along, and you race up to take my pass. See?”

This was not in McTodd’s line at all. He proposed a slight alteration in the scheme.

“Hadn’t you better get somebody else—?” he began.

“Don’t be a slack beast,” said Barry. “You want exercise awfully badly.”

And, as McTodd always did exactly as Barry wished, he gave in, and spent from four-thirty to five that afternoon in the prescribed manner. A suggestion on his part at five sharp that it wouldn’t be a bad idea to go and have some tea was not favourably received by the enthusiastic three-quarter, who proposed to devote what time remained before lockup to practising drop-kicking. It was a painful alternative that faced McTodd. His allegiance to Barry demanded that he should consent to the scheme. On the other hand, his allegiance to afternoon tea—equally strong—called him back to the house, where there was cake, and also muffins. In the end the question was solved by the appearance of Drummond, of Seymour’s, garbed in football things, and also anxious to practise drop-kicking. So McTodd was dismissed to his tea with opprobrious epithets, and Barry and Drummond settled down to a little serious and scientific work.

===Chinese===
「我说放学后咱们这么着，」巴里说，「练练跑动传球。这对你大有好处，我也要练全速接球。你按平常的步子小跑，我从后面冲刺追上去。」

麦克托德对此并无异议。按平常的步子小跑——时速五英里——正合他意。

「然后练完那个，」巴里满怀热情地接着说，「我要练回传给中锋。佩吉特上学期这手干得漂亮极了，我知道特雷弗也指望他的翼锋这么干。所以我往前冲，你跑上来接我的传球。明白？」

这可完全不在麦克托德的行当里。他提议对方案略作修改。

「你是不是最好另找个人——？」他开口道。

「别这么懒骨头，」巴里说，「你缺运动缺得厉害。」

于是，鉴于麦克托德向来对巴里言听计从，他让了步，那天下午四点半到五点便照章操练。五点整，他提议去喝点茶也不失为好主意，却没有得到那位热忱的三分卫的嘉许——后者打算把锁门前剩下的时光全部奉献给落踢练习。麦克托德面临一个痛苦的抉择：他对巴里的忠诚要求他应下这个方案；可他对下午茶——忠心同样坚定——的忠诚又召唤他回宿舍去，那儿有蛋糕，还有松饼。最后，问题靠德拉蒙德的出现解决了：这位西摩宿舍的人一身橄榄球装束，也急着练落踢。于是麦克托德顶着几句难听话被打发去喝茶，巴里和德拉蒙德埋头干起了一点严肃而科学的工作。

===Original===
Making allowances for the inevitable attack of nerves that attends a first appearance in higher football circles than one is accustomed to, Barry did well against the scratch team—certainly far better than Rand-Brown had done. His smallness was, of course, against him, and, on the only occasion on which he really got away, Paget overtook him and brought him down. But then Paget was exceptionally fast. In the two most important branches of the game, the taking of passes and tackling, Barry did well. As far as pluck went he had enough for two, and when the whistle blew for no-side he had not let Paget through once, and Trevor felt that his inclusion in the team had been justified. There was another scratch game on the Saturday. Barry played in it, and did much better. Paget had gone away by an early train, and the man he had to mark now was one of the masters, who had been good in his time, but was getting a trifle old for football. Barry scored twice, and on one occasion, by passing back to Trevor after the manner of Paget, enabled the captain to run in. And Trevor, like the captain in “Billy Taylor,” “werry much approved of what he’d done.” Barry began to be regarded in the school as a regular member of the fifteen. The first of the fixture-card matches, versus the Town, was due on the following Saturday, and it was generally expected that he would play. McTodd’s devotion increased every day. He even went to the length of taking long runs with him. And if there was one thing in the world that McTodd loathed, it was a long run.

===Chinese===
把初次登上高出自己惯常级别的橄榄球圈子时难免的紧张计算在内，巴里对那支临时队打得不错——肯定远比兰德-布朗打得好。他的小块头当然拖累他，他唯一真正挣脱开的那么一回，也被佩吉特追上放倒。可佩吉特本来就快得出奇。在这项运动最重要的两个环节——接球和擒抱——上，巴里干得漂亮。论胆气，他一个人够两个人用；终场哨响时，他一次也没放过佩吉特，特雷弗觉得把他招进队里是对的。星期六又有一场练习赛。巴里上场，打得好得多。佩吉特已搭早班车走了，这回他盯的是一位教员——此公当年也是好手，只是打橄榄球年纪略嫌大了些。巴里两度达阵，其中一回还照佩吉特的路数回传特雷弗，送队长一蹴而就。特雷弗呢，跟《比利·泰勒》（Billy Taylor）里的船长一样，「打心眼里赞成他干的事」。全校渐渐把巴里看作一队的正式成员。赛程卡上的第一场比赛——对镇队——定在下一个星期六，大家普遍认为他会出场。麦克托德的忠心与日俱增，甚至不惜陪他长跑。而天下若说还有一样麦克托德深恶痛绝的东西，那就是长跑。

===Original===
On the Thursday before the match against the Town, Clowes came chuckling to Trevor’s study after preparation, and asked him if he had heard the latest.

“Have you ever heard of the League?” he said.

Trevor pondered.

“I don’t think so,” he replied.

“How long have you been at the school?”

“Let’s see. It’ll be five years at the end of the summer term.”

“Ah, then you wouldn’t remember. I’ve been here a couple of terms longer than you, and the row about the League was in my first term.”

“What was the row?”

“Oh, only some chaps formed a sort of secret society in the place. Kind of Vehmgericht, you know. If they got their knife into anyone, he usually got beans, and could never find out where they came from. At first, as a matter of fact, the thing was quite a philanthropical concern. There used to be a good deal of bullying in the place then—at least, in some of the houses—and, as the prefects couldn’t or wouldn’t stop it, some fellows started this League.”

===Chinese===
对镇队比赛前的那个星期四，晚自习后，克劳斯窃笑着来到特雷弗的书房，问他听说了最新消息没有。

「你听说过联盟吗？」他说。

特雷弗想了想。

「没有印象。」他答道。

「你来学校几年了？」

「我算算。到夏季学期末就满五年。」

「啊，那你记不得。我比你早来两个学期，联盟那场风波就闹在我头一个学期。」

「什么风波？」

「哦，不过是几个人在这儿搞了个秘密社团。有点菲默法庭（Vehmgericht）的意思，懂吧。他们跟谁结了仇，那人多半要吃苦头，还永远查不出来路。其实这组织起初完全是家慈善机构。那时候学校里欺凌之事不少——至少有几个宿舍如此——级长们管不了也不肯管，于是便有人创办了这个联盟。」

===Original===
“Did it work?”

“Work! By Jove, I should think it did. Chaps who previously couldn’t get through the day without making some wretched kid’s life not worth living used to go about as nervous as cats, looking over their shoulders every other second. There was one man in particular, a chap called Leigh. He was hauled out of bed one night, blindfolded, and ducked in a cold bath. He was in the School House.”

“Why did the League bust up?”

“Well, partly because the fellows left, but chiefly because they didn’t stick to the philanthropist idea. If anybody did anything they didn’t like, they used to go for him. At last they put their foot into it badly. A chap called Robinson—in this house by the way—offended them in some way, and one morning he was found tied up in the bath, up to his neck in cold water. Apparently he’d been there about an hour. He got pneumonia, and almost died, and then the authorities began to get going. Robinson thought he had recognised the voice of one of the chaps—I forget his name. The chap was had up by the Old Man, and gave the show away entirely. About a dozen fellows were sacked, clean off the reel. Since then the thing has been dropped.”

===Chinese===
「管用吗？」

「管用！天哪，岂止管用。先前那些一天不把哪个倒霉孩子的生活搅得生不如死就过不完日子的人，如今个个成了惊弓之猫，隔一秒就回头张望一回。有一个尤其突出的，叫利（Leigh）的家伙。一天夜里他被从床上拖起来，蒙上眼睛，按进冷水澡盆里浸了个透。他是学校宿舍的。」

「那联盟怎么散伙的？」

「一来是人手散了，主要还是他们没守住慈善家的初心。谁干了他们看不顺眼的事，他们就动手收拾谁。到头来狠狠栽了个跟头。一个姓罗宾逊的家伙——顺便说一句，就在咱们宿舍——不知怎么得罪了他们，一天早晨人们发现他被捆在澡盆里，冷水齐脖子深。看样子泡了约莫一个钟头。他得了肺炎，险些丧命，校方这才动了真格。罗宾逊觉得他认出了其中一人的嗓音——名字我忘了。那人被老头子提了去，把整台戏和盘托出。大约十来个人被开除，一个不落。打那以后，这事就黄了。」

===Original===
“But what about it? What were you going to say when you came in?”

“Why, it’s been revived!”

“Rot!”

“It’s a fact. Do you know Mill, a prefect, in Seymour’s?”

“Only by sight.”

“I met him just now. He’s in a raving condition. His study’s been wrecked. You never saw such a sight. Everything upside down or smashed. He has been showing me the ruins.”

“I believe Mill is awfully barred in Seymour’s,” said Trevor. “Anybody might have ragged his study.”

“That’s just what I thought. He’s just the sort of man the League used to go for.”

“That doesn’t prove that it’s been revived, all the same,” objected Trevor.

“No, friend; but this does. Mill found it tied to a chair.”

===Chinese===
「可这跟你有什么相干？你进门时想说什么来着？」

「嘿，它复活啦！」

「胡扯！」

「千真万确。你认识西摩宿舍那个级长米尔吗？」

「只面熟。」

「我刚碰见他。他气得快疯了。他的书房被砸了。你没见过那副惨状。样样东西不是底朝天就是稀巴烂。他刚领我参观了废墟。」

「我记得米尔在西摩宿舍人缘极差，」特雷弗说，「谁都可能抄他的书房。」

「我也正是这么想的。他就是联盟当年专爱收拾的那号人。」

「可这照样证明不了它复活了。」特雷弗反驳道。

「证明不了，朋友；可这个能。米尔发现它拴在椅子上。」

===Original===
It was a small card. It looked like an ordinary visiting card. On it, in neat print, were the words, “*With the compliments of the League.*”

“That’s exactly the same sort of card as they used to use,” said Clowes. “I’ve seen some of them. What do you think of that?”

“I think whoever has started the thing is a pretty average-sized idiot. He’s bound to get caught some time or other, and then out he goes. The Old Man wouldn’t think twice about sacking a chap of that sort.”

“A chap of that sort,” said Clowes, “will take jolly good care he isn’t caught. But it’s rather sport, isn’t it?”

And he went off to his study.

Next day there was further evidence that the League was an actual going concern. When Trevor came down to breakfast, he found a letter by his plate. It was printed, as the card had been. It was signed “The President of the League.” And the purport of it was that the League did not wish Barry to continue to play for the first fifteen.

===Chinese===
那是一张小卡片，模样像张普通的拜访名片。上面用整齐的印刷体印着一行字：「*联盟敬赠。*」

「跟他们当年用的正是同一种卡片，」克劳斯说，「我见过几张。这个你怎么看？」

「我认为不管是谁挑的头，都算得上一个中规中矩的蠢材。他早晚要被逮住，到时候卷铺盖走人。开除了这号人，老头子连眼都不会眨一下。」

「这号人呢，」克劳斯说，「会加倍小心不让自己被抓。不过这事挺带劲的，对不对？」

说罢他回自己书房去了。

第二天，又有了新的证据，证明联盟确实是一家营业中的实体。特雷弗下楼吃早饭时，在自己盘子旁发现一封信。信和那张卡片一样是印刷体。落款是「联盟会长」。信的大意是：联盟不希望巴里继续代表一队出场。

## III The Mayor’s Statue / 三、市长的雕像

===Original===
One of the rules that governed the life of Donough O’Hara, the lighthearted descendant of the O’Haras of Castle Taterfields, Co. Clare, Ireland, was “Never refuse the offer of a free tea.” So, on receipt—per the Dexter’s fag referred to—of Trevor’s invitation, he scratched one engagement (with his mathematical master—not wholly unconnected with the working-out of Examples 200 to 206 in Hall and Knight’s Algebra), postponed another (with his friend and ally Moriarty, of Dexter’s, who wished to box with him in the gymnasium), and made his way at a leisurely pace towards Donaldson’s. He was feeling particularly pleased with himself today, for several reasons. He had begun the day well by scoring brilliantly off Mr. Dexter across the matutinal rasher and coffee. In morning school he had been put on to translate the one passage which he happened to have prepared—the first ten lines, in fact, of the hundred which formed the morning’s lesson. And in the final hour of afternoon school, which was devoted to French, he had discovered and exploited with great success an entirely new and original form of ragging. This, he felt, was the strenuous life; this was living one’s life as one’s life should be lived.

===Chinese===
支配着多诺·奥哈拉（Donough O’Hara）——爱尔兰克莱尔郡塔特菲尔德堡奥哈拉家的乐天后裔——人生的原则之一，是「白给的茶局，永远别推」。于是，经由前述那位德克斯特宿舍跑腿生之手收到特雷弗的邀约之后，他勾掉了一桩约会（同他的数学教员——与演算霍尔与奈特《代数》（Hall and Knight’s Algebra）第 200 至 206 题并非全无干系），又推迟了另一桩（同他的朋友兼战友、德克斯特宿舍的莫里亚蒂，后者想同他在体育馆练拳），然后优哉游哉地朝唐纳森宿舍踱去。他今天自我感觉格外良好，理由有好几条。这一天开局开得好：晨间熏肉和咖啡之间，他把德克斯特先生抢白得体无完肤。上午课上，他被叫起来翻译的恰好是他备了课的那一段——那天功课共一百行，他备的正是头十行。而下午最后一节是法语课，他在课上发现并成功地运用了一种全新的、原创的捣乱形式。他觉得，这才叫奋发的生活；这才叫把日子过成日子本来该有的样子。

===Original===
He met Trevor at the gate. As they were going in, a carriage and pair dashed past. Its cargo consisted of two people, the headmaster, looking bored, and a small, dapper man, with a very red face, who looked excited, and was talking volubly. Trevor and O’Hara raised their caps as the chariot swept by, but the salute passed unnoticed. The Head appeared to be wrapped in thought.

“What’s the Old Man doing in a carriage, I wonder,” said Trevor, looking after them. “Who’s that with him?”

“That,” said O’Hara, “is Sir Eustace Briggs.”

“Who’s Sir Eustace Briggs?”

O’Hara explained, in a rich brogue, that Sir Eustace was Mayor of Wrykyn, a keen politician, and a hater of the Irish nation, judging by his letters and speeches.

===Chinese===
他在大门口碰见特雷弗。两人正往里走，一辆双马马车疾驰而过。车上载着两个人：一位是满脸无聊的校长，另一位是个矮小利落、脸膛通红的人，一脸兴奋，正滔滔不绝地说着话。马车掠过时，特雷弗和奥哈拉举帽致意，致敬却无人领受。校长似乎沉入了深思。

「真不明白老头子坐马车干什么，」特雷弗目送着他们说，「跟他一块儿的那位是谁？」

「那位，」奥哈拉说，「是尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士（Sir Eustace Briggs）。」

「尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士是谁？」

奥哈拉用浓重的爱尔兰土音解释说，尤斯塔斯爵士是里金市长，热衷政治，而且——从他的文章和演说来看——憎恨爱尔兰民族。

===Original===
They went into Trevor’s study. Clowes was occupying the window in his usual manner.

“Hullo, O’Hara,” he said, “there is an air of quiet satisfaction about you that seems to show that you’ve been ragging Dexter. Have you?”

“Oh, that was only this morning at breakfast. The best rag was in French,” replied O’Hara, who then proceeded to explain in detail the methods he had employed to embitter the existence of the hapless Gallic exile with whom he had come in contact. It was that gentleman’s custom to sit on a certain desk while conducting the lesson. This desk chanced to be O’Hara’s. On the principle that a man may do what he likes with his own, he had entered the room privily in the dinner-hour, and removed the screws from his desk, with the result that for the first half-hour of the lesson the class had been occupied in excavating M. Gandinois from the ruins. That gentleman’s first act on regaining his equilibrium had been to send O’Hara out of the room, and O’Hara, who had foreseen this emergency, had spent a very pleasant half-hour in the passage with some mixed chocolates and a copy of Mr. Hornung’s *Amateur Cracksman*. It was his notion of a cheerful and instructive French lesson.

===Chinese===
他们进了特雷弗的书房。克劳斯正以惯常的姿势占据着窗户。

「你好啊，奥哈拉，」他说，「你周身透着一股安详的得意，像是刚捉弄过德克斯特。捉弄了吗？」

「哦，那不过是今天早上餐桌上的小意思。最精彩的一手在法语课上。」奥哈拉答道，随即详细讲解了他是用什么手段，把落到他手上的那位倒霉的高卢流亡者的日子搞苦的。那位先生授课时习惯坐在某张课桌上，而这张课桌碰巧是奥哈拉的。本着「自己的东西任凭自己处置」的原则，他趁午饭时间潜入教室，把自己课桌上的螺丝全拧了下来，结果那堂课的前半个钟头，全班都忙着从废墟里把甘迪努瓦先生（M. Gandinois）刨出来。那位先生站稳之后的第一件事，就是把奥哈拉轰出教室，而早有先见之明的奥哈拉，则在走廊里就着几颗什锦巧克力和一册霍恩先生的《业余神偷》（*Amateur Cracksman*），愉快地消磨了半个钟头。这就是他心目中一堂愉快而有教益的法语课。

===Original===
“What were you talking about when you came in?” asked Clowes. “Who’s been slanging Ireland, O’Hara?”

“The man Briggs.”

“What are you going to do about it? Aren’t you going to take any steps?”

“Is it steps?” said O’Hara, warmly, “and haven’t we—”

He stopped.

“Well?”

“Ye know,” he said, seriously, “ye mustn’t let it go any further. I shall get sacked if it’s found out. An’ so will Moriarty, too.”

“Why?” asked Trevor, looking up from the teapot he was filling, “what on earth have you been doing?”

“Wouldn’t it be rather a cheery idea,” suggested Clowes, “if you began at the beginning.”

“Well, ye see,” O’Hara began, “it was this way. The first I heard of it was from Dexter. He was trying to score off me as usual, an’ he said, ‘Have ye seen the paper this morning, O’Hara?’ I said, no, I had not. Then he said, ‘Ah,’ he said, ‘ye should look at it. There’s something there that ye’ll find interesting.’ I said, ‘Yes, sir?’ in me respectful way. ‘Yes,’ said he, ‘the Irish members have been making their customary disturbances in the House. Why is it, O’Hara,’ he said, ‘that Irishmen are always thrusting themselves forward and making disturbances for purposes of self-advertisement?’ ‘Why, indeed, sir?’ said I, not knowing what else to say, and after that the conversation ceased.”

===Chinese===
「你们进门时在谈什么？」克劳斯问，「谁骂爱尔兰了，奥哈拉？」

「布里格斯那家伙。」

「你们打算怎么办？不准备采取点措施吗？」

「措施？」奥哈拉热切地说，「俺们岂止——」

他停住了。

「嗯？」

「你们知道，」他正色道，「这事可不能再往外传。一旦查出来，俺要被开除的。莫里亚蒂也一样。」

「为什么？」特雷弗从正灌着的茶壶上抬起眼来问，「你们到底干了什么？」

「要是你从头讲起，」克劳斯建议道，「岂不更叫人愉快。」

「呃，是这么回事，」奥哈拉开讲，「事情是这样的。俺头一回听说这事是从德克斯特嘴里。他照例想占俺的上风，他说：『你看过今天早上的报纸没有，奥哈拉？』俺说没有，没看过。他说：『啊，』他说，『你该看看。上面有你觉得有趣的东西。』俺恭恭敬敬地说：『是吗，先生？』『是的，』他说，『爱尔兰议员们又在议会里照例大闹了一场。奥哈拉，这是为什么，』他说，『爱尔兰人总是这样往前蹿，为自我炒作而大闹特闹？』『可不是嘛，先生？』俺说，一时不知说什么好，此后谈话就结束了。」

===Original===
“Go on,” said Clowes.

“After breakfast Moriarty came to me with a paper, and showed me what they had been saying about the Irish. There was a letter from the man Briggs on the subject. ‘A very sensible and temperate letter from Sir Eustace Briggs,’ they called it, but bedad! if that was a temperate letter, I should like to know what an intemperate one is. Well, we read it through, and Moriarty said to me, ‘Can we let this stay as it is?’ And I said, ‘No. We can’t.’ ‘Well,’ said Moriarty to me, ‘what are we to do about it? I should like to tar and feather the man,’ he said. ‘We can’t do that,’ I said, ‘but why not tar and feather his statue?’ I said. So we thought we would. Ye know where the statue is, I suppose? It’s in the recreation ground just across the river.”

“I know the place,” said Clowes. “Go on. This is ripping. I always knew you were pretty mad, but this sounds as if it were going to beat all previous records.”

“Have ye seen the baths this term,” continued O’Hara, “since they shifted Dexter’s house into them? The beds are in two long rows along each wall. Moriarty’s and mine are the last two at the end farthest from the door.”

“Just under the gallery,” said Trevor. “I see.”

“That’s it. Well, at half-past ten sharp every night Dexter sees that we’re all in, locks the door, and goes off to sleep at the Old Man’s, and we don’t see him again till breakfast. He turns the gas off from outside. At half-past seven the next morning, Smith”—Smith was one of the school porters—“unlocks the door and calls us, and we go over to the Hall to breakfast.”

===Chinese===
「接着讲。」克劳斯说。

「早饭后莫里亚蒂拿着张报纸来找俺，让俺看他们是怎么写爱尔兰人的。里头有布里格斯那家伙就这个问题写的一封信。他们管它叫『尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士一封十分理智而克制的信』，可天晓得！那要算克制的信，俺倒想知道不克制的是什么样。呃，我们从头到尾读了一遍，莫里亚蒂对俺说：『这事能就这么算了？』俺说：『不能。算不了。』『那，』莫里亚蒂说，『咱们怎么办？俺恨不得给那家伙涂柏油、粘羽毛。』『那可不行，』俺说，『可干吗不给他雕像涂柏油、粘羽毛？』俺说。于是我们决定就这么办。雕像在哪儿你们知道吧？就在河对面的休闲广场里。」

「我知道那地方，」克劳斯说，「接着讲。太精彩了。我一向知道你够疯，可这事听起来要破所有的历史纪录了。」

「你们这学期去过浴场没有，」奥哈拉接着说，「自从他们把德克斯特宿舍搬进去以后？床沿两面墙排成长长的两行。莫里亚蒂和俺的在最里头、离门最远那端的最后两张。」

「就在楼廊底下。」特雷弗说，「明白了。」

「正是。呃，每天晚上十点半整，德克斯特看着我们都上了床，锁上门，到老头子家睡觉去，早饭前再不见他的人影。他从外头把煤气关掉。第二天早上七点半，史密斯（Smith）」——史密斯是学校门房之一——「开门叫醒我们，我们就到饭厅吃早饭去。」

===Original===
“Well?”

“Well, directly everybody was asleep last night—it wasn’t till after one, as there was a rag on—Moriarty and I got up, dressed, and climbed up into the gallery. Ye know the gallery windows? They open at the top, an’ it’s rather hard to get out of them. But we managed it, and dropped on to the gravel outside.”

“Long drop,” said Clowes.

“Yes. I hurt myself rather. But it was in a good cause. I dropped first, and while I was on the ground, Moriarty came on top of me. That’s how I got hurt. But it wasn’t much, and we cut across the grounds, and over the fence, and down to the river. It was a fine night, and not very dark, and everything smelt ripping down by the river.”

“Don’t get poetical,” said Clowes. “Stick to the point.”

“We got into the boathouse—”

“How?” asked the practical Trevor, for the boathouse was wont to be locked at one in the morning. “Moriarty had a key that fitted,” explained O’Hara, briefly. “We got in, and launched a boat—a big tub—put in the tar and a couple of brushes—there’s always tar in the boathouse—and rowed across.”

“Wait a bit,” interrupted Trevor, “you said tar and feathers. Where did you get the feathers?”

“We used leaves. They do just as well, and there were heaps on the bank. Well, when we landed, we tied up the boat, and bucked across to the Recreation Ground. We got over the railings—beastly, spiky railings—and went over to the statue. Ye know where the statue stands? It’s right in the middle of the place, where everybody can see it. Moriarty got up first, and I handed him the tar and a brush. Then I went up with the other brush, and we began. We did his face first. It was too dark to see really well, but I think we made a good job of it. When we had put about as much tar on as we thought would do, we took out the leaves—which we were carrying in our pockets—and spread them on. Then we did the rest of him, and after about half an hour, when we thought we’d done about enough, we got into our boat again, and came back.”

===Chinese===
「然后呢？」

「呃，昨晚等大家都睡熟——因为正闹着一场乱子，到一点多才成——俺和莫里亚蒂爬起来，穿好衣服，爬上了楼廊。你们见过楼廊的窗子吧？从顶上开的，钻出去相当费劲。可我们做到了，跳到了外头的砾石地上。」

「跳得可不近。」克劳斯说。

「是啊。俺摔得不轻。可这是为了正经事业。俺先跳下去，俺人还在地上，莫里亚蒂就落俺身上了。俺就是这么受伤的。不过不碍事，我们抄近路穿过场地，翻过栅栏，下到河边。夜里天色晴朗，不算太黑，河边样样东西闻着都香。」

「别吟起诗来了，」克劳斯说，「抓住重点。」

「我们进了船屋——」

「怎么进的？」讲求实际的特雷弗问，因为凌晨一点的船屋照例是上锁的。「莫里亚蒂有把对得上号的钥匙。」奥哈拉言简意赅地解释，「我们进去，推出一条船——一个大澡盆似的家伙——装上柏油和两把刷子——船屋里常年备着柏油——划了过去。」

「慢着，」特雷弗打断他，「你说了柏油和羽毛。羽毛是哪儿弄的？」

「我们用的树叶。效果一样好，岸上多的是。呃，上了岸，我们把船拴好，一路奔到休闲广场。翻过围栏——那栏子可恶，净是尖刺——走到雕像跟前。雕像立在哪儿你们知道吧？就在场子正当中，人人看得见的地方。莫里亚蒂先爬上去，俺把柏油和刷子递给他。然后俺带着另一把刷子也上去，我们开工了。先做他的脸。天太黑，看不真切，可俺想我们干得不坏。柏油上到我们觉得够份了，就掏出树叶——一直揣在口袋里的——往上贴。然后做他其余的部位，约莫半个钟头之后，我们觉得差不多了，就上船回来了。」

===Original===
“And what did you do till half-past seven?”

“We couldn’t get back the way we’d come, so we slept in the boathouse.”

“Well—I’m—hanged,” was Trevor’s comment on the story.

Clowes roared with laughter. O’Hara was a perpetual joy to him.

As O’Hara was going, Trevor asked him for his gold bat.

“You haven’t lost it, I hope?” he said.

O’Hara felt in his pocket, but brought his hand out at once and transferred it to another pocket. A look of anxiety came over his face, and was reflected in Trevor’s.

“I could have sworn it was in that pocket,” he said.

“You *haven’t* lost it?” queried Trevor again.

“He has,” said Clowes, confidently. “If you want to know where that bat is, I should say you’d find it somewhere between the baths and the statue. At the foot of the statue, for choice. It seems to me—correct me if I am wrong—that you have been and gone and done it, me broth av a bhoy.”

===Chinese===
「那到七点半之前你们干什么了？」

「原路回不去了，我们就在船屋里睡了。」

「好——我——服了。」这是特雷弗对这篇故事的评语。

克劳斯放声大笑。奥哈拉于他是长久的欢乐源泉。

奥哈拉临走时，特雷弗向他讨金球棒。

「没弄丢吧，我希望？」他说。

奥哈拉往口袋里一摸，手立刻抽了出来，又转进另一只口袋。焦虑爬上他的脸，也映在特雷弗脸上。

「俺敢对天发誓它就在那只口袋里。」他说。

「你*该不会*弄丢了吧？」特雷弗又问。

「他已经丢了。」克劳斯胸有成竹地说，「你要是想知道球棒在哪儿，我说你可以在浴场和雕像之间的什么地方找到它。要挑的话，就在雕像脚下。依我看——我说错了你纠正——你这可是木已成舟、覆水难收了，俺的好兄弟。」

===Original===
O’Hara gave up the search.

“It’s gone,” he said. “Man, I’m most awfully sorry. I’d sooner have lost a ten-pound note.”

“I don’t see why you should lose either,” snapped Trevor. “Why the blazes can’t you be more careful.”

O’Hara was too penitent for words. Clowes took it on himself to point out the bright side.

“There’s nothing to get sick about, really,” he said. “If the thing doesn’t turn up, though it probably will, you’ll simply have to tell the Old Man that it’s lost. He’ll have another made. You won’t be asked for it till just before Sports Day either, so you will have plenty of time to find it.”

The challenge cups, and also the bats, had to be given to the authorities before the sports, to be formally presented on Sports Day.

“Oh, I suppose it’ll be all right,” said Trevor, “but I hope it won’t be found anywhere near the statue.”

O’Hara said he hoped so too.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉放弃了搜寻。

「没了。」他说，「老兄，俺实在抱歉透了。俺宁可丢一张十镑的钞票。」

「我看不出你凭什么该丢哪一样。」特雷弗抢白道，「你到底能不能仔细点儿。」

奥哈拉悔恨得说不出话。克劳斯主动承担起指出光明面的责任。

「实在没什么可难受的，」他说，「那东西就算不露面——多半会露面——你无非得告诉老头子它丢了。他会叫人再打一只。而且运动会前才会向你要，你有的是时间找。」

各项挑战杯，连带这些小球棒，都得在运动会前交存校方，好在运动会当天正式颁发。

「哦，我想不会有事的，」特雷弗说，「只盼别在雕像附近什么地方给找出来。」

奥哈拉说他但愿如此。

## XIX The Mayor’s Visit / 市长的来访

===Original===
School prefects at Wrykyn did weekly essays for the headmaster. Those who had got their scholarships at the ’Varsity, or who were going up in the following year, used to take their essays to him after school and read them to him﻿—an unpopular and nerve-destroying practice, akin to suicide. Trevor had got his scholarship in the previous November. He was due at the headmaster’s private house at six o’clock on the present Tuesday. He was looking forward to the ordeal not without apprehension. The essay subject this week had been “One man’s meat is another man’s poison,” and Clowes, whose idea of English Essay was that it should be a medium for *intempestive* frivolity, had insisted on his beginning with, “While I cannot conscientiously go so far as to say that one man’s meat is another man’s poison, yet I am certainly of opinion that what is highly beneficial to one man may, on the other hand, to another man, differently constituted, be extremely deleterious, and, indeed, absolutely fatal.”

Trevor was not at all sure how the headmaster would take it. But Clowes had seemed so cut up at his suggestion that it had better be omitted, that he had allowed it to stand.

He was putting the final polish on this gem of English literature at half-past five, when Milton came in.

===Chinese===
里金（Wrykyn）的级长每周都要给校长写一篇议论文。凡是已经在大学拿到奖学金的，或是来年就要上大学的，照例要在放学后把文章带到校长私宅去，当面读给他听——这可是桩不得人心、摧残神经的苦役，与自杀无异。特雷弗（Trevor）去年十一月拿到了奖学金。本周二下午六点，他就该到校长私宅报到。他怀着不小的忐忑期待着这场酷刑。这星期的题目是「甲之美食，乙之砒霜」，而克劳斯（Clowes）——在他看来，英语议论文这东西，就该是*不合时宜的*插科打诨的载体——硬是让他以下面这句开场：「吾虽凭良心不敢遽然断言，甲之美食即乙之砒霜，然吾确实以为，于一人大有补益之物，反观之于另一禀赋迥异之人，或可极端有害，且诚然，绝对致命。」

特雷弗完全拿不准校长会作何感想。可当初他提议把这句删掉时，克劳斯那副伤心欲绝的模样让他于心不忍，只好由它留着。

五点半，他正在给这件英国文学的瑰宝做最后的抛光，弥尔顿（Milton）走了进来。

===Original===
“Busy?” said Milton.

Trevor said he would be through in a minute.

Milton took a chair, and waited.

Trevor scratched out two words and substituted two others, made a couple of picturesque blots, and, laying down his pen, announced that he had finished.

“What’s up?” he said.

===Chinese===
“忙吗？”弥尔顿说。

特雷弗说再有一分钟就好。

弥尔顿拉过一把椅子，等着。

特雷弗划掉两个字，换上另外两个字，又点上两朵颇具观赏性的墨渍，然后搁下笔，宣布大功告成。

“什么事？”他说。

===Original===
“It’s about the League,” said Milton.

“Found out anything?”

“Not anything much. But I’ve been making inquiries. You remember I asked you to let me look at those letters of yours?”

Trevor nodded. This had happened on the Sunday of that week.

“Well, I wanted to look at the postmarks.”

“By Jove, I never thought of that.”

===Chinese===
“是联盟（League）的事。”弥尔顿说。

“查出什么了？”

“没查出什么大不了的东西。不过我一直在打听。记得我请你把那几封信给我看看吗？”

特雷弗点点头。那是本周星期天的事。

“嗯，我想看看邮戳。”

“天哪，我压根没想到这一层。”

===Original===
Milton continued with the businesslike air of the detective who explains in the last chapter of the book how he did it.

“I found, as I thought, that both letters came from the same place.”

Trevor pulled out the letters in question. “So they do,” he said, “Chesterton.”

“Do you know Chesterton?” asked Milton.

“Only by name.”

“It’s a small hamlet about two miles from here across the downs. There’s only one shop in the place, which acts as post-office and tobacconist and everything else. I thought that if I went there and asked about those letters, they might remember who it was that sent them, if I showed them a photograph.”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿摆出一副干练的架势，活像侦探小说最后一章里向读者解说破案经过的大侦探。

“我果然发现，两封信是从同一个地方寄出的。”

特雷弗掏出那两封信。“还真是，”他说，“切斯特顿（Chesterton）。”

“你了解切斯特顿吗？”弥尔顿问。

“只闻其名。”

“那是个小村子，离这儿两英里，翻过丘原就到。全村只有一家铺子，邮政、烟草，还有别的什么营生，一概由它兼营。我想，要是我去那儿打听打听那两封信，再给他们看几张照片，他们说不定还记得信是谁寄的。”

===Original===
“By Jove,” said Trevor, “of course! Did you? What happened?”

“I went there yesterday afternoon. I took about half-a-dozen photographs of various chaps, including Rand-Brown.”

“But wait a bit. If Chesterton’s two miles off, Rand-Brown couldn’t have sent the letters. He wouldn’t have the time after school. He was on the grounds both the afternoons before I got the letters.”

“I know,” said Milton; “I didn’t think of that at the time.”

“Well?”

===Chinese===
“天哪，”特雷弗说，“当然！你去了？结果怎么样？”

“昨天下午去的。我带了六张左右各个家伙的照片，其中有兰德-布朗（Rand-Brown）的。”

“且慢。切斯特顿既然在两英里外，兰德-布朗就不可能寄这些信。放学后他没那工夫。我收到信之前的两个下午，他都在球场上。”

“我知道，”弥尔顿说，“我当时没想到这一点。”

“后来呢？”

===Original===
“One of the points about the Chesterton post-office is that there’s no letter-box outside. You have to go into the shop and hand anything you want to post across the counter. I thought this was a tremendous score for me. I thought they would be bound to remember who handed in the letters. There can’t be many at a place like that.”

“Did they remember?”

“They remembered the letters being given in distinctly, but as for knowing anything beyond that, they were simply futile. There was an old woman in the shop, aged about three hundred and ten, I should think. I shouldn’t say she had ever been very intelligent, but now she simply gibbered. I started off by laying out a shilling on some poisonous-looking sweets. I gave the lot to a village kid when I got out. I hope they didn’t kill him. Then, having scattered ground-bait in that way, I lugged out the photographs, mentioned the letters and the date they had been sent, and asked her to weigh in and identify the sender.”

“Did she?”

“My dear chap, she identified them all, one after the other. The first was one of Clowes. She was prepared to swear on oath that that was the chap who had sent the letters. Then I shot a photograph of you across the counter, and doubts began to creep in. She said she was certain it was one of those two ‘la-ads,’ but couldn’t quite say which. To keep her amused I fired in photograph number three﻿—Allardyce’s. She identified that, too. At the end of ten minutes she was pretty sure that it was one of the six﻿—the other three were Paget, Clephane, and Rand-Brown﻿—but she was not going to bind herself down to any particular one. As I had come to the end of my stock of photographs, and was getting a bit sick of the game, I got up to go, when in came another ornament of Chesterton from a room at the back of the shop. He was quite a kid, not more than a hundred and fifty at the outside, so, as a last chance, I tackled him on the subject. He looked at the photographs for about half an hour, mumbling something about it not being ‘thiccy ’un’ or ‘that ’un,’ or ‘that ’ere tother ’un,’ until I began to feel I’d had enough of it. Then it came out that the real chap who had sent the letters was a ‘la-ad’ with light hair, not so big as me﻿—”

===Chinese===
“切斯特顿那家邮政所有一个特点：门外没有信箱。要寄什么，都得走进店里，隔着柜台亲手递交。我原先以为这是我的一大胜算。我想他们必定记得信是谁交的。那种地方来往的人不会多。”

“他们记得吗？”

“信确凿是交进来了，这个他们记得清清楚楚，可除了这一点，他们简直一无是处。店里有个老太太，我看年纪约莫三百一十岁。且不说她当年可曾聪明过，如今她只会咿咿呀呀。我开场先花一先令，买了一包看着就有毒的糖果。出门后我把整包都给了村里一个小孩子。但愿没把他毒死。就这样撒完了打窝的饵料，我掏出照片，报上那两封信和寄出的日期，请她出手指认寄信人。”

“她认了吗？”

“老兄，她挨个儿全认了。头一张是克劳斯的。她已备好对天发誓，寄信的正是这位仁兄。接着我把你的一张照片隔着柜台推过去，她的疑心开始冒头。她说确信是那两个「伢子」之一，只是说不准是哪个。为了逗她继续玩下去，我又甩过去第三张——阿拉代斯（Allardyce）的。那张她也认了。十分钟下来，她已经相当有把握是六个人之一——另外三张是佩吉特（Paget）、克莱芬（Clephane）和兰德-布朗——但她不打算把自己拴死在哪一个上。我照片库存告罄，对这游戏也腻味起来了，正起身要走，铺子后头的屋里又进来一位切斯特顿的俊彦。他完全是个小不点儿，往多里说不超过一百五十岁，于是作为最后一线希望，我拿这个话题向他发起进攻。他对着照片端详了约莫半个钟头，嘴里咕哝着不是「这伢」就是「那伢」，再不然就是「那边的那个伢」，直到我开始觉得实在受够了。最后总算问出，真正寄信的是个「伢子」，浅色头发，个子没我大——”

===Original===
“That doesn’t help us much,” said Trevor.

“﻿—And a ‘prarper little gennlemun.’ So all we’ve got to do is to look for some young duke of polished manners and exterior, with a thatch of light hair.”

“There are three hundred and sixty-seven fellows with light hair in the school,” said Trevor, calmly.

“Thought it was three hundred and sixty-eight myself,” said Milton, “but I may be wrong. Anyhow, there you have the results of my investigations. If you can make anything out of them, you’re welcome to it. Goodbye.”

“Half a second,” said Trevor, as he got up; “had the fellow a cap of any sort?”

“No. Bareheaded. You wouldn’t expect him to give himself away by wearing a house-cap?”

===Chinese===
“这帮不上什么忙。”特雷弗说。

“——而且是个「像模像样的小绅士」。所以咱们只消去找某位谈吐仪表都打磨得溜光水滑的年轻公爵，再配一头浅色茅草顶就行了。”

“全校总共有三百六十七个浅头发的家伙。”特雷弗心平气和地说。

“我自己数的是三百六十八，”弥尔顿说，“不过我可能数错了。总之，我的调查结果就摆在这儿。你要能从中琢磨出什么名堂，尽管拿去用。再见。”

“慢着，”特雷弗起身说，“那家伙头上戴没戴什么帽子？”

“没有。光着脑袋。总不能指望他戴着宿舍帽自己把自己供出来吧？”

===Original===
Trevor went over to the headmaster’s revolving this discovery in his mind. It was not much of a clue, but the smallest clue is better than nothing. To find out that the sender of the League letters had fair hair narrowed the search down a little. It cleared the more raven-locked members of the school, at any rate. Besides, by combining his information with Milton’s, the search might be still further narrowed down. He knew that the polite letter-writer must be either in Seymour’s or in Donaldson’s. The number of fair-haired youths in the two houses was not excessive. Indeed, at the moment he could not recall any; which rather complicated matters.

He arrived at the headmaster’s door, and knocked. He was shown into a room at the side of the hall, near the door. The butler informed him that the headmaster was engaged at present. Trevor, who knew the butler slightly through having constantly been to see the headmaster on business via the front door, asked who was there.

“Sir Eustace Briggs,” said the butler, and disappeared in the direction of his lair beyond the green baize partition at the end of the hall.

Trevor went into the room, which was a sort of spare study, and sat down, wondering what had brought the mayor of Wrykyn to see the headmaster at this advanced hour.

A quarter of an hour later the sound of voices broke in upon his peace. The headmaster was coming down the hall with the intention of showing his visitor out. The door of Trevor’s room was ajar, and he could hear distinctly what was being said. He had no particular desire to play the eavesdropper, but the part was forced upon him.

===Chinese===
特雷弗朝校长宅邸走去，一路上把这一发现翻来覆去地盘算。线索算不上什么，可再小的线索也聊胜于无。查明联盟信件的寄信人长着浅色头发，多少把搜索范围缩小了一点。至少，全校那些一头鸦羽黑发的成员可以排除嫌疑了。再者，把他的情报和弥尔顿的合在一起，范围还能进一步收窄。他知道，那位彬彬有礼的写信人必定在西摩宿舍（Seymour’s）或唐纳森宿舍（Donaldson’s）二者之一。这两栋宿舍里的浅发少年并不算多。说实在的，眼下他一个都想不起来；这就把事情弄得更棘手了。

他到了校长宅邸门前，敲了敲门，被引进门厅侧面、靠近大门的一个房间。管家告诉他，校长此刻正有事。特雷弗因为常为公干从前门去见校长，跟这位管家有几分点头之交，便问来的是谁。

“尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士（Sir Eustace Briggs）。”管家说着，便朝门厅尽头绿呢隔墙后面他自己的老窝方向遁去了。

特雷弗走进那个房间——那是间备用书房——坐下来纳闷，里金市长这么晚的钟点跑来见校长，究竟所为何来。

一刻钟后，人声打破了这份清静。校长正沿着门厅走来，要送客出门。特雷弗那间的房门虚掩着，外面说的话他听得一清二楚。他倒没什么兴致扮演窃听者的角色，可这角色不由分说地派到了他头上。

===Original===
Sir Eustace seemed excited.

“It is far from being my habit,” he was saying, “to make unnecessary complaints respecting the conduct of the lads under your care.” (Sir Eustace Briggs had a distaste for the shorter and more colloquial forms of speech. He would have perished sooner than have substituted “complain of your boys” for the majestic formula he had used. He spoke as if he enjoyed choosing his words. He seemed to pause and think before each word. Unkind people﻿—who were jealous of his distinguished career﻿—used to say that he did this because he was afraid of dropping an aitch if he relaxed his vigilance.)

“But,” continued he, “I am reluctantly forced to the unpleasant conclusion that the dastardly outrage to which both I and the Press of the town have called your attention is to be attributed to one of the lads to whom I ’ave﻿—*have* (this with a jerk) referred.”

“I will make a thorough inquiry, Sir Eustace,” said the bass voice of the headmaster.

===Chinese===
尤斯塔斯爵士似乎很激动。

“这万万不合乎我的习惯，”他正说着，“就阁下所管教诸生之操行，提出不必要的申诉。”（尤斯塔斯·布里格斯爵士对简短口语化的表达方式一向没有好感。宁可性命不保，他也不肯拿“抱怨你们的学生”来替换他方才使用的那句威严的公式。他说话的派头，仿佛很享受挑选字眼的过程。每个词出口之前，他似乎都要停顿斟酌一番。刻薄的人——都是嫉妒他显赫仕途的家伙——常说，他这样做，是因为生怕稍一放松警惕，就吞掉一个词头的 h 音。）

“但是，”他接着说，“我却被迫痛心得出一个不愉快的结论：我本人与本城报界均已提请阁下留意的那个卑劣暴行，当出自“俺”——*我*（说到这里猛然一顿）所指的那批少年中的一位之手。”

“我将进行一次彻查，尤斯塔斯爵士。”校长低沉的嗓音说道。

===Original===
“I thank you,” said the mayor. “It would, under the circumstances, be nothing more, I think, than what is distinctly advisable. The man Samuel Wapshott, of whose narrative I have recently afforded you a brief synopsis, stated in no uncertain terms that he found at the foot of the statue on which the dastardly outrage was perpetrated a diminutive ornament, in shape like the bats that are used in the game of cricket. This ornament, he avers (with what truth I know not), was handed by him to a youth of an age coeval with that of the lads in the upper division of this school. The youth claimed it as his property, I was given to understand.”

“A thorough inquiry shall be made, Sir Eustace.”

“I thank you.”

And then the door shut, and the conversation ceased.

===Chinese===
“我谢谢你，”市长说，“在此情形之下，我想这也无非是确属明智之举。塞缪尔·沃普肖特（Samuel Wapshott）——其叙述我方才已向阁下略陈梗概——以毫不含糊的措辞声明：他在那尊惨遭卑劣暴行的雕像脚下，发现了一件小小的饰物，其形制近似板球戏中所用的球棒。他坚称（真伪若何，我不得而知），他已将此物交予一位少年，其年纪与贵校高年级部诸生不相上下。据我所闻，那位少年声称此物归他所有。”

“必将进行一次彻查，尤斯塔斯爵士。”

“我谢谢你。”

随后大门关上，谈话声戛然而止。

## XVI The Ripton Match / 里普顿之战

===Original===
It was a curious thing in connection with the matches between Ripton and Wrykyn, that Ripton always seemed to be the bigger team. They always had a gigantic pack of forwards, who looked capable of shoving a hole through one of the pyramids. Possibly they looked bigger to the Wrykinians than they really were. Strangers always look big on the football field. When you have grown accustomed to a person’s appearance, he does not look nearly so large. Milton, for instance, never struck anybody at Wrykyn as being particularly big for a school forward, and yet today he was the heaviest man on the field by a quarter of a stone. But, taken in the mass, the Ripton pack were far heavier than their rivals. There was a legend current among the lower forms at Wrykyn that fellows were allowed to stop on at Ripton till they were twenty-five, simply to play football. This is scarcely likely to have been based on fact. Few lower form legends are.

Jevons, the Ripton captain, through having played opposite Trevor for three seasons﻿—he was the Ripton left centre-three-quarter﻿—had come to be quite an intimate of his. Trevor had gone down with Milton and Allardyce to meet the team at the station, and conduct them up to the school.

===Chinese===
说起里普顿（Ripton）和里金之间的比赛，有桩怪事：里普顿的队伍看上去总似乎块头更大。他们总有一大群巨人般的前锋，瞧那架势，简直能把金字塔顶出个窟窿。也许在里金人眼里，他们比实际个头显得大。球场上，生面孔看着总显大。等你看惯了一个人的模样，他就远不那么魁梧了。就拿弥尔顿来说，在里金从没人觉得他这个校队前锋块头格外惊人，可今天他却是全场最重的人，还多出四分之一英石。不过就整体而论，里普顿的前锋集团确实远比对手沉重。里金低年级中流传着一个传说，说里普顿允许家伙们留校读到二十五岁，专门为了踢球。这话恐怕不大可能有什么事实根据。低年级的传说，本就少有根据。

杰维斯（Jevons）是里普顿队长，因为连续三个赛季与特雷弗对位交锋——他打的是里普顿的左中锋——两人已相当熟稔。特雷弗同弥尔顿和阿拉代斯一起到车站迎接客队，再领他们上学校去。

===Original===
“How have you been getting on since Christmas?” asked Jevons.

“Pretty well. We’ve lost Paget, I suppose you know?”

“That was the fast man on the wing, wasn’t it?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“圣诞以来过得怎么样？”杰维斯问。

“还不错。我们丢了佩吉特，你大概听说了吧？”

“就是翼锋线上那个快家伙，对吧？”

“对。”

===Original===
“Well, we’ve lost a man, too.”

“Oh, yes, that red-haired forward. I remember him.”

“It ought to make us pretty even. What’s the ground like?”

“Bit greasy, I should think. We had some rain late last night.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，我们也折了一员。”

“哦，对，那个红头发前锋。我记得他。”

“这样一来咱们就算半斤八两了。场地怎么样？”

“有点滑，我看是。昨天后半夜下了点雨。”

===Original===
The ground *was* a bit greasy. So was the ball. When Milton kicked off up the hill with what wind there was in his favour, the outsides of both teams found it difficult to hold the ball. Jevons caught it on his twenty-five line, and promptly handed it forward. The first scrum was formed in the heart of the enemy’s country.

A deep, swelling roar from either touchline greeted the school’s advantage. A feature of a big match was always the shouting. It rarely ceased throughout the whole course of the game, the monotonous but impressive sound of five hundred voices all shouting the same word. It was worth hearing. Sometimes the evenness of the noise would change to an excited crescendo as a school three-quarter got off, or the school back pulled up the attack with a fine piece of defence. Sometimes the shouting would give place to clapping when the school was being pressed and somebody had found touch with a long kick. But mostly the man on the ropes roared steadily and without cessation, and with the full force of his lungs, the word “*Wrykyn!*”

The scrum was a long one. For two minutes the forwards heaved and strained, now one side, now the other, gaining a few inches. The Wrykyn pack were doing all they knew to heel, but their opponents’ superior weight was telling. Ripton had got the ball, and were keeping it. Their game was to break through with it and rush. Then suddenly one of their forwards kicked it on, and just at that moment the opposition of the Wrykyn pack gave way, and the scrum broke up. The ball came out on the Wrykyn side, and Allardyce whipped it out to Deacon, who was playing half with him.

===Chinese===
场地*确实*有点滑。球也一样。弥尔顿趁着那点顺风朝坡上开球之后，两队翼锋都觉得球不好拿。杰维斯在自家二十五码线上接住球，二话不说往前方递了一手。第一个并列争球，在敌方腹地结成了。

两侧边线同时响起一阵深沉而渐涨的吼声，庆贺学校占了上风。大赛的一大特色，向来是呐喊。整场比赛它难得停歇——五百条嗓子齐吼同一个词，单调却气势逼人。这声音值得一听。有时，校队某个三分卫突围而出，或校队最后卫以一记漂亮的防守化解攻势，那均匀的声浪便会陡然转为激动的渐强。有时，校队被围攻、有人一记长踢将球开出边线解围，呐喊又会让位于掌声。但更多的时候，被逼到围绳边挨打的那一方，只是平稳地、一刻不停地、用足肺活量吼着那个词：“*里金*！”

这个并列争球持续了很久。整整两分钟，前锋们你推我挤、较劲发力，一会儿这边、一会儿那边，各推进几寸。里金前锋们想尽法子要把球往回勾，可对手的体重优势渐渐占了上风。里普顿得了球，并且把它牢牢控住。他们的打法是带球突破、集团冲锋。忽然，他们一个前锋把球往前一踢，正在这时，里金前锋集团的对抗垮了下来，争球散了。球从里金一侧出来，阿拉代斯一把将它甩给与他搭档打前卫的迪肯（Deacon）。

===Original===
“Ball’s out,” cried the Ripton half who was taking the scrum. “Break up. It’s out.”

And his colleague on the left darted across to stop Trevor, who had taken Deacon’s pass, and was running through on the right.

===Chinese===
“球出来了，”参与争球的里普顿前卫喊道，“散开。出来了。”

他在左侧的队友一个箭步横蹿过来拦特雷弗——特雷弗已接过迪肯的传球，正从右路突进。

===Original===
Trevor ran splendidly. He was a three-quarter who took a lot of stopping when he once got away. Jevons and the Ripton half met him almost simultaneously, and each slackened his pace for the fraction of a second, to allow the other to tackle. As they hesitated, Trevor passed them. He had long ago learned that to go hard when you have once started is the thing that pays.

He could see that Rand-Brown was racing up for the pass, and, as he reached the back, he sent the ball to him, waist-high. Then the back got to him, and he came down with a thud, with a vision, seen from the corner of his eye, of the ball bounding forward out of the wing three-quarter’s hands into touch. Rand-Brown had bungled the pass in the old familiar way, and lost a certain try.

===Chinese===
特雷弗跑得漂亮。他这种三分卫，一旦冲起来，很难拦得住。杰维斯和那个里普顿前卫几乎同时赶到，各自都放慢了一瞬，好让对方去擒抱。就在他俩迟疑的当口，特雷弗从两人之间穿了过去。他早就懂得：一旦起跑，就得一鼓作气猛冲到底，这才是划算的买卖。

他看见兰德-布朗正全速跑上来接应传球，便在奔到最后卫跟前时，把球齐腰送了过去。随即那最后卫扑到他身上，他轰然倒地，眼角余光里瞥见球从翼锋三分卫手中弹向前方、出了边线。兰德-布朗又以那套老掉牙的手法把传球搞砸了，丢掉了一次板上钉钉的达阵。

===Original===
The touch-judge ran up with his flag waving in the air, but the referee had other views.

“Knocked on inside,” he said; “scrum here.”

“Here” was, Trevor saw with unspeakable disgust, some three yards from the goal-line. Rand-Brown had only had to take the pass, and he must have scored.

===Chinese===
巡边员挥着旗子跑了上来，裁判却另有见解。

“阵线之内前掉球，”他说，“在这儿争球。”

特雷弗怀着难以言表的厌恶看到，“这儿”离球门线只有三码左右。兰德-布朗原本只需接住那记传球，就必得分无疑。

===Original===
The Ripton forwards were beginning to find their feet better now, and they carried the scrum. A truculent-looking warrior in one of those ear-guards which are tied on by strings underneath the chin, and which add fifty percent to the ferocity of a forward’s appearance, broke away with the ball at his feet, and swept down the field with the rest of the pack at his heels. Trevor arrived too late to pull up the rush, which had gone straight down the right touchline, and it was not till Strachan fell on the ball on the Wrykyn twenty-five line that the danger ceased to threaten.

Even now the school were in a bad way. The enemy were pressing keenly, and a real piece of combination among their three-quarters would only too probably end in a try. Fortunately for them, Allardyce and Deacon were a better pair of halves than the couple they were marking. Also, the Ripton forwards heeled slowly, and Allardyce had generally got his man safely buried in the mud before he could pass.

He was just getting round for the tenth time to bottle his opponent as before, when he slipped. When the ball came out he was on all fours, and the Ripton exponent, finding to his great satisfaction that he had not been tackled, whipped the ball out on the left, where a wing three-quarter hovered.

This was the man Rand-Brown was supposed to be marking, and once again did Barry’s substitute prove of what stuff his tackling powers were made. After his customary moment of hesitation, he had at the Riptonian’s neck. The Riptonian handed him off in a manner that recalled the palmy days of the old Prize Ring﻿—handing off was always slightly vigorous in the Ripton v. Wrykyn match﻿—and dashed over the line in the extreme corner.

===Chinese===
此刻里普顿前锋们渐渐找到状态，把争球压着打。一个面目凶悍的武士——头上戴着那种用带子在下巴底下系牢的护耳，凭这物件，一个前锋的凶相能添上五成——带球突围，沿球场直冲而下，其余的前锋集团紧随其后。特雷弗赶到时已经太迟，没能拦住这波冲锋；它顺着右边边线一路碾压，直到斯特拉坎（Strachan）在里金二十五码线上扑住球，险情才算解除。

即便此刻，学校的处境也不妙。对方攻势正猛，他们的三分卫只要来一回像样的配合，就极可能以一次达阵收场。所幸阿拉代斯和迪肯这对前卫，比他们对位盯防的那两个高出一筹。再者，里普顿前锋勾球迟缓，往往球还没传出去，阿拉代斯已把他盯的人妥妥地按进了泥里。

他正第十度绕过去、打算照旧把他那对手罩在原地，脚下一滑。球出来的时候，他正四肢着地，而那位里普顿好手发现自己居然没人盯防，大喜过望，抡起球就往左边甩，那边正有本方一个翼锋三分卫游弋接应。

这正是兰德-布朗本该盯住的人；而巴里（Barry）的替补再一次向世人展示了他擒抱功夫是何等材质。照例先迟疑了那么一瞬，他才朝那里普顿人的脖颈扑去。那一位一记推挡把他搡开，那手法直叫人想起旧日裸拳拳坛的全盛岁月——里普顿对里金一战里，推挡从来格外用力——随即从最远的边角处一跃冲过了阵线。

===Original===
There was anguish on the two touchlines. Trevor looked savage, but made no comment. The team lined up in silence.

It takes a very good kick to convert a try from the touchline. Jevons’ kick was a long one, but it fell short. Ripton led by a try to nothing.

A few more scrums near the halfway line, and a fine attempt at a dropped goal by the Ripton back, and it was halftime, with the score unaltered.

During the interval there were lemons. An excellent thing is your lemon at halftime. It cools the mouth, quenches the thirst, stimulates the desire to be at them again, and improves the play.

===Chinese===
两条边线上哀声一片。特雷弗神色狰狞，却一言不发。全队默默列队。

从边线近处为达阵追加射门，需要非常好的脚法。杰维斯这一踢够远，但差了些距离。里普顿以一次达阵对零分领先。

中线附近又几番争球，里普顿最后卫又来了一记漂亮的落踢攻门尝试，随后上半场结束，比分未变。

中场休息时照例分柠檬。中场时的柠檬，真是个好东西。它让口腔清凉，止住干渴，激发重新投入战斗的渴望，还能提升球艺。

===Original===
Possibly the Wrykyn team had been happier in their choice of lemons on this occasion, for no sooner had the game been restarted than Clowes ran the whole length of the field, dodged through the three-quarters, punted over the back’s head, and scored a really brilliant try, of the sort that Paget had been fond of scoring in the previous term. The man on the touchline brightened up wonderfully, and began to try and calculate the probable score by the end of the game, on the assumption that, as a try had been scored in the first two minutes, ten would be scored in the first twenty, and so on.

===Chinese===
也许这一回，里金队选柠檬的眼光更佳。下半场刚一开打，克劳斯便纵贯全场，从对方三分卫们中间连闪带躲地穿过，一记踢球越过最后卫头顶，攻入一次真正精彩的达阵——正是佩吉特上学期爱拿手的那一种。边线上那位先生顿时精神大振，开始盘算起终场可能的大比分，推理如下：既然头两分钟就拿下一次达阵，那么头二十分钟就该有十次，以此类推。

===Original===
But the calculations were based on false premises. After Strachan had failed to convert, and the game had been resumed with the score at one try all, play settled down in the centre, and neither side could pierce the other’s defence. Once Jevons got off for Ripton, but Trevor brought him down safely, and once Rand-Brown let his man through, as before, but Strachan was there to meet him, and the effort came to nothing. For Wrykyn, no one did much except tackle. The forwards were beaten by the heavier pack, and seldom let the ball out. Allardyce intercepted a pass when about ten minutes of play remained, and ran through to the back. But the back, who was a capable man and in his third season in the team, laid him low scientifically before he could reach the line.

Altogether it looked as if the match were going to end in a draw. The Wrykyn defence, with the exception of Rand-Brown, was too good to be penetrated, while the Ripton forwards, by always getting the ball in the scrums, kept them from attacking. It was about five minutes from the end of the game when the Ripton right centre-three-quarter, in trying to punt across to the wing, miskicked and sent the ball straight into the hands of Trevor’s colleague in the centre.
