Before his man could get round to him he had slipped through, with Trevor backing him up. The back, as a good back should, seeing two men coming at him, went for the man with the ball. But by the time he had brought him down, the ball was no longer where it had originally been. Trevor had got it, and was running in between the posts.

===Chinese===
可这套计算建立在错误的前提上。斯特拉坎追加射门失手，比赛以一次达阵平一次达阵的比分重新开始后，战局在中场稳定下来，双方都无法洞穿对方的防线。杰维斯有一次为里普顿突围，却被特雷弗稳稳撂倒；兰德-布朗又一次照旧放自己的人过去，好在斯特拉坎及时补位迎上，那次攻势无疾而终。里金这边，除了擒抱，人人都乏善可陈。前锋们被更重的对方集团压制，难得把球勾出来。比赛还剩十来分钟时，阿拉代斯拦截住一次传球，一路突破直奔最后卫而去。可那最后卫是个狠角色，在校队已是第三个赛季，赶在他触线之前，用科学的手法把他放倒了。

总的看来，这场比赛要以平局收场了。里金的防线——兰德-布朗除外——固若金汤，无从攻破；而里普顿前锋们又总在争球中得球，叫他们攻不出去。离终场约莫五分钟时，里普顿的右中锋想把球踢传给翼锋，却踢呲了，球不偏不倚落进特雷弗中锋搭档的手里。没等他盯防的人绕到跟前，他已经滑了出去，特雷弗在后策应。那最后卫眼见两人扑来，按一个好最后卫的本分，直取持球之人。可等他把人放倒，球早已不在原处。球到了特雷弗手里，正向两根门柱之间奔去。

===Original===
This time Strachan put on the extra two points without difficulty.

Ripton played their hardest for the remaining minutes, but without result. The game ended with Wrykyn a goal ahead﻿—a goal and a try to a try. For the second time in one season the Ripton match had ended in a victory﻿—a thing it was very rarely in the habit of doing.

---

The senior day-room at Seymour’s rejoiced considerably that night. The air was dark with flying cushions, and darker still, occasionally, when the usual humorist turned the gas out. Milton was out, for he had gone to the dinner which followed the Ripton match, and the man in command of the house in his absence was Mill. And the senior day-room had no respect whatever for Mill.

Barry joined in the revels as well as his ankle would let him, but he was not feeling happy. The disappointment of being out of the first still weighed on him.

At about eight, when things were beginning to grow really lively, and the noise seemed likely to crack the window at any moment, the door was flung open and Milton stalked in.

===Chinese===
这一回，斯特拉坎轻而易举地补上了追加的两分。

里普顿在剩下的几分钟里拼尽了全力，却毫无结果。比赛结束，里金领先一次攻门——一次攻门加一次达阵，对一次达阵。一个赛季里第二次，对里普顿之战以胜利告终——这种事，它可不习惯常有。

---

当晚，西摩宿舍的高年级活动室欢腾了很久。满屋坐垫乱飞，天昏地暗；偶尔更暗——每当那位照例出面的活宝把煤气灯拧灭的时候。弥尔顿不在，他去参加里普顿之战后的晚宴了，代管宿舍的是米尔（Mill）。而高年级活动室对米尔没有半点敬意。

巴里也随着脚踝许可的程度加入了狂欢，但他心里并不痛快。无缘一队的失落，仍沉甸甸地压在心头。

八点来钟，气氛正开始真正热烈起来，喧闹声眼看随时要把窗玻璃震裂，房门猛地被推开，弥尔顿大步走了进来。

===Original===
“What’s all this row?” he inquired. “Stop it at once.”

As a matter of fact, the row *had* stopped﻿—directly he came in.

“Is Barry here?” he asked.

“Yes,” said that youth.

“Congratulate you on your first, Barry. We’ve just had a meeting and given you your colours. Trevor told me to tell you.”

===Chinese===
“这是闹的哪出？”他质问道，“立刻给我停下。”

事实上，喧闹*确实*停了——就在他进门的那一刻。

“巴里在这儿吗？”他问。

“在。”那位少年说。

“恭喜你进了一队，巴里。我们刚开了会，决定授你队色。特雷弗让我来告诉你。”

## XVII The Watchers in the Vault / 地窖里的守望者

===Original===
For the next three seconds you could have heard a cannonball drop. And that was equivalent, in the senior day-room at Seymour’s, to a dead silence. Barry stood in the middle of the room leaning on the stick on which he supported life, now that his ankle had been injured, and turned red and white in regular rotation, as the magnificence of the news came home to him.

Then the small voice of Linton was heard.

“That’ll be six d. I’ll trouble you for, young Sammy,” said Linton. For he had betted an even sixpence with Master Samuel Menzies that Barry would get his first fifteen cap this term, and Barry had got it.

A great shout went up from every corner of the room. Barry was one of the most popular members of the house, and everyone had been sorry for him when his sprained ankle had apparently put him out of the running for the last cap.

===Chinese===
接下来三秒钟，连炮弹落地都听得见。在西摩宿舍的高年级活动室，这就等于死一般的寂静。巴里站在屋子中央，拄着那根自脚踝受伤以来赖以支撑生命的文明棍，红一阵白一阵有规律地变换着脸色，渐渐咂摸出这消息的分量。

随后响起了林顿（Linton）细细的嗓音。

“小萨米，麻烦你付我六个便士。”林顿说。原来他跟塞缪尔·门齐斯（Samuel Menzies）少爷对赌了六便士，赌巴里本学期能拿到一队队帽，而巴里拿到了。

屋子四角同时爆发出一阵大喊。巴里是宿舍里最受欢迎的人物之一，他那扭伤的脚踝眼看断送了最后一个队帽名额时，人人都替他惋惜。

===Original===
“Good old Barry,” said Drummond, delightedly. Barry thanked him in a dazed way.

Everyone crowded in to shake his hand. Barry thanked then all in a dazed way.

And then the senior day-room, in spite of the fact that Milton had returned, gave itself up to celebrating the occasion with one of the most deafening uproars that had ever been heard even in that factory of noise. A babel of voices discussed the match of the afternoon, each trying to outshout the other. In one corner Linton was beating wildly on a biscuit-tin with part of a broken chair. Shoeblossom was busy in the opposite corner executing an intricate step-dance on somebody else’s box. McTodd had got hold of the red-hot poker, and was burning his initials in huge letters on the seat of a chair. Everyone, in short, was enjoying himself, and it was not until an advanced hour that comparative quiet was restored. It was a great evening for Barry, the best he had ever experienced.

===Chinese===
“好样儿的巴里。”德拉蒙德（Drummond）喜滋滋地说。巴里晕晕乎乎地谢了他。

大家涌上来跟他握手。巴里晕晕乎乎地把众人一一谢过。

于是，高年级活动室——尽管弥尔顿已经回来——一头扎进了庆典，闹出即便在那座噪音工厂里也罕有其匹的震天喧嚣。七嘴八舌议论着下午的比赛，一个个都想压过别人。一个角落里，林顿正拿半把破椅子猛敲饼干筒。对面的角落里，休布洛瑟姆（Shoeblossom）忙着在别人的箱子上表演一套繁复的踢踏舞。麦克托德（McTodd）搞到了那根烧红的火钳，正用大号字母把姓名缩写烙在一把椅子的椅面上。总之，人人都玩得尽兴，直到夜深时分才恢复了相对的安静。对巴里来说，这是个盛大的夜晚，是他有生以来最好的一个。

===Original===
Clowes did not learn the news till he saw it on the notice-board, on the following Monday. When he saw it he whistled softly.

“I see you’ve given Barry his first,” he said to Trevor, when they met. “Rather sensational.”

“Milton and Allardyce both thought he deserved it. If he’d been playing instead of Rand-Brown, they wouldn’t have scored at all probably, and we should have got one more try.”

“That’s all right,” said Clowes. “He deserves it right enough, and I’m jolly glad you’ve given it him. But things will begin to move now, don’t you think? The League ought to have a word to say about the business. It’ll be a facer for them.”

===Chinese===
直到下星期一在布告栏上看见，克劳斯才得知这消息。一看之下，他轻轻吹了声口哨。

“我看你们把一队名额给巴里了，”碰到特雷弗时他说，“相当轰动。”

“弥尔顿和阿拉代斯都觉得他配得上。要是上场的是他而不是兰德-布朗，他们多半一分都得不到，我们还能多拿一次达阵。”

“这就对了，”克劳斯说，“他完全配得上，你们给了他，我高兴得很。不过这下事情要开始动了，你说是不是？联盟对这桩事总该有句话要说。对他们可是当头一棒。”

===Original===
“Do you remember,” asked Trevor, “saying that you thought it must be Rand-Brown who wrote those letters?”

“Yes. Well?”

“Well, Milton had an idea that it was Rand-Brown who ragged his study.”

“What made him think that?”

===Chinese===
“你还记得不，”特雷弗问，“你说过，写那些信的必定是兰德-布朗？”

“记得。怎么？”

“嗯，弥尔顿有个想法，把他书房翻个底朝天的也是兰德-布朗。”

“他怎么会这么想？”

===Original===
Trevor related the Shoeblossom incident.

Clowes became quite excited.

“Then Rand-Brown must be the man,” he said. “Why don’t you go and tackle him? Probably he’s got the bat in his study.”

“It’s not in his study,” said Trevor, “because I looked everywhere for it, and got him to turn out his pockets, too. And yet I’ll swear he knows something about it. One thing struck me as a bit suspicious. I went straight into his study and showed him that last letter﻿—about the bat, you know, and accused him of writing it. Now, if he hadn’t been in the business somehow, he wouldn’t have understood what was meant by their saying ‘the bat you lost.’ It might have been an ordinary cricket-bat for all he knew. But he offered to let me search the study. It didn’t strike me as rum till afterwards. Then it seemed fishy. What do you think?”

===Chinese===
特雷弗把休布洛瑟姆那件事讲了一遍。

克劳斯顿时兴奋起来。

“那兰德-布朗肯定就是那家伙，”他说，“你干吗不去会会他？没准球棒就藏在他书房里。”

“不在他书房，”特雷弗说，“因为我到处翻遍了，还让他把口袋也掏给我看过。可我还是敢发誓，他知道些内情。有件事我觉得有点可疑。我径直进他书房，把最后一封信拿给他看——就是关于球棒的那封，你晓得——并且指控是他写的。你想想，他要是跟这事毫无瓜葛，就不会明白信里说『你丢的那根球棒』是什么意思。在他听来，那没准就是根普普通通的板球棒。可他倒主动提出让我搜查书房。当时我没觉得不对劲，事后才咂摸出味儿来，太蹊跷了。你怎么看？”

===Original===
Clowes thought so too, but admitted that he did not see of what use the suspicion was going to be. Whether Rand-Brown knew anything about the affair or not, it was quite certain that the bat was not with him.

===Chinese===
克劳斯也这么想，但坦言看不出这点疑心能派上什么用场。兰德-布朗究竟知不知情暂且不论，球棒不在他手里，倒是千真万确的。

===Original===
O’Hara, meanwhile, had decided that the time had come for him to resume his detective duties. Moriarty agreed with him, and they resolved that that night they would patronise the vault instead of the gymnasium, and take a holiday as far as their boxing was concerned. There was plenty of time before the Aldershot competition.

Lockup was still at six, so at a quarter to that hour they slipped down into the vault, and took up their position.

A quarter of an hour passed. The lockup bell sounded faintly. Moriarty began to grow tired.

===Chinese===
与此同时，奥哈拉（O’Hara）认定重操侦探旧业的时机到了。莫里亚蒂（Moriarty）深表赞同，两人决定当晚不去体育馆，改去光顾地窖，拳击方面放自己一天假。离奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）比赛还早着呢。

锁门仍是六点，于是差一刻六点时，两人溜进地窖，各就各位。

一刻钟过去了。锁门铃声隐隐响起。莫里亚蒂开始腻烦了。

===Original===
“Is it worth it?” he said, “an’ wouldn’t they have come before, if they meant to come?”

“We’ll give them another quarter of an hour,” said O’Hara. “After that﻿—”

“*Sh!*” whispered Moriarty.

The door had opened. They could see a figure dimly outlined in the semidarkness. Footsteps passed down into the vault, and there came a sound as if the unknown had cannoned into a chair, followed by a sharp intake of breath, expressive of pain. A scraping sound, and a flash of light, and part of the vault was lit by a candle. O’Hara caught a glimpse of the unknown’s face as he rose from lighting the candle, but it was not enough to enable him to recognise him. The candle was standing on a chair, and the light it gave was too feeble to reach the face of anyone not on a level with it.

===Chinese===
“这值得吗？”他说，“他们要真打算来，不早就来了？”

“再给他们一刻钟，”奥哈拉说，“过了这村——”

“*嘘！*”莫里亚蒂低声道。

门开了。昏暗中隐约可见一个人影。脚步声往地窖深处下去，接着传来一声闷响，仿佛那陌生人撞上了椅子，随后是猛地吸进一口冷气、显然是疼的动静。一阵刮擦声，一道光闪过，蜡烛照亮了地窖的一角。陌生人俯身点完蜡烛直起身时，奥哈拉瞥见了他的脸，可还不足以认出是谁。蜡烛立在椅子上，光线太弱，照不到与它不在同一高度上的任何人的脸。

===Original===
The unknown began to drag chairs out into the neighbourhood of the light. O’Hara counted six.

The sixth chair had scarcely been placed in position when the door opened again. Six other figures appeared in the opening one after the other, and bolted into the vault like rabbits into a burrow. The last of them closed the door after them.

O’Hara nudged Moriarty, and Moriarty nudged O’Hara; but neither made a sound. They were not likely to be seen﻿—the blackness of the vault was too Egyptian for that﻿—but they were so near to the chairs that the least whisper must have been heard. Not a word had proceeded from the occupants of the chairs so far. If O’Hara’s suspicion was correct, and this was really the League holding a meeting, their methods were more secret than those of any other secret society in existence. Even the Nihilists probably exchanged a few remarks from time to time, when they met together to plot. But these men of mystery never opened their lips. It puzzled O’Hara.

===Chinese===
那陌生人开始把椅子一张张拖到亮光附近。奥哈拉数了数，一共六张。

第六张椅子刚摆到位，门又开了。另外六个人影在门口鱼贯出现，像兔子钻洞似的一头扎进地窖。走在最后的那位随手把门带上了。

奥哈拉用胳膊肘碰碰莫里亚蒂，莫里亚蒂也用胳膊肘碰碰奥哈拉；可谁都没出声。他们是不会被看见的——地窖的黑，够得上埃及黑暗之灾的级别——可他们离那圈椅子太近，最低声的耳语也必定被听见。到目前为止，椅子上的诸位一言未发。如果奥哈拉的猜测没错，这真是联盟在开会，那他们保密的做派可比世上任何秘密社团都严。就连虚无党人聚在一起密谋时，大概也不时交换几句言语。可这帮神秘人物硬是双唇紧闭。奥哈拉纳闷极了。

===Original===
The light of the candle was obscured for a moment, and a sound of puffing came from the darkness.

O’Hara nudged Moriarty again.

“Smoking!” said the nudge.

Moriarty nudged O’Hara.

“Smoking it is!” was the meaning of the movement.

A strong smell of tobacco showed that the diagnosis had been a true one. Each of the figures in turn lit his pipe at the candle, and sat back, still in silence. It could not have been very pleasant, smoking in almost pitch darkness, but it was breaking rules, which was probably the main consideration that swayed the smokers. They puffed away steadily, till the two Irishmen were wrapped about in invisible clouds.

===Chinese===
烛光暗了一瞬，黑暗中传来吸烟吐气的声音。

奥哈拉又碰了碰莫里亚蒂。

这一碰是说：“抽烟呢！”

莫里亚蒂碰了碰奥哈拉。

这一碰的意思是：“正是抽烟！”

浓烈的烟草味证明这一诊断准确无误。那几个人影挨个就着蜡烛点燃烟斗，靠回椅背，依旧一言不发。在近乎漆黑的地方抽烟，想必不怎么舒服，但这是在违反校规，而这多半才是左右烟客们的主要考量。他们不紧不慢地吞云吐雾，直到把两个爱尔兰人裹进了看不见的云雾里。

===Original===
Then a strange thing happened. I know that I am infringing copyright in making that statement, but it so exactly suits the occurrence, that perhaps Mr. Rider Haggard will not object. It *was* a strange thing that happened.

A rasping voice shattered the silence.

“You boys down there,” said the voice, “come here immediately. Come here, I say.”

It was the well-known voice of Mr. Robert Dexter, O’Hara and Moriarty’s beloved housemaster.

===Chinese===
接着，一件怪事发生了。我知道这么说侵犯版权，但这句话跟这桩事实在太贴切，里德·哈格德先生（Rider Haggard）想必不会见怪。发生的*确实*是件怪事。

一个刺耳的嗓音击碎了寂静。

“下面那些小子，”那嗓音说，“立刻上来。我说，立刻上来。”

那是罗伯特·德克斯特先生（Mr. Robert Dexter）——奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂敬爱的舍监——人尽皆知的嗓音。

===Original===
The two Irishmen simultaneously clutched one another, each afraid that the other would think﻿—from force of long habit﻿—that the housemaster was speaking to him. Both stood where they were. It was the men of mystery and tobacco that Dexter was after, they thought.

But they were wrong. What had brought Dexter to the vault was the fact that he had seen two boys, who looked uncommonly like O’Hara and Moriarty, go down the steps of the vault at a quarter to six. He had been doing his usual after-lockup prowl on the junior gravel, to intercept stragglers, and he had been a witness﻿—from a distance of fifty yards, in a very bad light﻿—of the descent into the vault. He had remained on the gravel ever since, in the hope of catching them as they came up; but as they had not come up, he had determined to make the first move himself. He had not seen the six unknowns go down, for, the evening being chilly, he had paced up and down, and they had by a lucky accident chosen a moment when his back was turned.

===Chinese===
两个爱尔兰人同时一把抓住对方，各自生怕对方出于长年积习，以为舍监叫的是自己。两人都站在原地没动。他们想，德克斯特要抓的是那帮神秘人物兼烟客。

可他们想错了。把德克斯特引到地窖来的，是这样一桩事实：他看见两个少年在差一刻六点时走下了地窖的台阶，模样长得出奇地像奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂。当时他正在低年级砾石场上做锁门后例行的巡弋，堵截晚归的散兵游勇，于是远远地——隔着五十码，光线又极差——目击了这次地窖之行。此后他一直守在砾石场上，指望等他们上来时人赃并获；可他们迟迟不上来，他便决定自己先走一步。那六个不速之客下去时他没瞧见，因为傍晚天凉，他来回踱步暖身，而他们碰巧拣中了他背过身去的一瞬。

===Original===
“Come up immediately,” he repeated.

Here a blast of tobacco-smoke rushed at him from the darkness. The candle had been extinguished at the first alarm, and he had not realised﻿—though he had suspected it﻿—that smoking had been going on.

A hurried whispering was in progress among the unknowns. Apparently they saw that the game was up, for they picked their way towards the door.

As each came up the steps and passed him, Mr. Dexter observed “Ha!” and appeared to make a note of his name. The last of the six was just leaving him after this process had been completed, when Mr. Dexter called him back.

===Chinese===
“立刻上来。”他又说了一遍。

一股烟草烟雾劈头从黑暗中朝他涌来。蜡烛在第一声警报时就已被吹灭，他虽然有所怀疑，却没意识到此前一直在抽烟。

那几个陌生人正低声急促地交换意见。显然他们明白大势已去，便择着路朝门口走去。

每上来一个、走过他身边，德克斯特先生便哼一声“哈！”，似乎把名字记下了一笔。六人中的最后一个刚走完这道工序要离开，德克斯特先生又把他叫了回来。

===Original===
“That is not all,” he said, suspiciously.

“Yes, sir,” said the last of the unknowns.

Neither of the Irishmen recognised the voice. Its owner was a stranger to them.

“I tell you it is not,” snapped Mr. Dexter. “You are concealing the truth from me. O’Hara and Moriarty are down there﻿—two boys in my own house. I saw them go down there.”

“They had nothing to do with us, sir. We saw nothing of them.”

“I have no doubt,” said the housemaster, “that you imagine that you are doing a very chivalrous thing by trying to hide them, but you will gain nothing by it. You may go.”

===Chinese===
“还没完。”他狐疑地说。

“完了，先生。”最后那位陌生人说。

两个爱尔兰人谁都没听出这嗓音。它的主人对他们来说是个陌生人。

“我告诉你，没完，”德克斯特先生厉声道，“你在向我隐瞒实情。奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂就在下面——我自己宿舍的两个学生。我亲眼看见他们下去的。”

“他们跟我们没关系，先生。我们根本没见到他们。”

“我不怀疑，”舍监说，“你们自以为包庇同伴是件很有骑士风度的事，可这样你们什么好处也捞不着。走吧。”

===Original===
He came to the top of the steps, and it seemed as if he intended to plunge into the darkness in search of the suspects. But, probably realising the futility of such a course, he changed his mind, and delivered an ultimatum from the top step.

“O’Hara and Moriarty.”

No reply.

“O’Hara and Moriarty, I know perfectly well that you are down there. Come up immediately.”

===Chinese===
他走到台阶顶上，看那架势，是要一头扎进黑暗里去搜捕嫌犯了。但多半是意识到此举徒劳，他改了主意，站在最高一级台阶上发表最后通牒。

“奥哈拉，莫里亚蒂。”

无人应答。

“奥哈拉，莫里亚蒂，我心里一清二楚，你们就在下面。立刻上来。”

===Original===
Dignified silence from the vault.

“Well, I shall wait here till you do choose to come up. You would be well advised to do so immediately. I warn you you will not tire me out.”

He turned, and the door slammed behind him.

“What’ll we do?” whispered Moriarty. It was at last safe to whisper.

“Wait,” said O’Hara, “I’m thinking.”

===Chinese===
地窖方面以庄严的沉默相对。

“好，我就在这儿等，等你们自愿上来。识相的马上就上来。我警告你们，休想把我耗垮。”

他一转身，门在他身后砰地关上了。

“咱们怎么办？”莫里亚蒂低声问。现在低语总算安全了。

“等等，”奥哈拉说，“我在想。”

===Original===
O’Hara thought. For many minutes he thought in vain. At last there came flooding back into his mind a memory of the days of his faghood. It was after that that he had been groping all the time. He remembered now. Once in those days there had been an unexpected function in the middle of term. There were needed for that function certain chairs. He could recall even now his furious disgust when he and a select body of fellow fags had been pounced upon by their form-master, and coerced into forming a line from the junior block to the cloisters, for the purpose of handing chairs. True, his form-master had stood ginger-beer after the event, with princely liberality, but the labour was of the sort that gallons of ginger-beer will not make pleasant. But he ceased to regret the episode now. He had been at the extreme end of the chair-handling chain. He had stood in a passage in the junior block, just by the door that led to the masters’ garden, and which﻿—he remembered﻿—was never locked till late at night. And while he stood there, a pair of hands﻿—apparently without a body﻿—had heaved up chair after chair through a black opening in the floor. In other words, a trapdoor connected with the vault in which he now was.

He imparted these reminiscences of childhood to Moriarty. They set off to search for the missing door, and, after wanderings and barkings of shins too painful to relate, they found it. Moriarty lit a match. The light fell on the trapdoor, and their last doubts were at an end. The thing opened inwards. The bolt was on their side, not in the passage above them. To shoot the bolt took them one second, to climb into the passage one minute. They stood at the side of the opening, and dusted their clothes.

===Chinese===
奥哈拉想着。想了好几分钟，全是白想。终于，当学童跑腿岁月的记忆潮水般涌回脑海。他一直苦苦摸索的正是那段往事。他想起来了。当年学期中段，曾有一场临时加出的典礼。那场典礼需要若干椅子。直到如今，他仍能回忆起自己的切齿痛恨：他和一小撮难兄难弟被班主任逮个正着，被迫排成一条从低年级楼到回廊的人链，专司传递椅子。诚然，事后班主任以王公般的慷慨请大家喝了姜汁汽水，可那种苦役，是几加仑姜汁汽水也冲不甜的。不过此刻他不再后悔那段经历了。他当年站在传椅链条的最末一环，守在低年级楼的一条过道里，就在那扇通往教师花园的门边——他记得——那扇门到深夜才上锁。他站在那儿时，有一双手——看上去没连着身子——从地板上一个黑洞里一张接一张往上举椅子。换句话说，那儿有一扇活板门，通着他此刻所在的地窖。

他把这些童年往事讲给莫里亚蒂听。两人出发去找那扇失踪的门，一番摸索周折、外加几记疼得不宜细述的胫骨磕碰之后，找到了。莫里亚蒂划亮一根火柴。光照在活板门上，最后的疑团烟消云散。门是朝里开的。门闩在他们这一侧，不在头顶的过道那边。闩门用了一秒，爬进过道用了一分钟。他们站在洞口边上，掸了掸衣服。

===Original===
“Bedad!” said Moriarty, suddenly.

“What?”

“Why, how are we to shut it?”

This was a problem that wanted some solving. Eventually they managed it, O’Hara leaning over and fishing for it, while Moriarty held his legs.

===Chinese===
“乖乖！”莫里亚蒂忽然叫了一声。

“怎么？”

“哎，咱们怎么把门关上？”

这可是个需要好好解决的问题。最后他们成功了：奥哈拉探身下去够那门，莫里亚蒂抱住他的双腿。

===Original===
As luck would have it﻿—and luck had stood by them well all through﻿—there was a bolt on top of the trapdoor, as well as beneath it.

“Supposing that had been shot!” said O’Hara, as they fastened the door in its place.

Moriarty did not care to suppose anything so unpleasant.

Mr. Dexter was still prowling about on the junior gravel, when the two Irishmen ran round and across the senior gravel to the gymnasium. Here they put in a few minutes’ gentle sparring, and then marched boldly up to Mr. Day (who happened to have looked in five minutes after their arrival) and got their paper.

===Chinese===
也是运气使然——这一晚运气始终眷顾他们——活板门上面也有一道闩，和下面一样。

“要是那道闩先插上了，可怎么好！”他们把门固定归位时，奥哈拉说。

莫里亚蒂不愿设想如此不愉快的情形。

德克斯特先生还在低年级砾石场上逡巡时，两个爱尔兰人已绕过道、穿过高年级砾石场，直奔体育馆。他们在那儿温和地空过了几分钟拳，随后大摇大摆走到戴先生（Mr. Day）跟前——他们到达五分钟后，戴先生恰巧进来转了一圈——领到了各自的条子。

===Original===
“What time did O’Hara and Moriarty arrive at the gymnasium?” asked Mr. Dexter of Mr. Day next morning.

“O’Hara and Moriarty? Really, I can’t remember. I know they *left* at about a quarter to seven.”

That profound thinker, Mr. Tony Weller, was never so correct as in his views respecting the value of an alibi. There are few better things in an emergency.

===Chinese===
“奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂是几点到体育馆的？”第二天早晨，德克斯特先生问戴先生。

“奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂？真不巧，记不得了。我只知道他们*离开*大约是在差一刻七点。”

那位深刻的思想家托尼·威勒先生（Tony Weller），他关于不在场证明之价值的见解从未如此正确过。危急关头，鲜有比这更好用的东西。

## XIV The White Figure / 白色人影

===Original===
“Suppose,” said Shoeblossom to Barry, as they were walking over to school on the morning following the day on which Milton’s study had passed through the hands of the League, “suppose you thought somebody had done something, but you weren’t quite certain who, but you knew it was someone, what would you do?”

===Chinese===
“假设，”休布洛瑟姆对巴里说——这是弥尔顿的书房经由联盟之手洗礼之后的第二天早晨，两人正往学校走，“假设你认为有人干了某件事，可你不太确定是谁，只知道是某个人，你会怎么办？”

===Original===
“What on *earth* do you mean?” inquired Barry.

“I was trying to make an A.B. case of it,” explained Shoeblossom.

“What’s an A.B. case?”

“I don’t know,” admitted Shoeblossom, frankly. “But it comes in a book of Stevenson’s. I think it must mean a sort of case where you call everyone A. and B. and don’t tell their names.”

===Chinese===
“你到底*什么*意思？”巴里问。

“我正想把它弄成个甲乙案（A.B. case）。”休布洛瑟姆解释道。

“什么是甲乙案？”

“我也不知道，”休布洛瑟姆坦白承认，“不过斯蒂文森（Stevenson）的一本书里有这说法。我想它大概是说一种案子，把人都称做甲先生、乙先生，而不说他们的名字。”

===Original===
“Well, go ahead.”

“It’s about Milton’s study.”

“What! what about it?”

“Well, you see, the night it was ragged I was sitting in my study with a dark lantern﻿—”

“What!”

===Chinese===
“好吧，说下去。”

“是关于弥尔顿书房的事。”

“什么！书房怎么了？”

“呃，是这样的，书房被捣乱那晚，我正提着一盏遮光提灯坐在自己书房里——”

“什么！”

===Original===
Shoeblossom proceeded to relate the moving narrative of his night-walking adventure. He dwelt movingly on his state of mind when standing behind the door, waiting for Mr. Seymour to come in and find him. He related with appropriate force the hair-raising episode of the weird white figure. And then he came to the conclusions he had since drawn (in calmer moments) from that apparition’s movements.

“You see,” he said, “I saw it coming out of Milton’s study, and that must have been about the time the study was ragged. And it went into Rigby’s dorm. So it must have been a chap in that dorm, who did it.”

Shoeblossom was quite clever at rare intervals. Even Barry, whose belief in his sanity was of the smallest, was compelled to admit that here, at any rate, he was talking sense.

===Chinese===
休布洛瑟姆接着讲述了他夜游历险的动人故事。讲到自己躲在门后、等着西摩先生（Mr. Seymour）进来撞见他时的心情，他说得哀婉动人。讲到那个怪异白色人影的骇人一幕，他用了相称的力度。最后才说到他（在心平气和的时刻）从那幽灵的行踪得出的结论。

“你明白，”他说，“我看见它是从弥尔顿书房里出来的，那准是书房被捣乱的当口。然后它进了里格比（Rigby）的寝室。所以干这事的，准是那间寝室里的哪个家伙。”

休布洛瑟姆隔三差五也能聪明一回。就连巴里——他对休布洛瑟姆神志健全的信心微乎其微——也不得不承认，这话至少说得在理。

===Original===
“What would you do?” asked Shoeblossom.

“Tell Milton, of course,” said Barry.

“But he’d give me beans for being out of the dorm, after lights-out.”

This was a distinct point to be considered. The attitude of Barry towards Milton was different from that of Shoeblossom. Barry regarded him﻿—through having played with him in important matches﻿—as a good sort of fellow who had always behaved decently to him. Leather-Twigg, on the other hand, looked on him with undisguised apprehension, as one in authority who would give him lines the first time he came into contact with him, and cane him if he ever did it again. He had a decided disinclination to see Milton on any pretext whatever.

===Chinese===
“换你怎么办？”休布洛瑟姆问。

“告诉弥尔顿呗。”巴里说。

“可他会因为我熄灯后溜出寝室狠收拾我的。”

这倒是个不容小觑的问题。巴里对弥尔顿的态度，与休布洛瑟姆的不同。巴里——因为在大赛中同场打过球——把他看作一个正派人，待自己一向不坏。而「莱瑟-特威格」（Leather-Twigg）——休布洛瑟姆的诨名——看待他则带着毫不掩饰的畏惧，视他为一个掌权的角色：头一回打交道就罚你抄书，再犯就用藤条抽你。无论借什么由头，他都打心底里不愿见弥尔顿。

===Original===
“Suppose I tell him?” suggested Barry.

“You’ll keep my name dark?” said Shoeblossom, alarmed.

Barry said he would make an A.B. case of it.

After school he went to Milton’s study, and found him still brooding over its departed glories.

===Chinese===
“我去转告他，如何？”巴里提议。

“你可要替我保密姓名？”休布洛瑟姆惊慌地说。

巴里说，他会把这办成一桩甲乙案。

放学后他去了弥尔顿的书房，发现他还在对那逝去的辉煌凭吊伤怀。

===Original===
“I say, Milton, can I speak to you for a second?”

“Hullo, Barry. Come in.”

Barry came in.

“I had forty-three photographs,” began Milton, without preamble. “All destroyed. And I’ve no money to buy any more. I had seventeen of Edna May.”

===Chinese===
“喂，弥尔顿，能跟你说句话吗？”

“你好，巴里。进来。”

巴里走了进来。

“我本来有四十三张照片，”弥尔顿开门见山，“全毁了。我又没钱再买。其中埃德娜·梅（Edna May）的就有十七张。”

===Original===
Barry, feeling that he was expected to say something, said, “By Jove! Really?”

“In various positions,” continued Milton. “All ruined.”

“Not really?” said Barry.

“There was one of Little Tich﻿—”

===Chinese===
巴里觉得该说点什么，便说：“天哪！真的？”

“各种姿势都有，”弥尔顿接着说，“全毁了。”

“不会吧？”巴里说。

“还有一张小蒂奇（Little Tich）的——”

===Original===
But Barry felt unequal to playing the part of chorus any longer. It was all very thrilling, but, if Milton was going to run through the entire list of his destroyed photographs, life would be too short for conversation on any other topic.

“I say, Milton,” he said, “it was about that that I came. I’m sorry﻿—”

Milton sat up.

“It wasn’t you who did this, was it?”

“No, no,” said Barry, hastily.

===Chinese===
可巴里自觉无力再扮演合唱队了。这一切固然扣人心弦，但要是弥尔顿打算把被毁照片的清单逐一过完，这辈子就别想谈别的题目了。

“我说，弥尔顿，”他说，“我就是为这事来的。很遗憾——”

弥尔顿坐直了身子。

“干这事的不至于是你吧？”

“不，不，”巴里连忙说。

===Original===
“Oh, I thought from your saying you were sorry﻿—”

“I was going to say I thought I could put you on the track of the chap who did do it﻿—”

For the second time since the interview began Milton sat up.

“Go on,” he said.

“﻿—But I’m sorry I can’t give you the name of the fellow who told me about it.”

===Chinese===
“哦，听你说很遗憾，我还以为——”

“我是想说，我想我能帮你摸到干这事那家伙的行踪——”

这是谈话开始以来，弥尔顿第二次坐直身子。

“说下去。”他说。

“——不过很遗憾，告诉我这事的那家伙的名字，我不能给你。”

===Original===
“That doesn’t matter,” said Milton. “Tell me the name of the fellow who did it. That’ll satisfy me.”

“I’m afraid I can’t do that, either.”

“Have you any idea what you *can* do?” asked Milton, satirically.

“I can tell you something which may put you on the right track.”

“That’ll do for a start. Well?”

===Chinese===
“那不要紧，”弥尔顿说，“把干事那家伙的名字告诉我就成。那我才满意。”

“恐怕那个我也给不了。”

“那你知道自己*能*做什么吗？”弥尔顿讥讽地问。

“我能告诉你一件也许能引上正路的事。”

“开场总算有了。说吧？”

===Original===
“Well, the chap who told me﻿—I’ll call him A.; I’m going to make an A.B. case of it﻿—was coming out of his study at about one o’clock in the morning﻿—”

“What the deuce was he doing that for?”

“Because he wanted to go back to bed,” said Barry.

“About time, too. Well?”

“As he was going past your study, a white figure emerged﻿—”

===Chinese===
“是这样的，告诉我这事的那家伙——我管他叫甲先生；我要把这办成甲乙案——凌晨一点来钟正从他书房里出来——”

“他深更半夜出来干什么？”

“因为他想回床上去。”巴里说。

“也差不多该回了。后来呢？”

“他路过你书房时，一个白色人影冒了出来——”

===Original===
“I should strongly advise you, young Barry,” said Milton, gravely, “not to try and rot me in any way. You’re a jolly good wing three-quarters, but you shouldn’t presume on it. I’d slay the Old Man himself if he rotted me about this business.”

Barry was quite pained at this sceptical attitude in one whom he was going out of his way to assist.

“I’m not rotting,” he protested. “This is all quite true.”

===Chinese===
“小巴里，我郑重奉劝你，”弥尔顿板着脸说，“别想拿任何方式涮我。你是个呱呱叫的翼锋三分卫，可也别仗着这个放肆。这事儿谁敢涮我，老头子亲自来我也照宰不误。”

巴里专门绕路来帮忙，对方却持这种怀疑态度，他心里相当不是滋味。

“我没涮你，”他抗议道，“句句是真。”

===Original===
“Well, go on. You were saying something about white figures emerging.”

“Not white figures. A white figure,” corrected Barry. “It came out of your study﻿—”

“﻿—And vanished through the wall?”

“It went into Rigby’s dorm.,” said Barry, sulkily. It was maddening to have an exclusive bit of news treated in this way.

“Did it, by Jove!” said Milton, interested at last. “Are you sure the chap who told you wasn’t pulling your leg? Who was it told you?”

===Chinese===
“好吧，说下去。你刚说到什么白色人影冒出来。”

“不是什么人影们。是一个人影，”巴里纠正道，“它从你书房里出来——”

“——然后穿墙消失了？”

“它进了里格比的寝室。”巴里闷闷地说。独家的猛料竟受这般对待，真是叫人火大。

“真有这事，天哪！”弥尔顿说，总算来了兴趣，“你确定告诉你的人不是逗你玩？是谁告诉你的？”

===Original===
“I promised him not to say.”

“Out with it, young Barry.”

“I won’t,” said Barry.

“You aren’t going to tell me?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
“我答应过他不说。”

“招了吧，小巴里。”

“不招。”巴里说。

“你不打算告诉我？”

“不打算。”

===Original===
Milton gave up the point with much cheerfulness. He liked Barry, and he realised that he had no right to try and make him break his promise.

“That’s all right,” he said. “Thanks very much, Barry. This may be useful.”

“I’d tell you his name if I hadn’t promised, you know, Milton.”

“It doesn’t matter,” said Milton. “It’s not important.”

“Oh, there was one thing I forgot. It was a biggish chap the fellow saw.”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿痛痛快快地放弃了这个话题。他喜欢巴里，也明白自己无权逼人毁约。

“这样也好，”他说，“多谢了，巴里。这情报没准有用。”

“要不是答应了，我准会告诉你名字的，你晓得，弥尔顿。”

“不要紧，”弥尔顿说，“不重要。”

“哦，有件事我忘了说。那家伙看见的是个块头挺大的家伙。”

===Original===
“How big! My size?”

“Not quite so tall, I should think. He said he was about Seymour’s size.”

“Thanks. That’s worth knowing. Thanks very much, Barry.”

When his visitor had gone, Milton proceeded to unearth one of the printed lists of the house which were used for purposes of roll-call. He meant to find out who were in Rigby’s dormitory. He put a tick against the names. There were eighteen of them. The next thing was to find out which of them was about the same height as Mr. Seymour. It was a somewhat vague description, for the housemaster stood about five feet nine or eight, and a good many of the dormitory were that height, or near it. At last, after much brain-work, he reduced the number of “possibles” to seven. These seven were Rigby himself, Linton, Rand-Brown, Griffith, Hunt, Kershaw, and Chapple. Rigby might be scratched off the list at once. He was one of Milton’s greatest friends. Exeunt also Griffith, Hunt, and Kershaw. They were mild youths, quite incapable of any deed of devilry. There remained, therefore, Chapple, Linton, and Rand-Brown. Chapple was a boy who was invariably late for breakfast. The inference was that he was not likely to forego his sleep for the purpose of wrecking studies. Chapple might disappear from the list. Now there were only Linton and Rand-Brown to be considered. His suspicions fell on Rand-Brown. Linton was the last person, he thought, to do such a low thing. He was a cheerful, rollicking individual, who was popular with everyone and seemed to like everyone. He was not an orderly member of the house, certainly, and on several occasions Milton had found it necessary to drop on him heavily for creating disturbances. But he was not the sort that bears malice. He took it all in the way of business, and came up smiling after it was over. No, everything pointed to Rand-Brown. He and Milton had never got on well together, and quite recently they had quarrelled openly over the former’s play in the Day’s match. Rand-Brown must be the man. But Milton was sensible enough to feel that so far he had no real evidence whatever. He must wait.

===Chinese===
“多大？跟我一般大？”

“没那么高，我看。他说差不多是西摩先生的个头。”

“谢谢。这值得知道。多谢了，巴里。”

客人走后，弥尔顿翻出一张宿舍的油印名单——本来是点名用的。他打算查清里格比寝室里都有谁。他在那些名字上打了勾，一共十八个人。下一步是查明其中谁的身高与西摩先生相仿。这描述相当含糊，因为这位舍监身高约莫五英尺九或八英寸，寝室里达到或接近这个个头的着实不少。经过一番苦思，他终于把「可能分子」压缩到七个：里格比本人、林顿、兰德-布朗、格里菲思（Griffith）、亨特（Hunt）、克肖（Kershaw）和查普尔（Chapple）。里格比可以立刻从名单上划掉，他是弥尔顿最好的朋友之一。格里菲思、亨特和克肖同样可以「退场」。他们都是温和少年，干不出任何伤天害理的勾当。于是剩下查普尔、林顿和兰德-布朗。查普尔这孩子吃早饭永远迟到。由此推断，他不大可能为了捣毁书房而牺牲睡眠。查普尔也可以从名单上消失。如今需要考虑的只剩林顿和兰德-布朗。他的怀疑落在兰德-布朗头上。林顿在他看来是最不可能干这种下作事的人。他是个乐呵呵、闹嚷嚷的家伙，人缘极好，看上去也喜欢所有人。他确实不是宿舍里的安分分子，有几次弥尔顿还不得不因为他捣乱而重罚他。但他不是那种记仇的人。他把受罚全当公事，罚完照旧笑嘻嘻地凑上来。不，一切迹象都指向兰德-布朗。他和弥尔顿素来不睦，就在不久前，还因兰德-布朗在戴氏宿舍那场球里的表现公开吵了一架。兰德-布朗必是那人无疑。但弥尔顿还不失清醒，觉得自己至今没有半点真凭实据。只能等。

===Original===
On the following afternoon Seymour’s turned out to play Donaldson’s.

The game, like most house-matches, was played with the utmost keenness. Both teams had good three-quarters, and they attacked in turn. Seymour’s had the best of it forward, where Milton was playing a great game, but Trevor in the centre was the best outside on the field, and pulled up rush after rush. By halftime neither side had scored.

After halftime Seymour’s, playing downhill, came away with a rush to the Donaldsonites’ half, and Rand-Brown, with one of the few decent runs he had made in good class football that term, ran in on the left. Milton took the kick, but failed, and Seymour’s led by three points. For the next twenty minutes nothing more was scored. Then, when five minutes more of play remained, Trevor gave Clowes an easy opening, and Clowes sprinted between the posts. The kick was an easy one, and what sporting reporters term “the major points” were easily added.

===Chinese===
第二天下午，西摩宿舍出场迎战唐纳森宿舍。

这场比赛和多数宿舍赛一样，踢得火花四溅。两队都有出色的三分卫，轮番进攻。前锋线上西摩占优，弥尔顿在那里大放异彩；但坐镇中路的特雷弗是全场最好的外线球员，一次次化解冲锋。上半场打完，双方均无建树。

下半场西摩顺坡而下，一阵冲锋杀到唐纳森人的半场，兰德-布朗以他本学期高水平比赛里屈指可数的漂亮一冲，从左边攻入阵地。弥尔顿主踢追加射门，没能命中，西摩以三分领先。此后二十分钟再无建树。随后，离终场还剩五分钟时，特雷弗给克劳斯送上一个轻松的空当，克劳斯直插门柱之间。这一脚追加很容易，体育记者所谓的「大分」轻松入账。

===Original===
When there are five more minutes to play in an important house-match, and one side has scored a goal and the other a try, play is apt to become spirited. Both teams were doing all they knew. The ball came out to Barry on the right. Barry’s abilities as a three-quarter rested chiefly on the fact that he could dodge well. This eel-like attribute compensated for a certain lack of pace. He was past the Donaldson’s three-quarters in an instant, and running for the line, with only the back to pass, and with Clowes in hot pursuit. Another wriggle took him past the back, but it also gave Clowes time to catch him up. Clowes was a far faster runner, and he got to him just as he reached the twenty-five line. They came down together with a crash, Clowes on top, and as they fell the whistle blew.

===Chinese===
一场重要的宿舍赛还剩五分钟，一方攻门得手、另一方握有达阵，比赛往往会变得火气十足。双方都拼尽了全力。球传到右路的巴里手中。巴里身为三分卫的本钱，主要在于他闪转腾挪的功夫。这一鳗鱼属性弥补了他速度上的些许不足。他一晃便过了唐纳森的三分卫，直奔阵线而去，前面只剩最后卫一人要过，身后克劳斯穷追不舍。再一扭，他又过了那最后卫，却也给了克劳斯追上来的时间。克劳斯跑得快得多，赶在他冲到二十五码线时扑到了他。两人轰然一起倒地，克劳斯压在上面，倒地的同时哨声响了。

===Original===
“No-side,” said Mr. Aldridge, the master who was refereeing.

Clowes got up.

“All over,” he said. “Jolly good game. Hullo, what’s up?”

For Barry seemed to be in trouble.

“You might give us a hand up,” said the latter. “I believe I’ve twisted my beastly ankle or something.”

===Chinese===
“终场。”担任裁判的奥尔德里奇先生（Mr. Aldridge）说。

克劳斯爬了起来。

“结束了，”他说，“好一场痛快的球。咦，怎么了？”

原来巴里似乎遇上了麻烦。

“拉我一把，”后者说，“我看我把这该死的脚踝扭了，还是怎么的。”

## VI Trevor Remains Firm / 特雷弗立场坚定

===Original===
The most immediate effect of telling anybody not to do a thing is to make him do it, in order to assert his independence. Trevor’s first act on receipt of the letter was to include Barry in the team against the Town. It was what he would have done in any case, but, under the circumstances, he felt a peculiar pleasure in doing it. The incident also had the effect of recalling to his mind the fact that he had tried Barry in the first instance on his own responsibility, without consulting the committee. The committee of the first fifteen consisted of the two old colours who came immediately after the captain on the list. The powers of a committee varied according to the determination and truculence of the members of it. On any definite and important step, affecting the welfare of the fifteen, the captain theoretically could not move without their approval. But if the captain happened to be strong-minded and the committee weak, they were apt to be slightly out of it, and the captain would develop a habit of consulting them a day or so after he had done a thing. He would give a man his colours, and inform the committee of it on the following afternoon, when the thing was done and could not be repealed.

Trevor was accustomed to ask the advice of his lieutenants fairly frequently. He never gave colours, for instance, off his own bat. It seemed to him that it might be as well to learn what views Milton and Allardyce had on the subject of Barry, and, after the Town team had gone back across the river, defeated by a goal and a try to nil, he changed and went over to Seymour’s to interview Milton.

Milton was in an armchair, watching Renford brew tea. His was one of the few studies in the school in which there was an armchair. With the majority of his contemporaries, it would only run to the portable kind that fold up.

===Chinese===
告诉一个人别做某事，最立竿见影的效果，就是他偏要去做，好宣示自己的独立。特雷弗收到那封信后的头一个举动，就是把巴里排进对镇队的阵容。这本是他无论如何都要做的事，但在此情形下，做起来更有一股别样的快意。这件事也让他想起：当初试用巴里，是他自作主张，没跟委员会商量。一队委员会由名单上紧随队长之后的两位老队色队员组成。委员会的权力大小，视其成员的决心与凶悍程度而定。凡涉及一队福祉的重大明确举措，理论上队长非经委员会批准不得行动。但若队长恰好个性强硬而委员会软弱，他们便容易被晾在一边，队长则养成习惯，等事情办完一两天后再去打个招呼。他会先授某队员队色，到第二天下午再知会委员会——那时生米已成熟饭，无可挽回了。

特雷弗惯于相当频繁地征询两位副手的意见。比如说，他从不擅自授人队色。他觉得不妨先听听弥尔顿和阿拉代斯对巴里有什么看法。镇队被一次攻门加一次达阵对零分打得渡河回府之后，他换了衣服，动身去西摩宿舍面见弥尔顿。

弥尔顿正陷在扶手椅里，看着伦福德（Renford）烧茶。全校的书房里，有扶手椅的寥寥无几，他这间是其中之一。他的多数同龄人，只置办得起那种折叠式的便携货。

===Original===
“Come and have some tea, Trevor,” said Milton.

“Thanks. If there’s any going.”

“Heaps. Is there anything to eat, Renford?”

The fag, appealed to on this important point, pondered darkly for a moment.

“There *was* some cake,” he said.

===Chinese===
“来喝点茶吧，特雷弗。”弥尔顿说。

“谢谢。要是还有的话。”

“多着呢。有吃的没有，伦福德？”

那跑腿生（fag）在这重大问题上被咨询，阴沉着脸盘算片刻。

“蛋糕*本来*是有的。”他说。

===Original===
“That’s all right,” interrupted Milton, cheerfully. “Scratch the cake. I ate it before the match. Isn’t there anything else?”

Milton had a healthy appetite.

“Then there used to be some biscuits.”

“Biscuits are off. I finished ’em yesterday. Look here, young Renford, what you’d better do is cut across to the shop and get some more cake and some more biscuits, and tell ’em to put it down to me. And don’t be long.”

===Chinese===
“那就好办，”弥尔顿欢快地打断，“蛋糕作废。赛前我给吃了。还有别的没有？”

弥尔顿胃口极佳。

“那饼干*本来*也是有的。”

“饼干也免了。昨天我就报销了。听我说，小伦福德，你最好跑一趟铺子，再买点蛋糕、再买点饼干，让他们记我账上。别磨蹭。”

===Original===
“A miles better idea would be to send him over to Donaldson’s to fetch something from my study,” suggested Trevor. “It isn’t nearly so far, and I’ve got heaps of stuff.”

“Ripping. Cut over to Donaldson’s, young Renford. As a matter of fact,” he added, confidentially, when the emissary had vanished, “I’m not half sure that the other dodge would have worked. They seem to think at the shop that I’ve had about enough things on tick lately. I haven’t settled up for last term yet. I’ve spent all I’ve got on this study. What do you think of those photographs?”

===Chinese===
“高明一万倍的主意是派他去唐纳森宿舍，从我院子里取点吃的，”特雷弗建议，“路近得多，我那儿存货堆积如山。”

“妙极。跑去唐纳森宿舍吧，小伦福德。”待这位特使消失后，他压低声音补了一句：“说实话，前一个法子灵不灵，我心里一点底都没有。铺子那边好像觉得，我近来赊的东西已经够多了。上学期的账我还没结呢。我所有的钱都砸进这间书房了。你觉得那些照片怎么样？”

===Original===
Trevor got up and inspected them. They filled the mantelpiece and most of the wall above it. They were exclusively theatrical photographs, and of a variety to suit all tastes. For the earnest student of the drama there was Sir Henry Irving in *The Bells*, and Mr. Martin Harvey in *The Only Way*. For the admirers of the merely beautiful there were Messrs. Dan Leno and Herbert Campbell.

“Not bad,” said Trevor. “Beastly waste of money.”

“Waste of money!” Milton was surprised and pained at the criticism. “Why, you must spend your money on *something*.”

“Rot, I call it,” said Trevor. “If you want to collect something, why don’t you collect something worth having?”

===Chinese===
特雷弗起身端详。照片摆满了壁炉台，连上方大半面墙也占了。清一色是剧照，品类丰富，雅俗咸宜。供严肃的戏剧研习者，有亨利·欧文爵士（Sir Henry Irving）的《钟声》剧照，马丁·哈维先生（Mr. Martin Harvey）的《唯一之路》；供纯粹爱美之士，有丹·利诺（Dan Leno）与赫伯特·坎贝尔（Herbert Campbell）二位先生的玉照。

“不错，”特雷弗说，“钱糟蹋得够呛。”

“糟蹋钱！”弥尔顿对这句批评又惊又痛，“哎，人总得把钱花在*点什么*上头吧。”

“我看是犯傻，”特雷弗说，“想收藏，干吗不收藏点值得收藏的？”

===Original===
Just then Renford came back with the supplies.

“Thanks,” said Milton, “put ’em down. Does the billy boil, young Renford?”

Renford asked for explanatory notes.

“You’re a bit of an ass at times, aren’t you?” said Milton, kindly. “What I meant was, is the tea ready? If it is, you can scoot. If it isn’t, buck up with it.”

===Chinese===
正在这时，伦福德带着补给回来了。

“谢谢，”弥尔顿说，“搁下吧。水开了没有，小伦福德？”

伦福德请求进一步说明。

“你有时候真有点犯蠢，是不是？”弥尔顿和气地说，“我是问，茶好了没有？好了你就撒腿跑吧。没好就抓紧。”

===Original===
A sound of bubbling and a rush of steam from the spout of the kettle proclaimed that the billy did boil. Renford extinguished the Etna, and left the room, while Milton, murmuring vague formulae about “one spoonful for each person and one for the pot,” got out of his chair with a groan﻿—for the Town match had been an energetic one﻿—and began to prepare tea.

“What I really came round about﻿—” began Trevor.

“Half a second. I can’t find the milk.”

He went to the door, and shouted for Renford. On that overworked youth’s appearance, the following dialogue took place.

===Chinese===
水壶咕嘟作响，壶嘴蒸汽直冲，宣告水确实开了。伦福德熄了那座「埃特纳」小炉子，退出房间。弥尔顿一面咕哝着「每人一勺、壶再加一勺」之类的含糊口诀，一面呻吟着从椅子里起身——对镇队那一战实在耗人体力——动手备茶。

“我这次来，其实是想说——”特雷弗开口。

“稍等。牛奶找不着了。”

他走到门口，喊伦福德。这位超负荷服役的少年露面后，发生了如下对话。

===Original===
“Where’s the milk?”

“What milk?”

“My milk.”

“There isn’t any.” This in a tone not untinged with triumph, as if the speaker realised that here was a distinct score to him.

===Chinese===
“牛奶呢？”

“什么牛奶？”

“我的牛奶。”

“没有牛奶。”这话的口气不无得意，仿佛说话人意识到自己稳稳得了一分。

===Original===
“No milk?”

“No.”

“Why not?”

“You never had any.”

===Chinese===
“没牛奶？”

“没。”

“怎么会没？”

“压根就没有过。”

===Original===
“Well, just cut across﻿—no, half a second. What are you doing downstairs?”

“Having tea.”

“Then you’ve got milk.”

“Only a little.” This apprehensively.

“Bring it up. You can have what we leave.”

Disgusted retirement of Master Renford.

===Chinese===
“那你快去跑一趟——不，且慢。你在楼下干什么？”

“喝茶。”

“那你有牛奶。”

“就一丁点儿。”这话带着忧色。

“拿上来。剩下的归你们。”

伦福德少爷厌恶地退场。

===Original===
“What I really came about,” said Trevor again, “was business.”

“Colours?” inquired Milton, rummaging in the tin for biscuits with sugar on them. “Good brand of biscuit you keep, Trevor.”

“Yes. I think we might give Alexander and Parker their third.”

===Chinese===
“我这次来，其实是正事。”特雷弗又说。

“队色？”弥尔顿问，一边在饼干筒里翻找带糖霜的饼干，“你这饼干牌子不错啊，特雷弗。”

“是不错。我想咱们可以给亚历山大（Alexander）和帕克（Parker）授三队队色。”

===Original===
“All right. Any others?”

“Barry his second, do you think?”

“Rather. He played a good game today. He’s an improvement on Rand-Brown.”

“Glad you think so. I was wondering whether it was the right thing to do, chucking Rand-Brown out after one trial like that. But still, if you think Barry’s better﻿—”

===Chinese===
“行。还有谁？”

“给巴里授二队队色，你看呢？”

“当然。他今天打了一场好球。比兰德-布朗强。”

“你这么想我就高兴了。我一直在琢磨，像这样试用一次就把兰德-布朗踢出去，是否妥当。不过，既然你觉得巴里更好——”

===Original===
“Streets better. I’ve had heaps of chances of watching them and comparing them, when they’ve been playing for the house. It isn’t only that Rand-Brown can’t tackle, and Barry can. Barry takes his passes much better, and doesn’t lose his head when he’s pressed.”

“Just what I thought,” said Trevor. “Then you’d go on playing him for the first?”

“Rather. He’ll get better every game, you’ll see, as he gets more used to playing in the first three-quarter line. And he’s as keen as anything on getting into the team. Practises taking passes and that sort of thing every day.”

===Chinese===
“好出一截。他们替宿舍打球时，我观察比较的机会多得很。不光是兰德-布朗不会擒抱、巴里会。巴里接传球好得多，被逼急了也不乱阵脚。”

“正合我意，”特雷弗说，“那你就主张让他继续打一队？”

“当然。他会一场比一场好，你瞧着吧，等他打惯了一队的三分卫线。而且他进队的心气高得很。天天练接传球什么的。”

===Original===
“Well, he’ll get his colours if we lick Ripton.”

“We ought to lick them. They’ve lost one of their forwards, Clifford, a red-haired chap, who was good out of touch. I don’t know if you remember him.”

“I suppose I ought to go and see Allardyce about these colours, now. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
“那咱们要是赢了里普顿，他就能拿队色。”

“咱们理应赢。他们折了个前锋，克利福德（Clifford），红头发的家伙，边线附近打得好。不知道你还记得他不。”

“这些队色的事，我想我该去找阿拉代斯商量一下了。再见。”

===Original===
There was running and passing on the Monday for everyone in the three teams. Trevor and Clowes met Mr. Seymour as they were returning. Mr. Seymour was the football master at Wrykyn.

“I see you’ve given Barry his second, Trevor.”

“Yes, sir.”

“I think you’re wise to play him for the first. He knows the game, which is the great thing, and he will improve with practice,” said Mr. Seymour, thus corroborating Milton’s words of the previous Saturday.

“I’m glad Seymour thinks Barry good,” said Trevor, as they walked on. “I shall go on playing him now.”

===Chinese===
星期一，三支队全员练了跑动与传球。归途中，特雷弗和克劳斯遇见了西摩先生。西摩先生是里金的橄榄球教师。

“我看你们给巴里授了二队队色，特雷弗。”

“是的，先生。”

“你让他打一队，我认为是明智的。他懂球，这是最要紧的，而且多练还会进步。”西摩先生说，恰好印证了弥尔顿上星期六的话。

“西摩也认为巴里好，我高兴，”往前走时特雷弗说，“这下我要继续用他了。”

===Original===
“Found out who wrote that letter yet?”

Trevor laughed.

“Not yet,” he said.

“Probably Rand-Brown,” suggested Clowes. “He’s the man who would gain most by Barry’s not playing. I hear he had a row with Mill just before his study was ragged.”

“Everybody in Seymour’s has had rows with Mill some time or other,” said Trevor.

Clowes stopped at the door of the junior day-room to find his fag. Trevor went on upstairs. In the passage he met Ruthven.

Ruthven seemed excited.

“I say. Trevor,” he exclaimed, “have you seen your study?”

“Why, what’s the matter with it?”

“You’d better go and look.”

===Chinese===
“那封信查出是谁写的没有？”

特雷弗笑了。

“还没有。”他说。

“多半是兰德-布朗，”克劳斯猜测，“巴里不上场，得利最大的就是他。听说就在他的书房被捣乱之前不久，他还跟米尔吵过一架。”

“西摩宿舍的人，谁没在什么时候跟米尔吵过架。”特雷弗说。

克劳斯在低年级活动室门口停下，去找他的跑腿生。特雷弗上楼继续走。在过道里，他遇见了拉思文（Ruthven）。

拉思文显得很激动。

“我说，特雷弗，”他嚷道，“你看过你的书房了吗？”

“怎么，书房怎么了？”

“你最好去看看。”

## XIII Victim Number Three / 第三号受害者

===Original===
“With reference to our last communication,” ran the letter﻿—the writer evidently believed in the commercial style﻿—“it may interest you to know that the bat you lost by the statue on the night of the 26th of January has come into our possession. *We observe that Barry is still playing for the first fifteen.*”

“And will jolly well continue to,” muttered Trevor, crumpling the paper viciously into a ball.

He went on writing the names for the Ripton match. The last name on the list was Barry’s.

===Chinese===
“续我方上次函告，”信上写道——写信人显然信奉商业书信体——“您或许乐于知悉：您一月二十六日夜间在雕像旁遗失的球棒，现已归入我方手中。*我们注意到，巴里仍在一队出赛。*”

“而且还要继续打下去，打得漂亮着呢。”特雷弗咕哝道，恶狠狠地把信纸揉成一团。

他接着写对里普顿一战的名单。名单上最后一个名字，是巴里。

===Original===
Then he sat back in his chair, and began to wrestle with this new development. Barry must play. That was certain. All the bluff in the world was not going to keep him from playing the best man at his disposal in the Ripton match. He himself did not count. It was the school he had to think of. This being so, what was likely to happen? Though nothing was said on the point, he felt certain that if he persisted in ignoring the League, that bat would find its way somehow﻿—by devious routes, possibly﻿—to the headmaster or someone else in authority. And then there would be questions﻿—awkward questions﻿—and things would begin to come out. Then a fresh point struck him, which was, that whatever might happen would affect, not himself, but O’Hara. This made it rather more of a problem how to act. Personally, he was one of those dogged characters who can put up with almost anything themselves. If this had been his affair, he would have gone on his way without hesitating. Evidently the writer of the letter was under the impression that he had been the hero (or villain) of the statue escapade.

If everything came out it did not require any great effort of prophecy to predict what the result would be. O’Hara would go. Promptly. He would receive his marching orders within ten minutes of the discovery of what he had done. He would be expelled twice over, so to speak, once for breaking out at night﻿—one of the most heinous offences in the school code﻿—and once for tarring the statue. Anything that gave the school a bad name in the town was a crime in the eyes of the powers, and this was such a particularly flagrant case. Yes, there was no doubt of that. O’Hara would take the first train home without waiting to pack up. Trevor knew his people well, and he could imagine their feelings when the prodigal strolled into their midst﻿—an old Wrykinian malgré lui. As the philosopher said of falling off a ladder, it is not the falling that matters: it is the sudden stopping at the other end. It is not the being expelled that is so peculiarly objectionable: it is the sudden homecoming. With this gloomy vision before him, Trevor almost wavered. But the thought that the selection of the team had nothing whatever to do with his personal feelings strengthened him. He was simply a machine, devised to select the fifteen best men in the school to meet Ripton. In his official capacity of football captain he was not supposed to have any feelings. However, he yielded in so far that he went to Clowes to ask his opinion.

===Chinese===
随后他仰靠回椅背，开始与这一新变故角力。巴里必须上场。这一点板上钉钉。世上一切虚张声势，都休想阻止他在里普顿之战中派上手下最好的人。他自己算不得什么。他要考虑的是学校。既如此，可能发生什么？虽无人把话挑明，但他确信，如果他执意无视联盟，那根球棒总会以某种方式——没准绕上几条弯路——送到校长或其他掌权者手里。届时将会有问话——令人难堪的问话——然后事情就会渐次败露。接着一个新念头击中了他：无论发生什么，遭殃的不是他自己，而是奥哈拉。这让如何行事成了一个更大的难题。就他个人而言，他属于那种自己几乎什么都能忍的倔脾气。倘若这是他一个人的事，他会毫不迟疑照旧走下去。写信人显然以为，雕像事件的英雄（或者说恶棍）是他。

万一事情全部败露，不用多大的预言功夫也能料到结局。奥哈拉得走。马上走。他干的那些事一经查明，十分钟之内他就能领到滚蛋令。不妨说，他会被开除两回：一回因为夜里溜出学校——校规中最重的罪状之一——一回因为给雕像涂柏油。凡是让学校在镇上丢脸的事，在当权者眼里就是罪过，而这桩案子又格外嚣张。是的，毫无悬念。奥哈拉会连行李都不等收拾，搭上头一班火车回家。特雷弗跟他家里人都熟，完全可以想象那位浪子晃进家门、俨然一名非自愿的老里金人时，一家老小的心情。正如那位哲学家谈论从梯子上摔下来时所说：要紧的不是摔，而是那一头猛地停住。被开除本身倒没那么特别可憎：可憎的是突如其来的回家。眼前浮动着这幅惨淡图景，特雷弗几乎动摇了。但一想到选人组队与他的个人情感毫无干系，他又硬气起来。他只是一台机器，专为挑选全校最优秀的十五人迎战里普顿而造。以橄榄球队长的公职身份而论，他不该有任何情感。不过他还是让了步：跑去征求克劳斯的意见。

===Original===
Clowes, having heard everything and seen the letter, unhesitatingly voted for the right course. If fifty mad Irishmen were to be expelled, Barry must play against Ripton. He was the best man, and in he must go.

“That’s what I thought,” said Trevor. “It’s bad for O’Hara, though.”

Clowes remarked somewhat tritely that business was business.

“Besides,” he went on, “you’re assuming that the thing this letter hints at will really come off. I don’t think it will. A man would have to be such an awful blackguard to go as low as that. The least grain of decency in him would stop him. I can imagine a man threatening to do it as a piece of bluff﻿—by the way, the letter doesn’t actually say anything of the sort, though I suppose it hints at it﻿—but I can’t imagine anybody out of a melodrama doing it.”

===Chinese===
克劳斯听完全部原委、看过那封信，毫不犹豫地投票赞成正确的路线。哪怕要开除五十个疯疯癫癫的爱尔兰人，对里普顿这一战也必须上巴里。他是最合适的人，非上不可。

“我也是这么想的，”特雷弗说，“不过对奥哈拉可够呛。”

克劳斯说了句略显老套的话：公事是公事。

“再说了，”他接着讲，“你这等于认定信里暗示的事真会发生。我看不会。一个人得坏到什么份上，才堕落到那一步。只要他身上还剩一星半点的体面，就不会干。我能想象有人拿这个当恐吓使——顺带说一句，信里其实并没明说，顶多是暗示——可我想象不出哪个现实中的人会真干，那得是情节剧里的角色。”

===Original===
“You can never tell,” said Trevor. He felt that this was but an outside chance. The forbearance of one’s antagonist is but a poor thing to trust to at the best of times.

“Are you going to tell O’Hara?” asked Clowes.

“I don’t see the good. Would you?”

“No. He can’t do anything, and it would only give him a bad time. There are pleasanter things, I should think, than going on from day to day not knowing whether you’re going to be sacked or not within the next twelve hours. Don’t tell him.”

“I won’t. And Barry plays against Ripton.”

“Certainly. He’s the best man.”

===Chinese===
“这可说不准。”特雷弗说。他觉得那终归只是个万一。对手的克制，往好里说也是靠不住的东西。

“你打算告诉奥哈拉吗？”克劳斯问。

“我看没好处。你会吗？”

“不会。他无能为力，只会让他煎熬。要我说，比这更难受的事不多：一天天提心吊胆，不知道未来十二个小时内会不会被开除。别告诉他。”

“我不告诉。巴里照样打里普顿。”

“当然。他是最合适的人。”

===Original===
“I’m going over to Seymour’s now,” said Trevor, after a pause, “to see Milton. We’ve drawn Seymour’s in the next round of the house-matches. I suppose you knew. I want to get it over before the Ripton match, for several reasons. About half the fifteen are playing on one side or the other, and it’ll give them a good chance of getting fit. Running and passing is all right, but a good, hard game’s the thing for putting you into form. And then I was thinking that, as the side that loses, whichever it is﻿—”

“Seymour’s, of course.”

“Hope so. Well, they’re bound to be a bit sick at losing, so they’ll play up all the harder on Saturday to console themselves for losing the cup.”

“My word, what strategy!” said Clowes. “You think of everything. When do you think of playing it, then?”

“Wednesday struck me as a good day. Don’t you think so?”

“It would do splendidly. It’ll be a good match. For all practical purposes, of course, it’s the final. If we beat Seymour’s, I don’t think the others will trouble us much.”

===Chinese===
“我现在就去西摩宿舍，”略停了停，特雷弗说，“去见弥尔顿。宿舍赛下一轮咱们抽到了西摩。你大概知道了。我想赶在里普顿比赛之前把这场打完，理由有好几条。一队里约有一半人分在这两边参赛，正好让他们把状态练出来。跑动传球固然要紧，可要进入竞技状态，还得靠一场硬碰硬的好球。另外我又想，输的那一方，不管是哪边——”

“自然是西摩输。”

“但愿如此。嗯，输了球他们准会窝一肚子火，到了星期六必定加倍卖力，好安慰自己丢掉银杯的创伤。”

“好家伙，好深的心机！”克劳斯说，“你什么都算到了。那打算哪天打？”

“星期三我看不错。你说呢？”

“再好不过。会是一场好球。实际上，这就是决赛了。咱们要是赢了西摩，别的宿舍我看都构不成威胁。”

===Original===
There was just time to see Milton before lockup. Trevor ran across to Seymour’s, and went up to his study.

“Come in,” said Milton, in answer to his knock.

Trevor went in, and stood surprised at the difference in the look of the place since the last time he had visited it. The walls, once covered with photographs, were bare. Milton, seated before the fire, was ruefully contemplating what looked like a heap of waste cardboard.

Trevor recognised the symptoms. He had had experience.

“You don’t mean to say they’ve been at you, too!” he cried.

===Chinese===
锁门前刚好还来得及见弥尔顿一面。特雷弗一路小跑奔向西摩宿舍，上楼进了他的书房。

“进来。”弥尔顿应着敲门声说。

特雷弗走进去，惊讶地呆立当场：自上次来访，这地方的模样已面目全非。原本挂满照片的墙壁，光秃秃的。弥尔顿坐在炉火前，哀伤地凝视着一堆形同废纸板的东西。

特雷弗认出了这些症状。他有过来人的经验。

“你可别说，连你也让他们光顾了！”他喊道。

===Original===
Milton’s normally cheerful face was thunderous and gloomy.

“Yes. I was thinking what I’d like to do to the man who ragged it.”

“It’s the League again, I suppose?”

Milton looked surprised.

“*Again?*” he said, “where did *you* hear of the League? This is the first time I’ve heard of its existence, whatever it is. What is the confounded thing, and why on earth have they played the fool here? What’s the meaning of this bally rot?”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿平日乐呵呵的脸，此刻乌云密布。

“可不是。我正在想，该怎么收拾捣乱的那家伙。”

“我猜又是联盟干的？”

弥尔顿一脸诧异。

“*又*？”他说，“你*打哪儿*听说联盟的？这玩意儿到底是个啥，我头一回听说它存在。这该死的东西究竟是什么，他们跑我这里撒什么疯？这堆破烂玩意儿是什么意思？”

===Original===
He exhibited one of the variety of cards of which Trevor had already seen two specimens. Trevor explained briefly the style and nature of the League, and mentioned that his study also had been wrecked.

“Your study? Why, what have they got against you?”

“I don’t know,” said Trevor. Nothing was to be gained by speaking of the letters he had received.

“Did they cut up your photographs?”

“Every one.”

“I tell you what it is, Trevor, old chap,” said Milton, with great solemnity, “there’s a lunatic in the school. That’s what I make of it. A lunatic whose form of madness is wrecking studies.”

===Chinese===
他亮出一张卡片——这类东西，特雷弗已经见过两张样品。特雷弗简略说明了联盟的风格与性质，并提到自己的书房也遭了洗劫。

“你的书房？奇了，他们跟你有什么仇？”

“不知道。”特雷弗说。提起自己收到的那些信，不会有任何好处。

“他们撕你的照片了吗？”

“一张不剩。”

“我跟你讲，特雷弗，老伙计，”弥尔顿极为郑重地说，“学校里出了个疯子。这就是我的结论。一个以捣毁书房为疯病形式的疯子。”

===Original===
“But the same chap couldn’t have done yours and mine. It must have been a Donaldson’s fellow who did mine, and one of your chaps who did yours and Mill’s.”

“Mill’s? By Jove, of course. I never thought of that. That was the League, too, I suppose?”

“Yes. One of those cards was tied to a chair, but Clowes took it away before anybody saw it.”

===Chinese===
“可干你我两家的不可能是同一个家伙。洗劫我院子的必是唐纳森的人，干你和米尔这票的必是你们自己人。”

“米尔的？天哪，可不是嘛。我竟没想到。那也是联盟干的喽？”

“是。有一张卡片就拴在椅子上，不过克劳斯赶在别人看见之前把它拿走了。”

===Original===
Milton returned to the details of the disaster.

“Was there any ink spilt in your room?”

“Pints,” said Trevor, shortly. The subject was painful.

“So there was here,” said Milton, mournfully. “Gallons.”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿重新回到这场灾难的细节上来。

“你屋里洒墨水了吗？”

“论品脱。”特雷弗简短地说。这话题令人痛心。

“这儿也是。”弥尔顿悲切地说，“论加仑。”

===Original===
There was silence for a while, each pondering over his wrongs.

“Gallons,” said Milton again. “I was ass enough to keep a large pot full of it here, and they used it all, every drop. You never saw such a sight.”

Trevor said he had seen one similar spectacle.

===Chinese===
一时沉默，两人各自咀嚼着自己的血泪账。

“论加仑。”弥尔顿又说了一遍，“我这蠢货，竟然在这儿存了满满一大罐，让他们用了个精光，一滴不剩。你没见过那阵仗。”

特雷弗说自己也见过一场类似的景观。

===Original===
“And my photographs! You remember those photographs I showed you? All ruined. Slit across with a knife. Some torn in half. I wish I knew who did that.”

Trevor said he wished so, too.

“There was one of Mrs. Patrick Campbell,” Milton continued in heartrending tones, “which was torn into sixteen pieces. I counted them. There they are on the mantelpiece. And there was one of Little Tich” (here he almost broke down), “which was so covered with ink that for half an hour I couldn’t recognise it. Fact.”

===Chinese===
“还有我的照片！记得我给你看的那些吗？全毁了。叫刀子划的。有几张撕成两半。我多想知道是谁干的。”

特雷弗说他也很想知道。

“有一张帕特里克·坎贝尔夫人（Mrs. Patrick Campbell）的，”弥尔顿用摧人心肝的声调继续说，“被撕成了十六片。我数过。就搁在壁炉台上。还有一张小蒂奇的——说到这里他几乎崩溃——整个糊满墨水，我足足认了半个钟头才认出来。真的。”

===Original===
Trevor nodded sympathetically.

“Yes,” said Milton. “Soaked.”

There was another silence. Trevor felt it would be almost an outrage to discuss so prosaic a topic as the date of a house-match with one so broken up. Yet time was flying, and lockup was drawing near.

“Are you willing to play﻿—” he began.

“I feel as if I could never play again,” interrupted Milton. “You’d hardly believe the amount of blotting-paper I’ve used today. It must have been a lunatic, Dick, old man.”

===Chinese===
特雷弗同情地点头。

“是啊，”弥尔顿说，“泡透了。”

又是一阵沉默。同一位伤心至此的人讨论宿舍赛日期这种乏味题目，特雷弗觉得近乎残忍。可时间在飞逝，锁门将近。

“你愿不愿意打——”他开口。

“我觉得我这辈子再也打不了球了。”弥尔顿打断他，“你简直没法相信我今天用掉了多少吸墨纸。准是个疯子干的，迪克，老伙计。”

===Original===
When Milton called Trevor “Dick,” it was a sign that he was moved. When he called him “Dick, old man,” it gave evidence of an internal upheaval without parallel.

“Why, who else but a lunatic would get up in the night to wreck another chap’s study? All this was done between eleven last night and seven this morning. I turned in at eleven, and when I came down here again at seven the place was a wreck. It must have been a lunatic.”

“How do you account for the printed card from the League?”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿管特雷弗叫“迪克”，就是他心绪激动的标志。管他叫“迪克，老伙计”，则证明他内里正经历着一场史无前例的大动荡。

“你想啊，不是疯子，谁会半夜爬起来去捣毁别人的书房？这一切都发生在昨夜十一点到今早七点之间。我十一点上床，七点再下来，这儿已成了一片废墟。准是个疯子。”

“那联盟的油印卡片，你怎么解释？”

===Original===
Milton murmured something about madmen’s cunning and diverting suspicion, and relapsed into silence. Trevor seized the opportunity to make the proposal he had come to make, that Donaldson’s v. Seymour’s should be played on the following Wednesday.

Milton agreed listlessly.

“Just where you’re standing,” he said, “I found a photograph of Sir Henry Irving so slashed about that I thought at first it was Huntley Wright in *San Toy*.”

===Chinese===
弥尔顿咕哝了几句疯子的狡诈与转移嫌疑之类，重新陷入沉默。特雷弗抓住时机，提出了他此行要提的动议：唐纳森对西摩之战定在下周三。

弥尔顿无精打采地同意了。

“就在你现在站的地方，我捡到一张亨利·欧文爵士的照片，被划得稀烂，我起初还以为是亨特利·赖特（Huntley Wright）在《圣童》（*San Toy*）里的剧照。”

===Original===
“Start at two-thirty sharp,” said Trevor.

“I had seventeen of Edna May,” continued the stricken Seymourite, monotonously. “In various attitudes. All destroyed.”

“On the first fifteen ground, of course,” said Trevor. “I’ll get Aldridge to referee. That’ll suit you, I suppose?”

“All right. Anything you like. Just by the fireplace I found the remains of Arthur Roberts in *H.M.S. Irresponsible*. And part of Seymour Hicks. Under the table﻿—”

Trevor departed.

===Chinese===
“两点半准时开球。”特雷弗说。

“我本来有十七张埃德娜·梅的，”这位痛遭打击的西摩人平板地继续道，“各种姿势。全毁了。”

“当然用一队球场。”特雷弗说，“我请奥尔德里奇做裁判。你没意见吧？”

“行。随你便。就在壁炉旁边，我发现了阿瑟·罗伯茨（Arthur Roberts）在《皇家“不负责任号”》（*H.M.S. Irresponsible*）里的残骸。还有半张西摩·希克斯（Seymour Hicks）。桌子底下——”

特雷弗抽身走了。

## XXIII What Renford Saw / 伦福德的所见

===Original===
The health of Master Harvey of Seymour’s was so delicately constituted that it was an absolute necessity that he should consume one or more hot buns during the quarter of an hour’s interval which split up morning school. He was tearing across the junior gravel towards the shop on the morning following Trevor’s sparring practice with O’Hara, when a melodious treble voice called his name. It was Renford. He stopped, to allow his friend to come up with him, and then made as if to resume his way to the shop. But Renford proposed an amendment. “Don’t go to the shop,” he said, “I want to talk.”

“Well, can’t you talk in the shop?”

“Not what I want to tell you. It’s private. Come for a stroll.”

Harvey hesitated. There were few things he enjoyed so much as exclusive items of school gossip (scandal preferably), but hot new buns were among those few things. However, he decided on this occasion to feed the mind at the expense of the body. He accepted Renford’s invitation.

===Chinese===
西摩宿舍的哈维（Harvey）少爷，体质娇贵到这般地步：上午课程中间那一刻钟的间歇里，他非得享用一个或数个热面包不可，此乃绝对必需。特雷弗与奥哈拉练拳之后的第二天早晨，他正飞奔着横穿低年级砾石场奔向小卖部，一个悦耳的童声高音喊出了他的名字。是伦福德。他停住脚，等朋友赶上来，然后作出继续赶路的姿态。可伦福德提出了修正案。“别去小卖部，”他说，“我要跟你谈谈。”

“哦，在小卖部就不能谈？”

“我要说的不一样。是机密。来散个步。”

哈维犹豫了。天下他最爱的东西不多，校内秘闻独一份（丑闻尤佳）算一样，刚出炉的热面包也是为数不多的几样之一。不过这一次，他决定牺牲口腹，喂养精神。他接受了伦福德的邀请。

===Original===
“What is it?” he asked, as they made for the football field. “What’s been happening?”

“It’s frightfully exciting,” said Renford.

“What’s up?”

“You mustn’t tell anyone.”

“All right. Of course not.”

“Well, then, there’s been a big fight, and I’m one of the only chaps who know about it so far.”

===Chinese===
“什么事？”两人朝球场走去时他问，“出什么事了？”

“刺激极了。”伦福德说。

“到底怎么了？”

“你可不能告诉任何人。”

“行。当然不告诉。”

“那好，打了一场大架，到目前为止，知道的人里头数得上我的没几个。”

===Original===
“A fight?” Harvey became excited. “Who between?”

Renford paused before delivering his news, to emphasise the importance of it.

“It was between O’Hara and Rand-Brown,” he said at length.

“*By Jove!*” said Harvey. Then a suspicion crept into his mind.

===Chinese===
“打架？”哈维兴奋起来，“谁跟谁？”

伦福德发布新闻前先顿了顿，以示其分量。

“奥哈拉和兰德-布朗。”他终于说。

“*天哪！*”哈维说。随即一丝疑心爬上心头。

===Original===
“Look here, Renford,” he said, “if you’re trying to green me﻿—”

“I’m not, you ass,” replied Renford indignantly. “It’s perfectly true. I saw it myself.”

“By Jove, did you really? Where was it? When did it come off? Was it a good one? Who won?”

“It was the best one I’ve ever seen.”

===Chinese===
“我说，伦福德，”他说，“你要是想涮我——”

“才没有，你这蠢货，”伦福德愤然答道，“千真万确。我亲眼看见的。”

“天哪，真的？在哪儿打的？什么时候？打得好不好？谁赢了？”

“是我看过的最精彩的一场。”

===Original===
“Did O’Hara beat him? I hope he did. O’Hara’s a jolly good sort.”

“Yes. They had six rounds. Rand-Brown got knocked out in the middle of the sixth.”

“What, do you mean really knocked out, or did he just chuck it?”

“No. He was really knocked out. He was on the floor for quite a time. By Jove, you should have seen it. O’Hara was ripping in the sixth round. He was all over him.”

“Tell us about it,” said Harvey, and Renford told.

===Chinese===
“奥哈拉赢了他？但愿如此。奥哈拉是个好样儿的。”

“赢了。他们打了六个回合。第六回合打到一半，兰德-布朗被击倒了。”

“什么，是真被打倒的，还是他自己撂挑子？”

“不。是真被击倒了。在地上躺了好一阵呢。天哪，你真该看看。第六回合奥哈拉神勇极了。完全压着他打。”

“细说一遍。”哈维说。于是伦福德细说起来。

===Original===
“I’d got up early,” he said, “to feed the ferrets, and I was just cutting over to the fives-courts with their grub, when, just as I got across the senior gravel, I saw O’Hara and Moriarty standing waiting near the second court. O’Hara knows all about the ferrets, so I didn’t try and cut or anything. I went up and began talking to him. I noticed he didn’t look particularly keen on seeing me at first. I asked him if he was going to play fives. Then he said no, and told me what he’d really come for. He said he and Rand-Brown had had a row, and they’d agreed to have it out that morning in one of the fives-courts. Of course, when I heard that, I was all on to see it, so I said I’d wait, if he didn’t mind. He said he didn’t care, so long as I didn’t tell everybody, so I said I wouldn’t tell anybody except you, so he said all right, then, I could stop if I wanted to. So that was how I saw it. Well, after we’d been waiting a few minutes, Rand-Brown came in sight, with that beast Merrett in our house, who’d come to second him. It was just like one of those duels you read about, you know. Then O’Hara said that as I was the only one there with a watch﻿—he and Rand-Brown were in footer clothes, and Merrett and Moriarty hadn’t got their tickers on them﻿—I’d better act as timekeeper. So I said all right, I would, and we went to the second fives-court. It’s the biggest of them, you know. I stood outside on the bench, looking through the wire netting over the door, so as not to be in the way when they started scrapping. O’Hara and Rand-Brown took off their blazers and sweaters, and chucked them to Moriarty and Merrett, and then Moriarty and Merrett went and stood in two corners, and O’Hara and Rand-Brown walked into the middle and stood up to one another. Rand-Brown was miles the heaviest﻿—by a stone, I should think﻿—and he was taller and had a longer reach. But O’Hara looked much fitter. Rand-Brown looked rather flabby.

===Chinese===
“我起了个大早，”他说，“去喂雪貂，正拎着它们的口粮抄路去墙手球场（fives-courts），刚穿过高年级砾石场，就看见奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂站在第二球场边上等着。奥哈拉对雪貂的事一清二楚，所以我也不想绕道躲开什么的。我走过去跟他搭话，注意到他起初看上去不太乐意见到我。我问他是不是要打墙手球。他说不是，接着讲了他们真正的来意。他说他跟兰德-布朗吵了一架，两人说好当天早上在墙手球场里的一间解决。当然，一听这话我特别想看，便说要是他不介意，我就等在这儿。他说不介意，只要我不四处声张，我回答除了你我谁也不告诉，他说那行，我想留就留着吧。事情就是这样让我赶上的。呃，我们等了几分钟，兰德-布朗出现了，同来的还有我们宿舍那个讨厌鬼梅雷特（Merrett），来给他当副手。你知道，活脱脱像书里读到的那些决斗。这时奥哈拉说，既然在场只有我一个人有表——他和兰德-布朗穿着橄榄球衣，梅雷特和莫里亚蒂身上也没带表——我不如来当计时员。我说行，我来，于是我们走向第二墙手球场。那是几座里最大的一座，你知道。我站在门外的长凳上，隔着门上方的铁丝网往里看，免得他们开打时碍事。奥哈拉和兰德-布朗脱下运动夹克和毛衣，扔给莫里亚蒂和梅雷特，然后莫里亚蒂和梅雷特各站一个角落，奥哈拉和兰德-布朗走进场中央，面对面站定。兰德-布朗重得多——我看重了一英石——个子也高，臂展也长。可奥哈拉看上去状态好得多。兰德-布朗看着相当松垮。

===Original===
“I sang out ‘Time’ through the wire netting, and they started off at once. O’Hara offered to shake hands, but Rand-Brown wouldn’t. So they began without it.

“The first round was awfully fast. They kept having long rallies all over the place. O’Hara was a jolly sight quicker, and Rand-Brown didn’t seem able to guard his hits at all. But he hit frightfully hard himself, great, heavy slogs, and O’Hara kept getting them in the face. At last he got one bang in the mouth which knocked him down flat. He was up again in a second, and was starting to rush, when I looked at the watch, and found that I’d given them nearly half a minute too much already. So I shouted ‘Time,’ and made up my mind I’d keep more of an eye on the watch next round. I’d got so jolly excited, watching them, that I’d forgot I was supposed to be keeping time for them. They had only asked for a minute between the rounds, but as I’d given them half a minute too long in the first round, I chucked in a bit extra in the rest, so that they were both pretty fit by the time I started them again.

===Chinese===
“我隔着铁丝网喊了声『Time』，他们立刻交上了手。奥哈拉提议握手，兰德-布朗不肯。于是免了这一套就开打了。

“第一回合快得吓人。他们在场上四处游走，对攻个不停。奥哈拉快得多，兰德-布朗似乎完全防不住他的拳。可他自己的拳头打得出奇的重，又沉又猛的大抡拳，奥哈拉的脸上挨了好几下。终于有一记正中嘴巴，把他打了个仰面朝天。他一秒钟就爬起来，正要猛冲，我一看表，发现已经多给了他们将近半分钟。于是我喊『Time』，并打定主意下一回合要盯紧表。刚才看他们打架看得太兴奋，忘了自己还兼着计时。他们每回合之间只要求休息一分钟，可第一回合既然拖长了半分钟，后面每回休息我便都额外多给一点，好让我重新开打时，两个人都缓得差不多了。

===Original===
“The second round was just like the first, and so was the third. O’Hara kept getting the worst of it. He was knocked down three or four times more, and once, when he’d rushed Rand-Brown against one of the walls, he hit out and missed, and barked his knuckles jolly badly against the wall. That was in the middle of the third round, and Rand-Brown had it all his own way for the rest of the round﻿—for about two minutes, that is to say. He hit O’Hara about all over the shop. I was so jolly keen on O’Hara’s winning, that I had half a mind to call time early, so as to give him time to recover. But I thought it would be a low thing to do, so I gave them their full three minutes.

===Chinese===
“第二回合跟第一回合一个样，第三回合也是。奥哈拉一直吃亏。他又被打倒三四次，有一回他把兰德-布朗逼到墙边，出拳打空，指关节狠狠磕在了墙上。那是在第三回合中段，余下的时间里——大约两分钟吧——兰德-布朗随心所欲地称王称霸，把奥哈拉满场追着打。我一心盼着奥哈拉赢，一度想提前喊停，好让他缓口气。可转念一想这未免下作，便足量给了他们整整三分钟。

===Original===
“Directly they began the fourth round, I noticed that things were going to change a bit. O’Hara had given up his rushing game, and was waiting for his man, and when he came at him he’d put in a hot counter, nearly always at the body. After a bit Rand-Brown began to get cautious, and wouldn’t rush, so the fourth round was the quietest there had been. In the last minute they didn’t hit each other at all. They simply sparred for openings. It was in the fifth round that O’Hara began to forge ahead. About halfway through he got in a ripper, right in the wind, which almost doubled Rand-Brown up, and then he started rushing again. Rand-Brown looked awfully bad at the end of the round. Round six was ripping. I never saw two chaps go for each other so. It was one long rally. Then﻿—how it happened I couldn’t see, they were so quick﻿—just as they had been at it a minute and a half, there was a crack, and the next thing I saw was Rand-Brown on the ground, looking beastly. He went down absolutely flat; his heels and head touched the ground at the same time.

===Chinese===
“第四回合一开始，我就看出局势要变了。奥哈拉放弃了猛冲猛打的战术，改为守株待兔，等对方扑上来，便还一记狠辣的反击，几乎全打在躯干上。过了一阵兰德-布朗也谨慎起来，不肯冲了，于是第四回合是迄今最安静的一回。最后一分钟两人干脆谁也没碰到谁，光顾着游斗找破绽。到第五回合，奥哈拉开始后来居上。打到一半，他结结实实捅进一记狠拳，正中心口窝，打得兰德-布朗几乎折成两截，随后他又开始了冲锋。这一回合结束时，兰德-布朗的脸色难看极了。第六回合精彩纷呈。我从没见过两个家伙这么拼命互殴。对攻从头贯到尾。然后——怎么回事我没看清，他们太快了——打到一分半钟的时候，咔嚓一声，我接下来看到的，就是兰德-布朗躺在地上，狼狈不堪。他是彻底平拍在地上的；脚跟和后脑同时着地。

===Original===
“I counted ten out loud in the professional way like they do at the National Sporting Club, you know, and then said ‘O’Hara wins.’ I felt an awful swell. After about another half-minute, Rand-Brown was all right again, and he got up and went back to the house with Merrett, and O’Hara and Moriarty went off to Dexter’s, and I gave the ferrets their grub, and cut back to breakfast.”

“Rand-Brown wasn’t at breakfast,” said Harvey.

“No. He went to bed. I wonder what’ll happen. Think there’ll be a row about it?”

“Shouldn’t think so,” said Harvey. “They never do make rows about fights, and neither of them is a prefect, so I don’t see what it matters if they *do* fight. But, I say﻿—”

“What’s up?”

“I wish,” said Harvey, his voice full of acute regret, “that it had been my turn to feed those ferrets.”

“I don’t,” said Renford cheerfully. “I wouldn’t have missed that mill for something. Hullo, there’s the bell. We’d better run.”

===Chinese===
“我照国家体育俱乐部（National Sporting Club）的那套职业派头，出声数满了十下，然后宣布『奥哈拉胜』。我感觉自己威风极了。又过了约莫半分钟，兰德-布朗缓过来了，爬起来跟梅雷特回宿舍去了，奥哈拉和莫里亚蒂动身去德克斯特那儿，我给雪貂喂了食，撒腿回去吃早饭。”

“兰德-布朗没来吃早饭。”哈维说。

“嗯。他上床躺着去了。不知道会怎么样。你说会不会闹出乱子？”

“我看不会，”哈维说，“打架这种事从来没人追究，他们俩又都不是级长，所以就算真打了，我看也没什么要紧。不过，我说——”

“怎么？”

“真可惜，”哈维说，声音里满是深切的遗憾，那天喂雪貂的要是轮到我就好了。

“我可不觉得可惜，”伦福德乐呵呵地说，“那场架给我什么都不换。咦，铃响了。咱们快跑。”

===Original===
When Trevor called at Seymour’s that afternoon to see Rand-Brown, with a view to challenging him to deadly combat, and found that O’Hara had been before him, he ought to have felt relieved. His actual feeling was one of acute annoyance. It seemed to him that O’Hara had exceeded the limits of friendship. It was all very well for him to take over the Rand-Brown contract, and settle it himself, in order to save Trevor from a very bad quarter of an hour, but Trevor was one of those people who object strongly to the interference of other people in their private business. He sought out O’Hara and complained. Within two minutes O’Hara’s golden eloquence had soothed him and made him view the matter in quite a different light. What O’Hara pointed out was that it was not Trevor’s affair at all, but his own. Who, he asked, had been likely to be damaged most by Rand-Brown’s manoeuvres in connection with the lost bat? Trevor was bound to admit that O’Hara was that person. Very well, then, said O’Hara, then who had a better right to fight Rand-Brown? And Trevor confessed that no one else had a better.

===Chinese===
当天下午，特雷弗到西摩宿舍找兰德-布朗，打算约他决一死战，却发现奥哈拉捷足先登，按说该感到如释重负。他真实的感受却是恼火得很。在他看来，奥哈拉超出了友谊的界限。他一手接过兰德-布朗这份合同、亲自了结，好让特雷弗免遭难堪的一刻钟，这固然是一片好意，可特雷弗偏偏属于那种强烈反感他人插手自己私事的人。他找到奥哈拉，发了牢骚。不到两分钟，奥哈拉的黄金口才便安抚了他，让他换了个全新的角度看这事。奥哈拉指出的是：这根本不是特雷弗的事，而是他自己的事。他问，就丢球棒一事而论，兰德-布朗的种种运作最可能坑害的是谁？特雷弗不得不承认，那人是奥哈拉。很好，奥哈拉说，那么谁更有权利跟兰德-布朗打这一架？特雷弗承认，没有谁比他更有了。

===Original===
“Then I suppose,” he said, “that I shall have to do nothing about it?”

“That’s it,” said O’Hara.

“It’ll be rather beastly meeting the man after this,” said Trevor, presently. “Do you think he might possibly leave at the end of term?”

“He’s leaving at the end of the week,” said O’Hara. “He was one of the fellows Dexter caught in the vault that evening. You won’t see much more of Rand-Brown.”

===Chinese===
“那我猜，”他说，“我就什么也不用干了？”

“正是。”奥哈拉说。

“今后见了这人，可够别扭的，”过了一会儿特雷弗说，“你看他有没有可能学期末就走？”

“他本周末就走，”奥哈拉说，“那天晚上德克斯特在地窖里逮住的人里头，就有他。兰德-布朗你见不了几面了。”

===Original===
“I’ll try and put up with that,” said Trevor.

“And so will I,” replied O’Hara. “And I shouldn’t think Milton would be so very grieved.”

“No,” said Trevor. “I tell you what will make him sick, though, and that is your having milled with Rand-Brown. It’s a job he’d have liked to have taken on himself.”

===Chinese===
“我尽量忍着。”特雷弗说。

“我也是，”奥哈拉答道，“再说我看弥尔顿也不会太伤心。”

“是啊，”特雷弗说，“不过有一件事准让他吐血：就是你跟兰德-布朗干的这一架。这活儿他原本巴不得自己揽下来呢。”

## VII “With the Compliments of the League” / “联盟敬赠”

===Original===
Trevor went and looked.

It was rather an interesting sight. An earthquake or a cyclone might have made it a little more picturesque, but not much more. The general effect was not unlike that of an American saloon, after a visit from Mrs. Carrie Nation (with hatchet). As in the case of Mill’s study, the only thing that did not seem to have suffered any great damage was the table. Everything else looked rather off colour. The mantelpiece had been swept as bare as a bone, and its contents littered the floor. Trevor dived among the debris and retrieved the latest addition to his art gallery, the photograph of this year’s first fifteen. It was a wreck. The glass was broken and the photograph itself slashed with a knife till most of the faces were unrecognisable. He picked up another treasure, last year’s first eleven. Smashed glass again. Faces cut about with knife as before. His collection of snapshots was torn into a thousand fragments, though, as Mr. Jerome said of the papier-mâché trout, there may only have been nine hundred. He did not count them. His bookshelf was empty. The books had gone to swell the contents of the floor. There was a Shakespeare with its cover off. Pages twenty-two to thirty-one of *Vice Versa* had parted from the parent establishment, and were lying by themselves near the door. *The Rogues’ March* lay just beyond them, and the look of the cover suggested that somebody had either been biting it or jumping on it with heavy boots.

===Chinese===
特雷弗去看了。

景象相当可观。地震或旋风或许能让它再添几分画意，但也添不了多少。总体效果，颇似卡丽·纳逊夫人（Mrs. Carrie Nation）提着斧头光顾之后的美国酒馆。跟米尔的书房一样，唯一似乎未遭重创的是桌子。其余一切看上去都有些蔫头耷脑。壁炉台被扫得像骨头一样精光，台上的物件撒了一地。特雷弗一头扎进废墟，抢救出他画廊的最新藏品——今年一队的合影。已是废品一张。玻璃碎了，照片本身叫刀子划得多数面孔无法辨认。他又拾起另一件珍藏：去年板球一队的合影。玻璃照碎。面孔照旧被刀划花。他的快照集被撕成一千块碎片——不过，正如杰罗姆先生（Mr. Jerome）议论那只纸模鳟鱼时所说，也可能只有九百块。他没有数。书架空了。书本全去给地板添堵了。一本莎士比亚掉了封皮。《正相反》（*Vice Versa*）的第二十二页到三十一页与母体机构分了家，独自躺在门边。《流氓进行曲》（*The Rogues’ March*）躺在再过去一点的地方，封面的惨状表明，有人要么啃过它，要么穿着重靴子在上面蹦跶过。

===Original===
There was other damage. Over the mantelpiece in happier days had hung a dozen sea gulls’ eggs, threaded on a string. The string was still there, as good as new, but of the eggs nothing was to be seen, save a fine parti-coloured powder﻿—on the floor, like everything else in the study. And a good deal of ink had been upset in one place and another.

Trevor had been staring at the ruins for some time, when he looked up to see Clowes standing in the doorway.

“Hullo,” said Clowes, “been tidying up?”

Trevor made a few hasty comments on the situation. Clowes listened approvingly.

===Chinese===
还有别的损失。往昔的好日子里，壁炉台上方曾悬挂着一打海鸥蛋，串在一根绳上。绳子还在，完好如新，海鸥蛋却踪影全无，只见一摊斑驳的细粉——也在地板上，跟书房里其余一切作伴。还有相当多的墨水，这儿那儿地泼洒了一地。

特雷弗对着这片废墟凝望了半晌，一抬头，看见克劳斯站在门口。

“咦，”克劳斯说，“在收拾屋子呢？”

特雷弗就当前局势发表了几句仓促的评论。克劳斯听得连连赞许。

===Original===
“Don’t you think,” he went on, eyeing the study with a critical air, “that you’ve got too many things on the floor, and too few anywhere else? And I should move some of those books on to the shelf, if I were you.”

Trevor breathed very hard.

“I should like to find the chap who did this,” he said softly.

Clowes advanced into the room and proceeded to pick up various misplaced articles of furniture in a helpful way.

“I thought so,” he said presently, “come and look here.”

===Chinese===
“你不觉得吗，”他继续说，用挑剔的目光打量着书房，“地板上的东西太多，别处的东西太少？换了是我，就把那些书挪几本上架去。”

特雷弗重重地喘着气。

“我真想找出干这事的家伙。”他轻声说。

克劳斯走进屋来，热心地把几件放错了位置的家具一一扶起。

“我料到了，”过了片刻他说，“过来看看这个。”

===Original===
Tied to a chair, exactly as it had been in the case of Mill, was a neat white card, and on it were the words, “With the Compliments of the League.”

“What are you going to do about this?” asked Clowes. “Come into my room and talk it over.”

“I’ll tidy this place up first,” said Trevor. He felt that the work would be a relief. “I don’t want people to see this. It mustn’t get about. I’m not going to have my study turned into a sort of sideshow, like Mill’s. You go and change. I shan’t be long.”

“I will never desert Mr. Micawber,” said Clowes. “Friend, my place is by your side. Shut the door and let’s get to work.”

===Chinese===
拴在椅子上——跟米尔那次分毫不差——是一张精致的白卡片，上书一行字：“联盟敬赠。”

“这事你打算怎么办？”克劳斯问，“到我屋里来商量商量。”

“我先把这儿收拾干净，”特雷弗说。他觉得这活儿干起来倒是种解脱。“我不想让人看见。绝不能传出去。我可不要我的书房变成米尔的那种杂耍摊。你去换衣服吧。我很快完事。”

“我永远不会抛弃米考伯先生（Mr. Micawber），”克劳斯说，“朋友，我的岗位在你身边。关门，动手吧。”

===Original===
Ten minutes later the room had resumed a more or less﻿—though principally less﻿—normal appearance. The books and chairs were back in their places. The ink was sopped up. The broken photographs were stacked in a neat pile in one corner, with a rug over them. The mantelpiece was still empty, but, as Clowes pointed out, it now merely looked as if Trevor had been pawning some of his household gods. There was no sign that a devastating secret society had raged through the study.

Then they adjourned to Clowes’ study, where Trevor sank into Clowes’ second-best chair﻿—Clowes, by an adroit movement, having appropriated the best one﻿—with a sigh of enjoyment. Running and passing, followed by the toil of furniture-shifting, had made him feel quite tired.

===Chinese===
十分钟后，房间恢复了多少算得上——主要是算不上——正常的外观。书本和椅子各归各位。墨水吸干了。破碎的照片在墙角码成整齐的一摞，上面盖了条小地毯。壁炉台依旧空着，但正如克劳斯所指出的，如今看上去不过是像特雷弗典当了几件家神罢了。毫无迹象表明，曾有一个破坏力惊人的秘密社团在这书房里发过飙。

随后两人移驾克劳斯的书房。特雷弗一屁股陷进克劳斯的次等椅——最好的一把，已被克劳斯用一个机敏的动作占了去——惬意地叹了口气。跑步传球，外加搬家具的辛劳，已经让他相当疲乏了。

===Original===
“It doesn’t look so bad now,” he said, thinking of the room they had left. “By the way, what did you do with that card?”

“Here it is. Want it?”

“You can keep it. I don’t want it.”

“Thanks. If this sort of things goes on, I shall get quite a nice collection of these cards. Start an album some day.”

“You know,” said Trevor, “this is getting serious.”

===Chinese===
“现在看上去不那么糟了，”他想着刚才那间屋子说，“对了，那张卡片你怎么处置的？”

“在这儿呢。要吗？”

“你留着吧。我不要。”

“多谢。这要再来上几桩，这些卡片我就能攒出相当漂亮的一批了。哪天开个收藏册。”

“你知道，”特雷弗说，“事情闹大了。”

===Original===
“It always does get serious when anything bad happens to one’s self. It always strikes one as rather funny when things happen to other people. When Mill’s study was wrecked, I bet you regarded it as an amusing and original ‘turn.’ What do you think of the present effort?”

“Who on earth can have done it?”

“The Pres﻿—”

“Oh, dry up. Of course it was. But who the blazes is he?”

“Nay, children, you have me there,” quoted Clowes. “I’ll tell you one thing, though. You remember what I said about it’s probably being Rand-Brown. He can’t have done this, that’s certain, because he was out in the fields the whole time. Though I don’t see who else could have anything to gain by Barry not getting his colours.”

===Chinese===
“坏事落到自己头上，事情总会闹大。落到别人头上，就总显得颇为滑稽。米尔的书房被砸那会儿，我敢说你把它当作一出新鲜别致的‘压轴戏’。那这一回的活儿，你又作何评价？”

“到底能是谁干的？”

“是会——”

“哦，闭嘴。当然是它干的。可他见鬼的到底是谁？”

“不，孩子们，这可问倒我了。”克劳斯引经据典地说。“不过有一件事我可以告诉你。记得我说过，很可能是兰德-布朗。这件事不可能是他干的，这点千真万确，因为整个那段时间他都在外面的场子上。可要说还有谁能让巴里拿不到队色而从中得利，我也想不出。”

===Original===
“There’s no reason to suspect him at all, as far as I can see. I don’t know much about him, bar the fact that he can’t play footer for nuts, but I’ve never heard anything against him. Have you?”

“I scarcely know him myself. He isn’t liked in Seymour’s, I believe.”

“Well, anyhow, this can’t be his work.”

“That’s what I said.”

“For all we know, the League may have got their knife into Barry for some reason. You said they used to get their knife into fellows in that way. Anyhow, I mean to find out who ragged my room.”

“It wouldn’t be a bad idea,” said Clowes.

===Chinese===
“依我看，根本没理由怀疑他。我对他了解不多，只知道他橄榄球打得稀烂，可从没听说过他有什么劣迹。你听说过吗？”

“我自己也几乎不认识他。听说他在西摩宿舍人缘不好。”

“嗯，反正这不可能是他干的。”

“我正是这么说的。”

“说不定联盟出于某种缘故跟巴里结了仇。你说过，他们从前就爱这么记恨人。总之，我打算查出捣我院子的是谁。”

“这主意不坏。”克劳斯说。

---

===Original===
